# The production-bar design fleet — full harvest (2026-07-17)

89 agents, 0 errors, 15.3M subagent tokens, 1987 tool uses. 115 design items,
91 adversarial verdicts (69 REFUTED, 22 CORRECTED), 1 completeness critic.

Harvested from journal.jsonl, NOT the completion notification (which truncated
at 1,183,831 chars). Extractor: the session scratchpad's harvest2.py — it joins a
verdict to its design through each verifier's own transcript, because journal
result lines carry no label. Every one of the 94 verdict agents is accounted for.

READ THE VERDICT WORD, NOT THE FLAG: the brief said 'default to refuted=true if
uncertain', so CORRECTED (the design survives, here are the fixes) also carries
refuted=true. 22 designs survive that a flag-read would have binned.

---

==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.cursor.speculative-compile
  A different, already-named fanout site — proposing N candidate annotations for one gradient decision and verifying each on its own forked graph checkpoint — needs a checkpoint-fork of the per-thread graph, not the disjoint-write reservation layer 4 needs, and the two must not be conflated into one mechanism.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the headline distinction (speculative-fork ≠ layer 4's disjoint reservation) is confirmed and understated by the artifact, but the design's load-bearing mechanism ("a bounded FORK of graph_handler's nodes/trail — a real, cheap copy"), its wire protocol, its gate, and its sequencing are each contradicted by the artifact and must not be built as written.
  corrections: 1. MECHANISM — delete "a real, cheap copy." Never copy `nodes`. The native form is a per-fork WRITE-REDIRECT at node granularity: the candidate's bind of `h` lands in the fork's own write-set; a read of `h` resolves fork-first, then falls through to the shared base image (unchanged, lock-free, per PLAN.md:269). The fork's write-set is a SINGLETON here (`h`, src/mentl.mn:359) — that is what "cheap" actually names, and it is cheap because nothing is copied, not because the copy is small. Do not assert which existing structure hosts it (the trail at src/graph.mn:120-123 stores the inverse/old value for `revert_trail`, so it is a rollback log, not a read-through overlay as-is; the `overlay_*` arrays at src/graph.mn:41-46 are a per-module FRESH-ALLOCATION registry, not copy-on-write) — the honest statement is that the fork's override set is new, tiny, and read-through, and that a `nodes` copy is refuted.

2. WIRE — the capability must not cross the thread boundary. `unlock_capability` is `with Pure`, a total match on the annotation alone (src/mentl.mn:523-531), and the parent ALREADY computes it at src/mentl.mn:254, outside the checkpoint region, from the `ann` it holds. "the chosen capability crosses back via the closure's existing result slot" re-derives a fact the parent reads live — Carried-Truth violated inside the design's own protocol. Only the boolean crosses (and it fits the single i32 at threading.mn:215-217, which the capability would not).

3. INTERNAL CONTRADICTION — the item says the two "must not be conflated into one mechanism"; INTERCONNECTION says "converge on one enhanced graph.mn capability offering two modes." One capability with two modes IS conflation behind a mode flag (drift 8). Pick one: either they are distinct primitives (then INTERCONNECTION is wrong), or one primitive whose variation is an ADT read live, never "modes." Note also that "permanent extension" is just an ephemeral one never rolled back — every write already appends to the trail (src/graph.mn:120-123), so the second mode may not exist at all.

4. GATE — unbuildable as written, and insensitive where it is buildable.
   (a) "Extend try_each_annotation with a ~> Thread-scheduled variant" collides with the schedule law. `try_each_annotation` has TWO live callers — src/mentl.mn:158 (`gradient_next`) and src/oracle.mn:219 (`candidates_at`) — so it is a reusable fn, and docs/SYNTAX.md:333-338 is explicit: the schedule "is read LIVE at the fanout's own install site … never across a call boundary, so a `><` inside a reusable fn is permanently `Seq`"; caller-selectable fanout in a reusable helper "is the open peer `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee`." The design's OWN cited comment names that peer (src/mentl.mn:240-241) and the design drops it. Also, "variant" = a second near-identical fn, which is the drift itself; the sequential baseline must come from installing `~> Seq` at the same fanout, not from keeping two fns.
   (b) The parity assertion is blind to the failure it gates. `verify_after_apply` returns true iff the chased node is NBound (src/mentl.mn:376-385) — a coarse boolean. A leaked sibling bind that leaves `h` NBound with the WRONG row still returns true, and "identical winning capability" is a pure function of which ann won (src/mentl.mn:523-531), so it carries zero extra information. The gate must compare the resulting TYPE/ROW at `h` per candidate against the sequential run, not the accept/reject boolean — otherwise it passes while isolation is broken.

5. SEQUENCING — "Independent of Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk … can land in parallel" is refuted by PLAN.md:846, which sequences `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` "behind `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk`, DEP-gated on band-A `sound-neg-under-poly`." Since the fanout site is a two-caller reusable fn, this item inherits that DEP on level-set-par-walk AND on the crown. Either state that dependency honestly, or scope the fanout so the `~> Thread` installs at the single use site inside `try_each_annotation` unconditionally — which forfeits caller-selectability (oracle.mn:219 gets threaded whether it wants it or not) and must be said out loud. The design's claimed sole prerequisite ("graph.mn growing SOME notion of a thread-safe write-extension") is incomplete on both counts.

6. NOVELTY — the verdict survives but is softer than claimed. Keeping portfolio solving is defensible via PLAN §1's OGIS/Synthesis-Modulo-Oracles framing, and "the fork mechanism underneath is native" is the right instinct. But the design then reaches for a foreign shape anyway: a solver-state SNAPSHOT is exactly what "a real, cheap copy" is, renamed. The native form is not a snapshot at all — the base image is never copied, never locked, and the fork's override set is one node. State that, and the novelty claim becomes real rather than a slogan.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.tools.thread-determinism-gate
  "It still compiles" is not a gate — the mechanical check this cluster needs is byte-identical WAT output for the SAME entry-module compile across two adversarial interleavings (a deterministic spawn-order reversal, and a real single-core-forced OS preemption), following the exact verdict-script convention already established by tools/march.sh and its siblings.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the need, the fixture design, and the sequencing verify exactly against the artifact, but the gate's central mechanism (Run B's "gate-only flag") names a function that does not and will not exist, its two possible homes each refute it, and the design excludes the one oracle the artifact already names, leaving a gate that checks agreement rather than correctness — and passes vacuously through .build/m2cache as literally specified.
  corrections: The item SHOULD exist and its sequencing is right. Six corrections; 1-3 are load-bearing.

1. DELETE THE FLAG; ADD A PEER HANDLER. Run B becomes a third Schedule peer beside parallel_compose (threading.mn:119) and sequential_compose (threading.mn:137) — a handler whose `spawn` arm dispatches the ffi_spawn calls in reverse (or with injected delay). Zero source flags, zero driver changes; the gate is three `~>` installs of ONE unmodified source. This is what threading.mn:289-290 ("peer handlers") and lower.mn:1789-1799 already exist to permit.

2. RESTORE `~> Seq` AS THE ORACLE. "never sequential_compose — that would prove nothing about concurrency" is contradicted three ways by the artifact: PLAN.md:1422-1423 names the thesis gate verbatim as "SAME source under `~> Seq`/`~> Thread`/`~> Gpu`, identical results, race-freedom carried by the ownership proof"; src/main.mn:190-192 names sequential_compose "the deterministic-output override"; threading.mn:298-300 makes Seq-vs-Thread the same-source comparison. Seq is not worthless — it is the ONLY oracle available. Without it the gate checks AGREEMENT, not CORRECTNESS: two threaded runs sharing one wrong-but-deterministic reservation drop the same decl and agree, and the gate is green. PLAN §3 L7 names this exact failure ("a buggy compiler self-reproduces to a wrong fixpoint"). The gate is Seq | Thread | Thread-reversed | Thread-under-taskset, all pairwise.

3. THE GATE MUST BYPASS .build/m2cache, EXPLICITLY AND IN A COMMENT. `wt_m2_key()` (tools/wt-env.sh) = sha256(wheel bytes + boot bytes + WT_RUN_FLAGS) — it contains NEITHER spawn order NOR CPU affinity. Runs A/B/C share one key; `wt_m2_ensure` returns one cached file; `diff -q` compares a file to itself. PASS, vacuously, forever, under a catastrophic race. The design walks into this by naming the convention that contains it — "the exact verdict-script convention already established by tools/march.sh and its siblings", and march.sh:82 IS `wt_m2_ensure`. Deeper: wt-env.sh's stated premise, "boot(wheel) is DETERMINISTIC ... so the compile is a pure function of (wheel bytes, boot bytes, run flags)", is exactly the property under test — a determinism gate sharing a cache keyed on the assumption of determinism is circular by construction. wt_state_key's own comment names the bug class: "under-inclusion is the bug — include every file whose change can change the verdict."

4. THE CILKSAN CITATION IS FABRICATED. "Cilksan (determinacy-race detection — PLAN band E's own text already names it as a gate)": grep for Cilksan|cilksan|TSan|ThreadSanitizer|determinacy across PLAN.md, CLAUDE.md, docs/SYNTAX.md returns ZERO hits. PLAN band E (PLAN.md:846) names `Hβ.parallel.thread-alloc-transitive-proof` ("verify ONLY after the leak closes") and `.race-freedom-ownership-proof` — no dynamic race detector, no gate. The novelty verdict's SUBSTANCE survives without the citation (dynamic detection as honest scaffold standing in for the unlanded static proof is defensible, and matches PLAN §0's V_Pending-over-fabrication choice). Delete the false citation; keep the argument (Law 5).

5. BLAST RADIUS UNSTATED. Landing Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk invalidates wt-env.sh's purity premise for all SEVEN consumers of the shared cache: march.sh:82, march-gate.sh:60, crown-gate.sh:23, proof-exactness-gate.sh:17, verify.sh:92, frontier-gate.sh:54 and :59. The design must say this. Name the peer positively: Hβ.tools.m2cache-key-under-concurrency.

6. Minor: "a single-word scoreboard line matching tools/state.sh's existing column convention" — state.sh has no column convention. Its `gate()` (state.sh:19-31) prints "▸ <label>" then the indented `tail -1` of the gate's output, surfacing RED|✗ lines on failure. Match THAT.

WHAT VERIFIES AND MUST BE KEPT: the artifact_state is TRUE (0 hits for thread-determinism-gate; the fanout is not built — driver.mn:183 plus its own comment at 168-170; march.sh's TRANSITION-vs-BROKEN classifier is real, spanning 119-148 with diff-line reporting at 126 and 134 exactly as cited). The 7 zero-import fixture list is EXACTLY correct — enumerating every .mn under src/ and lib/ with no `import` line returns those 7 files, no more, no fewer; the synthetic 16-32-module fixture is justified because 7 is a thin natural widest layer. Run C (taskset -c 0) is the design's best original contribution and survives unchanged: it needs zero Mentl-source changes and is the only leg testing real OS preemption — but it is ONE sample; say so. Run B's TARGET property is the right one: threading.mn:112-113 claims "output tuple order is preserved regardless of completion order — multi-core determinism by construction", and an unverified by-construction claim is exactly what deserves a gate. Right property, wrong mechanism. The sequencing (gate immediately after the first working fanout, before any perf tuning) is correct per Law 7.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.tools.bootstrap-via-driver-path
  Layer 4, even fully built and gated, will not speed up the wheel's own self-hosting fixpoint check (the ~10s number in PLAN §7's landing ledger) — that check compiles via the stdin/single-concatenated-blob path, which bypasses driver.mn entirely, so a separate, sequenced switch is required before the wheel's own bootstrap benefits at all.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the headline scoping finding is TRUE and I could not refute it (the fixpoint check really does go through the stdin blob and bypass driver.mn; every cited line checks out), but the design built on it is contradicted by the artifact: `mentl compile main` through the driver path today emits a module that DOES NOT ASSEMBLE, so "zero changes to the wheel's own source" is false and "already claims byte-for-byte equivalence" is a misread of a form-claim as a bytes-claim.
  corrections: 1. DELETE "already claims byte-for-byte equivalence to the blob form." driver.mn:196-201 claims the same FORM (one-namespace concatenation, deps-first/post-order/entry-last), never the same bytes. Its own wording kills the byte reading: blob = alphabetical (main at 15/50), post-order = main at 31/31.

2. DELETE "a change entirely inside the tooling layer, zero changes to the wheel's own source." Measured, the switch requires wheel-source changes or it emits a non-assembling module. Name the real prerequisite in positive form: **Hβ.driver.wheel-imports-are-the-manifest** — make the wheel's own import graph reach every file the blob concatenates (starting: nothing imports lib/prelude.mn), so the edge genuinely carries the fact instead of bash carrying it.

3. INVERT the Graph? interrogation. The design says this is "the purest instance of 'does the graph already know this' ... the only gap is that the bootstrap tooling never calls it." Backwards. The graph does NOT know this. PLAN §11's novelty audit asserts "the imports ARE the manifest (the kernel already holds the dep DAG as edges)" — for the wheel's OWN source that is currently FALSE. `find src lib | xargs cat` (wt-env.sh:71-80) is the real manifest: a bash-side side-ledger the import edges cannot reproduce. THAT is the Carried-Truth violation and the actual work item; the tooling switch is its trivial consequence, not the item.

4. ORDER is a second, independent prerequisite. Even after the set is made whole, post-order != alphabetical. PLAN §7 records source order moving emitted bytes ("interpolation source-order (204k→2.7k diff)"). Either reconcile the concatenation order to the blob's, or accept the emit change explicitly.

5. CORRECT the blast radius. "Zero changes to the wheel's own source" implies a Law-7 no-op. Adding import edges and changing concatenation order changes emitted bytes → m2 != m3 → a TRANSITION per PLAN §8 (march.sh runs the m4 leg itself and rules TRANSITION vs BROKEN; re-pin boot from m3, never m2). The design must say this; today it says the opposite.

6. KEEP THE GATE — it is the item's best part and it is not vacuous. It is mechanically checkable and it FAILS today on the sequential driver path, exactly where a good gate should. But state its measured verdict honestly (FAILS: 16,176,251 vs 16,168,850, non-assembling, 6 undefined symbols) instead of predicting it passes. It is the ENTRY criterion for the prerequisite peer, not a formality before the switch.

7. RE-SEQUENCE. The item is not "last in this cluster, after Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk and Hβ.tools.thread-determinism-gate." Its prerequisite (correction 2) is a correctness/Carried-Truth item independent of both and can land first — it needs no parallelism to exist. The parity gate can go green on the sequential driver path before layer 4 starts, which is precisely the isolation the design's own gate asks for.

8. NOVELTY VERDICT "not applicable" is a mild dodge. The blob IS the borrowed shape — a build script's hand-maintained file list standing in for the graph's own edges — and PLAN §11 already ruled on it ("the imports ARE the manifest"). The artifact currently contradicts that ruling. That is the novelty axis this item actually sits on, and stating it strengthens the finding rather than weakening it.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr
  This peer is DONE — PExpr was dissolved before this cluster started; PLAN.md band F still lists it as open, chained into smt-handler-swap, and must be corrected.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — PExpr is genuinely dissolved and the band F edit is right, but the design's "arm 1 fully satisfied / already the ultimate form" certification is contradicted by the write-only `Pat` in `PPatternMatch(Pat, Int)` (types.mn:1109, zero readers across 7 sites), the pure-deletion edit would leave the smt-handler-swap encoder's pattern-dropping arm (verify.mn:336) as an unnamed gap in the band it re-anchors, and the "66/66" gate number is stale (measured 71).
  corrections: THREE surgical corrections; the deliverable survives all of them.

1. STRIKE the arm-1 certification. The design asserts "Graph (arm 1) — fully satisfied; every operand IS a handle, chased live, never copied." FALSE for the whole ADT. `PPatternMatch(Pat, Int)` (types.mn:1109) carries a structural `Pat` that is not a handle and has ZERO readers — all 7 consumers wildcard it: query.mn:282, infer.mn:4428, verify.mn:68, verify.mn:108 (threads it through subst_self unread), verify.mn:261, verify.mn:336, types.mn:2066 (renders "matches _", discarding it). Correct text: "arm 1 satisfied for PCompare/PBoolNode operands and `self`-substitution; PPatternMatch's Pat field is write-only." Be honest about WHY this is not the PExpr trap recurring: `npat` (parser.mn:38) is never called, so `NPat` (types.mn:842) is a dormant node kind and the pattern lives nowhere else — a write-only field awaiting a reader, not a lossy copy. But zero-readers is precisely the shape PLAN §5.U names "the textbook Carried-Truth violation" (re: resume_kinds).

2. REPLACE band F line 848, do not merely DELETE. Dropping `Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr (dissolve PExpr) →` is right. But band F then begins at `Hβ.verify.smt-handler-swap` while the encoder that item installs a solver UNDER silently drops the pattern half of every match-narrowing predicate. Name the residue positively as a DEP of smt-handler-swap:
   - `Hβ.verify.pattern-match-smt-encoding` — predicate_to_smt (verify.mn:336) reduces `PPatternMatch(pat, n)` to `handle_to_smt(n)`. verify.mn:68's own comment promises "match-narrowing — the SMT tier evaluates"; the SMT tier's encoder wildcards it. body_to_smt's doc (verify.mn:360-366) states the law the arm breaks: a non-encodable shape "raises E_VerifyUnencodable (Located) and renders the unsat-forcing sentinel `false` — NEVER a fabricated `true`". The arm neither raises nor sentinels; for a bool scrutinee it asserts "scrut is true" instead of "scrut matches pat" — the wrong proposition. LATENT, not live: verify_smt is never installed (only verify_ledger, 10 sites — pipeline.mn:74/153/169/195/213/239/265, main.mn:278/341/504).
   - `Hβ.types.ppatternmatch-pat-is-handle` — weave the pattern through the already-built-but-dormant npat/NPat and carry the handle, dissolving the write-only field. This is the SAME design as the PExpr dissolution one altitude up: carry the handle, read live, delete the copy. Naming it is what makes the PExpr dissolution's own lesson generalize instead of stopping at the constructor it happened to hit.

3. FIX the stale gate number. "The existing 66/66 micro battery" → 71. Measured: `FORCE_VERIFY=1 bash tools/verify.sh` emits 71 `✓ micro` lines; PLAN §7 also says 71. 66 was the 2026-07-12 count (PLAN ledger, "M3 THE CUT ... 52→66"). Minor, but it is a remembered number in a design whose own discipline is read-the-board.

Also scope the novelty note honestly. "Mentl's dissolution of PExpr means the refinement predicate has no second IR" is true of the PREDICATE representation and false of the proof pipeline: predicate_to_smt/body_to_smt emit SMT-LIB strings — a second representation, hand-maintained, which has ALREADY drifted out of sync with verify.mn:68's promise. That drift proves the design's own Liquid-Haskell/F* point in reverse and belongs in the text as the sharp edge, not omitted.

GATE, honestly: "No new gate needed" holds for the claimed scope (the refine micros do exercise subst_self operand repointing). It is vacuous for the residue — no micro exercises match-narrowing SMT, because verify_smt is uninstalled. Say so rather than let the green board imply coverage.

BLAST RADIUS: correctly stated. A PLAN.md-only edit cannot touch m2==m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, or the 71 micros.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.own.region-class-aware-lookup
  The region-tag lookup resolves a value's union-find canonical handle before consulting region_index, so a returned value's tag is found even when the checked handle is an alias (a BlockExpr's own handle, a let-bound VarRef) rather than the exact handle tag_alloc/tag_alloc_join stamped.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — every cited line checks out, the mechanism is sound (I tried to break it on union-find root migration and the artifact disproved me), and the item is the named burden from its own landing commit; but the e-graph clause is unsound, the prescribed call `chase_handle(nodes, ...)` is unwritable, the change belongs at the one read choke-point rather than eight write sites, and the design missed the permanent gate already sitting in the repo.
  corrections: 1. DELETE THE E-GRAPH CLAUSE; NAME THE PEER INSTEAD. Strike "Chase both weaves, TVar-alias first then e-graph-canon". The native form is the opposite of composing them: if a region fact must survive saturation, it travels with the MERGE as a join — the deepest-region max own.mn:280-288 already computes across fields, lifted to the equivalence edge — never a read-time chase through canon. Name it positively: Hβ.own.region-tag-joins-at-canon-merge (a region fact is an allocation-site identity; it joins when two e-classes merge, deepest wins; it is never re-read through a value-equivalence pointer). The design's "matching how egraph_extract already reads graph_canon_at live" is a false parallel — egraph_extract chases canon to get the cheapest EQUAL TERM, which is the right question for extraction and the wrong one for lifetime.

2. `chase_handle(nodes, handle, 0)` IS UNWRITABLE — say `graph_chase_handle(handle)`, and own the row change. `nodes` is graph_handler's handler state (graph.mn:89-90: `graph_chase_handle(handle) => { resume(chase_handle(nodes, handle, 0)) }`); it is in scope nowhere else. Every `nodes` binding in infer.mn is an AST-node list, not the graph array (infer.mn:3940, 4135, 4382, 4476, 4478). The reachable form is the GraphRead op declared at types.mn:1222 — which is what unify (infer.mn:1765) and build_inst_mapping (infer.mn:3082, not 3081; 3081 is the comment) actually call, so "the identical find walk unify/instantiate run millions of times" is true of the OP, not the fn. This is not cosmetic: own.mn's lookup_tag (own.mn:245) and join_field_regions (own.mn:280) are declared `with !Mutate` and gain GraphRead — a ROW change on region_tracker's arms, not "one extra graph read". It is feasible and I verified why: graph_handler (pipeline.mn:79) is installed OUTSIDE region_tracker (pipeline.mn:73), so the arm's perform resolves, and the arms already perform Memory+Alloc (region_index_add, own.mn:249) and report (own.mn:231), so arm-internal performs are established. State the row change; do not call it free.

3. WRONG PLACEMENT, AND IT IS CARRIED-TRUTH'S OWN POINT — CHASE AT THE READ CHOKE-POINT, NOT AT EIGHT WRITE SITES. Because every tagged handle is NBound at tag time (evidence in the objection above), chasing at tag_alloc/tag_alloc_join is PROVABLY THE IDENTITY on every non-error path — dead code that would rot. The reads are own.mn:213 and own.mn:282, and both already funnel through ONE fn, lookup_tag (own.mn:245) — the single reader a44baa55 deliberately established ("Both lookup_tag callers were migrated to the index"). One chase inside lookup_tag covers both; the design's "change the handle argument at every call site" is the more-code form of a one-line choke-point fix (CENSUS-never-moles; the fix is LESS code). Two consequences the design must add: (a) own.mn:227's `region_index_add(region_index, value_handle, (install_span, parent))` — the TRANSFER re-tag — writes at the RAW handle and is named nowhere in the design (its enumeration is "lookup_tag/tag_alloc/tag_alloc_join"; 227 is none of the three). Under the design's placement it is consistent by luck; under the choke-point form it MUST chase, or the re-tag lands at the alias key while every read chases to the root and finds the stale tag. (b) The choke-point form fixes for free what the design's placement leaves broken: "the handle argument" is singular, but tag_alloc_join(handle, field_handles) has TWO handle arguments, and own.mn:282's per-field `lookup_tag(tags, fh)` reads RAW field handles from collect_handles/record_field_handles. `let inner = Held(2); (inner, 3)` therefore never pins the composite to inner's region — precisely what tag_alloc_join exists to prevent (own.mn:136-137, 184-191). The design cannot both prescribe `chase_handle(nodes, ...)` at the infer.mn perform sites AND cover own.mn:282, because `nodes` does not exist in own.mn; it is internally inconsistent on this point.

4. "THE SIX INFER.MN PERFORM SITES" UNDERCOUNTS — THERE ARE EIGHT, and the design's own ARTIFACT STATE paragraph names all of them: check_escape at infer.mn:434, tag_alloc_join at 650/711/717/755/781/806 (six), tag_alloc at 891. Say eight, or (better, per correction 3) say two — own.mn:213 and own.mn:282, both via lookup_tag.

5. THE GATE MISSES THE PERMANENT ONE ALREADY IN THE REPO. Part (1) is an honest no-regression but passes vacuously pre-fix (it is today's state). Part (2) is hand-added instrumentation the design itself says to remove — not a standing gate. The real gate is sitting in tests/micros/mn-region-transfer.mn, added by 82a7e420 to certify the transfer, and it is VACUOUS for its own outer fn: `fn outer() = { fn inner() = (1,2); let (a,b) = inner(); (a+b, a*b) }` has a BlockExpr body B; infer.mn:689 binds B→TVar(T) where T is the `(a+b, a*b)` tuple; infer.mn:717 tags T; infer.mn:434 checks B; the bucket holds T, not B → own.mn:215 None → resume. The transfer branch (own.mn:223-227) never executes for `outer`. Only `inner` (bare `(1,2)` body, whose body_handle IS the tagged handle) reaches it. So use THAT: post-fix, `outer` must reach the transfer branch, and the self-compile must still report zero escapes with block-bodied fns actually checked for the first time. It fails pre-fix (transfer unreached), it is permanent, and it is not instrumentation. This also corrects the design's motivation, which is understated: 82a7e420's headline — "the wheel self-compiles under LIVE regions with ZERO escapes — the Hylo-quiet bar on ~37k lines" — is inflated by this very miss, because every block-bodied fn in the wheel skips the verdict entirely. The design should say so: the zero is not yet earned, and "zero stays zero" becomes a real risk rather than a formality.

6. NOVELTY OVERCLAIM ON CYCLONE (ancillary, not spine). "Cyclone and MLKit both run region-checking as a SEPARATE pass over an already-elaborated IL with its OWN substitution machinery, distinct from the ML/C type checker" is accurate for MLKit (Tofte-Talpin region inference runs on a typed IL) but wrong for Cyclone: Cyclone's regions live IN the type system as region-annotated pointer types checked by the type checker with unification over region variables — not a bolted-on second system. Drop Cyclone or restate it. The verdict does not need it: it stands on the artifact alone (own.mn:239-267 IS a private hash-bucket re-deriving identity graph_chase_handle already answers), and the PLAN §5.O layer-2 tie-in is exact — "region" is literally one of the six named str_hash waypoints slated to dissolve into Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive.

BLAST RADIUS (design is honest, add one line): analysis-only, no lower reader (I confirmed zero region-op readers outside own.mn/infer.mn), so the emit is unchanged and m2 == m3 should hold in ONE generation rather than as a TRANSITION — say that explicitly. The one genuine risk is not bytes: it is that thousands of block-bodied fns reach the verdict logic for the first time, so the zero-escape assertion is a real prediction.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.own.region-class-aware-lookup
  The region-tag lookup resolves a value's union-find canonical handle before consulting region_index, so a returned value's tag is found even when the checked handle is an alias (a BlockExpr's own handle, a let-bound VarRef) rather than the exact handle tag_alloc/tag_alloc_join stamped.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — every artifact citation verified exact, and the mechanism is confirmed by measurement (I built the patched wheel: the design's own example `{ let v = Sm(7); v }` goes from missing to hitting, the wheel self-compile holds 0 E_RegionEscape, and m2==m3 stays byte-identical). But the design names a callable that cannot be written in own.mn, undercounts its own site inventory (seven, not six), specifies a gate that can never pass as worded (measured 2096 -> 1732, never zero), and rests its soundness on a reason that is false — the right conclusion, the wrong argument.
  corrections: 1. NAME THE RIGHT CALLABLE. Replace `chase_handle(nodes, handle, 0)` with the GraphRead op `graph_chase_handle(handle)` (types.mn:1222; arm graph.mn:89). `chase_handle` (graph.mn:423) needs `ref nodes` — graph_handler's private state (graph.mn:56) — which own.mn (imports: types, runtime/strings; own.mn:13-14) cannot reach. State the layering the fix depends on: region_tracker (pipeline.mn:73) is installed INSIDE graph_handler (pipeline.mn:79), so the arm-internal perform resolves outward; own.mn:231's `report` -> diagnostics_handler (pipeline.mn:81) is the existing precedent. Verified: the patched wheel compiles and reaches a byte-identical fixpoint.

2. SEVEN SITES, NOT SIX. "own.mn plus the six infer.mn perform sites" undercounts. The design's own artifact_state enumerates seven: tag_alloc_join at infer.mn:650, 711, 717, 755, 781, 806 AND tag_alloc at infer.mn:891 (LambdaExpr). I patched exactly 7. Also name the two own.mn lookup sites explicitly: own.mn:213 (check_escape) and own.mn:282 (join_field_regions), plus the transfer's re-tag at own.mn:227, which must chase to stay consistent with the lookup.

3. RE-SPECIFY THE GATE — scope it to the construction's SPAN, not a global counter. As worded ("untagged_return counter ... to zero") it can never pass: measured 2096 -> 1732 wheel-wide, 196 -> 183 on the example, because a scalar/param-passthrough return is correctly untagged. The mechanically checkable form, which I ran and which DOES fail pre-fix and pass post-fix: with the construction isolated, assert the miss AT ITS OWN SPAN disappears (line 3 `{ let v = Sm(7); v }` present pre-fix, absent post-fix — a clean 1 -> 0) while the correctly-untagged returns (scalar/passthru/main) still miss. Keep gate (1) — I verified it: patched m2 == m3 byte-identical (16,173,552 B), 0 E_RegionEscape wheel-wide, matching the 82a7e42 zero baseline the design correctly cites over a44baa55's superseded 23.

4. FIX THE SOUNDNESS ARGUMENT (right conclusion, wrong reason). Delete "the miss's outcome (silent pass) coincides with the correct transfer verdict" — it does not: the transfer WRITES (own.mn:227 re-tags to parent), the miss writes nothing, so the tag stays on a dying region. The true, stronger argument, which I tried to break and could not: (a) stale field tags exist BY CONSTRUCTION (own.mn:227 re-tags only value_handle, never fields); (b) region ids are monotonic (own.mn:146), so join_field_regions' MAX (own.mn:286) can never let a stale smaller id win; (c) measured 0 escapes on the wheel and on a purpose-built cross-fn probe. Also correct the claim that the chase does not cross fn boundaries: it DOES (infer.mn:381 monomorphic bind + infer.mn:1155 ret alias), which VOIDS 82a7e42's recorded premise "cross-fn value flow rides untagged CALL nodes". That premise was true only for RAW lookups; the chase is precisely what retires it. Re-base the soundness story on monotonic-ids + max-join, and flag it explicitly for stage 3, where a miss stops being a benign no-op and becomes a memory-safety hole.

5. FIX THE NOVELTY VERDICT'S ANALOGY (the core point survives). "region_index ... structurally identical to the hand-rolled str_hash waypoint pattern §5.O layer 2 names as scaffolding to dissolve" inverts the artifact: region_index is ALREADY handle-keyed ("The handle is a dense Int, so i32.eq buckets — no str_eq", own.mn:243), and the artifact calls it the TEMPLATE the str_hash waypoints mirror (backends/wasm.mn:197 "region_index is the handle-keyed template"; lib/runtime/imap.mn:4 "region_index is its handle-keyed sibling"). So it is NOT paid twice by the intern migration — §11's peer-audit inversion binds env/summary/esc/base/reach, not this. The real and sufficient novelty argument stands on its own: region_index re-derives node identity that chase_handle already answers (the same find build_inst_mapping calls at infer.mn:3081), so two identity systems collapse to one graph read.

6. KEEP AS-IS: every citation verified exact (own.mn:213/245/255-259/261-267; infer.mn:529-544/649-651/711/717/755/781/806/891/398/434/2470/3065/957/3081; graph.mn:423-445/221; egraph.mn:272; pipeline.mn:64). The e-graph ordering claim is right — saturate_pass is a later |> stage than infer (which runs inside driver_compile_entry), so no live conflict, and chasing both weaves is the correct ultimate form. 82a7e42's own commit message independently names this item ("The class-aware tag lookup (a block body unifies with its tail; the raw handle misses the class-mate) is the arena increment's named burden"), so the lands-first sequencing claim is corroborated by the artifact's own record.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.lower.reach-membership-o1
  reach_has's O(n) linear scan over the already-enqueued-name frontier becomes a direct O(1) array read once names are dense Int handles — no hash, no bucket, no collision scan at all.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the item is real, correctly named in PLAN §5.O layer 2, and its correctness hedge verified TRUE against the binary (reach_has_loop emits `(call $str_eq)`, not a pointer-eq lie); but the mechanism rests on a two-buffer misread that leaves the hot site O(n) as written, a sizing justification 296 emitted names disprove, and a density property item 1's own cited substrate does not deliver.
  corrections: 1. STATE AND PROVE THE FUSION (the mechanism's actual form). The only sound single-array shape is `seen = reached ∪ queued`, set at ENQUEUE (reach_enqueue:5417-5418), replacing BOTH tests at 5414 with one read. It is monotone because every name enters rbuf only via fbuf — roots included (reachable_from_main:5526 routes init_refs/runtime-contract/argv through reach_enqueue; "main" is seeded into fbuf at 5528) — and reach_grow:5318 moves the popped name into rbuf in the same step. The conflation is safe for the other two call sites ONLY because both run post-fixpoint: reach_grow returns at 5529 with fcount==0, so at reach_any_reached (5538) and reach_has (5539) queued==∅ and seen==reached exactly. That proof is load-bearing and absent; without it the item silently changes reach_any_reached's semantics from "reached" to "reached-or-queued".

2. FIX THE INVENTORY. Four call sites, three functions, two buffers: 5405 (reach_any_reached→rbuf), 5414 ×2 (reach_enqueue→rbuf AND fbuf), 5539 (filter→rbuf). The design audits one. Per the standing rule — complete the inventory with file:line before proposing the fix.

3. "DENSE" IS NOT WHAT ITEM 1'S CITED SUBSTRATE DELIVERS — this correction propagates UP into item 1. PLAN §5.O layer 1 says "fix IT to O(1) and its offsets ARE the universal name-handles". Those are data-section BYTE offsets. Measured in this build: 3,512 data segments spanning 4,096→108,244, stepping by string length (65536, 65548, 65568 — deltas 12, 20). That is ~3.4% dense, so an offset-indexed array is ~104K entries for ~3.5K names — not the "bounded and small... no collision handling at all" the design's justification rests on. Worse, the table is an EMIT handler (wasm.mn:191 `handler string_table with idx = string_index_new()`, populated by set_string_entries during emit_module) and does not exist when reach runs inside lower. Also: PLAN §5.O's "today O(n)-scanned at wasm.mn:199" is STALE — 9feb727 already made it a str_hash bucket index (wasm.mn:185-193). This item must say it requires a NEW lex-time dense-ordinal interner that stays live and growing through lower, distinct from the data-section offset table PLAN names, or its array has nothing to index by.

4. THE MISSED LAW-1 LEAP, AT THE EXACT SITE TOUCHED. lower.mn:2338 mints `"lambda_{int_to_str(handle)}"` and its own comment says "a unique name derived from its AST handle". The dense Int the design waits on item 1 to invent ALREADY EXISTS — the AST handle. lower destroys it into decimal text, reach str_eq's the text back (verified, m2.wat:327995), and item 1 would hash it back to an Int: Int → text → hash → Int. For 296 of 2,353 names the ultimate form is to never stringify, which deletes the interning, the hashing, and the compare together — LESS code, Law 1 exactly. The design's Graph? interrogation ("a membership fact about the same dense Name space item 1 establishes") misses that lower is the site DESTROYING that density. This is not a nitpick: it is the generative pass the design skipped, and it reframes the item from "accelerate the scan" to "stop laundering the handle".

5. NAME THE ULTIMATE PEER. PLAN.md:742-743 names `Hβ.lower.reach-edge-on-node` ("the emitted-fn set as EDGE-following from main, the name scan gone") in the SAME clause that names this item — and reach-edge-on-node DELETES reach_has, build_reach_index's smap, and the `seen` array together. The design never mentions it, so it reads as accelerating a scanner already slated for deletion. State the relationship explicitly: this is a waypoint, and its whole apparatus dissolves when reachability becomes a mark read live off the node.

6. THE PERF GATE IS VACUOUS AS WRITTEN. "Confirm reach_has no longer appears among top self-compile cost entries" passes trivially if it was never there — and the design admits the baseline is unknown. This is precisely the trap PLAN §5.O's own CORRECTION records ("the biggest cost they named measured 0.5–4%... fixing it moved nothing"). Require a BEFORE `perf` run (PLAN §8's recipe) reporting reach_has/reach_has_loop/str_eq's share of the ~10s self-compile. If it sits under the ±14% run-variance, say so and sequence layer 3 (the per-decl arena) first — do not land an unmeasured O(n) fix on a code-reading estimate. Keep the m2==m3 half unchanged; it is sound, and the design's argument for landing it separately from item 1's transition (so any diff is a bug, not an expected re-pin) is correct and worth preserving.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.verify.higher-order-refinement
  Genuinely deferred, correctly so — a refinement predicate reasoning about a function VALUE is undecidable in general regardless of how much native or SMT machinery is built, so the right move is to name it and stop rather than force a design.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the deferral is right (Rice's theorem survived my attack, and the single-token-alias form behaves exactly as predicted: parses, V_Pending, no false accept), but the artifact_state is factually false for the exact syntax written, and the item's own "negative fixture" gate FAILS today, exposing a live silent refinement-drop in the shipped tutorial that belongs in the production bar rather than the NOT-required list.
  corrections: 1. REWRITE artifact_state. Correct text: "apply_refinement_constraint (infer.mn:3302) and refinement_admits (verify.mn:125) do flow generically over the base type — both match the TRefined wrapper alone — but the PARSER never constructs TRefined for a multi-token base. `type SortFn = ([Int]->[Int]) where is_monotonic(self)` does NOT parse (P_UnexpectedToken on `where`); the refinement is dropped and the alias registers transparent. A higher-order refinement IS expressible today only via a single-token indirection (`type Sorter = [Int] -> [Int]`; `type SortFn = Sorter where is_monotonic(self)`), which parses, type-checks, and surfaces V_Pending — never a false accept, since predicate_decide's PBoolNode(_) => None (verify.mn:67) maps to false in refinement_admits."

2. THE GATE IS REAL AND FAILS TODAY — invert the item's "None to build." The design's own proposed negative fixture presupposes "the surface form isn't accidentally broken." It IS broken. Write the fixture as THREE legs, all mechanically checkable and all non-vacuous today: (a) inline-arrow base parses with zero P_UnexpectedToken — FAILS now; (b) the predicate always surfaces V_Pending, never a false accept — PASSES now via indirection; (c) a decidable-FALSE predicate on a multi-token base still yields E_RefinementRejected — FAILS now (measured 0 on `[Int] where 1 > 2` vs 1 on `Int where 1 > 2`).

3. SPLIT THE ITEM — the design conflates "we cannot decide HOF refinements" (true; defer) with "there is nothing here" (false). Keep Hβ.verify.higher-order-refinement in PLAN §11's NOT-required list on Rice grounds, unchanged. Add a NEW, REQUIRED peer to §11 column 2 ("no known silent-wrong class"): Hβ.parse.refined-alias-multi-token-base — a `where` clause on any multi-token base type is silently dropped, so the refinement is never checked. This is a known silent-wrong class and cannot sit in NOT-required.

4. THE FIX IS CARRIED-TRUTH AND IS LESS CODE — not a patch. The one-token probe RE-DERIVES "where does the base type end" by guessing, when parse_type_ty already returns that answer live: the else-branch at parser.mn:714-715 already computes `let (alias_ty, p_alias) = parse_type_ty(tokens, p2)` and `let term = skip_ws(tokens, p_alias)`. DELETE the speculative probe (parser.mn:688) and the dead `base_val` binding (parser.mn:687); parse the RHS type ONCE, then test `term` for TWhere alongside the existing TPipe/TPlus/TMinus/TAmp check. Two lines deleted, one scanner-guess removed, every base handled uniformly. Carry the handle, read live.

5. INTERCONNECTION IS FALSE AS WRITTEN. "Depends on nothing above; blocks nothing above. A named absence, not a component." The one-token where-probe is the SHARED ROOT with R3, this item's own cluster: R3's decidable arithmetic fragment cannot be exercised on ANY multi-token base (`[Int]`, `Span(...)`, arrow) because the predicate never reaches the ledger. R3's coverage is silently capped at single-token bases until this lands.

6. BLAST RADIUS the design should have stated. Landing the parse fix makes lib/tutorial/06-refinement.mn's ValidSpan refinement LIVE for the first time, so `Span(1, 1, 5, 3)` must now actually discharge and any latent violation in the tree surfaces. It also plausibly shifts the emitted tree (new RefineStmt nodes where AliasStmt stood), so it is a march TRANSITION candidate, not a Law-7 no-op — re-pin from m3 per tools/march.sh, and re-check frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros. SYNTAX.md documents `type NonEmpty = [a] where len(self) > 0`, which does not parse today; types.mn:743 writes `type NonEmptyList = List where len(self) > 0` with a bare single-token base — the codebase has been unconsciously routing around the bug, which is why the green board never caught it.

7. NOVELTY caution for the parting hypothesis. The design floats that `!E` might express a useful subset of higher-order refinements "as a re-derivation of an existing primitive." It correctly hedges this as an unsupported hypothesis, but name the sharp edge so a future session does not chase it: `!E` proves the absence of a CAPABILITY the function performs (an effect), which is orthogonal to an extensional VALUE property of its input->output relation. `is_monotonic` is not an effect and no row can express it. The row buys nothing here; the "Mentl has a native angle" instinct is weaker than it reads. Arm 4 is the wrong arm — if any arm applies it is arm 6 (Refinement), and Rice bounds it there.
