CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.infer.alias-preserving-unify
  The union-find's representative choice becomes refinement-ranked — a TAlias always wins over its bare base — decided once at the two UNION sites (graph_bind's re-bind and unify's both-bound arm), never as a special case threaded through unify_types's structural match arms.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the artifact_state is accurate at every cited line and the defect reproduces exactly as described, but the design's "purely additive" sequencing is contradicted by the emit path (it converts `(call $str_concat)` into `(unreachable)`), its gate passes vacuously over that regression, and its stated "TAlias ONLY, never TRefined" scope does not implement its stated safety property because refined aliases ARE TAliases.
  corrections: 1. SEQUENCING IS BACKWARDS — the emit peel LEADS, richer_ty follows. The design says "Depends on nothing in this cluster." False. The real Carried-Truth violation is one dispatch whose sibling arms read the same fact two ways: `BEq`/`BNe` → `emit_struct_eq` → `emit_eq_for_ty(fold_strip(lookup_ty(left_h)), neg)` (wasm.mn:3905, WITH the peel) vs `BConcat` → `match lookup_ty(left_h)` (wasm.mn:3834, RAW). `fold_strip` already exists as the home (types.mn:162-166) and its comment names this exact case: "A `Port` (= Int) folds as Int; a `Sample` (= Float where ..) folds as Float." CENSUS (not moles): `grep lookup_ty src/backends/wasm.mn` minus the peeled reads = exactly TWO hits, and wasm.mn:237's `ty_to_wasm` routes through `ty_to_wasm_type`, "a PROJECTION of repr_of", which peels at types.mn:91. So wasm.mn:3834 is the ONE unpeeled structural type read at emit. Land `BConcat => match fold_strip(lookup_ty(left_h))` FIRST — no new code, reads the proof at the home that already exists, Law-1 correct on its own merits. Name the residue `Hβ.emit.concat-reads-through-fold-strip`. THEN richer_ty is safe.

2. THE STATED SCOPE DOES NOT IMPLEMENT THE STATED SAFETY PROPERTY. "Scoped to `TAlias` ONLY, never `TRefined`" does not exclude refinements: infer.mn:222 registers every refined alias as `TAlias(name, TRefined(base_ty, pred))`. This is not theoretical — it aims at the wheel's spine: types.mn:740 `type Handle = Int where 0 <= self`, types.mn:742 `ValidOffset`, types.mn:750 `ValidSpan` (85 references across src/, 64 in infer.mn alone). Under any matcher that peels TRefined, `richer_ty` would adopt `TAlias("Handle", TRefined(TInt, pred))` over bare `TInt` across the entire self-compile — a direct fixpoint threat the design's guard does not stop. Restate the guard structurally: adopt only a TAlias whose `fold_strip`'d inner is not TRefined.

3. THE MATCHER IS UNDERSPECIFIED AT EXACTLY THE SAFETY/COVERAGE TRADEOFF. "a bare `base` it structurally matches" is undefined, and the two readings fail differently:
   - Narrow (`same_ground`, the design's own cited proof): fires ONLY for ground scalars — infer.mn:1993-2001 returns false for TList/TTuple/TFun/TName/TRecord/TRecordOpen/TRefined/TCont/TVar. So `type Bytes = [Int]` (SYNTAX.md's own documented alias) STILL loses its name — t3 proves the defect is live there. The gate goes green while the class stays open. A mole, not a census.
   - Broad (structural equality over compounds): `graph_bind(loser_root, TVar(winner_root))` discards the loser's binding and orphans its inner vars — precisely what infer.mn:1823-1829's comment names as the PAID-FOR config-fn/HOF field-access disconnect ("The raw bind discards the proof and orphans its inner vars"). Worse, `occurs_in` is SHALLOW (graph.mn:547 `TVar(id) => id == handle`, no chase), so it cannot catch a cycle through a bound intermediate. Pick one and say which; the design must not cite same_ground for a claim same_ground cannot carry.

4. THE GATE IS VACUOUS OVER THE REGRESSION — it must compile and run, not grep. Greping `mentl query` stdout for "Port" never invokes emit. And `type Port = Int` is precisely the SAFE case: p1.mn (Port arithmetic + compare, alias as representative) runs exit=42, because those paths route through repr_of/fold_strip. The gate is green while `type Name = String` traps at exit=134. Replace with: (a) `type Name = String; fn f(n: Name) = n ++ "!"` compiles to `(call $str_concat)` and RUNS — asserted on emitted WAT, not on stdout prose; (b) the query leg keeps the alias-name check but adds `type Bytes = [Int]` so the compound case cannot be silently skipped; (c) `grep -c "++ on unresolved element type"` on the emitted WAT == 0, the same census CLAUDE.md ⟲ already prescribes for the floor class.

5. FACTUAL, MINOR: "`same_ground` ... already makes TAlias and its base fully interchangeable for every type-check" is false as stated. same_ground is ASYMMETRIC: `same_ground(TAlias("Port",TInt), TInt)` = true (infer.mn:2002) but `same_ground(TInt, TAlias("Port",TInt))` = false (infer.mn:1987's TInt arm demands b be literally TInt). Interchangeability comes from the wrapper-peel at unify_types (infer.mn:1799-1812) and the TAlias arm (infer.mn:1955-1961), not from same_ground. This matters because same_ground is both the design's cited proof AND the natural implementation of `richer_ty`'s matcher.

6. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS HONESTLY. The wheel's own fixpoint (m2 == m3) appears to survive — src/ and lib/ have no transparent scalar/sequence aliases — but the design must SAY that and show the check, not omit the section. The user-program radius is real and must be named: every String-alias `++` regresses until correction 1 lands.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.diag.declared-row-contradiction
  `with IO + !IO` compiles silently today and collapses to an EfPure-equivalent gate (satisfiable only by a function that performs nothing) — never what any author meant by writing both terms — because `build_declared_row`'s fold never cross-checks its own two accumulators (positive names, negated names) for overlap.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the premise is confirmed empirically (and is sharper than stated), but the specified mechanism is unsound: `declared_names_of` is connective-blind, so the check fires a false "contradiction" on the coherent row `with IO - Network + !Network`, and the gate's negative control passes vacuously.
  corrections: 1. REPLACE THE SET — read the resolved row live, do not re-derive from the clause. Delete the `declared_names_of(effs)` call from this design entirely. Intersect `neg_names` against the names admitted by the fold's own `pos`, which is already in hand at infer.mn:581 and has already applied the connective algebra: `match pos { EfPure => [], EfClosed(names) => names, _ => [] }` (types.mn:375-376 guarantees a normalized declared row is EfPure/EfClosed; positive terms are `mk_ef_closed([name])`, effects.mn:32, so no row variable enters). This is strictly LESS code than the design (no second traversal, no helper call), and it is CORRECT on every case the design gets wrong:
   - `with IO + !IO` → pos=Closed[IO], admitted={IO}, neg=[IO] → {IO} → FIRES (correct)
   - `with IO - Network + !Network` → pos=Closed[IO], admitted={IO}, neg=[Network] → ∅ → silent (correct; the design fires)
   - `with IO & Network + !Network` → pos=EfPure, admitted=∅, neg=[Network] → ∅ → silent (correct; the design fires)
   - `with IO + !Network` → pos=Closed[IO], admitted={IO}, neg=[Network] → ∅ → silent (correct)
   This is the honest form of the design's own slogan: the row already proves what it admits — read it, don't re-derive it. `name_set_intersect` (effects.mn:812) still applies, now against the right operand.

2. FIX THE GATE — the negative control must exercise the intersection. `fn g() with IO + Network = 42` is vacuous (neg_names=[] hits the infer.mn:582 early return) and does not assemble. Replace it with TWO controls that have a negation and no contradiction, both of which the current design would FAIL:
   - `fn h() with IO + !Network = 42` — the mixed-row shape the code's own comment cites (`Cursor + IC + !Mutate`, infer.mn:566-568). Must NOT emit.
   - `fn h2() with IO - Network + !Network = 42` — the connective-blind killer. Must NOT emit. This is the control that distinguishes the corrected mechanism from the specified one.
   Keep `fn f() with IO + !IO = 42` as the positive case. Follow crown-gate.sh exactly: compile-only, `grep -c 'E_DeclaredRowContradiction'` against stderr (crown-gate.sh:31 pattern), want≥1 for the positive, want=0 for both controls. Drop the "exits 42" clause from the gate spec or run it through tools/run-micro.sh separately — crown-gate.sh's pattern never assembles or runs, so the design's spec is internally inconsistent about which harness it mirrors.

3. STATE THE EXISTING NEIGHBOR. The design must acknowledge `T_OverDeclared`, which already fires at the decl site for the *adjacent* fact ("declares Network + IO but body only uses Pure", verified on `with IO + Network`). It is not a competitor — it reports declared-wider-than-used, not self-contradiction — but the design's premise is materially strengthened by naming it: `IO + !IO` is silent precisely BECAUSE the collapse makes the declared row Pure-equivalent, so T_OverDeclared has nothing left to report. The contradiction erases the evidence of itself. Put that sentence in the design; it is the real argument for the item.

4. DROP THE "immediately above" claim. `declared_names_of` is at infer.mn:238; `build_declared_row` at infer.mn:573 — 335 lines apart. Cosmetic, but it is the kind of unchecked adjacency claim that made the reuse look free when it is not.

5. KEEP, unchanged and correct: `Applicability = Unspecified` over PLAN §11's `MachineApplicable` — the design is right that the row cannot tell which term the author meant, and right to say so explicitly against PLAN's own text. Keep productive-under-error (report, then build the row as today). Keep the two-span Reason (positive term's span + negated term's span) — with correction 1, the overlapping name comes from `pos`, so the design must carry the positive term's span through the fold to underline both, which the current accumulator `(pos, neg_names, saw_pure)` does not hold; either widen the accumulator to carry (name, span) pairs for negated terms, or name the single-span form as the honest scope and name the two-span form as a positive-form peer (Hβ.diag.declared-row-contradiction-dual-span).
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.ci.perf-ledger-self-commits
  The perf ledger is a plain git-tracked TSV that the same CI job appends to and commits back to main with the default GITHUB_TOKEN — never an uploaded artifact or a side dashboard — because the ledger's one home is the same repo every other Mentl historical record already lives in.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the thesis (plain git-tracked TSV, committed back by the same job, git as sole store, no dashboard) survived every attack I could mount, but the design as written is structurally split across two homes and two of its literal platform claims are false: `git push` exits 128 from the detached HEAD `actions/checkout` produces, and "contents:write by default" is not a default the repo controls.
  corrections: 1. THE COMMIT MOVES INTO run-board.sh, beside the append. One home for "record the measurement": run-board.sh:17-20 appends and commits, using its own `$ledger` handle (never a re-typed path in YAML), guarded on a push target existing. The workflow step becomes `bash tools/ci/run-board.sh`. This makes the by-hand MI300X path (run-board.sh:5-6, the only path that exists today) record identically, and honors PLAN.md:1401 "host-agnostic".

2. `git push` → `git push origin HEAD:main`. Proven: bare push from actions/checkout's default detached HEAD is rc=128. (Or check out with `ref: ${{ github.ref_name }}`; the refspec form is the one that works under both.)

3. DELETE "contents:write by default on same-repo pushes" — it is not a default the repo controls. GitHub's GITHUB_TOKEN default is read-only for repos/orgs created after Feb 2023 and is an org/repo setting (Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions); ampactor-labs/mentl is an org repo. The workflow MUST declare `permissions: contents: write` explicitly. A read-only token 403s the push and the ledger silently never records — the silent-failure class Law 2 deletes rather than wraps.

4. THE COMMIT SWALLOWS ITS OWN FAILURE, stated explicitly. `|| true` (or `continue-on-error: true`) so a lost race or an empty append can never red a WHOLE board. Replace "the board's own exit code … is what CI grades" with the precise claim: the ledger commit is best-effort and MUST NOT contribute to job status; only run-board.sh's board_rc does.

5. DROP "git blame attributes each regression to its commit" — false by one commit. Proven: blame names `f6841cba (github-actions[bot])`, not the measured sha `cafe1234` the row carries; the bot commit is the CHILD of the measured one. Replace with the argument that is actually true and actually load-bearing: the row's `commit` column self-describes the measured sha precisely BECAUSE the parent edge is unreliable — the design's own retry-with-rebase re-parents the bot commit. `git log -- tools/ci/perf-ledger.tsv` remains the query interface; blame is not.

6. THE GATE GROWS A LOCAL HALF and its second clause is fixed. Every other Mentl gate (march.sh, frontier-gate.sh, proof-exactness-gate.sh, crown-gate.sh) runs locally and mechanically; this one is a manual observation of a hosted service and cannot run until item 1 lands AND PLAN.md:1406 ("public-repo readiness — the landed commits are pushed") closes. The commit path is fully exercisable against a local bare remote with zero GitHub — I did exactly that. Keep `git log --oneline -5 --author=github-actions -- tools/ci/perf-ledger.tsv` (it works, non-vacuous), add the local bare-repo exercise, and fix clause 2: as written, "a push immediately following a CI-authored commit does not appear as a second triggered run" FAILS on correct behavior, since a real human push after a bot commit MUST trigger a run. The check wanted is "the bot's own commit produced no run": `gh run list --commit <bot-sha>` is empty.

7. NAME THE NOISE FLOOR, or the `seconds` column is a trip-wire on noise. PLAN.md:701-702 records ±14% run-variance on a DEDICATED local box ("below the ±14% run-variance — fixing it moved nothing" — the env_find_flat miss). PLAN.md:1401 requires CI host-agnostic; shared GitHub-hosted runners are noisier than that floor. So "did today's commit get slower" is unanswerable from `seconds` until CI sits on fixed hardware — which PLAN.md:1376 already concedes ("fixed hardware sharpens it once the MI300X arc begins") and PLAN §11 marks the MI300X NOT required. State it: on host-agnostic CI the honest per-commit signal is the `board` column (whole|red); `seconds` is a fixed-hardware cash-out, recorded now so the series exists when the host stabilizes, never read as a regression signal below ~15%. Without this line the ledger's first real use is a hunt against noise — the exact trap §5.O was written to prevent.

8. ADD THE BLAST-RADIUS LINE the design omits (the answer is clean, so say it): tools/ci/perf-ledger.tsv is outside wt_wheel (wt-env.sh:71-80, src|lib only) and outside wt_state_key (wt-env.sh:99-102), so the ledger commit touches neither the m2==m3 fixpoint nor any green gate nor the verify stamp. A design that does not state its blast radius is asking the reader to trust rather than check.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.ci.thin-transport-trigger
  A GitHub Actions workflow on GH-hosted (not self-hosted, not MI300X-gated) Ubuntu runners installs a version-pinned wasmtime+WABT toolchain and executes exactly one substantive step, `bash tools/ci/run-board.sh`, on every push to main — safe to trust because run-board.sh's exit-code plumbing is already correct end to end.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the spine is sound and PLAN-sanctioned (PLAN.md:1401 "CI, host-agnostic and REQUIRED: every push → the full board"), and run-board.sh's exit chain really is correct end to end as claimed; but one of the two bootstrap steps targets the wrong binary at a version it cannot have, the gate contradicts the item's own interconnection, the pin's stated justification is refuted by the venue, and the artifact_state miscounts the board.
  corrections: 1. BOOTSTRAP STEP 2 — name the right binary. Replace "a pinned wasm-tools/WABT (>=1.252, PLAN §8's own floor)" with WABT pinned at 1.0.39 (what this tree is green against; `wat2wasm --version` → 1.0.39), installed by an explicitly named mechanism — fetch the pinned WABT release tarball and put its bin/ on PATH — because no `curl|bash` WABT installer exists and no WABT release satisfies ">=1.252". PLAN §8's 1.252+ floor is `wasm-tools`, "the maintained WABT successor", which `grep -rln 'wasm-tools' tools/` proves the board invokes zero times. If wasm-tools is wanted for PLAN §8's `shrink` / `validate --features all`, that is a separate, currently-unused install: name it as such or drop it. Note the WABT flags are load-bearing (wt-env.sh:27 `--enable-threads --enable-tail-call`), so the pin must be a version carrying them.

2. THE GATE — make it behavioral, not a shape count. `grep -c '^\s*run:'` on YAML passes vacuously: it proves a file has three lines matching a regex, never that a red board turns the commit red. Replace with a forced-red round trip: push a scratch commit that breaks one micro, assert `gh run list --json conclusion` → "failure"; revert, assert "success". Drop "with nothing after it" — it contradicts this item's own interconnection and forecloses item 2. If the commit-back stays in this job it needs `if: always()` (run-board.sh:24 aborts on RED), and the gate must additionally assert that a RED run lands a `red` row, not just a green one.

3. THE PIN — say the true reason; the false one is a slogan. "load-bearing for item 2's perf ledger: an unpinned 'latest' wasmtime silently invalidates every row-to-row seconds comparison" does not hold on this venue: `ubuntu-latest` is a label over a mixed shared fleet, so the CPU beneath the pinned wasmtime is itself unpinned and the seconds are not row-to-row comparable regardless. run-board.sh:4 already says it — "fixed hardware makes the numbers comparable per commit" — and PLAN drew the partition the design collapses: col 5 (PLAN.md:1401) "CI, host-agnostic and REQUIRED" = the VERDICT, now; col 3 (PLAN.md:1376) "fixed hardware sharpens it once the MI300X arc begins" = the SECONDS, later. The true and sufficient justification: the pin buys VERDICT reproducibility — a wasmtime point-release must not be able to turn the board red on its own. State that the `seconds` column is deliberately banked as noise on GH-hosted runners until the MI300X arc.

4. RUN THE UNIVERSAL AUDIT ON THE LEDGER — the design audited the exit code and pronounced the whole script safe. CLAUDE.md ⊕ demands the structure be audited before the symptom: does the graph already know this? The design's own gate answers — `gh run list --json conclusion,headSha` returns (commit → verdict), and the same API carries startedAt/updatedAt and the timestamp. Three of perf-ledger.tsv's four columns (date, commit, board) are re-derived into a tracked file the Actions run history already connects; the fourth is per-correction-3 noise here. Native form: the run history IS the ledger — deleting run-board.sh:17-20 deletes item 2 whole (nothing to commit back), and the board's own elapsed rides as a job annotation on the same run. That is the fix-is-LESS-code form and it removes a whole workflow step. If the TSV survives, it must survive on a stated reason the API cannot serve.

5. THE ARTIFACT_STATE MISREADS state.sh in three places — small individually, but it is the file the safety claim rests on. (a) "5 full-board checks" — there are SIX: state.sh:41-46 MARCH / FRONTIER / PROOF-EXACTNESS / CROWN / EFFECT IDENTITY / PHANTOMS (`bash tools/comment-ratchet.sh`), committed at HEAD 7e47e850 "tools: the prose has a census too, and it was wired to nothing"; `grep -c '^  gate ' tools/state.sh` → 6; tree clean. (b) "lines 47-52 then exit 1" — the block is 48-53 with `exit 1` at 52. (c) "`--quick` mode (line 38, `bash tools/verify.sh || exit 1`)" — line 38 runs UNCONDITIONALLY; the `--quick` test is line 40. None breaks the trigger; together they mean the design was written against a board already one gate stale.

6. NAME THE COLD-CACHE COST OR NAME THE PEER. Measured here: one cold `boot(wheel)` → m2 is 44.6 s wall / 961 MB peak RSS, emitting 481,742 lines — PLAN.md:933's "The self-compile runs ~10s" did not reproduce. The board pays that TWICE (march.sh:82 `wt_m2_ensure` for m2, then the m3 leg) plus two ~480k-line wat2wasm assembles, plus 71 micros / 47 frontier / 9 proof-exactness / 5 crown / effect-identity / comment-ratchet. Seven tools read `.build/` (march, crown-gate, frontier-gate, verify, proof-exactness-gate, march-gate, wt-env) and an ephemeral runner starts with it EMPTY every run — so CI always pays in full the compile that wt-env.sh:82-132 built `.build/m2cache` to stop three gates re-deriving ("It costs ~13 minutes, and three gates used to re-derive it independently ... ONE keyed home now"). `wt_m2_key` (wt-env.sh:106) is a content hash of (wheel bytes, boot bytes, run flags), so caching across runs is sound. Either add `uses: actions/cache` on `.build/m2cache` — a `uses:` step, so the design's own 3-`run:`-line gate does not even object — or name `Hβ.ci.m2cache-across-runs` in positive form. The design names it neither, which is a hidden gap.

7. SEQUENCING claim "Nothing blocks this landing now" survives corrections 1-3 being applied first; the exit chain genuinely needs no work.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.closure.diverse-double-compilation
  Diverse double-compilation does not wait on the native backend — band J and NATIVE.md both compare a second BACKEND that shares the wheel's own frontend, when the technique needs a second COMPILER independent of the pinned boot's lineage, available now by re-bootstrapping the archived pre-first-light seed to its own fixpoint and sha256-comparing that against boot.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the headline is right and verified (native.mn shares the whole frontend per NATIVE.md S5/§4 and wasm.mn:1-6, so PLAN.md:856's "DEP native backend" is unsound and must go), but the design as written must not land: its novelty_verdict attacks a Wheeler that never existed (Wheeler's DDC is already a two-stage compile comparing generation 2, which march.sh:116 and PLAN §6 already encode), its stopping rule can wash out the very divergence it hunts, its gate compares the wrong layer and logs to a prose ledger that HEAD proves stale (boot = 2d4ccff8… vs PROVENANCE's e2babb24…, unrecorded re-pin f7a1b3a5), and two of its artifact citations are false.
  corrections: FOUR corrections; the item's headline survives all of them, so land it AMENDED, never as written.

1. KEEP the thesis — drop "DEP native backend" from PLAN.md:856. Verified sound and the item's real value. NATIVE.md S5 makes native.mn a `~> NativeOut` peer on the same LowIR; NATIVE.md §4 "Self-hosting the encoder — the WASM wheel cross-compiles native.mn ... before it self-hosts" puts its binary inside boot's lineage; wasm.mn:1-6 says the relationship in the compiler's own words. A Trojan in the shared frontend rides into both binaries identically. The DEP is unsound and should go. But strike the supporting sentence about roadmap:268 — that line reads "an INDEPENDENT compiler/backend", so "no native-backend clause" is false and the "LATER, unexamined narrowing" story is unsupported by its own citation. The DEP is wrong on the merits; it does not need a fabricated origin story.

2. DELETE the novelty_verdict and rewrite it honestly. Wheeler 2005 (ACSAC), verbatim: "recompile the purported source code twice: once with a second (trusted) compiler, and again using the result of the first compilation. If the result is bit-for-bit identical with the untrusted binary, then the source code accurately represents the binary." Two stages; compare generation 2 against the binary under test; the second stage exists precisely so T1 and T2 need NOT agree on codegen. So "compile once with T1 and T2, diff the FIRST-generation output", "requires two independently-written compilers to make identical low-level codegen choices", and "compare FIXPOINTS, not first generations" as the native improvement are all wrong. The honest residue is smaller and worth stating plainly: Mentl's contribution is that the ladder is ALREADY MECHANIZED (march.sh runs boot→m2→m3 and self-arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN), so DDC is a SUBSTITUTION OF THE FIRST RUNG, not a new technique. Also drop "which is why real-world DDC exercises are rare and expensive" — Wheeler performed DDC on tcc and GCC.

3. FIX the stopping rule and the gate's LAYER. Compare at generation 2 — m3b vs boot — which is where PLAN §6's own "the seed already out of the loop" bites. Iterating to T2's own fixpoint buys a full self-compile per extra generation for zero signal when T2 is honest (m3b == boot already, so m4b == m3b trivially), and can WASH OUT a genuine T2 miscompile whose byte-divergence is semantically inert — the exact signal the ladder exists to surface. And compare the .wat, not the .wasm: the wheel emits WAT, march.sh compares WAT (`diff -q`, lines 116/130/145), and PROVENANCE already records the two hashes separately ("boot/mentl.wasm sha256 e2babb24…" / "its .wat (m2 == m3 asserted) sha256 caa80b0c…"). A sha256 over .wasm silently makes wat2wasm part of the compared artifact.

4. NAME the shared TCB, and REPLACE the hand-extension with root-certification-plus-chain-assertion.
   (a) The independence claim is true of boot and silent about everything else. The archived seed's own bootstrap/build.sh sources tools/wt-env.sh for W2W — the SAME `wat2wasm --debug-names --enable-threads --enable-tail-call` that assembles boot — and both lineages execute under the same wasmtime. For an item whose whole purpose is closing trusting-trust, that is a hidden gap. Name it: `Hβ.closure.assembler-runtime-tcb-residual` — WABT and wasmtime are the un-diversified TCB both lineages share, a named !Outside, exactly as PLAN §0 names the external-SMT residual.
   (b) The stronger native form the design missed. At tag `first-light` the seed and the wheel source are CONTEMPORANEOUS — the seed compiles that wheel with zero hand-extension, because that is what first-light IS. That certifies the root pin (b3314001…, commit 87c0152) for free. Then the per-pin increment is what march.sh ALREADY computes: assert `sha256(boot_n) == sha256(assemble(m2_n))` at pin time — the step f7a1b3a5 skipped and that PROVENANCE re-types by hand. Root certification (once, no hand-WAT) + per-pin assertion (already running) carries the certification to today's boot by induction, with no seed maintenance at all, and dissolves the stale-ledger finding by making PROVENANCE's hash a DERIVED projection instead of a hand-typed line. This also closes what the design's one-shot comparison structurally cannot see: 26 human-mediated `cp m3.wasm boot/mentl.wasm` steps, 11 of them on 2026-07-16 alone, each an unaudited insertion point. The sharp edge is honest — the replay's cost grows with the chain and the early pins predate the perf loop (§7: 1400s → 10s) — but it is mechanical rather than manual, which is the right trade.
   (c) Consequently strike "Unblocked now ... git archaeology plus a hand-extension pass" and the "once per RELEASE/pin" cadence. Measured: 11 boot re-pins on 2026-07-16 alone; 26 total. Each pin moves the wheel's grammar and semantics, and T2 must track both by hand in WAT to compile it at all — that re-instates the maintenance half of PLAN §6's mirror-grind, the exact thing the fixpoint rule freed the project from and the seed's deletion closed.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.closure.correctness-oracle-internal
  PLAN's existing framing survives verification unchanged: the micro battery is today the compiler's only oracle for behavioral correctness (first-light proved byte-reproduction, not correctness), and that gap is a strictly deeper, later-sequenced claim than anything in items 1-3 or the exit-code work in item 5 can close.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the thesis survives every attack I could mount (byte-fixpoint ≠ correctness; the oracle is a finite EXTERNAL sample; Rice-shaped; JUSTIFY on novelty; correctly not-required for the S11 label), but the design's ARTIFACT STATE contradicts PLAN's own §7:931 on what "the board is green" means, and its offered gate proxy is a presence-boolean that cannot police what the design says it polices.
  corrections: 1. BOARD DESCRIPTION (load-bearing, must change). Replace "'the board is green' today means '71 hand-picked black-box programs produced their expected exit codes, and the wheel's self-diagnostic count sits at or below a hand-raised ratchet'" with PLAN §7:931's own enumeration: the board is tools/state.sh sequencing seven gates — verify.sh (71 micro exit codes + the census ratchet), march.sh (m2 == m3), frontier-gate.sh (47/0), proof-exactness-gate.sh (9/9), crown-gate.sh (5/5), effect-identity-gate.sh, comment-ratchet.sh. The design currently describes verify.sh and names it the board. Keep the conclusion verbatim — five external gates are still a finite external sample, not a proof — but state the sample's real size and shape.

2. ORACLE SHAPE (load-bearing). Delete "each a black-box 'run this compiled program, compare its process exit code to one integer' check" as a description of the whole. It is exactly right for tools/run-micro.sh:33-37 and wrong for the board. The repo already runs THREE oracle shapes: (a) black-box exit code (run-micro.sh); (b) compile-time diagnostic assertion (crown-gate.sh:29-40, which exists precisely because — its header, :2-5 — "the exit-code micro battery cannot express this"); (c) compiler-property assertion (proof-exactness-gate.sh:53-79 `expect_refusal` — nonzero exit AND zero WAT bytes AND diagnostic preserved). Shape (c) is the "property of the compiler" object the design says is unchecked. The gap is not "only program properties are checked" — it is that ALL THREE shapes are EXTERNAL bash asserting on stderr, while the peer names the wheel's own Verify as the endpoint. That is the honest, and stronger, boundary.

3. THE NARROW VERIFY CLAIM SURVIVES — keep it, and tighten it. "Nothing in src/verify.mn is consulted to decide whether a micro's OWN BEHAVIOR is correct" is TRUE: report_refinement_rejected (src/verify.mn:234) and report_verify_debt (src/verify.mn:239) discharge refinement obligations on the SOURCE being compiled. But add the caveat the artifact forces: proof-exactness-gate.sh's `expect_surfaced` DOES consult verify.mn's V_Pending projection as an oracle input for a compiler property. Verify's output is already an oracle — for refusal/debt behavior, not for micro behavior.

4. THE GATE IS VACUOUS (must be replaced or withdrawn). `grep -c V_Pending .build/m2cache/m2.err` returns 1 — I ran it. It returns 1 BY CONSTRUCTION whenever debt is nonempty: src/verify.mn:239-244 is `fn report_verify_debt(debt) = { if len(debt) == 0 { () } else { report(VPending(len(debt), span_zero())) } }` — exactly one summary line, or none. It is a presence boolean, not "a nonzero, honestly-reported count". The real count lives in the message text (.build/m2cache/m2.err:3140: "verify: V_Pending VerificationPending: 16 verification obligations pending at 0:0-0:0"). And it cannot make the discrimination the design assigns it: "never reach zero via silent assume-true, only via real proof or by staying visibly nonzero" — both routes to zero yield `grep -c` = 0, indistinguishably. If a proxy is offered, it must read the obligation count out of the message (e.g. the "N verification obligations pending" capture) and be paired with the census, which is the number that actually moves: 2266 at ceiling.

5. TAG REFERENCE (minor, immaterial to the conclusion). "first-light is tagged at 87c0152" is not as stated: the annotated tag `first-light` (tag object 77f10dbe) resolves to commit 005fa016 "plan: FIRST LIGHT — m3 == m4, both halves self-confirmed"; 87c01528 is its parent and the delta is a 21-line PLAN.md-only commit, so the code state at the tag IS 87c0152's. Cite the tag as 005fa016 (code = 87c01528). Related: the tag's own message records first-light's correctness half as "8/8 rungs, 52/52 micros" on 410,732-line wats — so under the historical reading the number is 52, not 71, and rungs are omitted; under the present reading it is the seven-gate board. The design's sentence is false under both readings, which is what makes correction 1 unavoidable rather than pedantic.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.dsp.transcendental-phase-magnitude
  sqrt, cos, and atan2 — the magnitude and phase primitives every CFC computation needs — complete lib/runtime/math.mn's transcendental series family, and nothing downstream in this cluster can be written before they land.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — cos/atan/atan2 survive intact, but the sqrt half is refuted by the exact line the design cites as its justification (math.mn:3 names sqrt as an instruction WASM *has*), and the gate is proven vacuous on the artifact: through boot/mentl.wasm the proposed composition returns exit=4 where the native instruction returns 7 — sqrt(9.0) != 3.0 while the design's own gate passes green.
  corrections: 1. STRIKE the sqrt series. sqrt is not a member of the transcendental series family — it is an algebraic op with a native opcode at every repr class. The design must say: sqrt is a UNARY INTRINSIC, the exact mirror of the live emit_binop_repr (wasm.mn:5750) — `LUnaryRepr(handle, USqrt, operand)` dispatched by repr_of(lookup_ty(operand)): RF64 -> f64.sqrt (0x9F), RF32 -> f32.sqrt (0x91), RV128 -> f32x4.sqrt / f64x2.sqrt. Kind is an ADT, never a string flag — the LConvert precedent stated at lower.mn:468. Recognized at lower beside float_of_int (lower.mn:833). The fix is LESS code: no series, no x<=0 guard, and atan sheds ~176 f64 ops per call (its two sqrt calls x 88).

2. CORRECT the artifact_state. math.mn:3-4 does not say WASM lacks sqrt; it enumerates sqrt/abs/ceil/floor/trunc/nearest/min/max/copysign as the nine f64 instructions WASM HAS. Any design citing that header must read the parenthetical. Replace the design's grep with one that reproduces: `grep -rn "fn sqrt\|fn atan2\|fn atan(\|fn cos(" lib/ src/ tests/` (exit 1).

3. RENAME / SPLIT the item. Hβ.dsp.transcendental-phase-magnitude covers two different kernel questions. Split: (a) the transcendental completion — cos(x) = sin(x + pi()/2.0), atan via half-angle + Taylor, atan2's 4-quadrant dispatch — all sound, all genuinely without an opcode, all lib-source-only; (b) Hβ.emit.unop-repr-projection — the unary mirror of emit_binop_repr covering the nine f64 instructions the header already enumerates, projected at repr. These have different blast radii and must not ride one landing.

4. FIX THE GATE — it must FAIL if the design is not landed. Replace the 1e-4 window (which a hard-coded constant passes) with the identity the algebraic op preserves and the series cannot: `sqrt(9.0) == 3.0 && sqrt(100.0) == 10.0 && sqrt(2.0)*sqrt(2.0) == 2.0`. Measured: exit=4 under the proposed series, exit=7 under f64.sqrt. This inherits mn-float-gate.mn's actual property ("a 3 is only reachable through real f64 literals, f64.mul, and f64.ge") instead of only its file shape. Also pass the libs — `bash tools/run-micro.sh <micro> <expected>` with no lib list cannot see math.mn at all.

5. CORRECT THE BLAST RADIUS. "Nothing blocks this — pure library-internal extension" is TRUE for cos/atan2 (lib-source-only holds m2 == m3 in one generation — the precedent PLAN §5.R band D records at Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc) and FALSE for the sqrt intrinsic: a new unary emit arm CHANGES EMITTED BYTES -> m2 != m3 -> the march's TRANSITION leg (m3 == m4, re-pin from m3, tools/march.sh / Hβ.tools.march-transition-native). Say so. Note also that atan calls sqrt twice, so the sqrt decision propagates into the accuracy and cost of the atan/atan2 half the design got right.

6. RUN THE CENSUS BEFORE LANDING. math.mn:40-43 float_floor already re-derives f64.floor (0x9C, in the header's own list) — the defect the design proposes to add is a class with one instance shipped. Enumerate all nine unary f64 instructions against the file in one measurement, then treat the class; do not land sqrt as a mole.

7. NAMED POSITIVE-FORM PEERS for what is NOT built here: Hβ.emit.unop-repr-projection (the unary mirror; sqrt/abs/ceil/floor/trunc/nearest/min/max/copysign at repr) · Hβ.math.float-floor-dissolves-into-f64-floor (math.mn:40 reads the opcode, the hand-roll deleted) · Hβ.math.series-term-count-honest (math.mn:73's "nine odd terms reach f64 epsilon" is an overclaim — measured residual ~1e-11..1e-13; either raise the term count or state the real bound, since cos/atan2 inherit it) · Hβ.simd.sqrt-lane-cashout (f32x4.sqrt/f64x2.sqrt under ~> Simd — the RV128 arm the series form structurally cannot reach; band E kin, the MI300X arc's consumer).
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.runtime.float-memory-ops
  load_f64/store_f64/load_f32/store_f32 as named ops on effect Memory, exposing WAT instructions the compiler's own record-field emission already uses internally — the one substrate change standing between Mentl and reading a raw float32/float64 scientific recording.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the gap is real and the f64 pair validates exactly as designed, but the f32 pair as specified fails wasm validation in both directions, and the mechanism the design names (`repr_wat_load`/`repr_wat_store`, "one dispatch table", "no new backend concept") does not exist as described.
  corrections: 1. THE f32 PAIR NEEDS TWO NEW OPCODES — say so. `load_f32` emits `(f32.load)(f64.promote_f32)`; `store_f32` emits `(f32.demote_f64)(f32.store)(i32.const 0)`. Both belong as a new arm-pair in the EXISTING `convert_op` ADT (wasm.mn:4310), whose own comment already scopes this family: "the one signed WASM convert op per direction ... Both are value-changing (never bit-reinterpreting)" — promote/demote are precisely that. The honest claim is "one new arm-pair in an existing ADT", not "no new backend concept". Verified: the corrected WAT validates clean.

2. DELETE `repr_wat_load`/`repr_wat_store` FROM THE DESIGN. They do not exist (`repr_load_op`/`repr_store_op`, wasm.mn:4286/4299). And stop claiming they are reused: the LWasiCall path (wasm.mn:3500-3557) never reaches them — it is a literal `else if import_name == "..."` chain emitting opcode text inline. Routing it through `repr_load_op(RF64)` instead would be a genuine improvement (read the repr, project the opcode — the proof becomes the dispatch), but the design proposes hardcoded strings. Pick one and say which.

3. THREE HOMES, NOT "ONE DISPATCH TABLE". Landing four ops edits: lib/runtime/memory.mn:20-45 (the decl), src/lower.mn:4664 `is_substrate_mem_op` (a `name == "..." ||` chain), and src/backends/wasm.mn:3517 (the else-if emit chain). All three are name-keyed string comparisons — drift-8, and the design's "purely widening an existing dispatch table by four names" undercounts 3x. memory.mn's own header says "This is the single source of truth for substrate operations. If an operation isn't declared here, it doesn't exist" — and then two string scanners re-derive that fact. Extending the chain is defensible scaffold (it is how every existing substrate op works), but only if NAMED: Hβ.runtime.substrate-op-name-is-handle — the decl carries the op identity; the two scanners read it live instead of re-scanning by name. This is the §5.O name-is-handle cluster PLAN §11 already inverted-sequenced ("intern-at-lex leads"), so the peer has a home rather than being invented here.

4. THE GATE IS SELF-CONSISTENT BUT NEVER TESTS THE USE CASE. store_f64→load_f64 proves only that Mentl agrees with itself; it cannot distinguish a correct f64 path from a consistently-wrong one (write 4 bytes, read 4 bytes, roundtrip green), and it never touches "foreign bytes → Float", which IS the stated purpose (reading a raw float32/float64 recording). Add the discriminating leg: `store_i32` a known IEEE-754 bit pattern, then `load_f64`/`load_f32` at that address and check the value. Proven: 0xF01B866E/0x400921F9 → 3.14159; 0x3FC00000 → 1.5 (wasmtime --invoke). That leg fails on a width or opcode error the roundtrip hides. Keep the roundtrip; it is not vacuous (the ops do not exist, so the micro would not compile) — but it is not sufficient.

5. THE NOVELTY VERDICT OVERCLAIMS — downgrade it to scaffold-tier. "Exactly as type-safe and effect-tracked as load_i32" is true, and it is an argument for parity, not for safety: `addr: Int` carries no in-bounds proof, no alignment proof, no proof it points at a float rather than mid-string. That IS C's `*(double*)(base + i*8)` with an effect row bolted on. The row is a real gain over C (transitive `!Memory`); the type safety is not, and "no foreign idiom is rejected in favor of an invented one" concedes the borrowed shape while the verdict still dresses it as native-stronger. Mentl's genuinely stronger form exists and the design skipped it — arm 6, refinement: SYNTAX.md's own `fn safe_get(xs: [a], i: ValidIndex(xs)) -> a = xs[i]`, where "when `i` is refined to a proven-valid index, the compiler elides the bounds check". Correct verdict: the raw-address op is the FLOOR, kept as named scaffold (consistent with load_i32, which it must not diverge from); the peer is Hβ.refine.memory-offset-validoffset.

6. INTERROGATIONS: 2 OF 8 PROJECTED. The design runs Graph and Row and stops. Refinement (6) is the one that changes the answer — see correction 5. Ownership (5) is the second: a float buffer in linear memory has an owner, and `!Alloc` on the read path is exactly the real-time DSP property §4⑤/§5.U turn on — the CFC pipeline is a DSP consumer, so this is not academic.

7. BLAST RADIUS: UNSTATED — state it. The change is emit-side (src/backends/wasm.mn + src/lower.mn), which engages PLAN §5.R band D's rule that "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation ... the transition form is for EMIT changes only". Here the new arms fire only on names no wheel source calls (verified: zero hits for load_f64/store_f64/load_f32/store_f32 across src/ and lib/), so both generations emit identically and m2 == m3 is expected to HOLD. The residual risk is the four new interned strings shifting the data section — the exact class that has forced a re-pin before (PLAN §7, the string-interner re-pin). Say "expected m2 == m3; tools/march.sh arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN itself" rather than say nothing. No effect on frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, or the 71 micros: purely additive names with no existing caller.

8. "THE ONE SUBSTRATE CHANGE" is an overclaim as phrased — it is four ops PLUS the convert_op arm-pair. The gap itself is real and correctly identified; only the count is wrong.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.cli.doc-test-new-verb-honesty
  mentl new is the reference-correct verb transport; mentl doc and mentl test both silently alias mentl check today, and test's own comment describes a compile-time assertion-lifting mechanism (verify_assert) that does not exist anywhere in the codebase.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the item's diagnosis is exact and its spine (delete the duplicate, discover by row not name, reuse the shim) survives, but the design as written is unsound: it discovers assertion helpers as tests and fabricates a passing verdict for them, and it consolidates onto lib/test.mn, which declares zero imports and does not resolve when imported (artifact-probed, exit 1).
  corrections: 1. STRIKE "REAL, WORKING" — lib/test.mn is UNPROVEN, NON-IMPORTABLE substrate; give it its manifest FIRST. It declares zero imports while calling map/fold/len/println/to_string; imported, it dies with E_MissingVariable at its own lines 80/87/105 (probed, exit 1). The design must say: lib/test.mn declares `import prelude` (+ runtime/io for println) before anything consolidates onto it — PLAN §11's own law, "the imports ARE the manifest", which prelude got (lib/prelude.mn:13-16) and test.mn never did. Kill the "zero new dependencies / buildable now" sequencing claim; the first landing is the manifest plus the first execution lib/test.mn has ever had.

2. DISCOVERY = row contains Test AND the parameter product is EMPTY. Not row alone. Both facts are two projections of the ONE lookup_ty(handle) chase the design already makes — TFun(List, Ty, EffRow) (types.mn:41) carries params and row on the same node; extract_row (mentl.mn:505) pulls one, a sibling params projection pulls the other. This is exactly PLAN §5.U's shape ("ONE cursor reading ONE heap record at N altitudes, joined at one match lookup_ty(h)"), so it is completing the read, not adding a filter. Nullary is not a naming convention smuggled back in — it is the medium's own runnability proof (SYNTAX §"Partial application": a fn with unfilled fields is a VALUE awaiting fields, never an execution). Only with this correction does the novelty claim become true: `#[test]` must be hand-applied and can be forgotten, and Rust needs a bespoke macro check to reject `#[test]` on a non-nullary fn — Mentl reads both facts off the type it already proved.

3. TWO ASTs, NOT ONE — name which one the synthesis writes into. analyze_fns → driver_check_entry (driver.mn:229-240) parses the ENTRY FILE ONLY ("Parse + infer the entry ONCE"); compile (pipeline.mn:64) → driver_compile_entry (driver.mn:209-227) parses the CONCATENATED DAG. A main minted into the check-entry AST does not exist in the compile-entry AST. Since the shim only ever receives WAT from `mentl compile`, the entry-synthesis must live on the COMPILE side inside the wasm; "test_run becomes run_run with a different entry-synthesis pre-pass" is imprecise — the shim bypasses test_run entirely. (Silver lining the design missed: entry-file-only scope is what keeps lib/test.mn's four expect_* helpers out of discovery; the fabricated-pass hole fires on the USER's own helpers in their own test file, which is the normal shape.)

4. THE GATE IS VACUOUS ON THE ACTUAL FAILURE MODE, AND MISPLACED. Its negative case — "one plain helper fn with no Test row" — is the trivially-excluded case; it would pass whether or not the arity read exists. It MUST include an expect_*-shaped fn (row contains Test, arity >= 1, the lib/test.mn:19-35 shape) and assert it is NOT reported as a passing test. Placement: tools/run-micro.sh takes `<micro.mn> [expected_exit]` and compiles+runs directly, never crossing `mentl test`; a gate that invokes the shim belongs beside tools/frontier-gate.sh / tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh, not tests/micros/. And run_tests returns `totals`, a tuple (lib/test.mn:99), not an exit code — the synthesized main must map failed>0 to 1 or the "exits NONZERO" leg cannot fire at all.

5. NAME THE DEFAULT-TARGET COLLISION. test_run defaults entry_module to "test" (main.mn:466-468). driver_module_path (driver.mn:47-66) tries `test.mn`, `src/test.mn` (absent), then `lib/test.mn` (PRESENT) — so in-repo, bare `mentl test` resolves its default to the stdlib framework itself. Change the default or name it positively.

6. KEEP, UNCHANGED: the delete of assert_reporter (main.mn:483-492) and its false comment (main.mn:450-458) — a genuine near-duplicate with zero other callers and a comment naming verify_assert, which has zero definitions repo-wide. This is the item's strongest and fully-earned ground: a documentation-trust violation inside the medium's own source, exactly what SYNTAX.md's comment law forbids ("a comment shows what IS, not what was"). Keep the shell-shim reuse (install.sh:34-50) as named scaffold-tier — its own comment already states the seam honestly ("WASI has no exec — the wheel's run verb names this exact seam; the shim owns it"), and run_run's stub makes the shim the only real crossing. Keep the `new` verdict as-is; it is correct as claimed.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.repo.public-readiness-residue
  License and public visibility are done and verified live; the one column-5-owned, S11-REQUIRED piece still missing is the CI transport — no .github/workflows exists despite tools/ci/run-board.sh already being a complete, runnable step with nothing to trigger it.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the spine survives every check I could run (PUBLIC + apache-2.0 from LICENSE-APACHE, remote main == local HEAD, zero .github/, run-board.sh complete and untriggered, gate measured RED), but the design's only new action for its own piece (1) is refuted outright on live evidence, its CONTRIBUTING.md argument rests on a false premise about CLAUDE.md, and its gate passes on an empty file.
  corrections: 1. DELETE the root-LICENSE cosmetic entirely. Piece (1) is "done, zero further action" full stop — no optional tail. The design must not cite the Rust convention for it: rust/serde/cargo all lack a root LICENSE and all report a single Apache-2.0, identically to mentl. Mentl is already at the convention.

2. REPLACE the CONTRIBUTING.md argument; KEEP the verdict. The stated premise is false: grepping CLAUDE.md for `pull request|PR|branch|submit|review|fork|issue` yields only false positives (`<|` branches, the `$ftN` fork). CLAUDE.md answers style exhaustively and names the gates, but answers NOTHING on "how do I submit a change" — 1 of the 3 questions the design says it "already answers exactly." Its audience is wrong too: it opens "You read and update exactly three files... every session" (CLAUDE.md:3-4) — session discipline for an agent, not for PLAN §11's stated "external early adopter who clones, installs, and ships a wasm artifact." The correct native argument is the project's own §0 law: "discipline written down cannot enforce itself... `// please don't allocate here` has never saved anyone." A CONTRIBUTING.md IS a `// please` file. THE BOARD IS THE CONTRIBUTION PROTOCOL — PLAN §11: "the green board (`tools/state.sh`) IS the release gate" — a mechanical refusal where CONTRIBUTING is a prose request. That argument is strictly stronger and survives the false premise. And the home for the human-facing residue is README, not CLAUDE.md: README.md:595 already carries "Ground truth is one command: `bash tools/verify.sh`."

3. FIX THE GATE — it passes vacuously. Demonstrated: `mkdir -p .github/workflows && touch board.yml` satisfies `test -n "$(find .github/workflows -name '*.yml')"`. It proves a file exists, not that CI runs the board. The design's own artifact-state section already used `gh` successfully, so the honest measurement is available: `gh run list --limit 1 --json conclusion --jq '.[0].conclusion'` == "success" on the pushed HEAD. Currently RED either way (`gh workflow list --all` returns empty), so the residual is real — the weak gate is a choice, and the wrong one.

4. NAME WHAT "(the GitHub-hosted-runner-safe parts)" HIDES. The parenthetical does real work and conceals a contradiction. run-board.sh:5-6 says "Usable as a GH Actions SELF-HOSTED runner step"; PLAN.md:1402 says "Any always-on Linux with wasmtime serves" — both target a persistent host. The design silently re-targets to ephemeral GH-hosted runners, where: (a) run-board.sh:10 `git pull --ff-only` is dead on a detached-HEAD checkout (masked by `|| true`); (b) run-board.sh:17-20 appends to the working-tree file `tools/ci/perf-ledger.tsv`, which is DESTROYED with the runner and never committed back — the script's entire stated purpose ("append the perf ledger", header line 3) produces nothing; (c) the numbers are noise across varying hardware, against PLAN §11 col 3 "fixed hardware sharpens it." The board itself is genuinely host-agnostic (needs only wasmtime + wat2wasm, both installable on ubuntu-latest). SPLIT THEM: the correctness board runs GH-hosted per push; the perf ledger requires a persistent host and is column 5's to schedule with the MI300X arc. Do not conflate them behind a parenthetical.

5. Minor factual repairs. (a) The cited HEAD `4a20b52f` is STALE — HEAD is now `7e47e850`; the substantive claim survives (`git ls-remote` → refs/heads/main == 7e47e850 == local HEAD), only the SHA rots. Cite the claim, not the SHA. (b) "the ONE column-5-owned, S11-REQUIRED piece still missing" OVERCLAIMS: PLAN.md:1405 also lists "Diverse double-compilation (band J)" under column 5, and it is absent from §11's NOT-required list, so it is a second required-and-missing column-5 item (itself DEP-blocked on the native backend, which IS not-required — a PLAN tension worth surfacing, not this item's fault). Say "the CI transport is the piece column 1's readiness bullet depends on," not "the one piece." (c) The design cites "Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin found live in PROVENANCE.md" as precedent; that peer appears NOWHERE in the artifact (grep across *.md/*.mn/*.sh: zero hits). boot/PROVENANCE.md exists, the peer name does not. Do not cite an unlanded sibling proposal with "found live" — that phrasing asserts artifact truth the artifact does not carry. (d) SECURITY.md's "genuinely outside the novelty audit's jurisdiction" is slightly overstated: GitHub's native private-vulnerability-reporting is a repo setting (currently everything under security_and_analysis reads disabled), so the reporting CHANNEL dissolves into a toggle; only the policy prose (scope, response expectation) is irreducible. Keep the file, narrow the claim.

6. BLAST RADIUS — the design never states it, and should. Every correction here is inert to the compile: no .mn, no tools/*.sh on the gate path, no boot re-pin. m2 == m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros are all untouched. Say so; a design that omits its blast radius reads as if it did not check.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.docs.readme-quickstart
  A five-command 'clone to your first proven program' block, added to README.md's closing section, using only verbs and output already measured working end to end — gated on the reachability-scoped-report fix so the fourth command's stderr is actually empty.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the item is PLAN.md:1276's own line and should land, but the design as specified would ship a broken quickstart: its flagship fifth command is a Carried-Truth violation against a ledger that has already drifted (f7a1b3a5 re-pinned boot/mentl.wasm without touching boot/PROVENANCE.md), its clone URL is SSH against a verified-PUBLIC repo, "five commands" omits two hard prerequisites, its gate clobbers the developer's global shim, and its one hard blocker is misdiagnosed (528 of the 668 stderr lines are error-severity, not report-scoping noise).
  corrections: 1. DROP command 5; put the pin on the board. Replace `sha256sum boot/mentl.wasm` + human eyeball with a PIN gate inside tools/state.sh (or march.sh's bless, where f7a1b3a5 forgot it), and let the README's fifth line be `bash tools/state.sh`. The design already wants state.sh as the ground-truth pointer — this collapses two lines into one and makes the medium check its own identity mechanically. Fold Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin into the board gate rather than leaving a prose ledger a human diffs. Only then is "the tool that proved your program is itself provable" a demonstration rather than a slogan.

2. RE-DIAGNOSE the blocker — it is not report-scoping. MEASURED on the live shim: command 4's stderr is 528 `error:`-severity lines and 140 `Warning:` lines, including `infer: E_MissingVariable error: alloc` ×38, `len` ×28, `list_index` ×28; `effects: E_EffectMismatch error: effect row mismatch: GraphRead vs Alloc + Memory + GraphRead`; `effects: E_PurityViolated error: expected Pure but found effects: Memory`; `ownership: E_OwnershipViolation error: ref binding 'acc' escapes its scope (returned)`. DECISIVE EXPERIMENT: `import runtime/io` + `fn main() = 0` (calls NOTHING) produces an IDENTICAL 668/528 count and an identical diagnostic kind-census to hello.mn — only type-variable numbering shifts; `fn main() = 0` with no import produces ZERO lines. So the report is the whole imported closure, and whether print_string's own reachable chain is diagnostic-free is UNMEASURED — yet the gate asserts "stderr empty." Measure that split BEFORE claiming the dependency. Deeper: a compile that exits 0 with byte-correct stdout while printing 528 `error:` lines violates PLAN §11 column 2's own bar ("Every claim it makes about a program is true; no known silent-wrong class"). Scoping by reachability HIDES the contradiction instead of resolving it — the silent-fallback the project deletes rather than wraps. This is a column-2 correctness item, not a column-1 README item.

3. Command 1 must be HTTPS: `git clone https://github.com/ampactor-labs/mentl.git`. `gh repo view` confirms `"visibility":"PUBLIC"`. SSH requires a GitHub account plus a registered key — it fails for exactly the audience PLAN §11 column 1 names ("the external early adopter who clones, installs, and ships"). The gate as designed passes for Morgan (keys registered) and fails for the reader: vacuous for its author.

4. It is NOT five commands. tools/wt-env.sh:25/28 require wasmtime (`$HOME/.wasmtime/bin/wasmtime`) and wat2wasm; tools/install.sh has ZERO dependency checks (grep for wasmtime|wat2wasm|command -v → no hits). PROVEN LIVE: with WASMTIME_BIN=/nonexistent, install.sh still prints `installed: … (live pointer)` and command 4 dies with a raw bash error, not a teaching refusal. install.sh asserts "installed" about a compiler that cannot run — the same Carried-Truth violation one layer down. Either name the prerequisites honestly above the block, or (native form) install.sh REFUSES with a teaching message when the toolchain is absent, per the medium's own law that every refusal teaches. The design verified install.sh on a box that already had the toolchain — it verified the wrong thing.

5. The gate is DESTRUCTIVE. tools/install.sh:19 is `BIN_DIR="${MENTL_BIN_DIR:-$HOME/.local/bin}"`. The gate runs `bash tools/install.sh` verbatim from a throwaway clone, so it repoints the developer's real ~/.local/bin/mentl (verified present, MENTL_HOME=/home/suds/Projects/mentl) at the throwaway, which is then deleted — leaving a dangling global shim. I reproduced the write live. It is destructive precisely BECAUSE install.sh is "a pointer, never a copy" — the property the README paragraph celebrates. The gate must set MENTL_BIN_DIR to a scratch path. Separately, the gate clones origin, so it tests origin/main, not the working tree: "does not merge until green" is circular for unpushed content.

6. Fix the dead link in the sentence you are already editing. `bootstrap/` does not exist (`ls: cannot access 'bootstrap/'`; PLAN §6: "THE SEED IS DELETED (7401c4b)"), yet README.md:595 — the exact sentence-run whose "Ground truth" tail the design rewrites — links `[bootstrap/](bootstrap/)`. PLAN §5.R R6 names "fix the doc-truth debt" on the critical path. Editing that sentence and leaving the lie is drift-9 (deferred-by-omission) at the doc layer. The design's premise that the closing section is sound and only needs an addition is false.

7. Minor factual: README.md is 597 lines, not 598; the closing section is 593-597. Use the form the medium itself teaches — `mentl new` prints `mentl run hello`, while the design's command 4 says `mentl run hello.mn`. Both work (verified), but the README should not teach a second form one line after the medium projected the first.

CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.synth.lambda-candidate-param-shape
  mentl edit traps on its very first cursor read, before rendering anything, because a synthesized lambda-shape candidate carries a param list of bare strings where the AST demands typed TParam records — a one-file, precisely-pinned fix that blocks both other items in this cluster from ever being observed running.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the stringify defect is real (LambdaExpr demands List[TParam], synth_param_names hands it List[String]), but the design's causal story is false, its GATE passes vacuously on an unfixed tree, and its fix runs backwards from Law 1: it ADDS a fresh-minting fn where the Carried-Truth fix DELETES 14 lines and reads what the graph already holds (measured: my pass-through fix compiles, assembles, and turns all four matrix rows green).
  corrections: 1. GATE — pin the ARITY, not the filename. The discriminating row must have a fn with >=1 param as the FIRST decl; both arity-0 rows pass unfixed and would freeze a permanently-green row into tools/frontier-gate.sh. Carry BOTH an arity-0 and an arity-1 row so the discriminator is legible in the matrix itself.

2. FIX DIRECTION — the design ADDS where Law 1 says DELETE. It proposes a new `synth_fresh_params(n)`. But TFun already carries the TParams (types.mn:41, `TFun(List, Ty, EffRow) // params: List[TParam]`), so synth_param_names RE-DERIVES a degraded String projection of what the graph holds live — Law 1 verbatim, and the fix is LESS code:
    fn synthesize_lambda_node(params, ret) = synth_node(LambdaExpr(params, synth_hole_node()))
  plus deleting synth_param_names/synth_param_loop (14 lines removed, 0 added).
  MEASURED, not asserted: compiles under the pinned boot (exit 0, wheel 40,736 lines), assembles (m2.wasm, 2,164,517 bytes), and turns all four matrix rows to EXIT=0 render=1 — including both arity-1 rows that trap at 134 today. Diagnostic classes fully normalized: 375 baseline vs 375 fixed, ZERO introduced, ZERO removed (raw 3140 -> 3136, the 4 being the deleted fns' own).

3. "Borrowing the caller's TParam identity was never correct even before the stringify bug" — REFUTED by the artifact. mint_params (infer.mn:3237-3248) mints a FRESH type handle per param (`let h = mint(FnParam(owner_name, idx, ...))`, line 3240) and binds `TVar(h)`; it never reads param_ty(p). Pass-through therefore shares NO type identity — only the name and authored Ownership, exactly what a candidate satisfying that target type should carry. The working pass-through build proves it.

4. `graph_fresh_ty` is misread as a TParam-minter. It returns a graph HANDLE (parser.mn:19-23: `fn fresh_handle(span) = { let h = graph_fresh_ty(Placeholder(span)); graph_index_span(span, h); h }`; op declared graph.mn:126). A TParam is a plain ADT record — `TParam(String, Ty, Ownership, Ownership, Option(Node))` (types.mn:207) — not a graph node. "Mint n fresh TParam records ... via graph_fresh_ty(Placeholder(span_zero()))" yields an Int and supplies neither field the trap site consumes.

5. TRAP SITE UNDER-DESCRIBED, and the missing half sinks the proposed fix. The trap is inside mint_params, which reads BOTH `param_name(p)` (infer.mn:3239) AND `param_authored(p)` (infer.mn:3246). So a "fresh" TParam must supply a NAME — and the design names no source for one. A nameless mint would `env_extend("", ...)`, fabricating an empty-string binder: the exact fabrication class the repo already paid for (protocol_parser_fabrication_substrate — fabricated empty-string on non-TIdent). Pass-through fabricates nothing.

6. BLAST RADIUS is unstated. The design says nothing about the march. This is a src/-source change with no emit change, so m2 == m3 should hold in one generation (PLAN §5.R band D: "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation ... the transition form is for EMIT changes only") — but the design must say so and run tools/march.sh. I verified compile + assemble + behavior matrix + diagnostic-class neutrality only; I did NOT verify the fixpoint.

7. INTERCONNECTION overstated the same way: it blocks the other two items only for fixtures whose caret lands on an arity >= 1 decl. A zero-param entry renders all eight aspects today (exit 0, 11 lines). Still a real blocker for realistic fixtures, not the unconditional one claimed.

8. The NOVELTY VERDICT ("Not a design choice; a construction defect. No borrowed form to audit") is what let the wrong direction through. Mint-fresh vs read-what-the-graph-holds IS a design choice, and it is precisely the Carried-Truth call. Declaring "no design choice here" skipped the one audit that mattered.
==============================================================================
CORRECTED (survives, with fixes): Hβ.verify-baseline.seed-alibi-stale
  tools/verify-baseline.txt — the file column 2's own new spine explicitly leans on as the census ratchet — still carries nine-plus footnotes saying gates "await the seed catching up," six days after the seed was deleted, and at least one of the nine is provably closed already while its footnote still says otherwise.
  verdict    : CORRECTED — the core claim survived a real attempt to kill it (I ran the micro: PASS exit=30 diags=0, so the footnote's "NOT enforced here" is provably false against the same file), but the design's gate is provably vacuous, two of its nine claimed targets are misreads of honest Law-7 refusals, and its scope is moles where the artifact hands you the whole census in one grep.
  corrections: Four corrections the design must absorb. The item lands; this is not a kill.

1. THE COUNT IS INFLATED — TWO OF THE NINE ARE NOT SEED ALIBIS, AND SWEEPING THEM WOULD DELETE HONEST LAW-7 REFUSALS.
- tools/verify-baseline.txt:216 Hβ.lower.fanout-thread-value-gate: "needs the wasi-threads RUNTIME to run end-to-end — (NOT forced; Law 7 — a schedule the gate can't verify deterministically is never claimed)". The blocker is the wasi-threads runtime. It survives the seed's deletion untouched.
- tools/verify-baseline.txt:245 Hβ.types.resume-world-mismatch-value-gate: "needs STEP 3's persist resume-catcher to construct the boundary — (NOT forced; Law 7...)". PLAN.md §5.R band B independently corroborates: "the runnable gate; layout-in-world coupling; DEP persist resume-catcher + STEP 1". The footnote is correct and present-tense TODAY.
Both name Law 7 explicitly. That is PLAN §9.7 honored ("a path the gate cannot construct deterministically is never claimed"), not an expired alibi. Nine drops to seven. tools/verify-baseline.txt:264 (show) is the hybrid — "the seed's show generator + the string runtime (the libless harness links neither)" — where the libless-harness half is a live blocker and only the seed half expires. Re-audit splits it; it does not delete it.

2. THE GATE MUST KEY ON THE HANDLE, NOT THE PROSE. Replace the four-pattern phrase-grep with the peer-handle census, which is complete in one measurement:
    grep -rhoE 'Hβ\.seed\.[a-z0-9-]+' src/ lib/ tools/ tests/ | sort -u
Measured: 20 distinct Hβ.seed.* peers. Every one names a compiler that does not exist, so the NAMESPACE is the ratchet and its target is zero. For each handle assert either (a) it resolves to a passing `micro:` line whose exit equals the value the footnote calls pending — then the footnote AND the peer are deleted (the multishot case: :203 vs :446, verified PASS); or (b) a live blocker named in present tense with a re-verify command attached. This cannot pass vacuously, and it is the design's own novelty verdict made mechanical — the replacement rule "no comment may state a blocking condition without a re-verify command" is unenforceable by prose-grep, because no grep detects "a blocking condition stated without a re-verify command." The handle is the edge; follow it.

3. THE SCOPE IS MOLES; THE ARTIFACT HANDS YOU THE CENSUS. The design's gate scopes to one file. Measured outside tools/verify-baseline.txt: 39 hits. tests/micros carries ~14 (mn-effarg-node:16,18 · mn-negation-poly:39,41 · mn-struct-eq-deep:20,26 · mn-fanout-persist:6,14 · mn-expect-recover:12 · mn-ownership-infer:16,18 · mn-diag-projection:19,22 · mn-compare-fold:23 · mn-egraph-saturate:19,21 · mn-fanout-simd:13,14,21 · mn-feedback-iir:20 · mn-float-gate:11 · mn-hash-fold:23 · mn-labeled-args:8,10 · mn-multishot:26). Beyond tests: lib/dsp/feedback.mn:29, lib/runtime/lsp_frame.mn:182, tools/verify.sh:72-73, and the sharpest one — src/types.mn:2043, THE WHEEL'S OWN SOURCE, "The mint is the seed catching up — peer". boot compiles that line. CLAUDE.md's own law governs: "CENSUS, never moles. The moment a second trap shares a first trap's shape, STOP fixing per-site. Enumerate the WHOLE class in one measurement." Fix the class, not the file. Note tests/micros/mn-multishot.mn:26 names Hβ.seed.multishot-capture — a TWENTIETH peer, distinct from the -producer the baseline names, for the same closed gate.
