REFUTED: Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk (multi-core half)
  The topological layer-partition is already the correctness proof (driver.mn:268-298, live since 7165bbb); layer 4 is turning `each` into `><` at that one site, which is only safe once the graph, the env, and linear memory each get a per-thread disjoint-write reservation keyed on the same deterministic layer-position index.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the item's one action (flip `each` to `><` at driver.mn:183/229) sits on a path the self-compile never executes (march.sh gen() passes no argv → main.mn:219 → pipeline.mn:143's compile_stdin, which never enters driver.mn), which PLAN.md:695-696 already states outright ("the level-set partition (driver.mn) is off the hot path") and which PLAN.md:754-758 contradicts on granularity ("DECL granularity on the compile spine"); so it delivers zero on the perf floor it is filed under, its m2==m3 gate passes vacuously because the changed code never runs during m2/m3 generation, its stride reservation is O(N²) against graph_extend_to's dense sentinel fill (graph.mn), and its "type-system proof" is a grep over comment blocks in a file where 241 of 267 fns declare no row and `!GraphWrite` appears nowhere in the repo.
  objection: The item proposes flipping `each` to `><` at driver.mn:183 and driver.mn:229 as the multi-core half of §5.O layer 4 — the performance floor. The self-compile never executes either site, so the change delivers exactly zero on the path layer 4 exists to accelerate, and its gate cannot fail.

Traced, not inferred:
- tools/march.sh:70-71 builds $WHEEL = `find src -name '*.mn' | sort | xargs cat` + `find lib ...`; march.sh's `gen()` = `timeout 9000 "$WT" run -D coredump=... "${WT_RUN_FLAGS[@]}" "$1" < "$WHEEL" > "$2" 2> "$3"` — NO argv, stdin redirect only.
- src/main.mn:176 `fn main(argv) = match parse_cli_args(argv) {` ... :219 `if stdin_is_tty() { welcome_run() } else { compile_stdin_run() }` — zero argv + non-tty stdin routes to compile_stdin_run.
- src/main.mn:382-388 `compile_stdin_run` calls `compile_stdin()`.
- src/pipeline.mn:142-143: `fn compile_stdin() = { read_stdin() |> frontend |> infer_program |> saturate_pass |> lower_program |> reachable_from_main |> executable_gate |> emit_module }`. No driver_check. No driver_partition_layers. No driver_check_module.
- The only callers of the layer walk are driver_check (driver.mn:178, its `each` at :183) and driver_check_entry (driver.mn:222, its `each` at :229), reached from pipeline.mn:165, 230, 258, 365 and main.mn:289, 354 — the check/doc/edit verbs.
- Even `mentl compile <module>` misses it: pipeline.mn:58-64 routes through `driver_compile_entry` (driver.mn:202-220), which is `driver_collect_dag` + `fold("", ...)` string concatenation + ONE `parse_program` + ONE `infer_program`. It never calls driver_partition_layers.
- PLAN.md:695-696 states it verbatim: "Zero parallelism (pipeline.mn:100): the whole self-compile is ONE sequential `|>` cursor, 8 cores idle; the level-set partition (driver.mn) is off the hot path."
- PLAN.md:754-758 defines what layer 4 actually is: "the level-set partition at DECL granularity on the compile spine, infer/lower/emit fanned across cores with (arena_id, offset) deterministic handle partitioning so native_m3==native_m4 holds."

So the item bound the peer's NAME (Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk — "driver") to driver.mn's module-granularity check-path walk, when the peer's SPEC places the multi-core half at decl granularity on the compile spine. The design half-admits it and calls the real target a refinement: "module-granularity first, matching what driver.mn partitions today; decl-granularity as the refinement." That is the named drift — "given the current substrate," the target shaped around the artifact's silhouette (CLAUDE.md red-flags; PLAN §9.3 "Sequence the WORK; never the TARGET").

The gate collapses with it. PLAN.md:761-763 says layer 4 "CHANGES bytes (handle numbers shift under the partition — a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3, the sharpest risk)". Under this design m2 == m3 would hold trivially and byte-identically — not because concurrency is deterministic, but because m2/m3 generation never runs the changed code. The item says nothing about the fixpoint at all, and its proposed micro (two threads, disjoint handle ranges, no overwrite) tests the reservation arithmetic, never the design's actual claim that parallel module-checking yields the same env and graph as the sequential walk. A gate that cannot fail is not a gate.

Two independent kills, in case the siting were somehow argued away:

(1) The reservation is O(N²), against the artifact. src/graph.mn `fn graph_extend_to(own nodes, min_size)`: when `min_size > len(nodes)` it does `make_list(new_cap)` then `graph_fill_free(filled, old_len, new_cap)`, which writes an NFree sentinel into EVERY slot from old_len to cap. A private graph_handler instance starts at `nodes = []` (graph.mn:57). So thread i's FIRST mint at handle = base(i) = i·stride triggers graph_extend_to(nodes, i·stride + 1) → a fill of i·stride sentinels before any real work. Across N threads: Σ i·stride = O(N²·stride/2) writes and memory. The design's own words — "Merge is a concatenation of each thread's grown array segment at its pre-reserved offset" — confirm each thread grows its own array, so the fill is unavoidable on this design. This violates the law the item is filed under (PLAN §5.O / §9.1: "O(1) is the only acceptable complexity for any operation... a scan re-derives what an edge already connects") and it deliberately sparsifies the handle space in the one compiler whose measured amplifier PLAN already names as "a cache-hostile working set that MULTIPLIES the constant factor of every pointer-chase."

(2) The "type-system proof" is a grep over comments. The design: "lower.mn:9-10 and wasm.mn:9 state, and the type row proves (grep -c 'with GraphWrite' on both files returns 0), that neither stage writes the graph — a type-system proof, not an empirical hope." Verified: lower.mn:1-15 and wasm.mn:1-12 show both cited lines are COMMENT blocks, not declarations. `grep -c '^fn ' src/lower.mn` = 267; `grep -c '^fn .*with ' src/lower.mn` = 26 — 241 fns carry INFERRED rows, so the absence of the source string proves the absence of a DECLARATION, never the absence of the EFFECT (SYNTAX.md: "declared row is a CONSTRAINT verified against the row inferred from the body's op-call sites"). lower.mn:9-10's own claim, "A stray perform graph_bind would fail at handler install," is false against the artifact: graph_handler is installed over the entire compile chain (pipeline.mn:79 for compile, pipeline.mn:158 for compile_stdin), so a stray graph_bind from lower would be caught and silently handled. And `grep -rn '!GraphWrite' src/ lib/` returns EMPTY — the crown, which PLAN §0 pt 2 calls "the only system on Earth that proves absence transitively" and whose by-name soundness gate landed at 29df478, is unused at the exact site where the design's safety argument depends on proving absence. The design reached for a source-text scanner where the medium's own negation primitive is the native, stronger, landable form. That is the novelty audit failing on the item's load-bearing claim.

For fairness: the item's factual base is otherwise accurate and it clearly read the artifact. Confirmed true: driver_partition_layers/peel at driver.mn:268/275; driver_collect_dag_with_deps at :240; driver_check at :178 with its each at :183; driver_check_entry at :222 with its each at :229; driver_check_module at :137 carrying GraphWrite + EnvWrite; graph_handler's single nodes/trail/next state at graph.mn:57 with handles minted from `next` at :126-144; env_handler's single entries_buf/entries_count/index at pipeline.mn:438; the $wasi_thread_spawn import at wasm.mn:1753; `(memory (export "memory") 65536 65536 shared)` at wasm.mn:1762; $heap_ptr init 1048576 at wasm.mn:1768; the $alloc body's plain global.get/global.set at wasm.mn:1782 contradicting its own neighbouring comment at wasm.mn:1765; the $wasi_thread_start trampoline at wasm.mn:2622-2648; `-S threads=y` at tools/wt-env.sh:26; and parallel_compose/wasi_threads installed at 8 sites each, including compile_stdin's chain. The wasm.mn:1765-vs-1782 contradiction is a genuine, live find worth its own peer. None of it rescues the item, because the site is wrong.
  fixes    : Five corrections survive; the first two are fatal to the item as scoped, the last three are salvage.

1. RE-SITE THE ITEM, OR DELETE IT. The design must say: the multi-core half of Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk lands at DECL granularity on the COMPILE SPINE (pipeline.mn:143's `frontend |> infer_program |> saturate_pass |> lower_program`), which is what PLAN §5.O layer 4 (PLAN.md:754-758) actually specifies — not at driver.mn:183/229, which PLAN.md:695-696 names as off the hot path and which main.mn:219 → pipeline.mn:142-143 proves the self-compile never enters. "Module-granularity first, matching what driver.mn partitions today; decl-granularity as the refinement" must be struck: it is the target lowered to the artifact's silhouette, and it delivers exactly zero on the measured 10s path. If the item genuinely wants faster `mentl check`, it must be re-filed out of §11 col 3 (the performance floor) and argued on check latency, where a user project's DAG makes the win near-nil anyway.

2. FIX THE DOC-TRUTH CONFLICT THE ITEM WALKED INTO (this is a real find, credit it). driver.mn:170-176 comments that "the multi-core half is a `><` fanout under its own `~> Thread` written at this site." PLAN.md:754-758 says decl-granularity on the compile spine. The artifact (march.sh gen(), main.mn:219, pipeline.mn:143) sides with PLAN. The comment is drift and should be corrected as part of PLAN §5.R's R6 doc-truth debt — it is what mis-aimed this design.

3. REPLACE THE STRIDE WITH THE REGION. Strike `stride = ALLOC_FACTOR * source_byte_length` entirely. It is a fabricated quantity (predicted allocation estimated from a source-byte proxy plus a safety margin), it is O(i·stride) per thread against graph_extend_to's dense sentinel fill, and its own overflow escape ("report + recompile that layer sequentially") is a surrender-fallback with a second code path to keep in sync — Law 2 says restructure or stop. The native form is already named and already listed as this item's dependency: a REGION per unit (own/Consume; the region drop IS the O(1) arena reset; !Alloc provable after reset) — Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena, §5.O layer 3, whose substrate is live (src/own.mn, src/types.mn, src/pipeline.mn; the dormant emit_memory_arena swap at wasm.mn:139). The design's own SEQUENCING paragraph warns against "inventing a boundary that then has to be reconciled with layer 3's later one" and then invents one a paragraph later. Let layer 3's region boundary BE the per-thread arena.

4. REPLACE THE GREP WITH THE CROWN. Strike "the type row proves (grep -c 'with GraphWrite' ... returns 0) ... a type-system proof, not an empirical hope." It is a byte-scan over source text that also scans comments; lower.mn:9-10 and wasm.mn:9 are comments; 26 of 267 lower.mn fns declare any row (grep -c '^fn ' = 267, grep -c '^fn .*with ' = 26), so 241 inferred rows sit outside the grep's reach; and lower.mn:9-10's "would fail at handler install" is false because graph_handler wraps the whole chain (pipeline.mn:79, 158). The design must instead DECLARE `with !GraphWrite` on lower_program and emit_module and let row_subsumes' EfNeg by-name gate (landed 29df478) discharge it. `grep -rn '!GraphWrite' src/ lib/` is empty today — that declaration IS the landable, gateable, strictly-stronger form, and writing it is a real contribution this item could make on its own. Reaching for a scanner where the medium's own proof-of-absence is the native primitive is the novelty audit failing on the item's load-bearing safety claim.

5. RE-ARGUE THE NOVELTY VERDICT HONESTLY. The rejection of "jemalloc/tcmalloc-style TLS heaps" rests on the claim that a generic thread-pool allocator "structurally lacks" a notion of job size. TLS arenas do not predict job size — they refill from a central heap on exhaustion, which is why they need no ALLOC_FACTOR and no overflow fallback. The design rejects the borrowed form for lacking a capability it does not need, and substitutes a guess. Either keep the refilling-slab shape and name it scaffold-tier honestly, or (correctly) replace it with the region — but the current argument is a slogan, not a proof of strength.

WHAT SURVIVES INTACT AND SHOULD BE SPLIT OUT NOW: the wasm.mn:1765-vs-1782 contradiction is real and artifact-confirmed. The comment at 1765 claims "$heap_ptr is read+written through atomic.rmw on the threading path"; the emitted $alloc body at 1782 is plain `(global.get $heap_ptr)` / `(global.set $heap_ptr)` with no atomics, while the module exports a `65536 65536 shared` memory (wasm.mn:1762) and imports `$wasi_thread_spawn` (wasm.mn:1753) with a live trampoline (wasm.mn:2622) under `-S threads=y` (tools/wt-env.sh:26). That is a latent silent-clobber the moment Thread.spawn performs, it is a correctness defect independent of this item's fate, and there is no thread micro in tests/micros/ (127 micros, none thread-related) to catch it. It deserves its own peer and its own gate — as an argument for items 2/3, not for item 1.

REFUTED: Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk (multi-core half)
  The topological layer-partition is already the correctness proof (driver.mn:268-298, live since 7165bbb); layer 4 is turning `each` into `><` at that one site, which is only safe once the graph, the env, and linear memory each get a per-thread disjoint-write reservation keyed on the same deterministic layer-position index.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's load-bearing artifact claim ("wasi-threads spawns a fresh module instance per thread ... only `memory` does [share]") is false against the binary: with the exact shape wasm.mn:1762 emits, threads spawn and run but linear memory is NOT shared, which inverts the problem from "partition the writes" to "nothing is reachable to merge," and both the design's proof (a grep that returns 0 for everything) and its gate (passes today, unlanded) are vacuous.
  objection: THE MEMORY IS NOT SHARED. wasm.mn:1762 emits `(memory (export "memory") 65536 65536 shared)` — a module-DEFINED memory. `shared` is a memory TYPE attribute (it makes atomics legal); it is not an instance-sharing mechanism. A module-defined memory is fresh per instantiation by the wasm spec, and wasi-threads re-instantiates the module per thread. So each thread gets its own 4GB image.

Race-free proof (wasmtime 43.0.1, canonical flags tools/wt-env.sh:26). Parent stores 0xCAFE at address 3000 STRICTLY BEFORE `wasi_thread_spawn`; child loads address 3000. No timing, no race:
  Mentl's exact shape        -> "CHILD SEES ZERO -> MEMORY IS *NOT* SHARED"
  spawn return value          -> tid=1 (spawn SUCCEEDS; child prints from fd_write, so it genuinely executes)
  child's i32.atomic.store@2048 -> never observed by parent (exit 7, 200M-spin poll)
The fix, verified at Mentl's real 4GB size: `(import "env" "memory" (memory $mem 65536 65536 shared))` + `(export "memory" (memory $mem))` -> "CHILD SEES PARENT WRITE -> SHARED". Note the asymmetry I measured: import-ONLY fails at the spawn call with "missing required memory export", so wasmtime needs import AND re-export.

This inverts the design's premise. There is no shared graph to race (graph.mn:56-60 `next` counter), no shared env (pipeline.mn:438 `entries_buf`), no shared heap to clobber. There is total isolation. The design's mechanism — reserve disjoint bases so writes don't collide — solves a problem that does not exist, and does not solve the one that does: a spawned thread's minted nodes live in an address space the parent cannot read, so the design's merge ("a concatenation of each thread's grown array segment at its pre-reserved offset") is not hard, it is unconstructible across two linear memories. Its one claimed free win — "lower and emit need none of this ... they fan out over decls for free" — dies too: a spawned thread cannot even GraphRead when the graph is in another memory.

SECOND KILL — the "type-system proof" is vacuous. The design claims lower/emit purity is proven because "grep -c 'with GraphWrite' on both files returns 0 — a type-system proof, not an empirical hope." That pattern structurally cannot match: rows are written mid-clause (driver.mn:138 `with Memory + Alloc + Filesystem + EnvRead + EnvWrite + GraphRead + GraphWrite + ...`). Demonstration: `grep -c 'with GraphWrite' src/driver.mn` returns **0** — for the very file the design says DOES write the graph. It returns 0 for everything. The conclusion is accidentally true (GraphWrite appears in lower.mn/wasm.mn only in comments: lower.mn:9, lower.mn:1836, wasm.mn:9) but the method is an empirical hope in a proof's costume — the exact inversion of the claim. Deeper: only 27 of 267 `fn` decls in lower.mn carry a `with` row at all, and `!GraphWrite`/`!EnvWrite` appear NOWHERE in src/ or lib/. SYNTAX.md §Negation: "`!E` proves ABSENCE ... Stronger than not-mentioning E because it propagates transitively." Absent mention is not absence.

THIRD KILL — the gate passes today, unlanded. "Assert both threads' handle ranges are disjoint and neither thread's minted node data was overwritten" is green RIGHT NOW for the wrong reason: two threads with private memories trivially never overwrite each other. The gate cannot distinguish "correctly partitioned" from "completely isolated." Vacuous by attack-order item 4.
  fixes    : 1. ARTIFACT STATE — strike the false premise. Replace "wasi-threads spawns a fresh module instance per thread (globals don't share; only `memory` does), two threads would both bump-allocate from the same address ... a silent clobber" with: "wasi-threads re-instantiates per thread; NEITHER globals NOR memory share, because wasm.mn:1762's memory is module-defined and `shared` is a type attribute, not an instance-sharing mechanism. Threads spawn (tid=1) and run, into total isolation. There is no clobber and no race — there is no reachable result." Likewise strike "so real OS threads spawn today when Thread.spawn performs" as evidence of readiness: they spawn into a private image, which is strictly less useful than not spawning.

2. NAME THE ROOT PREREQUISITE, upstream of items 2 and 3 and of §5.O layer 3: `Hβ.emit.shared-memory-import-reexport` — emit `(import "env" "memory" (memory $mem 65536 65536 shared))` + `(export "memory" (memory $mem))` in place of wasm.mn:1762's local definition. Verified working at Mentl's exact 4GB size. Until this lands, items 2 and 3 are no-ops: a distinct `$heap_ptr` base per thread partitions nothing when each thread owns a private memory. This also retires the wasm.mn:1765 comment's contradiction honestly — its "atomic.rmw on the threading path" is aspirational against the plain global.get/set body at wasm.mn:1782, but that body is not the bug; the bug is one altitude down.

3. BLAST RADIUS — the design is silent and must not be. Fixing the memory import changes the emitted preamble of EVERY module Mentl emits. Under PLAN §6 that is a TRANSITION (m2 != m3, m3 == m4, re-pin boot from m3), touching the boot chain — not the Law-7 no-op the design implies by treating the plumbing as already correct. This is the one piece of real fixpoint risk in the cluster and it exists only because the premise was misread.

4. DELETE THE STRIDE — adopt the design's own cited peer instead of inventing around it. `stride = ALLOC_FACTOR * source_byte_length`, "calibrated ... with a safety margin," plus "overflow ... report + recompile that layer sequentially," IS the borrowed size-class arena with a fudge factor and a surrender-fallback, not its replacement — so the NOVELTY VERDICT is backwards. It fabricates a fact (how many nodes will this decl mint?) that the graph knows only AFTER inference: a calibrated constant with a safety margin is a fabricated bound (§9.1), and the fallback is a surrender-fallback to be deleted, not wrapped (§9.2). It also breaks determinism — handles minted at base(i) vs. minted in the sequential fallback differ, so emitted bytes depend on whether a heuristic overflowed: a magic number gating m3 == m4. The native form is `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` (PLAN.md:864), which the design cites but demotes to "informs ... at a further remove": "fixed per-core (arena_id, offset) partition so handle assignment / code layout / rel-offsets are a pure function of the graph." Make the handle the PAIR (arena_id, offset). Then stride, ALLOC_FACTOR, calibration, overflow, and fallback all disappear — LESS code, deterministic by construction, and the same mechanism band N already committed to, inherited one altitude earlier rather than reinvented.

5. REPLACE THE PROOF WITH THE PROOF. Declare `with !GraphWrite + !EnvWrite` on the lower/emit entry points and let `row_subsumes`'s EfNeg by-name membership (29df478, crown 5/5) discharge it. That converts the design's grep into an actual type-system proof and makes its "lower/emit fan out for free" claim true rather than hoped. Name the residue: only 27/267 lower.mn fns declare rows, so the negation must be declared at the entry points where it is checkable, with the rest inferred against it.

6. RESEQUENCE THE GATE — positive sharing BEFORE absence-of-overwrite. Gate 1 (`Hβ.emit.shared-memory-import-reexport`): a two-thread micro where the child MUST OBSERVE a parent write issued strictly before spawn, and the parent MUST OBSERVE the child's minted nodes after join. This fails today and passes only once the import lands — mechanically checkable, non-vacuous. Gate 2 (this item): handle-range disjointness, which becomes meaningful only after Gate 1 proves the threads share an image at all. As written, Gate 2 alone would bless a design that does nothing.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.cursor.speculative-compile
  A different, already-named fanout site — proposing N candidate annotations for one gradient decision and verifying each on its own forked graph checkpoint — needs a checkpoint-fork of the per-thread graph, not the disjoint-write reservation layer 4 needs, and the two must not be conflated into one mechanism.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the gate cannot fail: caller-installed `~> Thread` reads Seq at the frame fence (src/lower.mn:1793) so the "parallel" variant IS the sequential baseline, while an internal install is the hardwired Thread-inject src/lower.mn:1750-1752 records as deleted; the SEQUENCING ("independent, can land in parallel") misses Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee, named at src/mentl.mn:241 in the second half of the very comment the design quotes from line 238; and the mechanism's "winning candidate's write is kept and re-applied" describes behavior neither caller has (src/mentl.mn:157-166 returns a suggestion; the apply is a TEXT patch, src/mentl.mn:277-279). The thesis, the interconnection, and the novelty verdict survive intact.
  objection: THE GATE IS REFUTED BY A FENCE THE DESIGN'S OWN QUOTED COMMENT NAMES — and it is refuted in both possible readings, so there is no escape.

src/lower.mn:1793, inside schedule_in_stack:
    if field_name_eq(stack_entry_name(e), "") { Seq }   // the frame fence — "the schedule is read at the fanout's own install site, never across a call boundary" (SYNTAX §Vocabulary)

try_each_annotation (src/mentl.mn:244) is a REUSABLE HELPER with two callers: gradient_next (src/mentl.mn:158) and oracle's candidates_at (src/oracle.mn:219). The gate says "Extend try_each_annotation with a ~> Thread-scheduled variant; assert candidate-by-candidate parity against the sequential baseline." Read it either way:

(a) `~> Thread` installs at try_each_annotation's OWN fanout site, inside the fn. Then it is not a "variant" — it hardwires Thread for BOTH callers unconditionally. That is precisely the construct src/lower.mn:1750-1752 records as DELETED ("`><` draws an edge; it does not schedule (the deleted Thread-inject was ...)"), it taxes gradient_next's per-keystroke felt path with N spawns for ~6-10 candidates, and it destroys the `!Thread` transitive proof SYNTAX.md §>< promises ("a real-time region can declare `with !Thread` and the medium PROVES, transitively, that no spawn occurs") for every caller. Drift, by the artifact's own record.

(b) The CALLER installs `~> Thread` around the call. The frame fence at lower.mn:1793 terminates schedule_in_stack at the frame boundary and returns Seq. The fanout inside try_each_annotation reads Seq. The "parallel" variant IS the sequential baseline; the gate compares Seq against Seq and passes vacuously. Refuted by attack #4.

This is not a novel finding — it is the parallel_map dissolution SYNTAX.md §Vocabulary already ruled on verbatim: "a standalone helper can never reach `Thread` scheduling at all: the schedule is read LIVE at the fanout's own install site ... so a `><` inside a reusable fn is permanently `Seq` while its name promises parallelism ... Caller-selectable fanout inside a reusable helper is the open peer `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` (PLAN §5.R band E)." try_each_annotation, a reusable helper serving two callers that want different schedules, IS the textbook case.

THE DAMNING PART: the design quotes src/mentl.mn:238 ("needs per-thread graph") and drops lines 239-241 of the SAME comment, which name the missing dependency at this exact site:
    238: // (peer Hβ.cursor.speculative-compile, needs per-thread graph) is a
    239: // `><` fanout under its own `~> Thread` at this site — the schedule is
    240: // read at the fanout's install, never across a call
    241: // (Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee). Symmetric with the oracle's
The design read half a comment and built its SEQUENCING on the half it dropped. That is why "Independent of Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk ... can land in parallel. Both depend on graph.mn growing SOME notion of a thread-safe write-extension, which is the actual shared prerequisite worth building once" is false: it names one prerequisite and misses the binding one. Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee is an established peer named at 10 sites (src/cursor.mn:151,285; lib/test.mn:76; src/mentl.mn:241,727; src/synth_proposer.mn:542; src/eight_loop.mn:209; src/driver.mn:172; src/pipeline.mn:335,616).

SECOND, INDEPENDENT REFUTATION — the design's mechanism describes behavior neither caller has. "only the eventual winning candidate's write is ever kept, and it is re-applied once, sequentially, by the parent" is fabricated. gradient_next (src/mentl.mn:157-166) does `match pick_highest_leverage(proven) { ... Some(pair) => Some(to_annotation_suggestion(ann, anchor_reason)) }` — it RETURNS a suggestion (src/mentl.mn:386: "Only PROVEN annotations reach the user"); the apply is a TEXT patch (src/mentl.mn:277-279). candidates_at (src/oracle.mn:219-221) maps survivors to QueueItems. No write is kept anywhere; a "parent replays the winner for real" step would be NEW machinery for a fact the graph does not hold (Anchor 2).

WHAT DISPROVED ME where I tried to refute and failed: the NOVELTY VERDICT is honest, not a slogan. I checked all three legs. PLAN §1 does name Oracle-Guided Inductive Synthesis / Synthesis-Modulo-Oracles (Jha–Seshia 2017; Polgreen–Reynolds–Seshia VMCAI 2022) as the medium's own architecture, so fork-verify-keep-winner genuinely is the thesis's shape rather than imported fluency. Portfolio SAT/SMT solving really is try-N-configs-on-N-threads-keep-best (ManySAT, cube-and-conquer), so the borrowed form is named accurately rather than waved at. And the fork mechanism really is native: graph_push_checkpoint (src/graph.mn:115-118) returns trail_len and pushes it on checkpoint_stack; graph_rollback (src/graph.mn:365-376) walks revert_trail backwards — a trail-checkpoint, not a foreign solver-state snapshot format. Keeping the portfolio pattern on this primitive is the correct call and I could not break it. Likewise threading.mn:214-217 (closure_result_addr = done+4) is real and does carry one i32 back, so the transport half of the boundary claim checks out — only the payload description ("and the chosen capability") is wrong.
  fixes    : 1. GATE — replace it; as written it cannot fail. "Extend try_each_annotation with a ~> Thread-scheduled variant" is impossible in both readings (see objection). Two honest replacements, pick one and say which:
   (i) DEP-gate on Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee (named at src/mentl.mn:241 — the second half of the comment the design quotes) and defer the gate until that peer lands; or
   (ii) THE ULTIMATE FORM: relocate the `><` fanout OUT of try_each_annotation to each caller's own site — gradient_next (src/mentl.mn:158) and candidates_at (src/oracle.mn:219) — where `>< + ~> Thread` installs directly and the frame fence is satisfied. PLAN §5.R band E explicitly prefers this ("direct `>< + ~> Thread` at the use site is sufficient and simpler; build only when a real consumer needs one fanout helper serving callers wanting different schedules"). It also keeps `!Thread` provable for gradient_next's per-keystroke felt path while letting oracle's batch path thread.

2. SEQUENCING — strike "Independent of Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk ... can land in parallel" and strike "the actual shared prerequisite worth building once" as the ONLY prerequisite. At this site the binding dep is Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee (or correction 1(ii) dissolves it). The thread-safe write-extension is a real second prerequisite, not the only one.

3. DESIGN — strike "only the eventual winning candidate's write is ever kept, and it is re-applied once, sequentially, by the parent after all candidates report" and "The parent picks the winner ... and replays it for real, once." NO write is ever kept at either caller. gradient_next (src/mentl.mn:157-166) wraps the winner via to_annotation_suggestion and RETURNS it as narration; the apply is a TEXT patch (src/mentl.mn:277-279: "The actual wrap ... is a TEXT patch — out of substrate, into the patcher's surface"); oracle's candidates_at (src/oracle.mn:219-221) maps survivors to QueueItems. The correct statement is STRICTLY STRONGER for the design's own thesis: every candidate rolls back unconditionally, nothing is ever kept, and only `ok : Bool` crosses back — which sharpens the ephemeral-fork vs disjoint-reservation distinction instead of weakening it with a fabricated exception.

4. FORK SCOPE — "a bounded FORK of graph_handler's nodes/trail" is insufficient as a general claim. verify_ledger (src/verify.mn:33, `handler verify_ledger with debt = []`) holds `debt` OUTSIDE graph state; graph_rollback (src/graph.mn:365-376) reverts only nodes/boundaries/next/trail_len/checkpoint_stack. apply_annotation_tentatively's ARefined arm (src/mentl.mn:264-267) performs `verify` into that ledger. Latent today — neither caller's candidate set emits ARefined (src/mentl.mn:155-156; gradient_candidates_at, src/oracle.mn:234-235, returns the same APure/ANotAlloc/ANotIO/ANotNetwork ++ ownership set) — but try_each_annotation accepts arbitrary candidates and ARefined is the arm unlocking CEliminateBoundsCheck (src/mentl.mn:528), i.e. exactly what the Verify arc wants next. Restate the requirement as: per-thread state for EVERY handler the candidate body touches, not "per-thread graph" (the comment's verbatim words, which the design accepted wholesale).

5. GATE LEG VACUOUS — drop "identical winning capability". unlock_capability is `with Pure` (src/mentl.mn:523), a total Annotation→Capability match, and it runs PARENT-side at src/mentl.mn:254 outside any checkpoint. If the Bool verdicts agree the capabilities agree by construction. This also corrects the design's boundary description: only `ok : Bool` crosses the fanout, never "the chosen capability".

6. NAME THE LIVE DEFECT this attack surfaced (positive form): Hβ.verify.ledger-debt-outlives-rollback. The comment at src/mentl.mn:265-266 ("Ledger captures; rollback removes.") is FALSE against src/graph.mn:365-376 — rollback never touches `debt`. This is a SEQUENTIAL correctness bug, not a threading one: the first ARefined candidate proposed will leak proof debt from a REJECTED speculative candidate into the user-visible ledger (src/verify.mn:242 reports VPending(len(debt))). It must be fixed before any candidate set grows ARefined, threaded or not.

WHAT SURVIVES UNCHANGED: the thesis (do not conflate ephemeral checkpoint-fork with disjoint permanent reservation — the two sites need different shapes); the INTERCONNECTION (one graph.mn capability, two modes: disjoint-permanent and disjoint-ephemeral-rolled-back-on-demand); and the NOVELTY VERDICT (keep portfolio solving, justified, built on the native trail-checkpoint) — I attacked that one and it held.

REFUTED: Hβ.driver.per-module-env-overlay
  Already named in PLAN band O as an incremental-compilation gap (env layering not yet built); giving it a per-thread dimension — not just a per-module one — turns it from a caching nicety into a hard correctness prerequisite for any parallel driver_check fanout.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design proposes to build a per-module overlay that ALREADY EXISTS in graph_handler (graph.mn:56-64, registering every mint in O(1)) and sits DORMANT because `graph_fork` is never performed; its central novelty claim ("the write set is disjoint BY CONSTRUCTION") is false against graph.mn:126-164; and I ran its gate against the pinned boot — it PASSES TODAY at exit 41, without the design landed.
  objection: THE WRITE SET IS NOT DISJOINT, AND THE DESIGN'S REASON TO EXIST INVERTS ON THAT FACT.

The novelty verdict's load-bearing sentence: "overlay-per-module-as-a-graph-edge makes the write set disjoint BY CONSTRUCTION (proven by the layer partition, never by a runtime lock)" — and the design's body: "no shared-table writes at all until the barrier."

Read src/graph.mn:126-164. EVERY `graph_fresh_ty` / `graph_fresh_row` writes six shared graph_handler fields:
  nodes (138) · next = next + 1 (139) · trail (140) · trail_len = trail_len + 1 (141) · overlay_bufs (142) · overlay_lens (143)
and reads `epoch` (130) + `current_overlay_idx` (132). Two threads in one layer both minting race on the single `next` scalar — lost update, two nodes at one handle, graph corruption. The layer partition (driver.mn:160-166) proves DEPENDENCY disjointness (module A does not import module B); it proves nothing about WRITE disjointness. The design conflates the two. An env overlay makes ENV writes disjoint and leaves `next`/`nodes`/`trail`/`epoch` racing — so the item's claimed status ("a hard correctness prerequisite for any parallel driver_check fanout") is exactly backwards: it delivers FALSE safety, the most expensive kind. The real prerequisite is already named in PLAN band N — `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` ("fixed per-core (arena_id, offset) partition") — which the design's INTERCONNECTION demotes to a "shares its shape" sibling (`Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base`) when it is a hard upstream DEPENDENCY.

SECOND (empirical, and mechanically undeniable) — THE GATE PASSES TODAY. I built the gate's exact fixture (two modules each declaring a same-named local `shared`; entry `a_thing() + b_thing() * 20`) and ran it through boot/mentl.wasm: compile exit 0, 0 diagnostics, **exit 41** = 1 + 2*20. Each module's local already resolved to its own binding — because pipeline.mn:459-470 (`env_scope_enter`/`env_scope_exit`) pops fn-local bindings at scope exit. The gate cannot fail if the design is not landed. Vacuous. The design's premise "a collision here today, under a flat table, is the exact bug" describes a bug that does not exist.

THIRD — THE OVERLAY ALREADY EXISTS AND IS DORMANT. graph.mn:56-64 declares overlay_names/overlay_bufs/overlay_lens/overlay_count/current_overlay_idx/current_overlay; graph.mn:131-132,151-152 + 724-733 register every minted handle into the current module's overlay in O(1); oracle.mn:400-409 (`current_overlay_of`/`overlay_owning`) reads them live. But `graph_fork` (types.mn:1245, handled graph.mn:318-346) is NEVER PERFORMED — zero call sites in src/ or lib/. So every handle lands in bucket 0, `"global"`, and oracle.mn:254 (`module_path: current_overlay_of(pos)`) reports "global" for every position: a live consumer reading a fact nobody writes. The design proposes to build a second overlay at the env altitude while the graph altitude's overlay is built, paid for on every allocation, and unactivated.

FOURTH — INTERROGATION 1 IS FALSE THREE WAYS. "The module node already exists in the DAG; the overlay is one more edge off it, never a new index." (a) `driver_collect_dag` (driver.mn:62-107) threads module_name STRINGS; `driver_partition_peel` (driver.mn:275) is typed `ref processed: [String], ref layers_rev: [[String]]`. The DAG is string-keyed — no node, no handle to hang an edge off. (b) `NModule` (types.mn:848) is the only module node kind and is NEVER CONSTRUCTED — all 12 references are match arms/comments, zero producers. Dead substrate. (c) synth_proposer.mn:291-296 records that this exact indirection was already tried and FAILED: "The NModule-decls indirection read a projection the edit session's weave does not always carry — main() leaked past the reach exclusion because its body was unreachable through it; the weave is the one truth both walks read." (d) The existing overlay IS "a new index" — three parallel arrays keyed by name-string, exactly what the design says it will never be.

FIFTH — THE NOVELTY VERDICT IS REFUTED BY THE CODEBASE. It rejects "per-crate DefId tables behind a query-database lock, as in incremental Rust frontends." graph.mn:13 says, at that exact altitude, in the substrate the design extends: **"Salsa 3.0 pattern: flat array + epoch + per-module overlay."** Salsa IS the Rust incremental query database (rust-analyzer's engine). The borrowed shape is already in the artifact, named, unaudited — and carries drift-7 (three parallel arrays, graph.mn:22-23, 41-46), a string-keyed O(n) scan (`overlay_find_loop`, graph.mn:741-745), and an admitted cached copy (graph.mn:46: "current_overlay — display name of the active module (redundant with names[idx])" — Carried-Truth violated, in the comment).

SIXTH — THE DESIGN REINTRODUCES A DELETED SIDE-LEDGER. "a small env fragment recording the names it exports" IS an exported-entries snapshot. driver.mn:150-156: "A module's exports live in env_handler because infer_program put them there — the graph carries them; there is no install step and no exported-entries snapshot." PLAN §5.U records the .kai layer DELETED WHOLE (2026-07-02) for exactly this: it "snapshotted env entries lossily, DROPPING Reason chains."

SEVENTH — CITATION DEFECT. "driver.mn's own header (lines 9-11) states plainly" — the header (driver.mn:1-13) is STALE ARCHAEOLOGY: lines 5-7 describe consulting "the .kai cache", deleted 2026-07-02 per PLAN §5.U, and contradicted by live code at driver.mn:150-156. The design anchors on a stale comment (global CLAUDE.md: "Treat AGENTS.md and similar docs as potentially stale"). The sub-claim survives only via independent corroboration at pipeline.mn:438 — which I confirmed exact (one handler, flat entries_buf/entries_count/scopes/index). infer.mn:859-867 also confirmed exact.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE THE DESIGN: pipeline.mn:438 and infer.mn:859-867 are cited accurately; the substantive "no per-module env overlay exists" claim is TRUE (env_handler has no module dimension); and the per-THREAD insight is genuinely novel and not already built — the graph overlay's `current_overlay_idx` is a single scalar in shared handler state, so it cannot serve N threads as-is. That residue is real and survives as a correction, not as this design.
  fixes    : WHAT THE DESIGN MUST SAY INSTEAD:

1. DROP the new env fragment. ACTIVATE the dormant substrate. `graph_fork(module_name)` (types.mn:1245, handled graph.mn:318-346) is declared, handled, and never performed; every mint already pays `overlay_register_at` into bucket 0 (graph.mn:131-132, 151-152). Perform it at the module boundary in `driver_check_module` (driver.mn:137-148). That is the Carried-Truth move — the fix is LESS code, and "audit dormant substrate first" is the standing protocol: "defined but uncalled" IS the slot for what you are about to build.

2. STATE THE REAL PREREQUISITE ORDER, WHICH THE DESIGN HAS BACKWARDS. `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` / per-thread `next` is a HARD UPSTREAM DEPENDENCY, not a shape-sharing sibling. Until `next`/`nodes`/`trail`/`trail_len`/`epoch` are per-thread partitioned (graph.mn:126-164 writes all six per mint), no overlay makes a parallel fanout safe. Sequence: per-thread handle partition → per-thread `current_overlay_idx` → `graph_fork` at the module boundary → `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk`. Delete the claim "no shared-table writes at all until the barrier"; it is false.

3. REBUILD THE GATE AS A REFUSAL GATE. The current gate is vacuous (proven: exit 41 today) and asserts behavior the authoritative surface forbids. SYNTAX.md §Imports: "A name collision is resolved once, at the import edge... The graph **refuses** two edges into one name rather than papering over it with a re-resolved dotted path." PLAN §11 column 2 (PLAN.md:1356) + band L (`Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name`, PLAN.md:860) require a DECL-SITE REFUSAL. The design's "assert each resolves to its own binding" canonizes the papering-over. The honest gate: a duplicate top-level name across two modules produces a decl-site diagnostic, nonzero exit, zero WAT — the same contract tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh already enforces for holes.

4. THE SANCTIONED CROSS-MODULE FORM NEEDS NO OVERLAY TABLE AT ALL. SYNTAX.md §Imports names it exactly: "binds each module's resolved env node once and reaches `.fft` through that single edge." A module env node is a RECORD (PLAN L1, the product node-kind); `mod.fft` is then ordinary structural field access — which infer.mn:867 (`infer_field_access_record`) ALREADY implements. A resolution-time per-module overlay re-derives what a record already is. Reframe the peer as `Hβ.infer.module-env-node-is-a-record`; the qualified-access-via-overlay reading is the residue SYNTAX.md calls "the residue the formatter rewrites, never the recommended disambiguator."

5. FIX THE EXISTING OVERLAY'S DRIFT BEFORE EXTENDING IT (it is the design's own altitude, unaudited): three parallel arrays (graph.mn:22-23, 41-46) → one record per overlay (drift-7); `overlay_find_loop`'s O(n) `==` name scan (graph.mn:741-745) → handle-keyed per §5.O layer 1 name-is-handle (Law 1 at the performance scale); delete `current_overlay` (graph.mn:46, admitted "redundant with names[idx]"). Note graph.mn:13's self-described "Salsa 3.0 pattern" is a BORROWED shape sitting unexamined in the substrate — the novelty audit must name it, then either keep it scaffold-tier or replace it.

6. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS HONESTLY. The design says only "full battery stays green." Activating `graph_fork` changes nothing byte-wise (overlays are read only by oracle.mn today), but any change to WHERE names resolve can shift emitted handle order → PLAN §5.O: "byte-identical where they change only HOW a fact is found, a TRANSITION where interning shifts emitted handle-order." Say which, and name the re-pin if TRANSITION.

7. BONUS — AN UNNAMED SILENT-WRONG CLASS FOUND WHILE TESTING THE GATE (not the design's fault; it violates PLAN §11 column 2's "no known silent-wrong class"). Two modules each declaring top-level `let shared` → compiler exit 0, ZERO diagnostics, 4,137 bytes of WAT that does not assemble: `out.wat:23:4: error: redefinition of global "$shared"`. The env resolves fine; the collision lands in EMIT as a duplicate WAT global — so an env overlay would not fix it, which is a further reason the design's reading-2 gate tests something its design does not address. Band L names `Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name` for `type` decls; the top-level `let`/global case is live and unnamed. Positive-form peer: `Hβ.diag.duplicate-toplevel-binding` — decl-site refusal at the one-namespace concatenation (driver.mn:202-215), same shape as band L's type case. Repro: two modules with `let shared = 1` / `let shared = 2`, entry importing both.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.driver.per-module-env-overlay
  Already named in PLAN band O as an incremental-compilation gap (env layering not yet built); giving it a per-thread dimension — not just a per-module one — turns it from a caching nicety into a hard correctness prerequisite for any parallel driver_check fanout.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's artifact state quotes a stale .kai-era header (driver.mn:9-13; `kai`/`IKAI` have zero hits in src/), its central "overlay attached to the module's node" has no node to attach to (`NModule` at types.mn:848 is minted nowhere in src/ or lib/; driver.mn:85-96 carries strings), and the exports fragment it proposes is the exact "exported-entries snapshot" the live code at driver.mn:131-135 and 150-152 says does not and should not exist.
  objection: The design's central mechanism — "a small env fragment recording the names it exports, attached to the module's node by an edge" — is refuted twice by the artifact, and its own citation is stale prose describing a subsystem deleted six weeks ago.

(a) THERE IS NO MODULE NODE. Interrogation 1 asserts "the module node already exists in the DAG; the overlay is one more edge off it." It does not exist. `NModule({path, decls, span})` is declared at types.mn:848 and CONSTRUCTED NOWHERE in src/ or lib/ — a repo-wide grep returns only the declaration plus consumers awaiting a producer (cursor.mn:404, cursor.mn:441, format.mn:323) and three infer no-ops (infer.mn:3984 `NModule(_) => acc`, infer.mn:4126 `=> UZero`, infer.mn:4308 `=> ()`). The driver's DAG is not nodes at all: `driver_collect_visit` (driver.mn:85-96) does `list_set(extended, count, module_name)` — module-name STRINGS. There is nothing to draw an edge from; minting NModule is an unnamed hard prerequisite the design skips while claiming the node is already there.

(b) THE ARTIFACT ALREADY REJECTED THE EXPORTS FRAGMENT — two lines below the design's own citation. driver.mn:131-135: "Infer one module: its exports install into env_handler as infer_program walks (the graph IS the record of what this module publishes — no snapshot, no side-file)." driver.mn:150-152: "A module's exports live in env_handler because infer_program put them there — the graph carries them; there is no install step and no exported-entries snapshot." An "env fragment recording the names it exports" IS an exported-entries snapshot — a copy of what the env already holds (Carried-Truth violated, the very law the design invokes), and the exact shape PLAN §5.U deleted for cause: the .kai layer "snapshotted env entries lossily, DROPPING Reason chains." The design claims a Reason-chain BENEFIT ("resolved via module M's overlay edge, strictly more provenance") from the shape whose deletion was justified by Reason-chain LOSS.

(c) THE CITED ARTIFACT STATE IS STALE ARCHAEOLOGY. The design quotes driver.mn:9-11 as what the code "states plainly." That header is the IC.2/.kai era: line 5 says it "consults the .kai cache," and `kai`/`IKAI` have ZERO hits anywhere in src/ or lib/ (deleted whole 2026-07-02, PLAN §5.U). "Per-module overlays land with IC.3's chase extension" names a roadmap superseded by `Hβ.persist.module-image-cache`. The design read a stale comment as artifact state and missed the live comment 120 lines down that contradicts its proposal — the Documentation Trust trap, and a design built on a misread of the code is refuted outright.

Secondary, each independently sufficient: the GATE is unwritable (Mentl has no non-exported bindings — zero `pub`/`export` hits in lexer.mn, parser.mn, SYNTAX.md) and asserts the answer the docs reject (SYNTAX.md §Imports: "The graph refuses two edges into one name rather than papering over it"; PLAN band L `Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name`: "the decl site deserves the refusal"); "write set disjoint BY CONSTRUCTION" is false while driver_check_module (driver.mn:138-139) carries `GraphWrite + Alloc + Memory`; `Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base` has zero hits in PLAN.md/docs/src.

WHAT I TRIED THAT FAILED TO REFUTE: I attempted to break the fixpoint claim by hunting cross-module duplicate top-level names in the wheel. Across 2,162 fns in src+lib there are exactly two (`add`, `main`), both in lib/tutorial/, and tools/march.sh:71 excludes that path from the wheel input (`-not -path '*/tutorial/*'`) — so the built namespace has zero cross-module duplicates and per-module resolution would not shift fn-name binding in the wheel. The design's "full battery stays green" survives on that axis. It was luck, not verification: the design never ran the check, and that same find-filter is the artifact's own admission that the one-namespace concatenation (driver.mn:192-201, `driver_compile_entry`) cannot tolerate two `main`s today.
  fixes    : 1. DROP driver.mn:9-13 as artifact state — it is stale .kai-era prose. Line 5 of that header cites the `.kai` cache; `kai`/`IKAI` have ZERO hits in src/ and lib/ (deleted whole 2026-07-02, PLAN §5.U). "IC.3's chase extension" names a dead roadmap. Cite the LIVE comments instead: driver.mn:131-135 and 150-157 — which state the design's own mechanism is the wrong form.

2. NAME THE REAL FIRST LANDING: `NModule` (types.mn:848) is an inert constructor — minted nowhere in src/ or lib/, with consumers waiting (cursor.mn:404,441; format.mn:323) and infer no-ops (3984/4126/4308). This is PLAN §5.R's destiny-audit pattern exactly: machinery real, wiring absent. The honest sequence is: wire NModule (parse_program emits it; driver_collect_dag carries nodes, not the strings at driver.mn:85-96) FIRST. Only then is "one more edge off the module's node" a true sentence rather than a fabrication.

3. THE OVERLAY IS NOT AN EXPORTS FRAGMENT. NModule already carries `decls: List` — the decls ARE what the module publishes; read them live. "A small env fragment recording the names it exports" is a snapshot of what the env already holds (Carried-Truth violated), and it is the precise shape PLAN §5.U deleted for cause: the .kai layer "snapshotted env entries lossily, DROPPING Reason chains." The design claims a Reason-chain BENEFIT from the shape whose deletion was justified by Reason-chain LOSS.

4. KILL THE "TWO PURPOSES" ARGUMENT AND THE SEQUENCING IT CARRIES. Cache-key precision is dead: `Hβ.persist.module-image-cache` (PLAN.md:866) keys on source + transitive dep hashes and memcpys the image whole — "env entries, oracle queue, Reason chains intact ... never a second serializer." An env overlay contributes nothing to that key. The peer's live scope (roadmap:367) is cross-module lookup + `Hβ.cli.user-defined-entry-handler-resolution`, NOT "IC.3 cache-key precision" as claimed. So "can land independently, it was already named for IC.3 caching" is unsupported.

5. REWRITE THE GATE — it is unwritable AND asserts the wrong answer. Mentl has no non-exported bindings (zero `pub`/`export` hits in lexer.mn, parser.mn, SYNTAX.md), so the fixture cannot be authored. And per-module shadowing is the papering-over SYNTAX.md §Imports forbids ("The graph refuses two edges into one name") and PLAN band L rejects (`Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name`: "the decl site deserves the refusal, per the E_ImportNameCollision precedent"). `ImportNameCollision` has zero hits in src/ — the refusal is spec, not built. NON-VACUOUS FORM: delete the `-not -path '*/tutorial/*'` filter at tools/march.sh:71 and require the ten `lib/tutorial/*` `main` decls to produce a decl-site REFUSAL rather than a silent pick. That filter is the standing proof that the one-namespace law is currently enforced by bash.

6. DOWNGRADE "hard prerequisite / write set disjoint BY CONSTRUCTION" to "one of N write hazards." driver_check_module (driver.mn:138-139) carries `Memory + Alloc + Filesystem + EnvRead + EnvWrite + GraphRead + GraphWrite + Diagnostic + LookupTy + Verify + InferCtx`. graph_bind / mint / bump-alloc remain shared-image writes across threads. Env-disjointness is not write-disjointness. PLAN §5.O layer 4 already names the real co-requisite — "(arena_id, offset) deterministic handle partitioning so native_m3==native_m4 holds" — and the design must name it as co-equal rather than claim the env overlay closes the hazard.

7. DELETE THE FABRICATED INTERCONNECTION. `Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base` has ZERO hits in PLAN.md, docs/, and src/. Cite PLAN §5.O layer 3 (`Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena`) or §2's "per-thread bump arenas" if that shape is meant.

8. STATE BLAST RADIUS HONESTLY. I measured it: 2,162 top-level fns across src+lib, exactly 2 cross-module duplicate names (`add`, `main`), both confined to lib/tutorial/, which tools/march.sh:71 excludes from the wheel input. The built namespace has zero cross-module duplicates, so fn-name-resolution blast radius on the wheel is genuinely nil — but the design asserted "full battery stays green" without checking. Assert it with that measurement or not at all.

REFUTED: Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee
  PLAN already scoped this peer skeptically; driver.mn's own layer-walk is exactly the test case this cluster forces, and it does not need this peer, because ~>'s existing dynamic handler resolution already gives every caller full per-call-site schedule control for free.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's central mechanism claim ("any caller can already override driver_check's internal fanout schedule by wrapping the call, zero edits to driver.mn") is false against the artifact: lower.mn:1793's frame fence, pushed on entry to every named fn body at lower.mn:3151, makes a callee-internal fanout read Seq unconditionally for every caller, and the design's own cited file says so three lines above the cited function.
  objection: THE MECHANISM DOES NOT EXIST. The design asserts `driver_check(m) ~> sequential_compose` shadows the outer chain for that one call. Three artifact facts kill it:

(1) The schedule resolves at LOWER time, not runtime: `fn lower_fanout_schedule() = schedule_in_stack(lower_handler_stack_now())` (src/lower.mn:1787). At lower time there is no evidence chain — only a lexical stack.

(2) That stack is FRAME-FENCED: `if field_name_eq(stack_entry_name(e), "") { Seq }` (src/lower.mn:1793), whose own inline comment is the design's exact negation — "the schedule is read at the fanout's own install site, never across a call boundary". `lower_frame_fence_push()` (src/lower.mn:237) fires on entry to every NAMED FN DECL body (src/lower.mn:3151), lambdas (2354), branch thunks (2901/2955), arms (3496), reified k (3978). `driver_check` is a named fn decl. Its body lowers behind the fence; its fanout reads `Seq` for every caller. Wrapped and unwrapped call sites are indistinguishable.

(3) The design TRUNCATES its own citation. It quotes SYNTAX.md's `><` section ("the cursor reads the strategy from the live handler stack") and stops before the clause that refutes it — SYNTAX.md:333-338: "never across a call boundary, so a `><` inside a reusable fn is permanently `Seq` while its name promises parallelism... Caller-selectable fanout inside a reusable helper is the open peer `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee`." SYNTAX.md is the surface AUTHORITY and rules the opposite. driver.mn:170-172 — three lines above the cited driver_check:178 — already says "the schedule is read at the fanout's install, never across a call (Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee)". The design cites the file and contradicts its comment.

SECOND, INDEPENDENT KILL (survives even if the fence were lifted). `schedule_in_stack` (lower.mn:1794-1797) keys on `handler_covers_effect(hname, "Thread"/"Simd"/"Gpu"/"Persist")` — the EFFECT covered, never the handler's identity. `parallel_compose` (threading.mn:119) and `sequential_compose` (threading.mn:137) both handle the SAME `effect Thread` (threading.mn:62-67: spawn/thread_join/current_id/num_cores). So `~> sequential_compose` resolves to `Threaded`, not `Seq` — the opposite of the design's assumption. `Seq` is the no-scheduler-installed default; it is unreachable by installing anything. The design's stated goal ("check_run wanting deterministic-verbose sequential output" via sequential_compose) does not select Seq.

GATE IS VACUOUS AND INVERTED. Run the proposed two-call-site test against the artifact: the sequential leg PASSES VACUOUSLY (observes Seq from the fence default, not from resolving sequential_compose — identical result if sequential_compose were deleted); the parallel leg FAILS (observes Seq, not Threaded). The design says "Passing this test IS the proof the existing mechanism suffices." It cannot pass. The design's own gate refutes the design. Note pipeline.mn:173 already IS this gate in production (`driver_check(entry_module) ~> ... ~> parallel_compose ~> wasi_threads`), and every green crucible installs in the SAME frame as the fanout — `(2 <| (make_adder, make_adder)) ~> sequential_compose` (tests/frontier/mn-scheduled-fanout-closure.mn:7). No crucible in the repo crosses a call boundary, because none can pass.

NOVELTY VERDICT IS A CONFLATION, NOT AN ARGUMENT. "Dynamic scoping via ~> already IS context injection" is true of RUNTIME ops (spawn/join do resolve across call boundaries via evidence). The schedule is not a runtime op — it is the lower-time decision of WHETHER spawn/join are emitted at all. Conflating the two layers is the whole error. The genuine dilemma the peer exists to close, which the design never sees: (a) driver.mn installs a schedule at its fanout site → lower emits Threaded, but that install is now innermost and statically shadows any caller's swap (lower_resolve_handler_for_op binds Tier-1 lexically, lower.mn:1718-1720) → not caller-selectable; (b) driver.mn installs nothing → fence → Seq → no spawn ops emitted → the caller's handler catches nothing → not caller-selectable. Both horns closed. That is exactly why the peer names COMPILE-TIME SPECIALIZATION — lowering the body once per install-context. "Specialized" in `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` is load-bearing; the design's "no compile-time specialization needs to be built" reads the word away.

DRIFT / BLAST RADIUS. The design lands nothing, so the fixpoint is safe — but its OUTPUT is the hazard. It banks a "do not build this" resting on prose (a truncated SYNTAX quote + a commit message) where an edge already encodes the fact live (the fence sentinel, lower.mn:1793) — Carried-Truth violated at the meta level, and Law 5 (verify with a tool) unrun. If banked, a future session building Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk writes `driver_check(m) ~> sequential_compose`, observes Seq, concludes it works, and ships a par-walk that is silently single-threaded at EVERY call site — the precise "name promises parallelism, body is permanently Seq" trap SYNTAX.md dissolved `parallel_map` to prevent. That is a silent-wrong class entering PLAN §11 column 2 (the correctness spine).
  fixes    : 1. THE PEER STANDS — do not bank a "do not build this". `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` is not dissolved by this cluster. PLAN §5.R band E's skeptical scoping is already correct and needs no edit. Delete the design's claim that "~>'s existing dynamic handler resolution already gives every caller full per-call-site schedule control for free" — it is false at lower.mn:1793.

2. THE ONE TRUE RESIDUE, RE-GROUNDED. The design's conclusion (Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk does not need this peer) is CORRECT, for a reason the design never states. Not "callers can already override" (false). The real reason: the par-walk writes `><` WITH its `~> Thread` at the same site, in the same frame, inside driver.mn — the form SYNTAX.md:333-338 prescribes ("genuine multi-core map is written `(map(f, a)) >< (map(f, b)) ~> Thread` at the site where the schedule installs"). One schedule, chosen by the driver. The peer is unneeded because NO CALLER WANTS A DIFFERENT SCHEDULE — not because callers can get one. This preserves the design's interconnection claim (par-walk needs no specialization machinery) on a true premise.

3. A LOAD-BEARING CONSTRAINT THE DESIGN HID — and the sharpest edge in this cluster. `driver_check`'s body today is `each((layer) => each(driver_check_module, layer))` (src/driver.mn:181-184). The inner iteration sits inside a LAMBDA, and lower.mn:2354 pushes a fence on lambda entry. So a `><` written inside that lambda is DOUBLE-fenced (lambda frame + fn frame) and reads `Seq` even with `~> Thread` installed two lines up in driver_check's own body. Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk must RESTRUCTURE driver_check's body so the `><` and its `~> Thread` sit in ONE frame — never add a handler at the top of driver_check and expect the lambda's fanout to see it. The design's conclusion would have buried this and produced a silently-Seq par-walk.

4. INVERT THE GATE. The design's gate cannot pass, so it cannot be the evidence. The honest, mechanically-checkable form is a REFUTATION gate: write the two-call-site test, observe BOTH legs yield Seq, and record that as the artifact proof the cross-call mechanism does not exist and the peer must stay named. Better and cheaper: the fence is already directly readable at lower.mn:1793 + 3151 — cite the edge, do not re-derive it from a test.

5. NAME THE HANDLER-IDENTITY BOUNDARY, positively. `schedule_in_stack` (lower.mn:1794-1797) distinguishes Thread/Simd/Gpu/Persist by covered effect but has no arm for WHICH Thread-class handler, so `Seq` is unreachable-by-install and `~> sequential_compose` reads as `Threaded`. The parallel_compose-vs-sequential_compose choice is a RUNTIME distinction (which handler catches spawn), separable from the lower-time Seq-vs-Threaded choice — but only reachable when the fanout's own frame installs a Thread-class handler, at which point that lexical install statically shadows callers (lower.mn:1718-1720). If this ever needs to be caller-selectable, that is `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` doing exactly the job it is named for.

REFUTED: Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee
  PLAN already scoped this peer skeptically; driver.mn's own layer-walk is exactly the test case this cluster forces, and it does not need this peer, because ~>'s existing dynamic handler resolution already gives every caller full per-call-site schedule control for free.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's load-bearing claim ("any caller of driver_check can already override its internal fanout's schedule by wrapping the call locally") is inverted by `src/lower.mn:1793`, a frame fence whose own code comment quotes the exact SYNTAX sentence the design cites, and whose whole purpose is to make a caller's `~>` installs structurally unreachable from a callee's fanout. The bottom-line "do not build" coincides with PLAN, but every piece of evidence offered for it is wrong, and the interconnection guidance would silently break the cluster's keystone item.
  objection: The design reads the fanout schedule as RUNTIME dynamic scoping. The artifact resolves it at LOWER, lexically, behind a fence at every named-fn boundary.

`src/lower.mn:1787`: `fn lower_fanout_schedule() = schedule_in_stack(lower_handler_stack_now())` — a compile-time read, consumed by `run_fanout_thunks` (`lower.mn:2781`) to pick which LowExpr to emit.

`src/lower.mn:1793`, inside `schedule_in_stack`:
    if field_name_eq(stack_entry_name(e), "") { Seq }   // the frame fence — "the schedule is read at the fanout's own install site, never across a call boundary" (SYNTAX §Vocabulary)

`src/lower.mn:237`: `fn lower_frame_fence_push() = lower_handler_push("", 0)`. `src/lower.mn:3151` is the NAMED FN DECL path: `ls_enter_frame(fn_name, …)` then `lower_frame_fence_push()`. So `driver_check`'s body lowers with a fence at the top of its lexical handler stack. Any `><` in that body walks from `last(stack)`, hits the `""` entry, returns `Seq` — unconditionally, for every caller, forever. `driver_check(m) ~> sequential_compose` cannot move it, and neither can `main.mn:236`'s `~> parallel_compose`.

This is not an oversight to route around: `lower.mn:228-236` records the fence as a correctness fix (cross-frame resolution produced `__k_60854`'s dangling `$__hstate_60873_BodyContext`).

Four consequences, each fatal to a section of the design:

1. FACTUAL. The design's own citation refutes it. It quotes commit 600bc88 including the words "at lower" and then draws a runtime-dynamic-scoping conclusion. SYNTAX §Vocabulary's parallel_map ruling — the ruling that NAMES this peer — reads "never across a call boundary, so a `><` inside a reusable fn is permanently `Seq` while its name promises parallelism." The design's INTERROGATIONS section inverts it verbatim: "always answered by whoever calls it, already, today." The fanout's schedule is answered at the FANOUT site; the fence exists to guarantee the call site is invisible.

2. GATE — refuted, and vacuous in the one leg that passes. Today `driver_check` has no `><` at all (`driver.mn:181-184` is `each((layer) => each(driver_check_module, layer))`). With a `><` and no in-body install, BOTH legs observe `Seq`: the `~> sequential_compose` leg "passes" because Seq is the default with zero mechanism involved, and the parallel leg fails. Worse, the gate's model is wrong even setting the fence aside. `handler_covers_effect` (`lower.mn:1745`) asks only whether a handler has an arm group for the named effect: `any((g) => group_ename(g) == ename, lookup_handler_arm_names_of(hname))`. `effect Thread` declares spawn/thread_join/current_id/num_cores (`lib/runtime/threading.mn:62-67`); `handler sequential_compose` (`threading.mn:137`) implements all four. So `handler_covers_effect("sequential_compose", "Thread")` is TRUE, and `lower.mn:1794` classifies a fanout under `~> sequential_compose` as **Threaded**, not Seq. The classifier keys on effect coverage, never on handler semantics. Any gate asserting "sequential_compose ⇒ Seq schedule" tests a model the artifact does not implement.

3. INTERCONNECTION — actively harmful, and contradicted by the file it cites. The design tells `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` to draw `><` topology while "never hardcoding a schedule handler inside driver.mn's own body." Under the fence that yields a permanently-`Seq` fanout: `lower.mn:2782` emits `Seq => LMakeTuple(handle, map(invoke_scheduled_boundary, branches))` — inline evaluation, zero spawn ops emitted, so no runtime handler is ever consulted. The multi-core half would land as a silent no-op whose name promises parallelism — precisely the parallel_map drift SYNTAX.md dissolved, reintroduced at driver.mn. `driver.mn:168-172` states the opposite plan and names this peer at that very site: "the multi-core half is a `><` fanout **under its own `~> Thread` written at this site** — the schedule is read at the fanout's install, never across a call (Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee)." The comment already knows the caller cannot reach in; the design read it and concluded the reverse.

4. NOVELTY — the slogan is false where it matters. "Dynamic scoping via ~> already IS context injection, applied for free to every fanout inside the callee's body" — the fence exists specifically to prevent that, for soundness. The design conflates two layers the artifact keeps apart: the fanout's SCHEDULE (lower-time, lexical, fenced — whether spawn/join are emitted at all) and the Thread effect's runtime op dispatch (evidence-tier, genuinely dynamic — what an emitted spawn DOES). `main.mn:187-188`'s documented opt-out ("install `~> sequential_compose` INSIDE a specific verb's chain to override") is real, and it is the second layer only. The design borrows the second layer's dynamism to claim the first layer's behavior.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the central claim. "Callers already have per-call-site schedule control for free" is false. `lower.mn:1787-1798` + the fence pushed at `lower.mn:3151` for every named fn mean NOTHING a caller writes can change a callee's fanout schedule. That absence IS the peer's content — the design refutes the peer's existence using the peer's own subject matter.

2. INVERT the interconnection. `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` MUST install a Thread-covering handler INSIDE `driver_check`'s body at the fanout site, exactly as `driver.mn:171-172` already specifies. The design's "never hardcode a schedule handler inside driver.mn's own body" must be struck: it produces a permanently-Seq fanout (`lower.mn:2782`), i.e. a multi-core half that lands green and does nothing.

3. NAME THE TWO LAYERS SEPARATELY; the design's value survives only after the split.
   - SCHEDULE (lower-time, lexical, frame-fenced, `lower_fanout_schedule`): decides Seq | Threaded | Simd | Gpu | Persisted, hence whether spawn/join are emitted. Caller-unreachable. This is what `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` would change, and nothing else touches it.
   - RUNTIME OP DISPATCH (evidence tier, dynamically scoped, `effect Thread`'s four ops): decides what an emitted spawn does. This is `main.mn:187-188`'s real, documented opt-out.
   The honest sentence the design was reaching for: once driver_check's fanout is hardcoded Threaded (layer 1, in-body), a caller writing `driver_check(m) ~> sequential_compose` DOES get synchronous execution via `sequential_compose`'s `spawn(task) => { let result = task() … }` (`threading.mn:138-142`) — with zero edits to driver.mn. That is execution control, not schedule control, and it is enough for this cluster. State it that way.

4. REWRITE the gate so it can fail. As specified it cannot discriminate. A gate that measures the actual fact: put a `><` in a named fn's body with no in-body install, call it from under `~> parallel_compose`, and assert the emitted WAT contains zero spawn calls — that MEASURES the fence and proves caller-side schedule control does not exist. Add the second leg the design's model gets backwards: `><` under an in-body `~> sequential_compose` classifies **Threaded** (`lower.mn:1794` + `threading.mn:137`), so assert spawn IS emitted and runs synchronously. Any gate keyed on "sequential_compose ⇒ Seq" is refuted before it runs.

5. KEEP the conclusion, on PLAN's grounds, not the design's. PLAN §5.R band E already says: "direct `>< + ~> Thread` at the use site is sufficient and simpler; build only when a real consumer needs one fanout helper serving callers wanting different schedules." driver_check is not that consumer — check_run and compile_run both want the layer walk parallel, and the deterministic-output need is served at the runtime layer (item 3). So: do not build the peer for this cluster. Record it as "PLAN's skeptical scoping stands; driver_check is not the triggering consumer" — and record the fence (`lower.mn:1793`) as the reason callers have no schedule control, so the next session does not re-derive the design's inversion from the same comment.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base
  Without a per-thread linear-memory base, the first real OS-thread spawn from the compiler's own driver silently clobbers shared memory on its first allocation — a live correctness bug waiting for layer 4 to trigger it, not a latent nicety, and it is new: no existing peer names it.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the hazard is real but the item's three load-bearing claims are false against the artifact: an existing peer already names this work (threading.mn:240), a built-but-dormant handler already targets it by a different mechanism the design never names (wasm.mn:170), the "one index, two consumers" reuse is fabricated (driver.mn:268-297 returns [[String]], no index), and the stated clobber cannot occur because the emitted memory is DEFINED, not imported — so nothing is shared to clobber.
  objection: "It is new: no existing peer names it" is false three times over, and the miss is structural — the design's INTERROGATIONS section answers only Graph? and Ownership?, skipping **Handler?**, the one interrogation that lands directly on the answer.

1. `Hβ.runtime.wasi-thread-spawn-seed` (threading.mn:238-240) scopes exactly this: "bootstrap/src/* imports wasi_thread_spawn from the WASI threads runtime **+ partitions per-thread bump arenas** + lowers thread-record allocation." PLAN §5.R band E carries that peer. The middle clause IS this item. Minting `Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base` is a second home for one truth (PLAN §7, "one home each").

2. `handler emit_memory_atomic_cas` (wasm.mn:170) is BUILT AND DORMANT for precisely this trigger: "install `~> emit_memory_atomic_cas` ... when the emitted program will run with parallel_compose (genuine multi-core dispatch)" (wasm.mn:158-160). Its comment already states the design's own premise before the design states it — "globals don't support atomics" (wasm.mn:154) — and therefore moves `$heap_ptr` out of a global into "a fixed memory cell", HEAP_PTR_ADDR = 0x40 (wasm.mn:164). The artifact's answer to "how does allocation survive threads" is an `EmitMemory` handler swap (effect decl wasm.mn:100-102), gated by two already-named peers the design never mentions: `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` (wasm.mn:113, 1781) and `Hβ.emit.cas-alloc-init-in-start` (wasm.mn:167). CLAUDE.md's "audit dormant substrate first" is written for exactly this: "defined but uncalled IS the slot for what you're about to build."

Worse, the two mechanisms are mutually exclusive at the representation level and the design never reconciles them: the design has `$wasi_thread_start` write a per-instance **global** `$heap_ptr`; `emit_memory_atomic_cas` deletes that global's role entirely in favour of a **memory cell**. If the CAS strategy lands there is no per-instance global left to set, and the design's mechanism has nothing to write to.

Compounding it — the design's stated failure mode cannot occur. The boot imports 15 functions and NO memory; the memory is DEFINED: `(memory (;0;) 65536 65536 shared)` (boot line 45, from wasm.mn:1762). By the very rule the design correctly invokes for globals ("globals are per-instance"), a DEFINED memory is also allocated fresh per instantiation — `shared` is a property of the memory TYPE (atomics legal, max required), not of instantiation identity; sharing one memory across instances requires importing one memory object. So the first failure is not "two threads clobber the 1MB base": the child's memory is a fresh zeroed region, `start_arg` (a parent-heap closure pointer) loads 0 at offset 0 → fn_idx 0 → `call_indirect` to table index 0 (wasm.mn:2632-2634), and `ffi_join`'s `wait_i32` on the parent's done flag (threading.mn:203) never fires. `arena_id` in the closure record cannot help: the child cannot read the closure record at all. The design fixes a bug strictly downstream of one it did not see.
  fixes    : WHAT SURVIVES (do not discard): the hazard is real. `$heap_ptr` is a mut per-instance global from a constant (wasm.mn:1768) and `$alloc` is a plain non-atomic bump (wasm.mn:1782). Any multi-instance threading model breaks on it. And the design's catch on wasm.mn:1765 is correct and worth more than the design claims: "$heap_ptr is read+written through atomic.rmw on the threading path" is not merely unimplemented, it is IMPOSSIBLE — wasm atomics operate on linear memory only, and the artifact itself says so 1,600 lines earlier ("globals don't support atomics", wasm.mn:154). That sentence is a false comment contradicted by its own file. Delete it on sight (Law 2: delete, don't decorate).

The novelty argument also survives on its merits and is stronger than the design realizes: "the thread's identity IS its closure" correctly rejects the jemalloc/tcmalloc TLS-keyed form, and disjoint per-thread arenas are strictly stronger than the artifact's own dormant CAS answer on §5.O grounds — one CAS'd bump pointer serializes every thread on one cache line, which is a contention point, not O(1). That is a real argument. It must be made BY NAME against `emit_memory_atomic_cas`, replacing it, not sitting silently beside it.

WHAT THE DESIGN MUST SAY INSTEAD:

1. Drop `Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base` as a new name. The work is already named by `Hβ.runtime.wasi-thread-spawn-seed`'s "partitions per-thread bump arenas" clause (threading.mn:240). Extend that peer; do not mint a second home.

2. Fix the prerequisite ORDER. The memory must be IMPORTED — `(import "env" "memory" (memory 65536 65536 shared))` replacing the defined memory at wasm.mn:1762 — before any arena question is live. That is the actual first blocker and it is unnamed today. It changes emit bytes, so it is a march TRANSITION (m2 ≠ m3, re-pin from m3 per tools/march.sh), which the design must state; it does not currently mention blast radius at all.

3. Land the arena as a FOURTH `EmitMemory` handler (`emit_memory_per_thread_arena`) on the existing swap surface (effect decl wasm.mn:100-102; siblings wasm.mn:116/139/170), gated on the already-named `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` (wasm.mn:113, 1781) — the seam that currently forces all three handlers to emit an identical `(call $alloc ...)` body. Refute `emit_memory_atomic_cas` by name on the contention argument above and delete it, rather than growing a fourth strategy beside a dormant third nobody named.

4. Delete the "one index, two consumers" claim entirely. `driver_partition_layers` → `driver_partition_peel` (driver.mn:268-297) returns `[[String]]` — layers of module NAMES. There is no layer-position index, no graph-handle reservation, no stride; driver.mn:168-169 claims only "the topological half ... the multi-core half is a `><`". The Graph? answer ("arena_id reads the same layer-index ... no second index invented") reads an edge that does not exist, so the design would INTRODUCE the index while claiming to read it — Carried-Truth inverted. Likewise INTERCONNECTION's "three instances of one reservation pattern" describes a pattern with zero existing instances. If an arena_id is genuinely needed, say honestly that this design MINTS a new fact and justify it.

5. Fix the gate — it currently cannot run, and the design stakes its entire "real bug, not hypothetical" claim on it ("watching it FAIL against today's shared-base $heap_ptr is itself the proof"). Verified on the pinned toolchain: wasmtime 43.0.1 rejects EVERY wasi-threads invocation with `shared memory support is disabled for this engine -- see Config::shared_memory` — for imported memory, for defined memory, and in both flag orders (`-W threads=y -S threads=y`). Notably `-W threads=y` ALONE accepts the shared memory (it proceeds to `unknown import: wasi::thread-spawn`), so the block is specific to the `-S threads` wasi-threads path. This is the same class of substrate block PLAN band B already records ("native conts are BLOCKED under WASI `_start` (wasmtime 43, verified 2026-07-11)") and the design does not name it. Per Law 3 a substrate block does not refute a dream-coded target — but it does refute a gate whose whole claimed value is "watch it fail today." Either name the wasmtime-43 block as an `!Outside` dependency (band-B precedent), or write a gate that CAN fail now: a structural emit assertion (wasm-tools validate + emit-diff) that each spawned closure carries a distinct base and that no two `$alloc` call sites share an initializer.

6. Engage threading.mn:180-186, which the design walks past: the Stage-3 closure-IS-thread-record collapse explicitly refuses "NO separate thread-record table, NO next_tid counter, NO partition arithmetic." The design re-introduces `arena_id * ARENA_STRIDE` into that same record.
