This is a weaker objection (that refusal is about thread-record table partitioning, not heap arenas), but a design adding partition arithmetic to the one record whose comment refuses partition arithmetic owes the reader a sentence.

7. Minor factual: the ARENA_STRIDE "calibrated constant times source_byte_length" with "a loud, diagnosed trap on overflow, **falling back to a sequential re-run of that layer**" is a surrender-fallback in the wheel (Law 2). A stride that can overflow is the wrong representation; the fallback would canonize it. Either the stride is proven bounded (a refinement — Interrogation 6, which the design also skips) or the design is not ready.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base
  Without a per-thread linear-memory base, the first real OS-thread spawn from the compiler's own driver silently clobbers shared memory on its first allocation — a live correctness bug waiting for layer 4 to trigger it, not a latent nicety, and it is new: no existing peer names it.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's load-bearing artifact claim ("the one truly shared resource") is empirically false: wasm.mn:1762 DEFINES+EXPORTS the memory instead of importing it, so spawned threads share no memory at all and the described clobber cannot occur; the item's "no existing peer names it" is false four ways, and its gate mis-attributes.
  objection: FACTUAL, and it collapses the premise. The design asserts: "wasi_thread_spawn instantiates a fresh module instance per thread sharing only `memory` (declared shared, wasm.mn:1762) ... Two threads allocating concurrently each believe they own the range starting at 1MB and write different records there — a genuine silent data clobber in the one truly shared resource." But wasm.mn:1762 emits `(memory (export "memory") 65536 65536 shared)` — a DEFINED+exported memory, not an import. A defined memory is created fresh per instantiation; `shared` governs whether it may be touched from multiple threads, not whether two instantiations share one. wasi-threads shares memory only when the module IMPORTS it.

Proven by A/B on modules differing ONLY in import-vs-define (parent writes 5 at 2MB, thread writes 100 + sets done + notifies, parent waits 1s then exits with what it reads):
  A, defined+exported (= wasm.mn:1762 today): exit=5, wall 1.010s — the wait TIMED OUT; parent never saw the thread's write.
  B, imported+re-exported (spec-conformant): exit=100, wall 0.016s — parent woken by the thread's notify.
The thread definitely runs: a variant whose body calls proc_exit(42) exits 42; another whose body stores 170 and reads it back reached proc_exit(170) (trap "invalid exit status", backtrace in wasm function 2 = the thread's $body). So the thread executes and its store succeeds — in its OWN private memory. There is no shared resource to clobber. Per the brief, a design built on a misread of the code is refuted outright.

It cannot fire for a second, independent reason: threading.mn:271 is `fn wasi_thread_spawn_intrinsic(start_arg) with Pure = start_arg` — a Pure sentinel returning its argument — and there is NO emit-side bridge (`$wasi_thread_spawn` appears at wasm.mn:1753 as an import and nowhere else; it is never called). No Mentl program can spawn. "A live correctness bug ... not a latent nicety" overstates: two unnamed prerequisites stand in front of it.

NOVELTY — "it is new: no existing peer names it" is false four ways: (1) `Hβ.runtime.wasi-thread-spawn-seed` (threading.mn:238-241; PLAN.md:846 band E) explicitly scopes "partitions per-thread bump arenas" — that peer already OWNS this content; (2) `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` (wasm.mn:113, 1781) owns the arena/CAS $alloc body swap; (3) `Hβ.emit.cas-alloc-init-in-start` (wasm.mn:167) owns base initialization; (4) PLAN.md:755 (§5.O layer 4) names "(arena_id, offset) deterministic handle partitioning" and PLAN.md:864 band N `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` names "fixed per-core (arena_id, offset) partition" — arena_id by name. Minting `Hβ.runtime.per-thread-arena-base` opens a second home for a truth an existing peer holds — the exact failure PLAN §11's named-peer audit corrected four times one section earlier (the name-is-handle cluster = ONE design in five names; partial-effectful-callee/.partial-local-callee MERGE). This is the fifth instance.

The design also attacks a problem an existing sibling handler dissolves, without naming it. `handler emit_memory_atomic_cas` (wasm.mn:170) already sits on the EmitMemory swap surface, and wasm.mn:152-169 already states the diagnosis the design presents as new: "Each alloc reads $heap_ptr from a fixed memory cell (atomic-accessible — globals don't support atomics) ... attempts atomic.rmw.cmpxchg; retries on contention. HEAP_PTR_ADDR = 0x40". Install it and the per-instance-global reset the design is built on ceases to exist — the pointer lives in shared memory, not a global.

GATE — mis-attributing, worse than vacuous. "Watching it FAIL against today's shared-base $heap_ptr is itself the proof this item is a real bug" — it would fail today for an unrelated reason (no shared memory, so the parent reads its own zeros at every base regardless of arena_id), and it would STILL fail after arena_id lands. A red gate that neither confirms nor refutes the hypothesis. CLAUDE.md ⟲: a label is a HYPOTHESIS until the artifact confirms it; a probe that disproves you does not crown the next symptom as root.

Two further drifts: the "falling back to a sequential re-run of that layer" is a diagnosed surrender fallback (Law 2) that re-introduces exactly the two-modes split band N's `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` says it "kills (the two-modes header flag, drift-8)". And blast radius is unnamed: the record's @8 capture base is the unified record's convention across all five roles (lower.mn:511, 3465, 4356; wasm.mn:1855, 2621, 2655 "state slots live at 8+i*4, matching resolve_state_slot_offset"), and "extend the closure record with one more field" never says where — before `captures` it shifts every capture/state/ev offset in all five roles; the memory-as-import prerequisite alone changes every module's import section (m2 != m3, a march TRANSITION + re-pin).

Disconfirming attempts that did NOT save the design: I tested whether the thread runs at all (it does — exit=42, which would have mooted this in the opposite direction); whether wasmtime shares a defined `shared` memory anyway (it does not — 1.010s timeout, exit=5); and whether my imported variant's first failure was wasmtime refusing imports (no — my own OOB at 17 pages, then "missing required memory export"; both my test bugs, and once fixed it returned exit=100).
  fixes    : The CONCLUSION survives; the stated reason, the novelty claim, the gate, and the peer name do not.

1. REPLACE the justification. Drop "silent clobber of shared memory" — it is false. The real, stronger reason the design misses is DETERMINISM. `emit_memory_atomic_cas` (wasm.mn:170) already prevents any clobber, but CAS makes allocation ORDER race-dependent, so addresses/handles differ run-to-run and m3 != m4 — the fixpoint dies. That is exactly why PLAN.md:271 says "per-thread bump arenas + inference-stable handles = deterministic parallel codegen" and PLAN.md:864 ties (arena_id, offset) to "handle assignment ... a pure function of the graph → native_m3==native_m4". Correct framing: per-thread arenas beat CAS on the self-hosting path because CAS is correct-but-nondeterministic and Mentl's fixpoint is a correctness gate a lock-free allocator cannot satisfy. Non-optional, for a reason that survives.

2. DELETE the new peer name; fold into `Hβ.runtime.wasi-thread-spawn-seed`, which already scopes "partitions per-thread bump arenas" (threading.mn:240; PLAN.md:846). One home per truth. Note its interconnection with `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` and `Hβ.emit.cas-alloc-init-in-start` rather than re-deriving their ground.

3. MINT the item that IS genuinely new and that the design walked past while asserting its opposite: the emitted memory must become an import. `Hβ.emit.shared-memory-as-import` — replace `(memory (export "memory") 65536 65536 shared)` (wasm.mn:1762) with `(import "env" "memory" (memory 65536 65536 shared))` plus an explicit `(export "memory" ...)` (wasmtime requires BOTH: it errors "missing required memory export" without the export, and shares nothing without the import). This is the actual correctness precondition for layer 4, nothing names it, and it is the first thing the two-thread gate should test.

4. NAME the second prerequisite positively: the spawn bridge. `wasi_thread_spawn_intrinsic` (threading.mn:271) is `with Pure = start_arg` with no emit-side recognition; `Hβ.runtime.wasi-thread-spawn-seed`'s Stage-3 bridge must land before any spawn exists.

5. FIX the gate so it discriminates. Stage it: (a) prove shared memory — thread writes a sentinel, parent reads it (fails today with the defined memory: exit=5 after a 1.010s timeout; passes at exit=100 once imported); only then (b) prove arena disjointness with two allocating threads. Gate (b) is unreadable until (a) is green, and today's red on (b) says nothing about arenas.

6. REPLACE `FIXED_BASE + arena_id * ARENA_STRIDE` with handler state. The stride-with-calibrated-constant-and-overflow-fallback IS the borrowed static per-thread heap carve, with the lookup moved into a field — the novelty verdict's own rejected shape. Allocation is already a handler (`EmitMemory`, wasm.mn:100); the arena base is that handler's STATE, and the handler record IS the closure the thread runs (PLAN §5.U: handler = state = closure = evidence = continuation). §5.O layer 3's region-drop-as-arena-reset then makes the bound elastic per region, so the overflow escape hatch — and its drift-8 two-modes fallback — never needs to exist.

7. STATE the blast radius: place any new field after `result` (never before `captures`, which shifts all five roles' offsets), and name that the import change plus any layout change cross a generation — m2 != m3, a march TRANSITION with a re-pin from m3, per PLAN §5.O layer 4's own "layer 4 CHANGES bytes".
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.cursor.work-stealing-via-gradient
  The claim "the gradient argmax IS the priority queue" is true and load-bearing at the speculative-compile candidate-ranking site, and false — or rather vacuous — at the driver's layer-walk site, because the layer partition has already removed every ordering question a priority queue could answer.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the item's headline asymmetry (the gradient is not the driver's scorer) is correct, but every load-bearing support under it fails: its artifact_state is false (the GradientQueue reading it calls unbuilt is built at src/cursor.mn:733-777, and src/cursor.mn:713 — the handle's own home — already assigns gradient_pop to the driver, a committed line the item never cites), its central argument ("no remaining preference among ready units") is measurably false (12.8% makespan penalty at 8 cores, decl granularity, on the wheel's own layer partition), and its gate passes vacuously at baseline today while forbidding the correct form.
  objection: "There is no remaining preference among ready units for a priority function to express, because they are all equally ready by construction" conflates READINESS (a boolean the partition dissolves) with COST (a scalar the partition never touches), and the artifact measures the difference.

driver.mn:183 (`|> each((layer) => each(driver_check_module, layer))`) is a join barrier: the outer `each` is sequential, so layer N completes before N+1. Under a barrier, layer makespan = max core load = P||Cmax — strongly NP-hard, and order-dependent exactly when costs are heterogeneous. The partition answers "what MAY run now." It says nothing about "which SHOULD run first," and equal readiness does not imply equal cost.

Measured, reproducing driver_collect_visit (driver.mn:72) + driver_partition_peel (driver.mn:275) exactly on the wheel's own DAG from `main` (31 modules, 10 layers):

- MODULE granularity: layer 5 holds backends/wasm (6,771 lines) beside driver (298) — a 22.7x spread among "equally ready" units; layer 4 holds lower (5,542) beside runtime/combinators (375). Ordering penalty (ascending vs LPT): 14.0% at P=2, 5.9% at P=4, 0.0% at P=8. At 8 cores and layer width <= 6, order genuinely stops mattering — here the item is RIGHT, and I confirm it.

- DECL granularity — which PLAN §5.O layer 4 literally specifies ("the level-set partition at DECL granularity on the compile spine") and which the item's own text admits ("modules or decls within one topological layer"): 2,477 top-level decls; emit_expr is 874 lines against a mean of 15.9 (a 55x spread); 21% of decls are <=5 lines. Ordering penalty: 12.8% at P=8 — the stated target (PLAN §5.O: "Zero parallelism (pipeline.mn:100) ... 8 cores idle") — rising to 28.6% at P=16 and 61.3% at P=32. LPT hits the makespan lower bound exactly (4922 = lb); ascending order is 12.8% worse. Graham 1969 is the general statement: arbitrary list order is bounded only by (2 - 1/m)·OPT, LPT by (4/3 - 1/(3m))·OPT.

Proxy honesty: LOC is a cost proxy, and PLAN §5.O's own lesson is "perf the artifact, never trust a code-reading estimate." But the item's claim requires costs to be EQUAL, not merely proxied — a 55x spread refutes equality under any monotone proxy, and the item measured nothing at all.

The item's own answer is then the Carried-Truth violation it exists to prevent. The graph ALREADY holds each decl's weight: span_area (query.mn:557, `with Pure`, O(1)), span_of_handle (query.mn:476), parse_span_of (query.mn:569) — and enclosing_fn_scan (cursor.mn:680) already calls `span_area(dspan)` on exactly the FnStmt decl nodes a decl-granularity fanout would schedule. "No order needed" DISCARDS a fact the graph proves live and pays 12.8% for it. PLAN §9.1 at the performance scale — "every scanner in the compiler is a place we forgot the graph already knew" — has a dual the item walks past: every avoidable straggler is a place we forgot the graph already knew.
  fixes    : Six corrections. The asymmetry survives; the reason, the artifact claim, the gate, and the novelty verdict do not.

1. ARTIFACT STATE — replace outright. "No code implements either reading today ... verified by reading both candidate sites" is false. `effect GradientQueue { gradient_pop() -> Option; gradient_peek_n(n: Int) -> List; gradient_step() -> Option }` is declared at src/cursor.mn:733-737 and `handler gradient_queue_default` implemented at src/cursor.mn:739-777, over a built scoring chain: cursor_argmax_compute (cursor.mn:228), enumerate_gradient_positions (274), score_all_positions (286), score_one_position (291-310), top_n_by_score (784), sort_by_score_desc (789). What IS true is narrower: zero CONSUMER call sites (gradient_pop/peek_n/step appear only in the decl, the handler, and comments). Say that instead.

   The item missed the site that matters most. src/cursor.mn:710-716 is the handle's own home and reads: "Hβ.cursor.work-stealing-via-gradient handle. The substrate primitive that the four named peer handles compose on: Hμ.driver.topological-layer-par-map consumes gradient_pop / Hμ.cursor.speculative-compile consumes gradient_peek_n / ...". The committed artifact already states the item's split — and states it the OTHER way, assigning gradient_pop to the driver. That line IS the design the item is correcting. The item never quotes it, never cites it, never says it must go. So the deliverable is not a free-floating note: it is the DELETION of cursor.mn:713 and a rewrite of the 710-716 comment block. Which also voids "SEQUENCING: no landing order of its own" — cursor.mn is on the self-compile path, so the correction is an edit to a compiled source file, not a review-only annotation. Naming a landing while claiming none is the drift-9 shape.

2. THE REASON — replace order-vacuity with domain mismatch. The gradient is not "an argmax over a set with no meaningful order." It is UNDEFINED over the driver's domain. score_one_position (cursor.mn:291-310) computes `float_of_int(gates) * proximity` where gates = `len(unlocks)` of an AnnotationSuggestion or hole_gate (cursor.mn:245), and proximity = caret_proximity_weight (cursor.mn:323) — a decay from a CARET. enumerate_gradient_positions (cursor.mn:274) filters graph HANDLES by teach_gradient. The driver's layers are `[[String]]` (pinned at driver.mn:275). A module name is not a graph handle; a decl in a compile fanout has no caret and no unlocks. That is a type-domain mismatch, and it is both true and stronger than the item's argument — it survives the measurement that kills "no meaningful order."

3. DELETE the claim "there is no remaining preference among ready units for a priority function to express." Replace with: the partition dissolves DEPENDENCE; it leaves COST untouched, and cost is what a barrier's makespan is made of. Cite the measurement (12.8% at P=8, decl granularity; 55x decl spread; backends/wasm 6,771 beside driver 298 in layer 5).

4. NAME THE NATIVE FORM POSITIVELY — the item names no peer and leaves a real gap hidden, which is the drift it invokes. The driver's scorer is neither the gradient (item is right to reject) nor nothing (item's answer): fan each layer out DESCENDING BY span_area read live from the decl node — query.mn:557, `with Pure`, O(1), already built, already used at cursor.mn:680. Not a heuristic estimate, not a side-ledger, not a name-keyed lookup: a graph read. Name it, e.g. `Hβ.driver.layer-fanout-by-span-area`, sequenced under Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk. This is the strictly-stronger native form the item's own NOVELTY section was reaching for and missed.

5. THE GATE — both halves are broken; rebuild both.
   Half 1 ("`grep -n gradient src/driver.mn` must return zero hits") passes VACUOUSLY: run at baseline today it exits 1 with zero hits. It is green before anything lands and stays green if nobody touches driver.mn — it cannot distinguish "the correction was carried" from "no one worked here." It is a lint, not a gate. It also gates a TOKEN, not a property: it would fail the CORRECT span_area fanout if anything in it were named gradient_*, and pass an incorrect caret-scored one named score_*. Replace with a gate that can fail: with the multi-core layer fanout landed, (a) m3 is byte-identical at P=1 and P=8 (the determinism property Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition owes), and (b) the layer fanout's dispatch order is descending span_area — checkable as a makespan assertion against the per-layer max-unit lower bound, which FAILS if the fanout ships in arbitrary order.
   Half 2 cites the wrong module. "mentl.mn's suggestion/tier machinery" does not exist: gradient scoring lives in cursor.mn (cursor_argmax_compute:228, score_all_positions:286, score_one_position:291); mentl.mn has only to_annotation_suggestion:180 / suggestion_kind_of:192 / suggestion_reason:211 — boundary WRAPPING, not scoring — and `grep -c tier src/mentl.mn` returns 0. The tier is cursor.mn:233 ("A structural tier, never a magic weight"). Point the gate at cursor.mn's gradient_peek_n (cursor.mn:757) — the primitive the artifact already reserved for speculative-compile.

6. NOVELTY VERDICT — two overclaims out, one real advantage in.
   "the topological partition ... makes the scheduling PROBLEM vanish for a layer's ready set" — false. It dissolves dependence resolution; load balancing (P||Cmax) survives it and is strongly NP-hard.
   "a strictly stronger move for a fully-known static DAG than any runtime heuristic" — refuted by the most-deployed instance of the item's exact proposal, which the item never names: OpenMP `schedule(static)` IS "partition the independent ready set statically, before any thread runs." OpenMP ships `schedule(dynamic)` and `schedule(guided)` precisely because static partitioning of an independent ready set straggles under heterogeneous iteration cost — the measured 12.8%. The item names Rayon/Cilk/Go, the dynamic-DAG cases where its critique correctly lands (Blumofe-Leiserson work-stealing targets dynamically unfolding fork-join DAGs; the LIFO-local/FIFO-steal discipline buys T_P <= T_1/P + O(T_inf)), and omits the one prior art it is proposing.
   The sleight of hand to name: "a fully-known static DAG" silently equates known STRUCTURE with known COST. The structure is known. The cost is known only if you read it — and the item's gate forbids the read. The honest native advantage, which is real and which the item threw away: Cilk must PROBE cost at runtime and OpenMP must be TOLD a chunk size, while Mentl READS the weight off the decl node (span_area) — the graph already proved it. That is the stronger claim, and it is the opposite of the item's.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.cursor.work-stealing-via-gradient
  The claim "the gradient argmax IS the priority queue" is true and load-bearing at the speculative-compile candidate-ranking site, and false — or rather vacuous — at the driver's layer-walk site, because the layer partition has already removed every ordering question a priority queue could answer.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state claim ("no code implements either reading; verified by reading both candidate sites") is false: `Hβ.cursor.work-stealing-via-gradient` has a built code home at src/cursor.mn:708-737 (effect GradientQueue + a fully-implemented handler) that the item never read, and src/cursor.mn:713 already writes the exact driver-consumes-gradient drift the item calls hypothetical. The headline conclusion (gradient at the driver = drift, at speculative-compile = real) is correct, but it survives on an artifact argument the item never makes, while the item's stated reason, novelty claim, both gates, and sequencing are each refuted.
  objection: THE ITEM DID NOT READ THE SITE WHERE THE PEER LIVES, AND THE DRIFT IT "PRE-EMPTS" IS ALREADY WRITTEN. The item asserts "No code implements either reading today... verified by reading both candidate sites." src/cursor.mn:710 reads "Hβ.cursor.work-stealing-via-gradient handle. The substrate primitive that the four named peer handles compose on:" — followed at cursor.mn:713 by "Hμ.driver.topological-layer-par-map consumes gradient_pop". The reading the item calls vacuous and hypothetical is a named consumer in the peer's own doc block. Below it sits built code, not design: `effect GradientQueue { gradient_pop() -> Option; gradient_peek_n(n: Int) -> List; gradient_step() -> Option }` (cursor.mn:733-737) and `handler gradient_queue_default with caret = Caret(...)` (cursor.mn:739) with all three arms implemented (745-774), backed by real fns — cursor_argmax_compute (cursor.mn:228), enumerate_gradient_positions (274), score_all_positions (286), score_one_position (291), scope_distance_decay (337), top_n_by_score (784), sort_by_score_desc (789). The item read src/driver.mn (grep gradient -> exit 1, zero hits) and src/mentl.mn and concluded "no code implements either reading"; the peer's home is a third file it never opened. Per attack 1, a design built on a misread of the code is refuted outright — and here the misread inverts the remedy: this is not a review note for a hypothetical future implementer, it is a written line requiring DELETION (Anchor 2: delete, don't decorate), which refutes the item's own SEQUENCING ("No landing order of its own... not a separate build").

FOUR CONSEQUENCES, each independently substantiated:

(1) THE REASON IS INVERTED AND LICENSES THE FIXPOINT-BREAKING BUG. The item argues the units are "mutually independent... a set with no meaningful order." driver.mn:164 says "Modules WITHIN a layer are dep-independent" — the artifact's careful word, which the item upgrades. Dep-independence is not write-independence: driver_check_module (driver.mn:137-138) carries `EnvWrite + GraphWrite`, so every module in a layer writes the same env and the same graph. Order is load-bearing — it is the emitted bytes. PLAN.md:762: "layer 4 CHANGES bytes (handle numbers shift under the partition — a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3, the sharpest risk)". The set is the opposite of orderless. And the true reason is already decided by a peer the item never cites: PLAN.md:864, `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` — "fixed per-core (arena_id, offset) partition so handle assignment / code layout / rel-offsets are a pure function of the graph -> native_m3==native_m4; work-stealing a value-reproducible-only ~> Schedule handler never on the self-hosting path". The peer says assignment matters SO much it must be statically fixed; the item says it does not matter at all. An implementer who believes "these units are interchangeable, order doesn't matter" is precisely the implementer who ships a nondeterministic layer fanout and breaks m3==m4. The item claims no blast radius; its reason, believed, is the blast radius.

(2) THE SHARPER ARGUMENT THE ITEM MISSED. gradient_pop resolves to cursor_argmax_compute(caret) (cursor.mn:746), whose score is gates x scope_distance_decay(caret_span, target_span) (cursor.mn:337) — distance from an EDITOR CARET to an annotation-suggestion site or a `??` hole. A batch self-compile has no caret. So a driver consuming gradient_pop would rank compile units by "which annotation-suggestion site is nearest the developer's cursor." That is the category error, provable at file:line — stronger than "the set has no order," and it does not license the determinism bug.

(3) NOVELTY OVERCLAIM. "the topological partition... makes the scheduling PROBLEM vanish for a layer's ready set, a strictly stronger move for a fully-known static DAG than any runtime heuristic" is false. Statically partitioning independent tasks onto cores IS the scheduling problem (P||Cmax, NP-hard). Graham 1969: arbitrary list scheduling is (2 - 1/m)-approximate, LPT (4/3 - 1/(3m)). Cilk/Rayon work-steal because task DURATIONS are unknown, not only because the DAG is unknown — and per-module compile time is unknown before compiling. A fixed partition with unequal module costs eats a load-imbalance tax that work-stealing would erase. Mentl's real win is a TRADE — determinism bought with makespan — which is exactly how PLAN.md:864 frames it (work-stealing survives OFF the spine, "value-reproducible-only"). "Strictly stronger than any runtime heuristic" erases the tradeoff and mis-states why Mentl wins.

(4) BOTH GATES REFUTED. Gate 1 ("once level-set-par-walk lands, `grep -n gradient src/driver.mn` must return zero hits") runs TODAY as exit 1, zero hits — a vacuous pass with nothing landed, and its precondition is already half-met (PLAN.md:846 and driver.mn:168-172: the topological layer-partition is LIVE, 7165bbb). It is aimed at the wrong file: the drift sits at cursor.mn:713, where the grep never looks. And it greps a WORD, not an EDGE — a `driver_pick_next(layer)` ranked by an imported cursor_argmax_compute contains no substring "gradient" in driver.mn and passes clean. A gate that returns PASS in the presence of the exact drift it names is refuted; keyword-matching for a fact an edge already carries is the name-lookup drift in the gate meant to catch name-lookup drift. Gate 2 misnames its target: mentl.mn has NO scorer — gradient_next (mentl.mn:148) selects via pick_highest_leverage after try_each_annotation, and to_annotation_suggestion (180) / suggestion_kind_of (192) are boundary wrappers building the AnnotationSuggestion shape. The scoring machinery is cursor.mn:286/291/337/784/789, and the top-K primitive the gate wants built is already built: gradient_peek_n (cursor.mn:735, 757-761).

FINDING THE ITEM MISSED AT THE SITE IT CLAIMS TO HAVE READ. GradientQueue is fully dormant: `grep -rn "gradient_pop\|gradient_peek_n\|gradient_step" src/` outside cursor.mn returns exit 1 — three ops, four named consumers in a comment, zero call sites. This is PLAN §5.R's destiny-audit class verbatim (machinery real, wiring absent). Worse, the doc block's boast (cursor.mn:719-725: "The queue has zero memory cost — it's a recomputed argmax over the live graph at the caret. No priority-queue data structure (drift 7 refusal)") trades a data structure for a full rescan per pop: cursor_argmax_compute -> enumerate_gradient_positions -> score_all_positions -> score_one_position -> teach_gradient(handle) (cursor.mn:292) -> mentl.mn:120 gradient_next -> try_each_annotation (mentl.mn:244), which runs graph_push_checkpoint / verify / graph_rollback PER ANNOTATION PER POSITION PER POP. That is §5.O Law 1 at the performance scale ("every scanner in the compiler is a place we forgot the graph already knew") wearing a drift-7 refusal as a badge — the deeper finding at the exact site the item says it verified.

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTEMPT TO REFUTE THE CONCLUSION: I tried to save the driver reading on load-balance grounds — same-layer modules have unequal compile costs, so a priority function over the ready set is real (Graham/LPT). The artifact disproved me as a defense of THIS peer: the only score in the repo is caret-proximity (cursor.mn:337), which is not a cost model, so gradient-argmax cannot express the one real preference that exists; and PLAN.md:864 forecloses dynamic scheduling on the spine regardless of the score's quality. The item's conclusion is right. Its artifact state, its reason, its novelty verdict, its gates, and its sequencing are not.
  fixes    : 1. ARTIFACT STATE — replace outright. Not "no code implements either reading." The peer's code home is src/cursor.mn:708-737: a built `effect GradientQueue` (733-737) and `handler gradient_queue_default` (739-774+) implementing gradient_pop / gradient_peek_n / gradient_step over cursor_argmax_compute (228), enumerate_gradient_positions (274), score_all_positions (286), scope_distance_decay (337), top_n_by_score (784), sort_by_score_desc (789). The driver reading is already WRITTEN at cursor.mn:713 ("Hμ.driver.topological-layer-par-map consumes gradient_pop"). The effect is dormant: zero consumers outside cursor.mn.

2. SEQUENCING — replace "no landing order of its own; a correction to carry into review" with a LANDING: delete the consumer line cursor.mn:713. Anchor 2 — a wrong design is deleted, not annotated. Renaming it or leaving it as a comment is decoration. While there, resolve the Hμ/Hβ naming split (cursor.mn:713-716 write Hμ.*; PLAN.md:846 writes Hβ.cursor.speculative-compile) — one home per truth.

3. THE REASON — replace "the units are mutually independent / the set has no meaningful order" with the artifact's truth, which is the opposite and is why the conclusion holds harder: same-layer modules are DEP-independent only (driver.mn:164), never write-independent — driver_check_module carries EnvWrite + GraphWrite (driver.mn:137-138), so every unit in a layer writes the same env and graph, and the traversal order determines handle-minting order, which IS the emitted bytes (PLAN.md:762, "layer 4 CHANGES bytes"). The driver must not schedule dynamically NOT because order is meaningless but because order is so load-bearing that the unit->core assignment must be a pure function of the graph — already decided by Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition (PLAN.md:864), which excludes work-stealing from the self-hosting path by name. The item must cite that peer instead of contradicting it. The design as written licenses the exact bug the determinism keystone exists to prevent.

4. THE CATEGORY-ERROR ARGUMENT — state it at file:line instead of by assertion: gradient_pop returns cursor_argmax_compute(caret) (cursor.mn:746), scored as gates x scope_distance_decay(caret_span, target_span) (cursor.mn:337) — distance from an editor caret. A batch self-compile has no caret. That is why the driver reading is drift, and it is checkable.

5. NOVELTY VERDICT — drop "makes the scheduling PROBLEM vanish... strictly stronger than any runtime heuristic." A static partition of independent tasks IS a scheduling problem (P||Cmax, NP-hard; Graham 1969: list scheduling 2 - 1/m, LPT 4/3 - 1/(3m)). Cilk/Rayon work-steal because DURATIONS are unknown, and per-module compile time is unknown before compiling — so a fixed partition pays a real load-imbalance tax. Name the tradeoff: Mentl BUYS determinism (native_m3==native_m4) and PAYS makespan, and confines work-stealing to the off-spine, value-reproducible-only ~> Schedule handler exactly as PLAN.md:864 already scopes it. That is a defensible trade, not a domination.

6. GATE 1 — replace the keyword grep. `grep -n gradient src/driver.mn` returns zero TODAY with nothing landed (vacuous), is aimed at the wrong file (the drift is at cursor.mn:713), and matches a WORD where an EDGE carries the fact (`driver_pick_next` calling an imported cursor_argmax_compute passes clean). The mechanical gate is the one the cluster header already names — byte-identical under concurrency: with the layer fanout threaded, tools/march.sh must still assert m2 == m3, PLUS a repeat-run determinism check (compile the wheel twice under `~> Thread`, diff the WAT byte-for-byte). That FAILS on any nondeterministic scheduler regardless of identifier names. Keep a structural check as its complement: the driver's layer dispatch must not reach the GradientQueue effect at all — assert its effect row, since a row is the graph's own answer to "what can this touch."

7. GATE 2 — retarget. mentl.mn holds no scorer: gradient_next (mentl.mn:148) picks via pick_highest_leverage; to_annotation_suggestion (180) and suggestion_kind_of (192) are boundary wrappers. The scorer is cursor.mn (score_all_positions 286, score_one_position 291, top_n_by_score 784, sort_by_score_desc 789) and the top-K primitive already exists as gradient_peek_n (cursor.mn:735, 757-761). The gate is: speculative-compile's parallel candidate dispatch (the `>< ~> Thread` at mentl.mn:238-244's try_each_annotation) must consume gradient_peek_n, not re-derive a ranking. Note the honest scope, per PLAN.md:507: gates x proximity is sound ONLY over the finite annotation lattice — which is exactly what try_each_annotation ranks, so the confirm holds there and nowhere wider. Drop the item's added "or more likely to survive Verify fastest": no scorer in the repo models Verify latency, and conflating an intent ranker with a cost model is the same category error at the other site.

8. ADD THE FINDING THE ITEM MISSED — name it positively rather than leaving it hidden: Hβ.cursor.gradient-queue-argmax-is-a-rescan. The block's drift-7 refusal (cursor.mn:719-725, "zero memory cost... No priority-queue data structure") buys the refusal with a full rescan per pop — score_one_position calls teach_gradient (cursor.mn:292) -> gradient_next (mentl.mn:120) -> try_each_annotation (mentl.mn:244), running checkpoint/verify/rollback per annotation per position per pop. Refusing a data structure by rescanning is §5.O Law 1 violated ("every scanner in the compiler is a place we forgot the graph already knew"), wearing a drift refusal as a badge.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition
  This is PLAN's own band-N (native backend) peer, not a duplicate of the driver's layer-4 mechanism — it is the identical (index, stride) reservation pattern one altitude further out, in a compiled executable's own register/frame/relocation layout, correctly deferred past the production bar and must not be built or merged into this cluster's landing.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the item's whole reason for existing is a scope boundary the artifact draws the other way: PLAN.md:757 names `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` as literally one of the two halves of §5.O layer 4 (the driver's own parallel-cursor layer, required via §11 column 3), and NATIVE.md:66-68 confirms keystone 2's root is *handle assignment* with executable byte layout as its consequence — so "not a duplicate of the driver's layer-4 mechanism, must not be built or merged into this cluster's landing" is false, its gate cites the wrong stage (S0, not S11) and tests the wrong property (disjointness, not determinism), and its one concrete proposal (`block_partition -> [(unit, base, stride)]`) is a materialized side-ledger of a fact the handle already carries.
  objection: **The item inverts the artifact's own dependency direction, and the inversion is load-bearing.**

The item rules: "This is PLAN's own band-N (native backend) peer, not a duplicate of the driver's layer-4 mechanism — it is the identical (index, stride) reservation pattern one altitude further out ... must not be built or merged into this cluster's landing."

PLAN.md:754-757 says the opposite, verbatim:

> 4. **Parallel cursors** — the level-set partition at DECL granularity on the compile spine, infer/lower/emit fanned across cores with (arena_id, offset) deterministic handle partitioning so native_m3==native_m4 holds (`Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` multi-core half + `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition`).

"multi-core **half**" is the qualifier: the driver peer supplies one half of layer 4, this peer supplies the other. It is not cross-referenced, not deferred, not "one altitude further out" — it is named as constitutive of layer 4. And PLAN §11 column 3 (the performance floor, a *required* bar column) lists "Layer 4: parallel cursors" as a bar item. So the item defers half of a required landing.

NATIVE.md:65-68 settles which way the mechanism runs, and refutes the item's altitude story directly:

> **Keystone 2 — deterministic handle-space partitioning.** `native_m3 == native_m4` requires the encoder's byte output to be a pure deterministic function of the graph, which requires **handle assignment** — and *therefore* code layout and every emitted rel-offset — to be independent of core scheduling and allocation order.

Keystone 2's root is **handle assignment** — the compiler's own graph handles under concurrent cursors. Code layout and rel-offsets are the *consequence* ("and therefore"), not the mechanism. The item's split — "at the altitude of 'this IS the final executable's byte layout' instead of 'this is the compiler's own scratch while it thinks'" — is exactly backwards. There is ONE mechanism (handle assignment deterministic under N cursors) whose downstream fallout is byte layout. That is precisely *why* PLAN:757 cites this peer for the driver's layer 4: layer 4's problem IS handle assignment under concurrency, and this peer IS that.

**The blast radius the item denies.** Follow the item's ruling and §11 column 3's layer 4 lands with the driver's multi-core `><` fanout (src/driver.mn:168-169: "The layer partition IS the topological half of `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk`; the multi-core half is a `><`") and *no* deterministic handle partitioning — handle numbers assigned in core-arrival order, differing run to run. PLAN.md:762-764 already names layer 4 as "the sharpest risk" precisely because "handle numbers shift under the partition". Nondeterministic handle numbers break the m2 == m3 fixpoint that tools/march.sh:117 asserts ("✓✓ FIXED POINT holds: m2 == m3"). The item's BLAST RADIUS section says the opposite — that this must *not* be merged into the cluster's landing.

I tried to save the item on the reading "PLAN:757 is merely a cross-citation, and the peer really is band-N-only." It fails twice. First, PLAN §11 NOT-REQUIRED (PLAN.md:1426-1429) defers "native backend (docs/NATIVE.md)" — the *backend*, not every fact a peer with an `Hβ.native.*` prefix touches; reading the namespace prefix as the scope, when PLAN:757's citation edge is the live fact, is a name-keyed lookup where an edge already connects the answer (PLAN §2's canonical re-derivation). Second, if PLAN genuinely overloads one name across two altitudes, then it violates the three-doc contract's "each truth has exactly one home", and *ruling on this peer's scope was this item's entire job* — surfacing and splitting the name is the required move (PLAN §11's named-peer audit precedent: verdict 1 collapses five names into one design; verdict 3 MERGES two peers into one landing). The item does neither. It keeps the name, invents a third construct (`block_partition`) to bridge the two, and calls the multiplication reuse.
  fixes    : **1. Split the overloaded name; the driver half is REQUIRED, not deferred.**
PLAN.md:757 and PLAN.md:864 must not both own `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition`. The driver-altitude fact — handle assignment is a pure function of the graph under N concurrent cursors — is required by §11 column 3's layer 4 and belongs in this cluster under a driver-altitude name (`Hβ.driver.deterministic-arena-partition`). Band N's keystone 2 is that same mechanism's native *consumer*: NATIVE.md:67-68's code layout and rel-offsets fall out of it. Deferred: the encoder's byte-layout half (S11). Not deferred: the handle assignment layer 4 cannot ship without.

**2. The gate cites the wrong stage.** The item: "once docs/NATIVE.md's S0 lands". NATIVE.md:211-213 — S0 is "Reserved-register manifest + per-ISA Target descriptor (image-base, frontier, cursor/PC registers, the bounded-`%rsp` trap-scratch role, RegClass→physical-file table)". Keystone 2 is **S11** (NATIVE.md:244-245), eleven rungs out, behind S1-S10. The item ties its gate to a stage with no relation to its mechanism.

**3. The gate tests the wrong property and passes on the bug it exists to catch.** The item: "for all i ≠ j, native_handle_partition(unit_i) ∩ native_handle_partition(unit_j) == ∅". NATIVE.md:67-69 states the requirement as *determinism* — "independent of core scheduling and allocation order". Disjointness is not determinism: a partition can be perfectly disjoint on every run and still hand core 0 arena 3 this run and arena 1 the next. Handle numbers shift, bytes shift, `native_m3 ≠ native_m4` — and the proposed gate reports green. The real gate is already named and stronger: NATIVE.md:198 and NATIVE.md:249-251, `native_m3 == native_m4` at S13. At the *driver* altitude the gate is runnable today — `bash tools/march.sh`, which asserts m2 == m3 (tools/march.sh:117) and self-arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN (tools/march.sh:10-12). "Not gateable today" is false at the altitude §11 column 3 requires.

**4. The gate is itself a Law-1 violation.** Pairwise ∩ over all i ≠ j is O(N²) — a scan re-deriving what an `(arena_id, offset)` construction proves *by construction*. A gate for the O(1) architecture's layer 4 (PLAN §9.1: "a scan re-derives what an edge already connects") must not be a scan.

**5. Delete the `block_partition(units, stride_fn) -> [(unit, base, stride)]` extraction.** A returned list of `(unit, base, stride)` triples materializes a side-ledger of a fact the handle already carries. Interrogation 1 (Graph?): the arena for a decl is a projection at its handle — `arena_of(h)`, read live, O(1). PLAN §2: "Reading what an edge already connects BY NAME (a ledger, an index, an env re-lookup) instead of following the edge is the canonical re-derivation." There is nothing to "share" between the two altitudes because there is no table — both read the same live projection. (Also: "helper" is not Mentl vocabulary — fn / projection / primitive / stage / arm.)

**6. The job description collapses three homes into one.** The item: this peer's job is to "assign register classes, stack-frame offsets, and relocatable-symbol offsets". The artifact homes those at three separate peers:
- *Register classes* → NATIVE.md:215, **S1**, `repr_regclass(Repr) → RegClass`, "pure terminal beside" repr_wat/repr_width; PLAN.md:864 `Hβ.native.repr-regclass`. It is a function of **Repr**, never of decl identity and never of core order — the item's "as a pure function of decl identity, never of which core got there first" is a category error: regclass never depended on core order.
- *Stack-frame offsets* → NATIVE.md:55-63, **keystone 1**, `Hβ.native.frame-rep-from-cardinality-trail`; frames are image records read from the cardinality/ownership lattice, with %rsp demoted to a "bounded-`%rsp` trap-scratch role" (NATIVE.md:212). There is no per-core stack-frame stride problem in this design; the item imported one from conventional compilers.
- *Rel-offsets / code layout* → NATIVE.md:67-68, keystone 2's *consequence*.
Only the third is this peer's, and it is downstream of handle assignment.

**7. Fix the vocabulary — and the mechanism it implies.** The item says "(index, stride)" and "static-index-times-stride" throughout. PLAN.md:755 and NATIVE.md:69 both say **`(arena_id, offset)`**. These are not the same scheme: `(arena_id, offset)` is contiguous per-core arenas; index×stride is interleaved slots. PLAN §5.O layer 3 (PLAN.md:749-753) buys cache-residency exactly by collapsing the working set to one decl's live set; interleaving would scatter each decl's live set across every core's stride slots and destroy the win layer 4 sits directly on top of. The item asserts "the identical shape" without checking, while renaming the design's own vocabulary.

**8. The interrogation refusal is false against the artifact.** The item: "Shared with the driver item — no new interrogation content." NATIVE.md:69-73 carries interrogation-2 (Handler?) and interrogation-4 content specific to *this* altitude: "work-stealing is a value-reproducible-only `~> Schedule` handler **never installed on the self-hosting path** (the same handler-swap logic as persistence, `§4④`). This kills the 'two determinism modes' header flag — a drift-8 tell (a flag = an unbuilt ADT/handler) the critic caught in three layers." PLAN.md:864 repeats it. That is a live `~> Schedule` handler ruling plus a named drift-8 kill, and the item omits both while declaring the interrogations empty.

**9. The novelty verdict takes the easy half.** "Reject a generic worker-thread-local counter or lock scheme" refutes a proposal nobody made. The artifact's actual novelty ruling is the one the item drops: work-stealing *survives* — as a value-reproducible-only `~> Schedule` handler kept off the self-hosting path (NATIVE.md:69-71) — and the "two determinism modes" header flag dies as drift-8 (NATIVE.md:72-73). That is a real native-form-beats-borrowed-form argument (the conventional compiler ships a `--deterministic` flag; Mentl makes the nondeterministic mode a handler you simply do not install). The item's verdict is a slogan where the artifact has the argument.

**What survives.** The sequencing *conclusion* — don't build the native encoder's rel-offset determinism now — is correct, for reasons the item didn't give. NATIVE.md:244's S11 sits behind S0-S10 (register manifest, regclass, use-profile, frame classifier, call/ret codegen, FP/SIMD pinning), none of which exist: src/backends/ holds only wasm.mn, and no native.mn exists anywhere in the tree. PLAN §11 NOT-REQUIRED does name the native backend. So the *encoder* half is genuinely deferred. What is not deferred, and what the item wrongly defers with it, is the driver's own handle-assignment determinism under `><` — which src/driver.mn:168-169 already names as the open multi-core half of a live topological partition.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition
  This is PLAN's own band-N (native backend) peer, not a duplicate of the driver's layer-4 mechanism — it is the identical (index, stride) reservation pattern one altitude further out, in a compiled executable's own register/frame/relocation layout, correctly deferred past the production bar and must not be built or merged into this cluster's landing.
  verdict  : REFUTED — PLAN.md:757 names `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` as one of the two halves OF §5.O layer 4, and PLAN.md:1375 puts layer 4 inside the production bar, so the item's "correctly deferred, must not be merged into this cluster's landing" is contradicted by the artifact it cites; three further refutations (mechanism substituted, primitive is a side-ledger re-deriving an existing peer, gate vacuous) each stand alone.
  objection: The item defers this peer by reading its NAME PREFIX, not by following the edge PLAN draws — the name-keyed re-derivation the discipline exists to police, committed against the peer registry itself.

The chain the item relies on: §11 NOT-REQUIRED names "native backend (docs/NATIVE.md)" (PLAN.md:1428), the peer is spelled `Hβ.native.*`, therefore out of scope. But PLAN.md:754-758 (§5.O layer 4) says:

  "4. **Parallel cursors** — the level-set partition at DECL granularity on the
     compile spine, infer/lower/emit fanned across cores with (arena_id, offset)
     deterministic handle partitioning so native_m3==native_m4 holds
     (`Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` multi-core half + `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition`)."

"multi-core HALF" modifies the first peer; the second is the other half. Layer 4 IS both. And PLAN.md:1375 (§11 column 3, the production bar's performance floor) lists "Layer 4: parallel cursors". So PLAN places this peer's mechanism INSIDE the bar. PLAN.md:761-763 confirms the mechanism is a WASM-stage artifact: "layer 4 CHANGES bytes (handle numbers shift under the partition — a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3, the sharpest risk)" — handle numbers cannot shift under a partition that does not exist at the WASM stage. The item's "Depends on that item landing first" inverts a conjunction into a sequence.

The peer does not defer; it SPLITS, at the altitude docs/NATIVE.md:65-69 already draws — keystone 2 covers "handle assignment — and therefore code layout and every emitted rel-offset". Handle assignment is layer 4's, in the bar. Code layout + rel-offsets are S11/native, deferred. The item collapses both into "native-only".

Three more, each independently fatal:

(2) MECHANISM SUBSTITUTED, presented as "identical". Three artifact sites say `(arena_id, offset)` — PLAN.md:755, PLAN.md:864, docs/NATIVE.md:69 ("Fixed per-core `(arena_id, offset)` partition"). The item says "(index, stride)", "static-index-times-stride", `stride_fn`. Zero uses of arena_id. These are not the same shape: `(arena_id, offset)` is a compound address — arena_id is the decl's arena from §5.O layer 3 (PLAN.md:749-753; wasm.mn:139's dormant `emit_memory_arena` swap), offset bumps within it, uncapped. `base + i*stride` is a flat address requiring stride = a static per-decl CAP, and `stride_fn` must ESTIMATE handles-per-decl — a fact the graph does not hold until the decl is compiled. Overrun has exactly two answers: trap (a ceiling on decl size) or spill to a shared counter (the surrender-fallback Law 2 deletes). The substitute reintroduces a cap the artifact's form does not have.

(3) THE EXTRACTED PRIMITIVE IS A SIDE-LEDGER, AND IT RE-DERIVES AN EXISTING PEER. `block_partition(units, stride_fn) -> [(unit, base, stride)]` returns a list of triples — each unit's base carried ALONGSIDE the unit. That is the exact shape §5.O layer 2 deletes by name (PLAN.md:739-740, `Hβ.lower.esc-row-on-node`: "a FIELD on the fn's node read live, the side-ledger deleted"). The arena's base is a field on the arena's node. And PLAN.md:844 already names the ONE primitive for disjoint base assignment: `Hβ.emit.image-map-fold` — "each region's base IS the previous region's limit ..., overlap unconstructible". The item's INTERCONNECTION promises this peer "does not re-derive the reservation math this cluster works out first"; the item then proposes a second base-assignment primitive without citing the first.

(4) THE GATE IS REFUTED TWICE. "Not gateable today — no native codegen surface exists" is contradicted by the item's own cited document: docs/NATIVE.md:207 — "Everything through **S12 is verifiable in the WASM peer before native exists** — prove each projection against the standing `m3 == m4` ... That is the discipline's whole point." Keystone 2 IS S11 (docs/NATIVE.md:244: "**S11** Deterministic handle-space partition (**keystone 2**)"). S11 ≤ S12, so it is peer-verifiable now. Worse, the proposed future gate is vacuous on the failure it exists to prevent: it asserts pairwise DISJOINTNESS, but the property is DETERMINISM (docs/NATIVE.md:66-69: "a pure deterministic function of the graph ... independent of core scheduling and allocation order"). A lock-protected shared bump counter — the item's OWN rejected borrowed form — hands each core a disjoint block every run and a DIFFERENT block each run: gate passes, peer not landed, fixpoint dies. A gate that cannot distinguish the rejected form from the native form is no gate. (It is also O(n²) pairwise, inside the item serving §5.O, whose law is O(1) — PLAN §9.1.)

WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE — three claims held:
- "native.mn does not exist": TRUE. src/backends/ holds only wasm.mn (312679 bytes); `find . -name 'native*.mn'` returns nothing; grep for NativeOut / repr_regclass / native_handle_partition / block_partition returns nothing.
- "§11 NOT-REQUIRED names the native backend": TRUE, PLAN.md:1428.
- "band N describes this peer as KEYSTONE 2 ... S0-S18, native first-light at S13": TRUE, PLAN.md:864; docs/NATIVE.md:244 (S11=keystone 2), :251-253 (S13 = NATIVE FIRST LIGHT).
- The PRINCIPLE "one novelty verdict, cited from both sites, not re-derived at each" is correct — it is the Carried-Truth Law applied to the peer registry, and worth keeping. The item states it and then violates it by citing a mechanism the artifact does not hold.
  fixes    : 1. SPLIT the peer; do not defer it. Say: "keystone 2 covers two altitudes (docs/NATIVE.md:67-68 — 'handle assignment — and therefore code layout and every emitted rel-offset'). The HANDLE-ASSIGNMENT half is §5.O layer 4's own content (PLAN.md:757 names it as layer 4's second half; PLAN.md:1375 puts layer 4 in the production bar) and lands WITH this cluster. The CODE-LAYOUT + REL-OFFSET half is S11, native-only, deferred past the bar." Delete "must not be built or merged into this cluster's landing" and "Depends on that item landing first" — PLAN draws a conjunction, not a sequence.

2. MECHANISM: write `(arena_id, offset)`, the artifact's form (PLAN.md:755, PLAN.md:864, docs/NATIVE.md:69). Delete `(index, stride)`, "static-index-times-stride", and `stride_fn` outright. arena_id is the decl's arena from §5.O layer 3 (PLAN.md:749-753; the dormant `emit_memory_arena` swap at src/backends/wasm.mn:139); offset bumps within it, uncapped. A stride is a per-decl CAP whose size `stride_fn` must fabricate before the decl is compiled, and whose overrun answer is a trap or a shared-counter fallback. The pair has no cap, no estimate, and no cross-unit arithmetic — strictly stronger, and it is what the artifact already says.

3. DELETE `block_partition(units, stride_fn) -> [(unit, base, stride)]`. A list of (unit, base) is a side-ledger of a fact the arena's node carries — the shape `Hβ.lower.esc-row-on-node` deletes (PLAN.md:739-740). Read the base off the arena node live. Where a base fold IS needed (the arena table itself, computed once before fanout), it is the EXISTING named peer `Hβ.emit.image-map-fold` (PLAN.md:844) — "each region's base IS the previous region's limit ..., overlap unconstructible" — extended to the arena table. Cite it; do not extract a second primitive. Also drop "helper" — the vocabulary is fn / projection / primitive.

4. GATE: delete "Not gateable today" — docs/NATIVE.md:207 says the opposite and calls it "the discipline's whole point": "Everything through S12 is verifiable in the WASM peer before native exists — prove each projection against the standing m3 == m4". Replace pairwise disjointness (which passes on a lock-protected counter — the form the item claims to reject — while the fixpoint dies) with BYTE-IDENTITY UNDER CONCURRENCY, checkable today: run the parallel compile N times over the same source and assert byte-identical WAT every run, plus the march's own m3 == m4 (tools/march.sh, PLAN §8) which already arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN. That gate FAILS if the partition is not a pure function of decl identity. Better still per PLAN §0 and image-map-fold: make overlap UNCONSTRUCTIBLE in the representation, so there is nothing left to assert.

5. NOVELTY VERDICT: carry docs/NATIVE.md:69-73's full content, not its borrowed-advice shadow. Work-stealing is NOT rejected — it is DEMOTED to "a value-reproducible-only `~> Schedule` handler never installed on the self-hosting path (the same handler-swap logic as persistence, §4④)", and that demotion "kills the 'two determinism modes' header flag — a drift-8 tell ... the critic caught in three layers". "Reject a generic worker-thread-local counter or lock scheme" drops the handler-swap — the half that makes the verdict Mentl-native rather than generic determinism advice.

6. BLAST RADIUS must be stated, not waived. Since the handle-assignment half lands in this cluster, the item inherits PLAN.md:761-763: "layer 4 CHANGES bytes (handle numbers shift under the partition — a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3, the sharpest risk, well-precedented)." Say that: the landing is a TRANSITION, m2 ≠ m3 is expected and correct, m3 == m4 is the verdict, boot re-pins from m3.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.tools.thread-determinism-gate
  "It still compiles" is not a gate — the mechanical check this cluster needs is byte-identical WAT output for the SAME entry-module compile across two adversarial interleavings (a deterministic spawn-order reversal, and a real single-core-forced OS preemption), following the exact verdict-script convention already established by tools/march.sh and its siblings.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the gate's adversarial arm (Run B) targets driver_compile_entry, which contains no layer walk at all (driver.mn:202-219; driver_partition_layers' only two callers are the WAT-less check paths at driver.mn:182/228), and the three-run set has no oracle because it explicitly excludes the Seq reference that PLAN:1422-1424 names as the thesis gate and lower.mn:1752-1754 proves deterministic by construction.
  objection: The design's own adversarial lever is unimplementable against the artifact it names, and the gate it leaves behind is self-certifying.

(a) FACTUAL. "Compile the same entry module three ways via compile()/driver_compile_entry ... Run B: reverse each layer's spawn order ... a one-line, gate-only flag in whichever function iterates a layer to spawn." No such function is on that path. src/pipeline.mn:64 is `entry_module |> driver_compile_entry |> saturate_pass |> lower_program |> reachable_from_main |> executable_gate |> emit_module`. src/driver.mn:202-219 is `driver_collect_dag(entry_module)` then a `fold("", ...)` concatenating every module's source into ONE string, then `parse_program(lex(source))`, then `infer_program(ast)`. Grepping that body for partition|layer|each|spawn|>< returns empty. `driver_partition_layers` (defined src/driver.mn:268) has exactly two callers — src/driver.mn:182 (driver_check) and src/driver.mn:228 (driver_check_entry) — both CHECK paths producing diagnostics, zero WAT. Run B has no site.

(b) NO ORACLE. A==B==C proves the threaded path reproduces itself, not that it is correct: a fanout that deterministically drops a decl or deterministically mis-assigns handles yields three identical wrong WAT files and the gate prints PASS. src/lower.mn:1752-1754 states Seq is deterministic by construction ("branches evaluate inline, in source order, results tupled — deterministic, zero spawn cost"), which makes Seq the ground truth. PLAN.md:1422-1424 names precisely this as "the thesis gate: SAME source under `~> Seq`/`~> Thread`/`~> Gpu`, identical results, race-freedom carried by the ownership proof." The design rejects it by name — "never sequential_compose — that would prove nothing about concurrency" — inverting the artifact's own oracle. sequential_compose (lib/runtime/threading.mn:137-153) is exactly that reference implementation.

(c) NOVELTY overclaim. The audit honestly names TSan/Cilksan as the borrowed form and keeps empirical checking as scaffold — defensible. But it then keeps `taskset -c 0`, an OS lever, when the core-count axis is ALREADY a handler op in the tree: `num_cores() -> Int` is an op of `effect Thread` (lib/runtime/threading.mn:66); parallel_compose resumes `ffi_num_cores()` (:129); sequential_compose resumes `1` (:151). A handler arm returning a fixed N is the native form and is strictly stronger — deterministic, reproducible, no process privileges, and it sweeps the partition space (1/2/8/31) instead of sampling one preemption slice. Mentl structure supplied the stronger form and the design took the borrowed one.

(d) WRONG GRANULARITY / MISSING PREREQ. PLAN.md:754-757 defines §5.O layer 4 as "the level-set partition at DECL granularity on the compile spine, infer/lower/emit fanned across cores with (arena_id, offset) deterministic handle partitioning", and PLAN.md:696 says "the level-set partition (driver.mn) is off the hot path." The design gates module-granularity driver layers — off the hot path, and not where layer 4's determinism risk (handle assignment under the arena partition) lives. Worse, the compile path concatenates all modules into one program deliberately (src/driver.mn:194-201: "an executable IS the one-namespace concatenation of its dep DAG ... Parsed and inferred as ONE program"; PLAN §7 ledger: "driver_compile_entry = the one-namespace DAG concatenation"), so module-granularity cursors cannot exist there until `Hβ.driver.per-module-env-overlay` — the peer that same comment names as "the sequenced refinement" — lands. INTERCONNECTION names only level-set-par-walk and bootstrap-via-driver-path; it misses this hard prerequisite.

What I tried and could not break: the artifact_state's negative claim is true (no thread-determinism gate in `ls tools/`); march.sh's TRANSITION-vs-BROKEN classifier is real with diff-line-count reporting at exactly lines 126 and 134 as cited; the 7 zero-import fixtures are exactly the named files (verified by find+grep -c '^import '); sequential_compose exists as named; and the scaffold justification (empirical stand-in for Hβ.parallel.thread-alloc-transitive-proof, PLAN band E) is the honest shape matching PLAN §0's V_Pending-over-fabrication choice. The gate's INTENT survives; its mechanism, target, and run-set do not.
  fixes    : 1. DROP the claim that the gate runs "via compile()/driver_compile_entry" with a layer-spawn flag. driver_compile_entry (src/driver.mn:202-219) is a one-namespace concatenation with no layer walk; driver_partition_layers' only callers (src/driver.mn:182, :228) are check paths emitting zero WAT. State plainly: today the WAT-producing path has no fanout site, so Run B has no host function.

2. NAME THE MISSING PREREQUISITE. Add `Hβ.driver.per-module-env-overlay` as a hard DEP alongside `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk`. Until each module has its own env overlay, the compile path parses all modules as ONE program (src/driver.mn:194-201) and module-granularity cursors are structurally unconstructible. The driver.mn comment already names it "the sequenced refinement".

3. FIX THE GRANULARITY. Per PLAN.md:754-757 layer 4 is DECL granularity on the compile spine with (arena_id, offset) handle partitioning; PLAN.md:696 says the driver's module-level partition is off the hot path. The gate's invariant must be "emitted handle assignment is a pure function of the graph under a fixed (arena_id, offset) partition" — the same property `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition` asserts for native_m3==native_m4 — not "module spawn order does not matter."

4. ADD THE ORACLE — this is the load-bearing correction. Run S (`~> sequential_compose`, Seq, deterministic by construction per src/lower.mn:1752-1754) is the ground-truth reference; every threaded run must diff clean against S, not merely against each other. This is PLAN.md:1422-1424's own thesis gate ("SAME source under `~> Seq`/`~> Thread`/`~> Gpu`, identical results"). Delete the sentence "never sequential_compose — that would prove nothing about concurrency"; it is exactly backwards. Without S the gate certifies that the threaded path agrees with itself and three identical wrong answers pass.

5. REPLACE taskset WITH A HANDLER SWAP. `num_cores()` is an op of `effect Thread` (lib/runtime/threading.mn:66); parallel_compose resumes ffi_num_cores() (:129), sequential_compose resumes 1 (:151). Sweep the partition with handler arms pinning num_cores to 1/2/8/31 — deterministic, reproducible, no OS privileges, and it varies the (arena_id, offset) partition itself rather than sampling one preemption slice. Keep taskset only as an explicitly named scaffold-tier SECOND lever for real OS interleaving, never as the primary axis. State this in the novelty verdict: the borrowed lever (taskset/TSan) is replaced on the core-count axis because the medium already carries it as a `~>` handler arm.

6. FIX THE state.sh WIRING CLAIM. tools/state.sh has no "columns"; it has a gate() function that runs the script, prints the last line on exit 0, and on nonzero greps `-E "RED|✗"` (head -8) plus the last line. The gate must exit nonzero on divergence and mark failures with `RED` or `✗` to be legible, and be added as one `gate "THREAD DETERMINISM (...)" bash tools/thread-determinism-gate.sh` line.

7. KEEP AS-IS: the artifact_state's negative claim, the march.sh orthogonality argument (lines 126-148 verified exact), the 7 zero-import fixture list (verified exact), the synthetic 16-32 leaf-module fixture, and the scaffold framing as the named stand-in for Hβ.parallel.thread-alloc-transitive-proof.

REFUTED: Hβ.tools.bootstrap-via-driver-path
  Layer 4, even fully built and gated, will not speed up the wheel's own self-hosting fixpoint check (the ~10s number in PLAN §7's landing ledger) — that check compiles via the stdin/single-concatenated-blob path, which bypasses driver.mn entirely, so a separate, sequenced switch is required before the wheel's own bootstrap benefits at all.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the item's scoping half is CONFIRMED (the fixpoint check really does bypass driver.mn), but its design is refuted twice over by the artifact: driver_compile_entry contains no layer partition, so the proposed switch buys zero parallelism; and "zero changes to the wheel's own source" is false — running `compile main` today yields 3,069 E_MissingVariable against the stdin path's 118, because main's import DAG reaches 31 of the 50 wheel files and nothing in src/ imports prelude.
  objection: The design's own purpose is unreachable by the change it proposes: switching the bootstrap to `compile main` would engage NO layer partition, because `driver_compile_entry` does not have one. Both `driver_partition_layers` call sites are on the CHECK path — driver.mn:182 (inside `driver_check`, whose body is `entry_module |> driver_collect_dag_with_deps |> driver_partition_layers |> each(...)`) and driver.mn:228 (inside `driver_check_entry`). `driver_compile_entry` (driver.mn:202-220) is `driver_collect_dag(entry_module)` → `fold("", (acc, m) => "{acc}{fs_read_file(p)}\n", modules)` → `parse_program(lex(source))` → `infer_program(ast)`: ONE parse, ONE infer, over ONE concatenated blob — the same single sequential cursor as `compile_stdin`'s `read_stdin() |> frontend |> infer_program` (pipeline.mn:143). And pipeline.mn:64 calls `driver_compile_entry`, never `driver_check_entry`. So the switch relocates the byte source (an fs_read_file fold instead of fd 0) and changes nothing about how the program is walked. The design mistakes "compile routes through driver.mn" for "compile routes through the layer partition"; its INTERCONNECTION claim ("the item that makes the driver item's headline claim actually true for the compile everyone measures") is therefore false, as is the ARTIFACT STATE's closing line "only the second benefits from any work in this cluster" — the compile path benefits no more than the stdin path, because both are one sequential parse+infer. PLAN §5.O already says this in its own words at PLAN.md:696 ("the level-set partition (driver.mn) is off the hot path") and scopes layer 4 at PLAN.md:754 to "DECL granularity on the compile spine" — decls in one program, not modules in a DAG — which the single-blob spine already presents without any driver involvement.

Second, independently fatal, and empirical: "a change entirely inside the tooling layer, zero changes to the wheel's own source" is refuted by running it. `timeout 600 wasmtime run -W threads=y -W shared-memory=y -W tail-call=y -S threads=y --dir . boot/mentl.wasm compile main` → exit 0, 16,176,251 bytes of WAT, and a stderr census of 3,069 E_MissingVariable (`len`, `list_index`, `map`, `Some`, `None`), 2,699 E_OccursCheck, 1 E_MissingModule — against the cached stdin m2's 118 E_MissingVariable / 118 E_OccursCheck / 0 E_MissingModule (.build/m2cache/m2.err). `cmp .build/m2cache/m2.wat driver_m2.wat` → differ at byte 1057, line 13. Cause, verified: main's import DAG (src/main.mn:48-63, walked through driver_module_path's src/-then-lib/ resolution) reaches 31 of the 50 files `wt_wheel src lib` emits. `grep -rn "import prelude" src/ lib/` returns ZERO declarations — only a comment at driver.mn:31 and prelude's own header at lib/prelude.mn:8 — so lib/prelude.mn, lib/runtime/{lists,math,memory,tuples,buffer,search,persist,net,cache_map,imap}.mn, src/format.mn, lib/dsp/*, lib/ml/* (19 files) never enter driver_compile_entry's fold. The wheel relies on the blob to supply that vocabulary as top-level names in one namespace. The switch therefore REQUIRES wheel-source changes, which is the design's central claim inverted.

Third, the misread that produced both: the design says driver_compile_entry "already claims byte-for-byte equivalence to the blob form." The cited comment (driver.mn:194-196) says no such thing. Verbatim: "an executable IS the one-namespace concatenation of its dep DAG — deps first, post-order, entry last — the exact form the wheel's own build proves daily (`find src lib | xargs cat`)." It claims the FORM (one-namespace concatenation) is the proven one, and names its own ORDER in the same sentence — post-order DAG (types, strings, effects, graph, infer, lower, backends/wasm, …, main last), which is not the blob's alphabetical src-then-lib order (backends/wasm, cursor, cursor_transport, driver, effects, …). Order is byte-load-bearing in this compiler by PLAN §7's own ledger ("interpolation source-order (204k→2.7k diff)"). "Same form" was upgraded to "same bytes" with nothing in the artifact supporting it.

What survives: the item's core scoping observation is TRUE and worth keeping. wt_m2_ensure (wt-env.sh:112-132) feeds wt_wheel's blob (wt-env.sh:71-80) via stdin (wt-env.sh:125-126); main.mn:219 forks to compile_stdin_run (main.mn:382-388) → compile_stdin (pipeline.mn:142-143), whose header (pipeline.mn:86-98) states it skips driver_check and fs_read_file; compile_run (main.mn:367-375) → compile (pipeline.mn:58-84) → driver_compile_entry (pipeline.mn:64). Every cited line number checks out. The parity gate the design proposes is also real and non-vacuous — I ran it and it FAILS today (3,069 vs 118 missing-variable errors; WAT differs at byte 1057). It just gates something two orders of magnitude larger than "the last item in this cluster's landing order."
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the claim that this is tooling-only. State instead: `compile main` today emits 3,069 E_MissingVariable vs the stdin path's 118, because main's import DAG covers 31 of 50 wheel files and no file in src/** declares `import prelude` (grep: zero hits). The switch is gated on a WHEEL-SOURCE landing: the wheel must declare the import edges the blob currently supplies implicitly.

2. DELETE the claim that the switch delivers layer 4's benefit. State instead: driver_compile_entry (driver.mn:202-220) has no layer partition — driver_partition_layers is called only at driver.mn:182 (driver_check) and driver.mn:228 (driver_check_entry), both on the CHECK path — so routing the bootstrap through it yields the same single sequential parse+infer over the same single blob. Engaging layer 4 on the compile spine is a change to the COMPILE path itself, not a change of byte source. PLAN.md:754 already scopes layer 4 at DECL granularity on the compile spine, which the existing single-blob program presents directly; PLAN.md:696 already records that driver.mn's module-level partition is off the hot path. The honest conclusion is the opposite of this item's: the bootstrap does not need to move to the driver path for layer 4 to fire at all — layer 4 must fan out decls on the spine both paths share.

3. DELETE "already claims byte-for-byte equivalence to the blob form." The comment (driver.mn:194-196) claims the same FORM and names a DIFFERENT order in the same sentence ("deps first, post-order, entry last"). Post-order DAG order (types, strings, effects, graph, infer, …, main) is not alphabetical src-then-lib order (backends/wasm, cursor, cursor_transport, driver, effects, …), and decl order is byte-load-bearing (PLAN §7: "interpolation source-order (204k→2.7k diff)"). Any switch must reconcile order, not assume it.

4. KEEP the parity gate — it is the design's best part and it correctly FAILS today. But re-scope it: it is the acceptance gate on the wheel-source landing in (1), not a pre-flight check before a tooling tweak. Its measured failure mode is a 26x missing-variable blowup and a WAT divergence at byte 1057, not a subtle reordering.

5. RE-ROOT the item on the Carried-Truth reading it gestures at but does not carry. `find src lib | sort | xargs cat` IS a side-ledger of a dep DAG — the tooling re-deriving by filesystem scan and alphabetical sort a program the graph should already hold as import edges, and PLAN §11 already rejects that shape by name ("manifest files -> the imports ARE the manifest (the kernel already holds the dep DAG as edges)"). But the artifact shows the wheel does NOT hold those edges, so the blob is not a redundant copy of the DAG — it is currently the only home of that truth, which is why deleting it breaks the compile. The honest positive-form peer is Hbeta.driver.wheel-declares-its-imports: land the import edges in src/**.mn so the DAG becomes the truth (the imports ARE the manifest, for the wheel itself, not only for user projects), with the parity gate from (4) as its acceptance criterion. The tooling switch then falls out for free and needs no item of its own. Sequencing: this is independent of Hbeta.driver.level-set-par-walk in both directions — it neither gates it nor is gated by it — so "last item in this cluster's landing order" is wrong; it is not in this cluster.

6. BLAST RADIUS the design omits entirely: the wheel-source landing in (5) changes which decls exist and in what order, which changes emitted handle numbering and string-intern offsets. That is a march TRANSITION (m2 != m3, re-pin boot from m3 per tools/march.sh), not a Law-7 byte-identical change. The design says nothing about the fixpoint at all.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.infer.region-polymorphism
  A function's parameters and return carry region variables — quantified and instantiated by the same Forall/instantiate walk that already generalizes ordinary type and effect-row variables — so region identity flows across call boundaries instead of resetting to 'untagged' at every CallExpr, which is what makes a real mutation-site check buildable without false-flooding the ref-buffer idiom.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state survives (I confirmed nearly every citation), but the DESIGN's own mechanism is refuted three ways independently: its mint-condition ("ref-or-own-marked parameter") mints ZERO region variables for `region_index_add` (own.mn:249, no markers) — the design's own headline example and its entire argument for why polymorphism is "necessary, not optional"; the quantification site it names (infer.mn:289) runs in an earlier pass (pre_register_fn_sig, reached from infer.mn:174) than the mint site it names (infer_fn, infer.mn:363), so the Forall list is frozen before the region vars exist; and all three gates pass vacuously at HEAD.
  objection: THE MINT-CONDITION EXCLUDES ITS OWN MOTIVATING EXAMPLE, AND INVERTS A RESOLVED §4⑤ DECISION.

The design: "mint a fresh region variable ... for every ref-or-own-marked parameter and for the return", justified by "own.mn's own region_index_add is called from at least three sites (own.mn:181,198,227) that will become three different ambient regions ... a monomorphic region parameter would force all three calls to unify."

The artifact: `src/own.mn:249` reads `fn region_index_add(idx, handle, tag) with Memory + Alloc = {`. Zero authored markers. Its siblings DO carry them (own.mn:245 `lookup_tag(ref idx, handle)`, own.mn:269 `region_on_stack(ref stack, rid)`, own.mn:280 `join_field_regions(ref tags, ref fields, current)`) — so this is not a repo-wide absence, it is precisely the function the design is built around. Under the design's stated rule, `region_index_add` gets no region variables at all, and the three call sites it exists to disambiguate stay exactly as monomorphic as they are today.

This cannot be repaired by reading INFERRED ownership instead, and that is the deeper kill. The only site the Forall is built is `pre_register_fn_sig` (infer.mn:247-290, `env_extend(name, Forall(free_in_ty(fn_ty), fn_ty), ...)` at 289), reached from `pre_register_decls` at infer.mn:174 — an earlier pass over all stmts. `infer_ownership` runs at infer.mn:446, inside `infer_fn` (363), AFTER the body walk. At quantification time only the AUTHORED marker exists. So the design's rule is authored-only by construction — which inverts PLAN §4⑤ and the Hylo-quiet bar: markers are CONSTRAINTS verified against the inferred, never the source of truth ("if the developer has to think about it, the inference failed"). The design makes an annotation decide whether the region FACT EXISTS, i.e. exactly the Rust `'a`-at-non-elidable-boundaries shape its own novelty section rejects by name.

TWO MORE INDEPENDENT KILLS, both artifact-grounded:

(a) ONE OF THREE WALKS — the design ships a known silent-wrong AND an occurs-check hole. It names only `free_in_ty` (infer.mn:2976-2992). But `subst_ty` (infer.mn:3099) → `subst_params` (3153) → `param_with_ty` (types.mn:253, `let TParam(n, _, a, r, d) = p; TParam(n, t, a, r, d)`) copies every non-ty slot VERBATIM — a 6th region slot rides unfreshened into every instantiation, sharing ONE region across all call sites: the exact monomorphic collapse the design says it must avoid. The artifact has already paid for this asymmetry and comments it twice — infer.mn:3134-3140 (TRecordOpen): "free_in_ty COLLECTS v ... instantiate MUST freshen it. Dropping v here shared the SAME row tail across every instantiation of an open-record scheme — a proven let-poly asymmetry"; infer.mn:3146-3148 (TCont world): "freshen its row-var like TFun's eff (free_in/subst symmetry)". And the third walk, `occurs_in`, is in a file the design never mentions — graph.mn:542, `occurs_in_params` at graph.mn:552. CLAUDE.md ⟲ records the 2026-07-08 HANG as precisely this: the three walks free_in_ty/occurs_in/subst_ty must AGREE.

(b) THE RETURN REGION HAS NO HOME, and the only home is the largest arity change in the compiler. `type Ty = ... | TFun(List, Ty, EffRow)` — types.mn:41, three slots, no region. The design gives params a TParam slot and the return nothing. Measured: `TFun(` = 83 sites across src/lib; `TParam(` = 22. For calibration, PLAN §5.U calls TCont's arity change "the one coordinated breaking edit ... across ~14 destructure sites" — `TCont(` measures 25 sites. TFun is 3.3× that, unnamed and unsequenced.
