"The same product, not a parallel list" is true for params and silently false for the return.

(c) THE GATE CANNOT FAIL (attack vector 4). Gate (1) asserts "zero E_RegionEscape / mutation-check false positives" — but no mutation-site check exists (confirmed: no store site in own.mn is checked against a region; the only region enforcement is `check_escape`, infer.mn:434, and `ERegionEscape` is raised at exactly one site, own.mn:231), and the design scopes mutation-site checking OUT ("currently-nonexistent ... outside this arc"). Zero false positives from a nonexistent check passes at HEAD. Gate (2) "stays Hylo-quiet at zero escapes (matching 82a7e42's baseline)" IS the baseline — a no-regression check, and its qualifier "with mutation-site checking turned on" cannot be run. Gate (3) is generic board-green. None fails if the design is never built.

WHAT I TRIED TO REFUTE AND COULD NOT — the central novelty claim, which the design UNDERSELLS. I expected to kill "a third free variable riding the exact Ty/Forall/instantiate/unify substrate ... not three type systems" by showing effect rows already run a PARALLEL sort (free_in_row / subst_row / unify_row / graph_bind_row), making regions a third mirror — i.e. MLKit's second type system after all. The artifact disproved me: `type Scheme = Forall(List, Ty)` (types.mn:303-304) is ALREADY domain-heterogeneous — ONE list holds type vars and row vars together — and `build_inst_mapping` (infer.mn:3076-3088) reads the domain LIVE off the graph node kind rather than tracking it: `let fresh = if is_row_handle(root) { mint_row(...) } else { mint(...) }`, commented "A quantified var's domain is carried in the graph — NRowFree means a row variable ... NFree means a type variable ... Read the domain live; never re-derive it." A third domain is a third arm on a live domain projection. That is a stronger, Carried-Truth-native argument than the free_in_row comment the design actually cites. Also honest and correct: the equality-vs-subtyping question is named as the open interrogation rather than waved away, and the m2==m3 TRANSITION risk from shifted instantiate handle order is stated straight.

CITATION IMPRECISION (correction, not a kill): "read live off the handler's HandlerKind row (types.mn:351)". types.mn:351 IS HandlerKind — but its `Int` field is the RESIDUAL row (what the arms perform), not the absorbed effect. types.mn:359-361 states the absorbed effect "is NOT stored: it is a PROJECTION of `arms`", and infer.mn:1319-1320 repeats it: "The handled effect is a live projection of the arms (§2 follow-the-edge), never a stored field — arms→op→EffectOpScheme carries it." So "does this handler absorb Alloc" is `derive_handler_ename(arms) == "Alloc"`, never a row read. The design names the right mechanism two clauses later (handler_e_r/derive_handler_ename), so this is internal inconsistency. Everything else checked out: infer.mn:393/435 bracket infer_fn's body unconditionally; infer.mn:547-553 names Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb; call_is_alloc_returning (infer.mn:529-544) gates ConstructorScheme/RecordSchemeKind only, called at infer.mn:649-650; lib/runtime/memory.mn:5-6 states "No handler is written in Mentl for these — the WASM runtime IS the handler"; own.mn:225 reads `list_index(region_stack, depth - 2)`; TParam at types.mn:206-213. Minor off-by-ones (infer_pipe_tee is 1277-1308; handler_e_r is defined at 1313, not 1286 — 1286 is its call site) are not material.
  fixes    : The design survives ONLY if it says all six of these instead.

1. MINT-CONDITION — delete "for every ref-or-own-marked parameter". Mint a region variable for EVERY parameter and the return, unconditionally, in `pre_register_fn_sig` (infer.mn:247-290) alongside the existing `mint_param_placeholders`/`mint(FnReturn(...))`/`graph_fresh_row` — the three mints that already happen there with no marker gate. Ownership does not gate the region's EXISTENCE; the region is a fact of every binding. An unmarked param is the common case by §4⑤ design (the Hylo-quiet bar), and `region_index_add` (own.mn:249) proves it: the design's own headline example carries no marker. If ownership is to influence anything, it influences the region's USE at the mutation check (a separate build), never whether the graph holds the fact.

2. MINT/QUANTIFY SITE — replace "At infer_fn, mint ... so Forall(free_in_ty(fn_ty), fn_ty) (infer.mn:289) quantifies them" with: mint in `pre_register_fn_sig`, where line 289's Forall is built. State the pass ordering explicitly: `pre_register_decls` (infer.mn:174) runs over all stmts BEFORE `infer_stmt_list` reaches `infer_fn` (infer.mn:363); infer_fn mints its own fresh handles (366-373) and unifies them into the pre-registered ones via the `NBound` arm (378-379). Region vars must be born on the pre-register side or instantiate never freshens them. Note the `_ =>` arm at infer.mn:380-383 re-registers `Forall([], fn_ty)` — monomorphic — and say which arm regions land in.

3. THE THREE WALKS — name all three, not one. `free_in_ty`/`free_in_params` (infer.mn:2976-2996), `subst_ty`/`subst_params` (infer.mn:3099-3154), and `occurs_in`/`occurs_in_params` (graph.mn:542-552, a different file the design omits entirely). Per CLAUDE.md ⟲ they must AGREE — the 2026-07-08 hang was their disagreement. Concretely: `param_with_ty` (types.mn:253) copies the non-ty slots verbatim, so `subst_params` must gain a region-aware updater (the `param_with_resolved` shape at types.mn:254) or every instantiation shares one region — the TRecordOpen bug (infer.mn:3134-3140) re-run at the region slot.

4. THE RETURN REGION — say where it lives. `TFun(List, Ty, EffRow)` (types.mn:41) has no slot, and the design's own text requires one ("and for the return"). Either (a) name the TFun 4th slot as a coordinated breaking edit and SIZE it — 83 `TFun(` sites vs TCont's 25 / "~14 destructures", which PLAN §5.U calls "the one coordinated breaking edit" — sequencing it under the unpatchability theorem; or (b) drop the return region from this stage and say so, which forfeits Hβ.own.region-return-transfer's generalization (the design's own claimed feed) and must be stated as forfeited, not left silent. A third option worth interrogating and rejecting or taking explicitly: region-as-effect-row-member, riding TFun's EXISTING EffRow slot — PLAN §4⑤ already holds "ownership IS an effect", the row already crosses call boundaries, `free_in_row`/`subst_row`/`occurs_in_row` already agree, and it needs zero arity change. The design never considers the one home that is already there.

5. THE GATE — replace all three legs with one that fails at HEAD. The current legs cannot fail: (1) asserts zero false positives from a check that does not exist (own.mn raises ERegionEscape at exactly one site, own.mn:231, from check_escape at infer.mn:434 — no store site is region-checked); (2) asserts the 82a7e42 baseline is preserved, which is HEAD; (3) is board-green. A gate that bites: a micro calling ONE polymorphic fn from two sibling regions, asserting the two call sites instantiate DISTINCT region handles — a positive projection (the query handler already renders quantified vars as display ids, infer.mn:3070-3072), which today returns nothing because no region variable exists. Keep board-green + m2==m3/TRANSITION as no-regression, but do not count them as the gate.

6. NOVELTY — two fixes. Delete "the ML value-restriction failure mode at the region layer": the value restriction is a restriction ON generalization (only syntactic values generalize), adopted to keep polymorphic mutable refs sound. The design describes plain monomorphism-vs-let-polymorphism. The mislabel inverts the concept's actual bearing — with `region_index` mutable (own.mn:138), the value restriction is the CONSTRAINT ON region generalization, not the argument for it, and that is a real open question this design should name rather than borrow the term backwards. Second, UPGRADE the surviving claim: cite `Forall(List, Ty)` (types.mn:303-304) holding both domains in one list and `build_inst_mapping`'s live domain read (infer.mn:3076-3088, "A quantified var's domain is carried in the graph ... Read the domain live; never re-derive it") — the domain is a projection of the graph node kind, so a third domain is a third arm, not a third type system. That is the Carried-Truth argument, stronger than free_in_row's comment, and it is the one part of this design the artifact actually supports.

Also fix the trigger citation: the Alloc-absorb test is `derive_handler_ename(arms) == "Alloc"`, not a HandlerKind ROW read — types.mn:359-361 and infer.mn:1319-1320 both state the handled effect is a live projection of `arms`, never stored; HandlerKind's Int is the RESIDUAL row.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.infer.region-polymorphism
  A function's parameters and return carry region variables — quantified and instantiated by the same Forall/instantiate walk that already generalizes ordinary type and effect-row variables — so region identity flows across call boundaries instead of resetting to 'untagged' at every CallExpr, which is what makes a real mutation-site check buildable without false-flooding the ref-buffer idiom.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's only capability gate passes at HEAD (I ran it: diags=0, exit 42, zero E_RegionEscape), and it passes because the design puts the region variable in the one slot on the fn type that the unify walk provably ignores (TParam), then re-adds the unification by hand at every CallExpr — contradicting the exact row precedent (infer.mn:1872) the design cites as its model.
  objection: TWO objections, linked at the root. Both survived my attempt to disprove them.

(1) THE GATE IS VACUOUS — MEASURED, NOT ARGUED. I built gate (1)'s exact micro (two call sites to a single ref-mutating helper mirroring own.mn:249-253 region_index_add, from two distinct sibling fn regions) and ran it through the pinned boot at HEAD: PASS, exit=42, diags=0, zero E_RegionEscape. It cannot fail without the design, because (a) mutation-site checking does not exist — the design itself concedes this — and (b) E_RegionEscape fires only from check_escape, whose SOLE call site is infer.mn:434, at fn RETURN, never against a call site's arguments (verified: grep shows infer.mn:434 is the only non-comment caller). Gate (2) requires "mutation-site checking turned on", a switch this item explicitly does not build, so it is unrunnable at landing. Gate (3) is a no-regression floor. No gate in this design fails-without-the-design.

(2) THE REGION IS IN THE WRONG HOME — AND THE DESIGN'S OWN CITATION REFUTES IT. The design cites free_in_row's comment (infer.mn:3006-3008, "the row var is quantified exactly like a TVar") as its model. But the row rides IN the Ty — TFun(List, Ty, EffRow), types.mn:41 — so unify_types' TFun arm unifies it in the SAME walk: `pair_fn_params(pa, pb, ...)` / `unify_types(ra, rb, ...)` / `unify_row(ea, eb, span, reason)` (infer.mn:1858-1873). No "after type unification, also unify the rows" pass exists anywhere. The design instead extends TParam — and TParam's non-Ty slots are structurally OUTSIDE the unify walk: pair_fn_params → unify_param_lists touches only types (infer.mn:2302-2309), and param_authored/param_resolved are never unified, only read at env_extend (infer.mn:3246, 3812). That is precisely why the design must bolt on "At each CallExpr, after ordinary type unification, also unify each argument's chased tag against the callee's freshly-instantiated region variable" — a second pass that exists only because the fact was placed beside the type instead of in it. Put the region in the Ty and that CallExpr edit is DELETED (Law 1: the fix is LESS code).

Three corroborating kills:

(3) THE free_in_ty ↔ subst_ty SYMMETRY — A PAID-FOR BUG REINTRODUCED. The design names free_in_ty (2976-2992) and build_inst_mapping (3063-3088) but NOT subst_ty/subst_params. subst_params is `map((p) => param_with_ty(p, subst_ty(param_ty(p), mapping)), params)` (infer.mn:3153-3154) and param_with_ty preserves (n, a, r, d) unchanged (types.mn:253) — so a TParam region slot passes through instantiate UNFRESHENED: generalize quantifies it, instantiate strands it, every call site shares ONE region var. That is exactly the monomorphic collapse the design says it must avoid ("the ML value-restriction failure mode at the region layer"). subst_ty's TRecordOpen arm documents this as already paid for: "Dropping v here shared the SAME row tail across every instantiation ... Carried-Truth: the free_in_ty ↔ subst_ty symmetry was broken at this arm" (infer.mn:3128-3134). CLAUDE.md ⟲ states the law the inventory misses (free_in_ty/occurs_in/subst_ty must AGREE). The cited range also excludes the real site: free_in_params is at infer.mn:2996, not 2976-2992.

(4) TWO IDENTITY SPACES — PLAN §11'S OWN AUDIT INVERTED. Region identity today is an Int from own.mn's PRIVATE counter (`let rid = next_region + 1`, own.mn:145; `next_region = rid`, own.mn:148) in region_index, a 65536-bucket handle-keyed hash in handler state (own.mn:247-267) — a second minting authority beside the graph's mint/graph_fresh_ty. The design adds a graph-handle region var as a SECOND representation and bridges them ("unify each argument's chased tag against the ... region variable" = unify an Int rid against a handle). PLAN §5.O names **region** explicitly in the waypoint list to dissolve ("summary/region/env/base/esc/reach — six hand-rolled copies ... are ONE primitive, Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive"), and PLAN §11 already ruled this exact inversion for the name-is-handle cluster: "the banked smap-first sequencing is INVERTED ... the migration paid twice; intern-at-lex leads, the maps land handle-keyed once." e887bde's own subject — "the region tag speaks truth", fixing an offset-0 read where "every diagnostic's 'region N' was the value's own handle" — is the artifact pointing at the ultimate form: a region IS a handle.

(5) NOVELTY OVERCLAIM + A STRICTLY WEAKER REPRESENTATION THAN BOTH TOOLS NAMED. Rust ALREADY quantifies and instantiates lifetimes on the same substrate as type params (GenericParamDefKind::Lifetime in the same Generics, substituted through the same GenericArgs; ty::Region is part of the type, `&'a T`). What is separate in Rust is region CONSTRAINT SOLVING (NLL/borrowck), not region QUANTIFICATION — so "Rust's borrow checker is more separate still" conflates the two and lands the claimed novelty on ground Rust already holds. Meanwhile one region slot per TParam cannot express a param whose nested boxed values live in different regions — `(&'a [i32], &'b [i32])` in Rust, `(int list at ρ1 * int list at ρ2) at ρ3` in MLKit — while own.mn's tag_alloc_join (own.mn:186-199) ALREADY computes region per-field and joins them, so the TParam slot flattens structure the substrate already carries. And the return's region has NO home at all: the design mints one "for the return" but extends only TParam; TFun(List, Ty, EffRow) (types.mn:41) has no return-region slot.

WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE (honest): the ARTIFACT STATE is accurate on every cited line. region_enter/region_exit are unconditional at infer.mn:393/435; the Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb comment is at infer.mn:547-553; call_is_alloc_returning (infer.mn:529-544) gates tag_alloc_join at infer.mn:649-651 to ConstructorScheme/RecordSchemeKind only; check_escape's sole call site is infer.mn:434; no Mentl-level Alloc-absorbing handler exists (memory.mn:1-15: "No handler is written in Mentl for these — the WASM runtime IS the handler"); region_tracker IS installed (pipeline.mn:73,152,194,238,264); TParam's shape and HandlerKind are as cited (types.mn:206-213, 351); infer_pipe_tee/handler_e_r read the absorbed name live (infer.mn:1277-1307, 1286-1296); 82a7e42's message does say "cross-fn value flow rides untagged CALL nodes" (line 22). The THESIS also survives: region-polymorphism is genuinely needed, must cross the call boundary, and should ride one generalize/instantiate walk rather than become a second type system; the Row interrogation (region-opening is a ~>-install fact read off HandlerKind, not a per-decl rule) is correct. The design's own adversarial note (equality vs subtyping under polymorphism) is honest and stays open. The kill is the REPRESENTATION and the GATE, not the goal.
  fixes    : 1. THE REGION IS A HANDLE, NOT AN Int. Delete own.mn's private counter (`next_region`, own.mn:145/148) and mint the region as a graph node via the graph's own mint. region_index (own.mn:247-267) dissolves into an EDGE on the value's node — Law 1's LESS code, and PLAN §5.O's own "region" waypoint discharged rather than built upon. Free region = NFree, bound region = NBound (the graph's existing two node kinds); unify region~region = graph_bind/chase. No third domain in is_row_handle (infer.mn:3090-3094), no Int-vs-handle bridge. region_on_stack's list scan (own.mn:269-274) becomes an edge walk (O(1), §5.O). Name this `Hβ.own.region-is-a-handle` and SEQUENCE IT FIRST — it must lead exactly as PLAN §11 ruled intern-at-lex must lead the name-is-handle cluster, or the rid→handle migration is paid twice.

2. THE REGION LIVES IN THE Ty, NOT ON TParam. Follow the row's own precedent (TFun(List, Ty, EffRow), types.mn:41 → `unify_row(ea, eb, span, reason)` inside unify_types' TFun arm, infer.mn:1872). Placed in the Ty where the value's type is, `unify_types(arg_ty, param_ty)` unifies the regions in the SAME walk and the design's "at each CallExpr, after ordinary type unification, also unify..." edit is DELETED. This simultaneously gives the return's region a home for free (TFun's ret is a Ty) and restores the per-boxed-value granularity tag_alloc_join (own.mn:186-199) already computes and the TParam slot would flatten.

3. COMPLETE THE SITE INVENTORY. The design names free_in_ty and build_inst_mapping; it must also name free_in_params (infer.mn:2996 — outside the cited 2976-2992 range), subst_ty/subst_params (infer.mn:3099/3153-3154), and occurs_in. CLAUDE.md ⟲: the three "vars of a type" walks must AGREE. Without subst_params, generalize quantifies the region var and instantiate strands it — the exact monomorphic collapse the design says it exists to prevent, and the bug subst_ty's TRecordOpen arm (infer.mn:3128-3134) already documents as paid for.

4. REPLACE THE GATES — the current ones cannot fail. Gate (1) passes at HEAD (measured: diags=0, exit 42, zero E_RegionEscape). Gate (2) requires a mutation-checking switch this item does not build. A gate that fails-without-the-design must assert the fact the design CREATES: that a polymorphic helper's region variable is FRESHENED per call site — two call sites' instantiated region handles are distinct (h_a != h_b). That is false today (no region var exists) and false under the design's own unfixed subst_params (stranded, so h_a == h_b), and true only on the complete form. That single assertion kills the pre-state, the incomplete design, and the stranding bug at once.

5. DROP THE RUST OVERCLAIM. Rust's 'a already rides Generics/GenericArgs on the same substitution as type params; only constraint solving (NLL/borrowck) is a separate phase. State the honest differentiators instead: regions are INFERRED, never authored (§4⑤'s Hylo-quiet bar — "if the developer has to think about it, the inference failed"), and region-opening is read LIVE off the ~> install's HandlerKind row (the design's own correct Row interrogation, the infer_pipe_tee precedent at infer.mn:1286-1296) rather than being a separate dataflow pass over an elaborated IL. Against MLKit the honest claim is one walk instead of a second type system — but that claim is only EARNED once the region sits in the Ty (correction 2); on TParam it is a slogan.

6. Keep the design's real contributions: the Row interrogation (region-opening is a ~>-Alloc-absorb fact, absorbing Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb, infer.mn:547-553), the sequencing after stage 1 and before stage 3, and the honest open question on equality-vs-subtyping unification. Restate the item as building on Hβ.own.region-is-a-handle, with the region as a Ty-resident handle — then it is LESS code, not more.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena
  The emitted program's linear memory splits into two disjoint, separately managed address ranges — a durable IMAGE (escape-proven-live values, the persist/memcpy boundary) and per-activation SCRATCH (arena-allocated, reset to O(1) on a proven-escape-free decl's return) — replacing the single ever-growing 4GB bump heap with a working set bounded by whichever decl is currently active.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is near-exact and survives attack, but the design's eligibility predicate ("zero escapes") is constant-true on the wheel and would free every returned value, its gate (2) compares two handlers that emit byte-identical WAT, and its central novelty claim (!Alloc(region_R)) is false against the landed by-name negation.
  objection: THE ELIGIBILITY PREDICATE IS INVERTED — a use-after-free on every returned value in the wheel.

The design's gating condition (a): "a decl is arena-eligible only if check_escape proves zero escapes". Read check_escape (own.mn:201-236). It has no "escapes and is live" verdict. Its four arms are:
  - region_id == 0        -> resume()                       (global lifetime)
  - region_id == top_rid  -> re-tag to the PARENT region    (own.mn:219-222 — THE RETURN TRANSFER; the comment at :203 says "the caller owns it now")
  - region_on_stack(...)  -> resume()                       (outer live region)
  - else                  -> report(ERegionEscape(...))     (own.mn:231 — the DEAD-region escape)

An escape is an ERROR REPORT, not a routing fact. So in any correct program escapes are zero BY CONSTRUCTION — PLAN §7 confirms it landed that way: "regions live: fn body = region, return = TRANSFER; Hylo-quiet on the wheel (0 escapes) — 82a7e42".

Therefore gate (a) is constant-true across the entire wheel. Every fn becomes arena-eligible. Every fn returning a freshly-allocated record gets $arena_ptr reset on return, freeing the record it just TRANSFERRED to its caller — corrupted on the caller's first read. And the design's headline split ("IMAGE = escape-proven-live values" vs "SCRATCH = escape-free decl") bipartitions on a predicate that is constant-FALSE in every well-typed program: nothing would ever route to the image except region-0 globals.

The fact the design needed is stated verbatim in the code it cites (own.mn:207-210): "everything STILL TAGGED WITH THE DYING REGION is provably transient." That is a PER-VALUE tag read at exit, not a per-decl gate. The design cites Hβ.own.region-return-transfer in its own novelty section and then reads the wrong predicate — and PLAN's named-residue index says the sound half is unbuilt: "Hβ.own.region-return-transfer (LANDED at the check; the caller-side re-tag under region polymorphism is the arena increment)". Stage 2 supplying region-polymorphic params does not convert "zero escapes" into "nothing transfers out"; the design never names the result-region-as-parameter form (own.mn:203) as what makes reset-on-return sound.

WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE (the artifact_state is unusually honest): $arena_ptr IS dead — grep finds only the wasm.mn:1774 declaration emit plus comments at :129/:1769. $alloc IS hard-coded to $heap_ptr, wasm.mn:1782 exactly. memory 65536 65536 shared at wasm.mn:1762, $arena_ptr at :1774 — both exact. All five region_tracker installs (pipeline.mn:73,152,194,238,264) — exact. Emit reads no region fact: lower.mn has 0 hits for region_index/check_escape/current_region; wasm.mn has 1, a comment at :197. lower.mn's "region" IS the captured-evidence-region (:336,:440,:449,:505,:511) — the naming-collision flag is correct and worth keeping. a44baa55 IS "own: O(1) region tracker — self-compile 749s to 490s", so the refusal to conflate the compiler's analysis speed with the emitted program's memory is honest. emit_one_fn_to_string is at wasm.mn:1827. classify_fixpoint is landed (infer.mn:3892), so guard (b) is well-sourced and genuinely surfaces a dependency PLAN's gloss omits. The design's own admission ("TODAY THIS IS NOT YET TRUE: region_index is a private hashmap invisible to lower/emit... stating it as already-unified would be the 'ultimate form reachable now' drift") is correct and is the strongest thing in the document.
  fixes    : 1. ELIGIBILITY (the correctness kill). Gate on the PER-VALUE region tag at exit — own.mn:207-210's own words, "everything still tagged with the dying region is provably transient" — never on "zero escapes", which own.mn:201-236 makes constant-true in every correct program (PLAN §7, "0 escapes" on the wheel). The reset must free only dying-region tenants; values re-tagged to the parent (own.mn:219-222) must survive. The alternative sound form is the result-region-as-parameter the code already names (own.mn:203): the callee allocates the result into the CALLER's region, so its own reset is safe by construction. Say which one, and say that the caller-side half is unbuilt (PLAN named-residue: "Hβ.own.region-return-transfer (LANDED at the check; the caller-side re-tag under region polymorphism is the arena increment)").

2. GATE (2) IS UNSATISFIABLE — rewrite it. wasm.mn:116 (bump), :139 (arena), :170 (cas) emit byte-identical WAT: `(local.set $<t> (call $alloc (i32.const <n>)))`. Swapping handlers produces zero bytes of difference, so "bounded under emit_memory_arena vs unbounded under emit_memory_bump on the identical source" compares a module against itself. The gate must compare a per-decl-arena BUILD against the current bump build. This also contradicts the design's own artifact_state: if $arena_ptr is never written (true), emit_memory_arena cannot bound anything.

3. DELETE, DON'T ACTIVATE (Law 2). emit_memory_arena and emit_memory_atomic_cas are DEAD CLONES, not a dormant swap surface — three handlers, one emission. PLAN's "Activates the dormant emit_memory_arena swap (wasm.mn:139)" is wrong and should be corrected with this landing. The block comment at wasm.mn:127-138 ("Here the emit is identical to bump except for the pointer name") is STALE and contradicted by the arm's own inline comment ("Same strategy-invariant call site"). Per Law 2 the clones are decoration to delete, not scaffolding to revive.

4. NAME THE ROUTING DEPENDENCY. Snapshot/restore of $arena_ptr is a no-op while every alloc is `(call $alloc)` against a body that bumps $heap_ptr (wasm.mn:1782). The routing is Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap, named in the file the design cites (wasm.mn:110-117) and seam-gated: "a body-emitting `~>` perform trips the same evidence seam today". Add it to SEQUENCING as the blocking dependency, and state that `emit_alloc(Int, String)` (wasm.mn:101) carries no region argument — per-site routing needs an effect-signature change.

5. DROP OR RE-ROOT THE !Alloc(region) NOVELTY CLAIM. effects.mn:351-355: `EParameterized(s, _) => s` — the negation membership discards the argument, and name_in_forbidden (effects.mn:371-373) compares eff_name_str only. So `!Alloc(region_R)` forbids EVERY Alloc(...) — all allocation, every region — not "no code touches R's pointer". Delete the analogy "exactly as !Thread is proven today": !Thread is a BARE name, which is precisely why by-name works for it; parameterized negation is where it breaks. This silently depends on Hβ.effects.parameterized-negation-instance, named as the open refinement in the comment DIRECTLY ABOVE eff_name_str (effects.mn:344) and listed open in PLAN §4③ and §11 column 2. State the blast radius the design omits: 284 `with ... Alloc` sites and 49 `!Alloc` sites across src/ + lib/.

6. GATE (1) MUST BUILD THE FOLD, NOT EXTEND IT. "checked by the same mechanism ALREADY proving the other regions don't overlap" — no such mechanism exists. Hβ.emit.image-map-fold is a named peer (PLAN Band D, "new 2026-07-10") with exactly one code reference: wasm.mn:1269, a comment pointing AT the peer. The static layout is hard-coded wat_emit string literals with magic constants (1048576 at :1768/:1774; 0x800/0x1000/0x40 in comments at :1785-1795). Remove "already"; sequence image-map-fold as a prerequisite build.

7. NAME PLAN'S OWN STATED GATE. PLAN.md:750: "Per-decl arena (Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena, GATED ON Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb)". The design claims "This IS Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena by name — the existing peer" and omits the peer's own gate. wasm.mn:134 restates it ("a future refinement once a `~>` install that absorbs Alloc opens a region"); infer.mn:552 carries it too.

8. DISCLOSE BLAST RADIUS AND RECONCILE PLAN. A per-fn arena prologue/epilogue plus a third region changes every fn body's emitted WAT: m2 != m3 in one generation -> TRANSITION, re-pin from m3 (Hβ.tools.march-transition-native). PLAN §5.O currently asserts "layer 3 is output-invariant (internal scratch strategy)" — false for an emit-changing prologue/epilogue. The design inherits that claim by naming itself the peer and never reconciles it. State the effect on the fixpoint and on the green board (71 micros, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5).

KEEP UNCHANGED: the axis distinction from a44baa55; the lower.mn evidence-region naming-collision flag; the MultiShot guard (b), which is well-sourced (classify_fixpoint, infer.mn:3892) and surfaces a real dependency PLAN's gloss omits; the refusal to claim region_index is already graph-unified; and the framing of "the region drop IS the arena reset" as this stage's design target rather than a landed fact.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena
  The emitted program's linear memory splits into two disjoint, separately managed address ranges — a durable IMAGE (escape-proven-live values, the persist/memcpy boundary) and per-activation SCRATCH (arena-allocated, reset to O(1) on a proven-escape-free decl's return) — replacing the single ever-growing 4GB bump heap with a working set bounded by whichever decl is currently active.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is accurate line-for-line, but the design's central mechanism (snapshot $arena_ptr at decl entry, restore at "every exit path — the ordinary tail return AND any early suspend unwind") targets two exit paths that do not exist in Mentl's emitted code: a suspension does not unwind (it raises a flag and poisons the dataflow with a dummy, wasm.mn:3099-3149), and a tail exit is `return_call_indirect`, which replaces the frame and has no epilogue slot (wasm.mn:955, 1009, 3341).
  objection: THERE IS NO SUSPEND UNWIND TO HOOK, AND THE GUARD BUILT ON IT CHECKS THE WRONG FRAME.

The design: "at every exit path — the ordinary tail return AND any early suspend unwind, per Hβ.continuations.multishot-reexecution-driver's own 'suspend unwinds... so the arm runs outside the body's stack' (a suspend IS an early exit from this frame too) — restore $arena_ptr from that local."

The artifact says the opposite. `LYield` (wasm.mn:3099-3149) does NOT return. It parks k in `$yield_k`, sets `$yield_op`, raises `$yield_flag`, and evaluates to a width-honest dummy (`(f64.const 0)`/`(i32.const 0)` per `repr_of`). The adjacent `LKExtend` arm states the law outright (wasm.mn:3154-3158): "evaluate to the frame's width-honest dummy with $yield_flag AND $yield_op UNTOUCHED — THE UNWIND CONTINUES AS PURE DATAFLOW THROUGH THE SAME VALUE POSITION THE FAST PATH USES ... A RETURN HERE WOULD SKIP AN INSTALL'S DRIVER BRACKET." Mentl's suspension is a flag plus poisoned dataflow: every enclosing frame keeps executing normally on the dummy and returns through its ORDINARY return path, out to the driver's redrive poll (`$op___redrive_<hname>`, wasm.mn:913-930), with an unhandled-yield trap backstop at `_start` (wasm.mn:2595-2597).

Three consequences, each fatal as written:

(1) NO HOOK EXISTS. There is no early exit. A frame cannot distinguish "returning normally" from "unwinding a suspension" without reading `$yield_flag`. The design's epilogue fires identically on both.

(2) THE GUARD CHECKS THE WRONG FRAME. Guard (b) — "no MultiShot-cardinality continuation reifies a captured pointer into this activation's scratch (the already-landed resume-discipline classifier supplies this per-op)" — is per-OP, at a handler ARM. The frames that corrupt are the ones a yield merely CROSSES: they have no op, no arm, are escape-free, and pass (a) and (b) trivially. `$__k_extend` (wasm.mn:1006, 3159) composes each crossed frame's `frame_k` onto `$yield_k` — "the chain rebuilds itself lazily, one fresh record per crossed suspension" (wasm.mn:951-952) — capturing that frame's live state. Whether a decl is crossed is a property of the DYNAMIC call stack at suspend time; no per-op classifier holds it. Under the design, an innocent arena-eligible frame resets `$arena_ptr` on its way out while `$yield_k` holds a composed k pointing into the scratch it just freed. That is the exact silent-wrong class §11 column 2 exists to eliminate.

(3) THE DESIGN'S OWN ANSWER IS TWO PARAGRAPHS UP, UNUSED. The sound eligibility fact is transitive — "can a MultiShot yield occur ANYWHERE beneath me?" — which is precisely what an effect ROW computes, i.e. the design's own `Alloc(region)` arm-4 derivation. Guard (b) is a name-keyed cross-reference to a side-ledger where the row already connects the fact transitively: a Carried-Truth violation inside the design's own gating condition, in an item that invokes Law 1 elsewhere.

CITED AUTHORITY IS DEAD TWICE OVER. PLAN.md:816 states plainly: "`Hβ.continuations.multishot-reexecution-driver` is SUPERSEDED (the 2026-07-11 pivot — re-execution restarts, the felt spec demands resume)". The quoted "suspend unwinds" sentence belongs to that entry's NATIVE-cont description, which the same line marks: "native conts are BLOCKED under WASI `_start` ... `--invoke` works, `_start` (every real program) does not, and no flag avoids it." The design's exit-path authority is a superseded peer quoting a mechanism blocked for every real program. The LANDED mechanism is `Hβ.lower.continuation-reification-codegen`, which behaves oppositely.

TAIL CALLS (independent, also fatal). wasm.mn:3341 emits `if tail { "(return_call_indirect (type " }`, because (wasm.mn:3337-3339) "Without it every evidence-threading recursion burned a frame per step." wasm.mn:955: "a clear flag feeds r to frame_k as return_call_indirect — CONSTANT STACK AT ANY COMPOSITION DEPTH." A `return_call_indirect` replaces the frame; no instruction follows it. So "the ordinary tail return" is not hookable. Either the epilogue is skipped on tail exits — the arena never resets on the tail-resumptive path PLAN §6 puts at ~85% of dispatch, and the headline benefit evaporates exactly where it was promised — or tail calls demote to `call`+epilogue+`return`, breaking wasm.mn:955's constant-stack invariant. The design names neither horn.

GATE 2 IS UNREACHABLE, ITS BLOCKER UNNAMED. `emit_memory_arena` (wasm.mn:139-150) and `emit_memory_bump` (wasm.mn:117-127) emit BYTE-IDENTICAL WAT — both `(local.set $<target> (call $alloc (i32.const <size>)))`. The swap is a no-op distinction today. wasm.mn:107-116 names the blocker the design never mentions: "The allocation call site is strategy-INVARIANT ... Per-strategy $alloc BODIES chosen by the installed handler are the seam-gated follow-up `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` (a body-emitting `~>` perform trips the same evidence seam today); until it closes the body is bump, emitted directly." Gate 2 ("bounded under emit_memory_arena where it grows unbounded under emit_memory_bump on the identical source") cannot fail-then-pass on this item's landing, and cannot pass at all until that peer closes on the ev_perform_entry seam. The design's own SEQUENCING concedes this ("Unblocks stage 4: the codegen swap...") while still claiming the gate — an internal contradiction, and by the design's own standard ("a hidden gap is drift; a NAMED positive-form peer is the ultimate form") an unnamed dependency is drift.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DISPROVED ME: I expected a factual refutation and did not get one. Every cited line is exact — 1762/1768/1774/1782/1827-1833, own.mn:138, pipeline.mn:73,152,194,238,264. `$arena_ptr` really is dead scaffolding (only wasm.mn:129 comment + the 1774 declaration). `region_index` really is unread by lower/emit (wasm.mn:197 is a comment naming it as the handle-keyed TEMPLATE for the str_hash waypoint shape, not a read). The a44baa55 distinction is correct and is a trap most readers would fall into. I also credit the novelty section's self-refutation — "TODAY THIS IS NOT YET TRUE: region_index is a private hashmap invisible to lower/emit ... stating it as already-unified would be the 'ultimate form reachable now' drift" — as exactly the discipline CLAUDE.md demands and rare in practice. The design is refuted on control-flow mechanism, not on honesty or grounding.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the entry/exit prologue-epilogue mechanism. It is the C call-stack shape ("LIFO-nested like a call stack" is the design's own phrase) and PLAN band N already rejects it BY NAME: `Hβ.native.frame-rep-from-cardinality-trail` — "OneShot activations are trail-reclaimed image frames (graph.mn's graph_rollback is the activation spine, NOT %rsp which demotes to a bounded CPU-mandated trap scratch)". The design never mentions band N's keystone 1 and reinvents the shape it rejects. Reconcile with band N or state why it diverges. Note honestly: today's trail (graph.mn:57,73-79,115 — `graph_push_checkpoint` captures `trail_len`, `graph_rollback` pops) brackets the COMPILER'S OWN graph writes, not the emitted program's activations, so band N's spine is the target form, not a landed one.

2. THE RESET SITE IS THE DRIVER BRACKET, NOT THE FN BOUNDARY. The emitted code already has exactly one place that knows a suspension is in flight and brackets it: the redrive loop (`$op___redrive_<hname>`, wasm.mn:913-930) polling `$yield_flag`. That is the substrate's existing activation bracket — reset there, where the fact is read live, instead of emitting a second bracket into every fn prologue that cannot see `$yield_flag` and must re-derive its own liveness. Carried-Truth: follow the bracket that exists.

3. DELETE GUARD (b); THE ROW SUBSUMES IT. `Alloc(region)` riding `Sample(rate)`'s parameterization is the design's best idea and is stronger than the design realizes: the transitive "can a MultiShot yield occur beneath me?" question IS a row computation, exactly as `!Thread`/`!Alloc` are proven transitively today. A per-op classifier cross-reference cannot answer a dynamic-call-stack question and is a side-ledger consult where an edge already connects the fact. Arena-eligibility should read `!Yield(region_R)` (or the `Alloc(region)` dual) off the row, transitively — one mechanism, no guard.

4. NAME THE TWO UNNAMED DEPENDENCIES. `Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap` (wasm.mn:113, 1781) is the actual blocker for any observable arena behaviour, and it is gated on the ev_perform_entry evidence seam. Both belong in INTERCONNECTION and SEQUENCING. Until the body-swap closes, `emit_memory_arena` is byte-identical to `emit_memory_bump` and there is no swap to activate — PLAN §5.O layer 3's "Activates the dormant emit_memory_arena swap (wasm.mn:139)" is itself doc-truth debt worth correcting: the handler is not dormant, it is a no-op peer.

5. REASSIGN GATE 2 TO STAGE 4 AND GIVE THIS ITEM A GATE IT CAN FAIL. Gate 1 (the layout fold gains a scratch region with base == previous region's limit, extending Hβ.emit.image-map-fold) is sound and mechanically checkable — keep it. A real gate for THIS item: `region_index` becomes a graph-carried field such that a lower-side or emit-side read of a decl's escape verdict returns the same value infer computed, asserted on a fixture where the two would diverge if the fact were re-derived rather than read. That fails today (nothing outside own.mn can read it) and passes only on this item's landing. Gate 3 (per-slab persist) depends on Band B's persist path and belongs with it.

6. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS, WHICH THE DESIGN OMITS ENTIRELY. Any change to the fn prologue/epilogue or to `$alloc`'s body changes emitted bytes for every fn in the wheel — a TRANSITION under tools/march.sh (m2 != m3, re-pin from m3 after the m4 leg rules TRANSITION vs BROKEN), not a Law-7 no-op. PLAN §5.O claims layer 3 is "output-invariant (internal scratch strategy)"; that is false for any mechanism that emits prologue/epilogue instructions. The design says nothing about m2 == m3, the 71 micros, frontier 47/0, or crown 5/5. Silence on the fixpoint in an item that rewrites every function's frame is the omission a reviewer should refuse first.

7. KEEP, with credit: the artifact_state (accurate line-for-line), the a44baa55 distinction (compiler's own scan speed vs the compiled program's runtime memory — a real and easily-conflated axis), the lower.mn "region" naming-collision flag (correct: wasm.mn:197's mention is a comment, not a read), and the novelty section's refusal to claim the region_index unification as landed.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.emit.per-decl-alloc-dispatch
  Per-decl, compile-time-chosen allocator dispatch — each function body compiles its own emit_alloc performs to EITHER $alloc (bump, permanent) or a new $alloc_arena (arena, reset-on-return), decided once per declaration from stage 3's proven eligibility fact, never a runtime branch — the precise mechanism PLAN's phrase 'activate the dormant emit_memory_arena swap' requires but does not itself specify.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's total dependency on Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap is disproved by the artifact ($arena_ptr's global is already emitted by a plain wat_emit at wasm.mn:1774 and ships dead at m2.wat:56, so $alloc_arena's body needs no seam fix), and its per-DECL granularity is unsound against own.mn:201-243's own return-transfer semantics, which make arena-reset safety a per-VALUE fact the graph already holds and the design discards.
  objection: PER-DECL GRANULARITY EMITS A USE-AFTER-RESET — a silent-wrong class, in the one cluster item that changes emitted bytes.

own.mn:201-243 (check_escape) defines the arena-reset safety condition, in the artifact's own words at own.mn:211-216: "OWN region -> THE RETURN TRANSFER ... the value leaves through the return, so it re-tags into the PARENT region — the caller owns it now. This is what makes the region drop safe as the layer-3 arena reset: everything still tagged with the dying region is provably transient." The transfer arm (own.mn:222-227) re-tags the returned value to the parent via region_index_add. So the reset reclaims exactly the values STILL tagged with the dying region; the returned value is, by construction, not among them.

The design routes every emit_alloc in an eligible decl to $alloc_arena — "decided once per declaration," "each function body compiles its own emit_alloc performs to EITHER $alloc ... or a new $alloc_arena." Then the returned/transferred record IS in the arena and dies at the epilogue reset. Use-after-reset.

The design's OWN named example is the counterexample. It cites "own.mn's own helpers the concrete example" of "most decls escape-free scratch-builders." own.mn:265-271 region_bucket_scan is precisely that shape — a bucket walk with no escape — and it allocates and returns Some(tag). Under per-decl dispatch the Some record is reclaimed before the caller reads it. own.mn:249-254 region_index_add (`with Memory + Alloc`) returns a heap list; own.mn:245 lookup_tag returns Some(tag)/None. Of the helpers named, only the scalar-returning ones (join_field_regions -> Int, region_on_stack -> Bool) are eligible under any sound reading. So the design is unsound at its stated granularity, or — once "eligible" is tightened to "no allocation reaches the return" — it excludes every heap-returning decl and is close to vacuous on the wheel.

This is a Carried-Truth violation in the design itself. The graph ALREADY holds the fact per value: own.mn:168 region_index maps value_handle -> (install_span, region_id), and check_escape's transfer arm keeps it live and correct at the return boundary. The design re-derives a coarser per-DECL boolean from that live per-value tag and dispatches on the summary — "is this fact computed, copied, snapshotted, or re-derived anywhere it could be read live?" Yes, and it is the bug.

Per-VALUE dispatch keeps every property the design claims and loses none: each emit_alloc site emits a fixed fn name chosen at compile time from THAT value's tag (zero runtime branch, zero per-call overhead, identical "the proof becomes the dispatch" framing), it is SOUND (the transferred record emits `call $alloc` and survives the reset; the scratch emits `call $alloc_arena` and is reclaimed), and it reaps the arena on exactly the heap-returning scratch-builders per-decl must exclude wholesale. Per-decl buys nothing and costs soundness plus reach.

The real blocker the design should have named is one line above the one it cites: emit_alloc(Int, String) -> () at wasm.mn:101 carries size and target_local but NOT the value handle the region tag is keyed by. THAT discard is the Carried-Truth violation already in the artifact, and threading the handle (or reading the region live off the LowIR node the emit already holds) is this item's actual content. Per-decl is the patch that avoids the restructure.

SUPPORTING REFUTATION (independently fatal, mechanically airtight): the claimed dependency does not exist. The design states "Depends entirely on the existing, separately tracked Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap for the evidence-seam fix that lets $alloc_arena's body exist at all." False. wasm.mn:1782 emits $alloc's body with a plain wat_emit inside emit_memory_decl — no handler, no perform, no seam (the comment at wasm.mn:1779-1781 says exactly why: it is emitted directly to AVOID the seam). Eight lines earlier, wasm.mn:1774 emits `(global $arena_ptr (mut i32) (i32.const 1048576))` the same way, unconditionally — and I confirmed it ships: .build/m2cache/m2.wat:56 carries it, with `grep -c arena_ptr` = 1 across 481,742 lines, i.e. the declaration and zero readers. A `wat_emit("  (func $alloc_arena ...")` at wasm.mn:1783 is the same move as its neighbour, already proven in the pinned boot's output. The body-swap peer asks a different question — whether the INSTALLED HANDLER chooses $alloc's body — and the design's two-separately-named-fns + call-site-name-choice architecture routes around that question by construction. The design invents the dependency and then uses it to justify its sequencing ("Last in the arc," "fully blocked").

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE THE DESIGN (it survived): gate (3)'s TRANSITION reasoning is correct and is more honest than PLAN itself. boot has no dispatch, so m2 (wheel compiled by boot) carries no $alloc_arena calls; m2 DOES contain the dispatch logic as source, so m2's emit of the wheel (m3) carries them; m3 == m4. m2 != m3, TRANSITION, re-pin from m3 — exactly as the design says, and exactly what Hβ.tools.march-transition-native arbitrates. This contradicts PLAN §5.O's own self-hosting line, "layer 3 is output-invariant (internal scratch strategy)," which is wrong for the emit half (or at best ambiguous between "emitted bytes unchanged" and "compiled program's behaviour unchanged"). The design is right and PLAN needs the correction. I also could not refute the artifact-state claims: emit_memory_arena is at wasm.mn:139-150, installed nowhere (`grep -rn "~> emit_memory" src/ lib/` returns only bump), and I diffed the arms programmatically rather than by eye — bump's wat_emit payload == arena's == atomic_cas's, all three `(call $alloc (i32.const ...))`, True on both comparisons.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the fabricated dependency. The design must NOT claim blockage on Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap. Evidence to cite instead: wasm.mn:1774 emits $arena_ptr's global with a plain wat_emit and it ships dead at .build/m2cache/m2.wat:56 (1 occurrence in 481,742 lines); wasm.mn:1782 emits $alloc's body the same way, and wasm.mn:1779-1781 says it is direct precisely to avoid the seam. $alloc_arena's body is a sibling wat_emit at wasm.mn:1783. Consequence: this item is NOT "last in the arc" and NOT gated on the seam peer — it is gated only on stage 3's eligibility fact. The sequencing paragraph and the INTERCONNECTION paragraph both need rewriting. The body-swap peer stays named for what it actually is: whether the INSTALLED HANDLER chooses $alloc's body — a question this design routes around rather than consumes.

2. GRANULARITY: per-DECL becomes per-ALLOCATION-SITE, read live. The eligibility fact is not a per-decl boolean; own.mn:168's region_index already maps value_handle -> (install_span, region_id), and check_escape's transfer arm (own.mn:222-227) re-tags the returned value to the parent before the region exits. The emit reads THAT tag per allocation: region_id == this fn's region -> `call $alloc_arena` (reclaimed at the epilogue reset); parent/outer/0 -> `call $alloc` (survives). Still one compile-time choice per site, still zero runtime branch, still "the proof becomes the dispatch" — but sound, and it reaps the arena on heap-returning scratch-builders (own.mn:265-271 region_bucket_scan) that per-decl must exclude entirely. The item's NAME is wrong for the right idea: retire Hβ.emit.per-decl-alloc-dispatch in favour of Hβ.emit.alloc-region-from-live-tag (or equivalent). PLAN §5.O layer 3's "each decl's transient scratch" phrasing is the source of the error — "transient scratch" is a SUBSET of a decl's allocations, never all of them.

3. THE ACTUAL RESTRUCTURE THIS ITEM IS: emit_alloc(Int, String) -> () at wasm.mn:101 discards the value handle that region_index is keyed by. Thread the handle into the effect signature, or read the region off the LowIR node the emit already holds (the latter is preferable — Carried-Truth: do not pass what an edge already connects). This is the Carried-Truth fix; the per-decl handler-swap is the patch that exists to avoid it. Note this also makes the two handlers collapse: once the arm reads the tag, ONE arm emits either name and emit_memory_bump/emit_memory_arena stop being two handlers at all — the fix is LESS code, which is the tell that it is the right one.

4. NOVELTY VERDICT is dishonest by omission and must name the real comparand. It refutes a soft target (bumpalo / Zig std.mem.Allocator / C arenas thread an allocator VALUE — true, and Mentl genuinely does not). The closest prior art is per-allocation-site escape analysis driving stack-vs-heap: Go (shipping since ~1.0; `-gcflags=-m` prints "escapes to heap" per SITE) and HotSpot escape analysis with scalar replacement. That is a compile-time, per-site choice from a proof — the design's exact claimed novelty, ~15 years old, at FINER granularity than the proposal. Against it, per-decl is strictly weaker. Mentl's real structural advantage is that the region tag is a LIVE GRAPH EDGE maintained by inference (own.mn:168 + the check_escape transfer), not a separate analysis pass a backend re-runs — but that advantage is only cashed by reading the per-value tag. Coarsened to per-decl, Mentl lands BELOW the borrowed shape the verdict declines to name. Also drop the "C-FFI-style marshaling" label: passing a &Bump is not FFI marshaling, and PLAN §11's GPU rejection was about how a value CROSSES to a device. Borrowing that rejection's authority for allocator selection is a slogan doing argumentative work.

5. DROP emit_memory_atomic_cas (wasm.mn:170-181) as precedent — verified mechanically as a THIRD decoy: its arm's wat_emit payload is byte-identical to bump's, emitting `(call $alloc (i32.const ...))` with no CAS and no atomics. The design's artifact-state paragraph correctly calls bump/arena "decoys ... not a working strategy divergence" and its novelty paragraph then cites a third decoy as "the same mechanism ... already demonstrates for thread-safety." Nothing in the file demonstrates the mechanism; that is the honest statement.

6. GATE: add the return-transfer crucible — the gate that would actually fail. Gates (1) and (2) as written pass while the design is unsound: (1) asserts $alloc_arena calls APPEAR, (2) measures a memory-growth curve. Neither catches use-after-reset, because a returned arena record reads CORRECTLY until allocation pressure overwrites it — nondeterministic, load-dependent, and the 71 micros are small enough to never clobber. The missing crucible: an arena-eligible decl that returns a heap record (region_bucket_scan's shape), whose caller forces N allocations after the return, then reads the record and asserts its contents. It fails loudly under per-decl dispatch and passes under per-value. Without it, refutation 2 ships green — and a compiler that miscompiles its own scratch reproduces to a WRONG fixpoint (PLAN §3 L7), which the byte-diff cannot see.

7. KEEP gate (3)'s TRANSITION reasoning verbatim; it is correct. Add the doc-truth correction it implies: PLAN §5.O's self-hosting line "layer 3 is output-invariant (internal scratch strategy)" is wrong (or ambiguous) for the emit half — layer 3 changes emitted bytes and is a TRANSITION, not output-invariant. That correction belongs in PLAN §5.O, and this item is what surfaces it.

8. CENSUS: the artifact-state names one bump install (pipeline.mn:69). There are three module-scope installs — pipeline.mn:69, 148, 190 — plus the per-fn local at wasm.mn:1832. The design touches only wasm.mn:1832, so the three are unaffected, but the incomplete inventory is exactly what the Investigation Discipline forbids, and the reader cannot tell from the design whether module-scope allocs (the `$__init_lets` / fold-helper allocs the wasm.mn:1822-1824 comment names) were considered for arena treatment. State explicitly that module-scope allocation stays bump and why (its lifetime is the program's).
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.emit.per-decl-alloc-dispatch
  Per-decl, compile-time-chosen allocator dispatch — each function body compiles its own emit_alloc performs to EITHER $alloc (bump, permanent) or a new $alloc_arena (arena, reset-on-return), decided once per declaration from stage 3's proven eligibility fact, never a runtime branch — the precise mechanism PLAN's phrase 'activate the dormant emit_memory_arena swap' requires but does not itself specify.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's "depends entirely on Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap" is false against the artifact ($alloc's body is a plain wat_emit at wasm.mn:1782, no perform, no seam — $alloc_arena's is line 1783), and the mechanism it *does* specify (a one-op EmitMemory handler swap) is structurally incapable of emitting the prologue/epilogue reset that is the entire payoff.
  objection: THE DESIGN INVENTED ITS OWN BLOCKER, THEN THE MECHANISM IT SPECIFIES CAN'T DO THE JOB.

R1 — THE DEPENDENCY IS FABRICATED (factual). The design: "Depends entirely on ... Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap for the evidence-seam fix that lets $alloc_arena's body exist at all — this item consumes that peer," and doubles down: "must not paper over by implying the swap is 'just a pipeline.mn line' — it is not, verified by direct WAT comparison."

The artifact says otherwise. $alloc's body is emitted at src/backends/wasm.mn:1782 as a plain `wat_emit("  (func $alloc (param $size i32) ...")` string literal inside `fn emit_memory_decl()` (wasm.mn:1745), called directly at wasm.mn:308. No perform. No handler. No evidence threading. `$arena_ptr` is emitted identically at wasm.mn:1774. Emitting $alloc_arena's body is ONE MORE wat_emit at line 1783 — it cannot trip ev_perform_entry because it performs nothing.

The seam-gated peer (wasm.mn:113-114) is about CHOOSING which single $alloc body to emit via an install. This design doesn't choose a body — it emits BOTH unconditionally and varies only the call-target NAME inside the arm, a string the arm already emits today (wasm.mn:120: `wat_emit(" (call $alloc (i32.const ")`). It routed around the seam and then declared itself blocked by it. That mis-sequences the whole cluster ("SEQUENCING: Last in the arc") behind a dependency the artifact says does not apply — "the deeper ideal is a follow-up" aimed at itself.

R2 — THE SPECIFIED DISPATCH CANNOT DELIVER THE PAYOFF (mechanism). `effect EmitMemory { emit_alloc(Int, String) -> () }` (wasm.mn:100-101) has exactly ONE op. A handler installed at emit_one_fn_to_string (wasm.mn:1827-1833) can vary only what is emitted AT AN ALLOC SITE. The snapshot/reset prologue/epilogue is a FRAME emission belonging to `fn emit_fn_body` (wasm.mn:1927), which emits `(func $`, params, result, locals — and never performs an EmitMemory op. No arm of a one-op effect reaches it.

So "install ~> emit_memory_arena_body (calling $alloc_arena, WITH THE PROLOGUE SNAPSHOT / EPILOGUE RESET wired around every exit path including suspend)" smuggles an entire second mechanism into a parenthetical — on an item whose stated purpose is "the precise mechanism PLAN's phrase requires but does not itself specify." It specifies the easy half. The call-target half ALONE is a strict no-op in benefit: allocating from $arena_ptr with no reset is a second monotonic bump heap. SEQUENCING claims "the 4GB working set actually collapsing ... is only true once this stage lands" — as written it collapses nothing.

The design's OWN GATE refutes it: leg (1) demands output "contain calls to $alloc_arena AND the snapshot/reset prologue/epilogue." The specified dispatch cannot emit the prologue/epilogue. The gate fails against the mechanism.

R3 — NOVELTY CITES A DECOY, NAMES THE WRONG TRADITION. (a) The verdict rests on "the same mechanism emit_memory_atomic_cas (wasm.mn:170-181) ALREADY DEMONSTRATES for thread-safety." Mechanically diffed all three arms (wasm.mn:116-125 / 139-150 / 170-181): the CAS arm is byte-identical to bump — same five wat_emit calls, same `(call $alloc`. It is a THIRD decoy. The design correctly called bump/arena "decoys ... not a working strategy divergence" in its artifact state, then cited the third decoy as a working demonstration two paragraphs later. Nothing is demonstrated.

(b) "C/C++ arena allocators (and Rust's bumpalo) select an allocator via an explicit VALUE" — true, and irrelevant: that was never this design's shape. The real prior art for "per-site allocation strategy chosen at compile time from a static escape fact, zero runtime branch, no allocator value threaded" is ESCAPE ANALYSIS — Go's compiler (`-gcflags=-m`, "does not escape" → stack alloc), HotSpot's escape analysis → scalar replacement, MLton. The design names none, so it never argues against the one tradition it resembles — and the resemblance is unflattering: Go decides PER ALLOCATION SITE; this decides PER DECLARATION, strictly coarser (one escaping alloc forces the whole body back to bump). Invoking PLAN §11's GPU "C-FFI marshaling" rejection is a mis-aimed authority citation: that rejection is about device-boundary serialization, not allocator parameters.

R4 — INVENTORY INCOMPLETE. Artifact state: emit_memory_bump installed "at pipeline.mn:69 module-scope and wasm.mn:1832 per-fn-locally." There are THREE module-scope installs: pipeline.mn:69, pipeline.mn:148 (compile_stdin), pipeline.mn:190. Doesn't break the per-decl dispatch site, but a design opening on a claimed-verified inventory missed two of three.

WHAT I TRIED THAT FAILED TO REFUTE (the design's real contribution, confirmed): all three EmitMemory handlers ARE byte-identical (diffed, not inferred); `$arena_ptr` occurs exactly twice repo-wide (wasm.mn:129 comment, wasm.mn:1774 emission) and is READ NOWHERE; `$alloc_arena` occurs nowhere; the pipeline swap IS a genuine no-op. The gate mechanics are real (tools/emit-diff.py exists; tools/march.sh:112-129 runs the m4 leg and arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN), and TRANSITION is the correct expectation for an emit change. The stage-3 dependency is grounded (`effect RegionTrack`, types.mn:986-992; `handler region_tracker`, own.mn:138). The artifact-state finding survives; the design built on top of it does not.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE THE FALSE DEPENDENCY, DON'T RENAME IT. Strike "Depends entirely on Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap ... lets $alloc_arena's body exist at all" and the "verified by direct WAT comparison" flourish attached to it. $alloc_arena's body ships as `wat_emit("  (func $alloc_arena ...")` at wasm.mn:1783, beside 1782, inside the same plain `emit_memory_decl()` (wasm.mn:1745) — no perform, no handler, no seam, no peer consumed. The seam peer governs choosing ONE body by install; this design emits BOTH and chooses the call-target name in the arm, which already works. SEQUENCING must lose "depends on the separately gated Hβ.emit.memory-strategy-body-swap" — this stage is gated on stage 3 alone.

2. THE EFFECT MUST GROW TO MIRROR ITS OWN ANALYSIS-SIDE SIBLING. `effect EmitMemory` (wasm.mn:100-101) carries one op and therefore cannot express region reclamation. It must carry the op set `RegionTrack` already carries at types.mn:987-988:

    effect EmitMemory {
      emit_alloc(Int, String) -> ()
      emit_region_enter() -> ()
      emit_region_exit() -> ()
    }

`emit_fn_body` (wasm.mn:1927) performs `emit_region_enter()` at the frame preamble and `emit_region_exit()` at every exit. `emit_memory_bump`'s two new arms emit nothing (bump has no region — byte-identical output for ineligible decls falls out by construction, satisfying gate leg 1's second half for free). `emit_memory_arena`'s arms emit the `$arena_ptr` snapshot and reset. NOW the per-decl `~>` install at emit_one_fn_to_string (wasm.mn:1827-1833) genuinely carries both halves, the eligibility fact is read ONCE at the install site and never again (Carried-Truth holds), and "the swap IS a handler choice" stops being a slogan. The emit-side effect was missing exactly the two ops its analysis-side sibling has — that is the design's actual content, and it is a better item than the one submitted.

3. REPLACE THE NOVELTY VERDICT WHOLESALE. Drop the emit_memory_atomic_cas citation (a third decoy, byte-identical at wasm.mn:170-181 — it demonstrates nothing) and drop the bumpalo/C-FFI framing (wrong tradition, mis-aimed §11 citation). Name the real borrowed form: ESCAPE ANALYSIS — Go (`-gcflags=-m`, "does not escape" → stack alloc), HotSpot scalar replacement, MLton. State the honest verdict: the SHAPE is the same (compile-time, per-site, zero runtime branch, no allocator value), and Go's is finer-grained. The native form is stronger for one reason and it must be argued, not sloganed: Mentl's fact is a DECLARED, PROVABLE obligation in the type system (RegionTrack / `Consume` / `!Alloc`, types.mn:986) that the developer can name, read via the cursor, and rely on — where Go's is a compiler-internal heuristic invisible at the surface and unstable across releases. That is why it is a `~>` install and not a flag. Keep as named scaffold-tier: nothing here.

4. NAME THE TWO HIDDEN GAPS POSITIVELY.
   - `Hβ.emit.per-alloc-site-region-dispatch` — per-decl is coarser than Go's per-site; one escaping alloc forces the whole body to bump. The design forecloses the finer form silently. Name it or justify per-decl against the landed region granularity (PLAN §7, 82a7e42: fn body = region, return = TRANSFER).
   - `Hβ.emit.arena-suspend-transfer` — "every exit path INCLUDING SUSPEND" is not merely unspecified, it is the one exit where reset is WRONG. A continuation record allocated in the arena must OUTLIVE the frame (§5.U: persist = memcpy of that record; the dormant k points at it). Resetting `$arena_ptr` on suspend frees it. That is a silent-wrong-value class in the middle of PLAN §11 column 2's "no known silent-wrong class" bar. It needs a named peer and a crucible, not a parenthetical.

5. FIX THE INVENTORY. Three module-scope installs of emit_memory_bump: pipeline.mn:69, pipeline.mn:148, pipeline.mn:190 — not one.

6. KEEP UNCHANGED: the artifact-state core (three byte-identical handlers; $arena_ptr emitted at wasm.mn:1774 and read nowhere; the pipeline swap is a no-op), the gate's TRANSITION expectation (tools/march.sh:112-129 arbitrates m3==m4 → re-pin from m3), and the stage-3 eligibility dependency. Once correction 2 lands, gate leg 1 becomes a real assertion that fails without the design — as written, it fails WITH it.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr
  This peer is DONE — PExpr was dissolved before this cluster started; PLAN.md band F still lists it as open, chained into smt-handler-swap, and must be corrected.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the peer's name is `predicate-is-expr` ("a predicate IS an expr"), and only the OPERAND half landed; `type Predicate` (types.mn:1102-1114) is still a second IR whose boolean SPINE is re-derived at parser.mn:2475-2489 from an AST the graph already minted, so the item deletes an OPEN peer and ships a false novelty claim.
  objection: parser.mn:2475-2489 `node_to_predicate` walks an expression tree that `parse_expr` ALREADY minted into the graph (parser.mn:2456-2458) and re-derives a parallel boolean spine from it: `NExpr(BinOpExpr(BAnd, l, r)) => PAnd(node_to_predicate(l, span), node_to_predicate(r, span))`, `BOr => POr`, `UnaryOpExpr(UNot, inner) => PNot`, `LitBool(true) => PTrue`, `BinOpExpr(BEq/BLt/BLe, l, r) => compare_pred(...)`. `node_to_pexpr` was deleted; `node_to_predicate` is its surviving sibling doing the same re-derivation one altitude up. Its OWN catch-all refutes it: `_ => PBoolNode(node_handle(node))` (parser.mn:2489) — any bool expr IS its graph node, by handle. `where even(self)` is already one handle today; `where a && b` chose to copy. `PBoolNode(root_handle)` is the entire predicate, constructible with today's substrate.

The copy is lossy and demonstrably drifts — three receipts:
(1) The `BNe` hole. The graph holds `BinOpExpr(BNe, l, r)` (types.mn:531), renders it (types.mn:575 `BNe => "!="`), and the parser lexes `!=` to it (parser.mn:249). `node_to_predicate` has NO BNe arm, so `where self != 0` falls to PBoolNode and `predicate_decide`'s `PBoolNode(_) => None` (verify.mn:68) FABRICATES undecidability for a comparison trivially decidable on two constants — while `self == 0` decides (verify.mn:63,74-83). The decidable fragment's shape is an accident of which arms got transcribed. That is Law 1 verbatim: the graph proved it; the consumer discarded it.
(2) `show_cmp_op`'s `_ => "?"` (types.mn:2071-2076) — a surrender-fallback fabricating an operator rendering, guarding the hole the spine created. CLAUDE.md's red-flag table: delete, don't wrap.
(3) BGt/BGe normalize (`compare_pred(BLt, r, l)`, parser.mn:2479-2480): source `self > 0` becomes predicate `0 < self`, and `show_predicate` (types.mn:2058-2069) renders the normalized copy — the Why engine reports what the developer did not write. Arm-8 Reason fidelity lost exactly where the copy drifted from the node.

This lands on the item's own claimed differentiator. Its novelty verdict says "LH and F* generate a separate VC-IR from the source AST, a translation kept in sync with the type-checker by convention... Mentl's dissolution of PExpr means the refinement predicate has no second IR." parser.mn:2475 IS that translation, `type Predicate` IS that second IR, and the missing BNe arm IS the sync failing by convention — the precise failure mode the item names in LH/F* and claims Mentl escaped. Mentl escaped it at the leaf and reproduced it at the spine. The claim is reachable, not true; it becomes true at the dissolution, which is the peer's payoff and the reason to keep it open.

The sharp edge, named honestly because it is the strongest counter to my own objection: collapsing PCompare into PBoolNode is NOT free today — `predicate_decide` returns None for PBoolNode (verify.mn:68), so the naive collapse would lose the decidable arm. That is not a reason to keep the spine; it is the proof that the dissolution and R3 are ONE job. `predicate_decide` must become a live const-fold over Bool-typed expr nodes — which is the e-graph's own const-fold (`Hβ.egraph.const-fold-minted-node-full-edges`, PLAN §5.R band G). R3 has to build that fold regardless. Building R3 on the spine transcribes arithmetic BinOps into PCompare-shaped arms a second time and entrenches the copy; the item's own INTERCONNECTION section half-sees this when it plans "recursive substitution through field/call/binop trees" inside `subst_self` — that is verify.mn re-implementing expression traversal, the second IR GROWING.

What the item gets right, verified line by line: the operand half IS landed and IS load-bearing. types.mn:1102-1114 declares Predicate with Int handles exactly as claimed; types.mn:1116-1119 carries the dissolution note verbatim; `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) and `subst_self_operand` (verify.mn:114-117) both chase `graph_node_body`; 531c0064 (2026-06-22) does predate 730dfe8 (2026-07-14). This is a careful misread of the peer's scope, not a careless one — it read the PLAN's parenthetical gloss "(dissolve PExpr)" and never checked the peer's definition at its detail home, docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md:185, which has two clauses: "Dissolve node_to_pexpr" (landed) AND "A refinement predicate becomes a refined Expr; Verify reads the Expr directly" (open).
  fixes    : 1. DO NOT delete `Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr` from PLAN.md:848. It is OPEN. The chain into `Hβ.verify.smt-handler-swap` is CORRECT and must stay: `predicate_to_smt`/`body_to_smt` (verify.mn:329, 367) walk the spine today, so the SMT handler inherits the copy — the roadmap's dependency edges (post-first-light-roadmap.md:88, :183) were right.

2. Correct band F to record the landed half and name the open half positively, replacing "(dissolve PExpr)": `Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr` (operand half LANDED 531c0064; the SPINE remains — PAnd/POr/PNot/PTrue/PCompare at types.mn:1102-1114 re-derive BinOpExpr/UnaryOpExpr/LitBool the graph already holds, built by node_to_predicate at parser.mn:2475. Ultimate form: Predicate = the handle of one Bool-typed Expr node — the ADT's own `_ => PBoolNode(node_handle(node))` catch-all generalized; predicate_decide = a live const-fold over the graph, converging with `Hβ.egraph.const-fold-minted-node-full-edges`) → `Hβ.verify.smt-handler-swap`.

3. Re-sequence: dissolve the spine WITH R3, not after it, and not "already done." R3's decidable arithmetic fragment over a live Bool-expr handle is one const-fold; over the spine it is a second transcription of the BinOp ADT. One job — the fix is LESS code, per Law 1. The `BNe` hole (decidable `==`, undecidable `!=`, for no reason but a missing arm) is the fragment's shape being dictated by the copy today; it dissolves for free when the predicate is a handle.

4. Fix the novelty verdict — it is not "N/A, nothing to audit." The "LH/F* keep two predicate IRs, Mentl has one" claim is FALSE today (parser.mn:2475 is the translation; the missing BNe arm is the sync failing by convention) and must not ship as a differentiator until the spine is gone. Stating it becomes honest at the dissolution; that is the reason to keep the peer, not to close it.

5. Delete `show_cmp_op`'s `_ => "?"` (types.mn:2071-2076) as part of the dissolution — a surrender-fallback fabricating a rendering for a BinOp the graph renders correctly at types.mn:575. Deleted, never wrapped (Law 2). `show_pred_operand`'s `_ => "<expr>"` (types.mn:2079-2088) is the same fabrication one level down: `self * 2 <= 100` renders as `<expr> <= 100` while the graph holds the whole tree.

6. GATE: the item's gate passes vacuously and must be replaced, not waived. The micros gate the LEAF half, which is green and shipping; nothing in the battery fails on the spine copy. The gate that would actually fail: a micro asserting `type X = Int where self != 0` DECIDES (rejects `let bad: X = 0` with E_RefinementRejected on stderr, exactly as tests/micros/mn-refine-reject.mn does for `<=`). It fails today and passes at the dissolution.

7. Factual staleness: "66/66 micro battery" is the 2026-07-12 M3-cut count (PLAN §7 landing ledger: "k gates enter baseline (52→66)"). The board is 71 micros (PLAN §7 header).

8. If the peer text ever does change, it lives in six places, not one: PLAN.md:848, post-first-light-roadmap.md:88/:183/:185/:192, destiny-audit-2026-07-14.md:53. Two of those (:88, :183) are DEPENDS-ON edges gating other peers, and PLAN §5.R cites the roadmap as the detail home ("full detail with SOTA refs + file:line anchors"), so it is live, not archaeology. The claimed "one-line PLAN.md edit" would strand two dependency edges even under the item's own theory.

REFUTED: Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment
  Name the actual decidable fragment as literal-constant folding under self-substitution, then widen it in two structural moves — recursive substitution through field/call/binop trees, and one shared predicate-decomposer feeding both construction paths — rather than reaching for a general arithmetic solver.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's motivating example (`self.count <= len(self.data)`) never reaches subst_self at all: parse_type_decl probes for `where` at a fixed one-token offset, so a record or list base never enters the refinement branch; both gates would stay RED after both parts land, and part (1) cites an unbuilt peer whose name ("shares-never-clones") forbids the tree-clone part (1) builds.
  objection: FACTUAL, and it collapses the item's spine: the design diagnoses the wrong function. It says `self.count <= len(self.data)` "can never decide, even when every field is literal" because subst_self_operand (verify.mn:114-117) matches only a top-level VarRef("self"). The true root is that the predicate is never parsed into a Predicate at all. parse_type_decl (src/parser.mn:681) decides the refinement branch on a FIXED one-token lookahead:

  687:  let base_val = ident_at(tokens, p2)      // dead binding — never read
  688:  let probe = skip_ws(tokens, p2 + 1)
  689:  if at(tokens, probe, TWhere) {

So `where` is reachable only when the base type is a single-token identifier. A record base falls to `} else if at(tokens, p2, TLBrace)` (parser.mn:693), parses the fields, and returns WITHOUT consuming the trailing `where`. Measured through the pinned boot (wt_run boot/mentl.wasm):
 - `type Positive = Int where 0 < self` + `let ok: Positive = 5` → exit 0, 2488 WAT bytes, zero V_Pending (R2 works — the design's premise holds HERE).
 - `type Buffer = {count: Int} where self.count > 0` → exit 1, ZERO WAT: `parser: P_UnexpectedToken ... where at 1:28`, `infer: E_MissingVariable: self`, `graph: E_OccursCheck`, `infer: E_TypeMismatch: Buffer vs { count: t27@e14 | r28@e14 }`, `verify: E_UnresolvedHole (handle 2)`.
 - `type NonEmpty = [Int] where len(self) > 0` — SYNTAX.md's OWN §Refinement-types example — → exit 1, zero WAT, same P_UnexpectedToken class.
subst_self is never called for either shape. Deepening it is a fix downstream of a gate that never opens.

This falsifies three of the item's own claims:
(a) GATE 1's premise "both cases currently defer to V_Pending unconditionally" is FALSE — measured: exit 1, zero WAT, E_UnresolvedHole. Neither part touches parser.mn, so mn-refine-field-projection.mn stays unparseable after landing. The gate stays RED — it is not vacuous, it is unpassable.
(b) SEQUENCING "Blocked on nothing — every touched function exists and is fully read" is FALSE. It is blocked on parse_type_decl, a function the design never read.
(c) INTERCONNECTION "[Hβ.refine.buffer-invariant / Hβ.dsp.hz-ceiling-ambient-sample-rate] cannot be written as working `where` clauses until part (1) lands" names the wrong blocker: the parser refuses them.

GATE 2 fails independently, and I proved it by subtraction rather than by argument. Measured `if x > 0 { let y: Positive = x }` with `let x = 5` → `V_Pending: 1`. I then deleted the `if` entirely — `let x = 5; let y: Positive = x` → `V_Pending: 1`, identical. The narrowing contributes NOTHING. The blocker is node_const (verify.mn:85-91) returning None on `NExpr(VarRef("x"))` — a variable-to-literal chase the design explicitly disclaims ("genuine variables ... stays honest V_Pending exactly as today"). Part (2) is provably irrelevant to it: walk_refinement's path_pred (infer.mn:4231-4239) flows only through narrowing_pred_handle (infer.mn:4421) → graph_narrow_set → graph_narrow_at → lower.mn:4743 (`list_index_proven` vs `list_index`). It never reaches verify; apply_refinement_constraint (infer.mn:3302-3313) takes only (ty, value_handle, span) — no assumption context, no implication check. So gate 2's stated purpose — "proving the consolidation in part (2) actually threads narrowing into refinement discharge" — asserts a wiring that does not exist and that the design does not build.

DRIFT, part (1): "reuse its sharing helper rather than growing an independent one" — Hβ.infer.instantiate-shares-never-clones is an OPEN peer (PLAN.md:741, PLAN.md:1367; infer.mn:2687's subst_ty tree-clone is still there). There is no helper to reuse, so the design either grows the walker it promises not to, or is blocked on an unbuilt peer. Worse: the peer's name IS its content — instantiate SHARES, NEVER CLONES; PLAN §5.O names that tree-clone as a Carried-Truth violation to delete. Citing it as the MODEL for a new Expr-level tree-cloner inherits the bug it forbids. Row miss: subst_self is `with GraphRead` (verify.mn:101); minting needs GraphWrite/+Alloc, widening refinement_admits (verify.mn:125, `with GraphRead`) and the synth candidate gate (synth_proposer.mn:137). The item's arm-4 interrogation worries about the `len` whitelist's purity while its own part (1) is the actual row change. Under-spec: node_const gets only a `len` whitelist, never a FieldAccess arm — so `node_const(FieldAccess(value_h, "count"))` still hits `_ => None` and gate 1's case fails even in a world where the parser accepted it.

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTEMPT TO REFUTE IT: part (2) is factually correct and I could not break it. node_to_predicate (parser.mn:2473-2487) has BEq/BLt/BLe direct, BGt/BGe normalized with swapped operands at parser.mn:2479-2480 exactly as claimed, BAnd/BOr/UNot recursive, and NO BNe arm (falls to `_ => PBoolNode(node_handle(node))` at 2485). predicate_of_bool_expr (infer.mn:4414) is verbatim `fn predicate_of_bool_expr(node) = PBoolNode(node_handle(node))` — a total stub, used at infer.mn:4235 (if-conditions) and infer.mn:4452 (assert args), exactly as claimed. Two projections of one operation, one rich, one stubbed. The novelty verdict's core claim is also true and checkable: node_const reads graph_node_body (types.mn:1228, an effect op) directly, so there IS no second VC-IR to keep synchronized — the Liquid/Dafny contrast is fair, not a slogan. Every other cited line checked out exactly: predicate_decide verify.mn:61-70, compare_decide 74-80, node_const 85-91, subst_self 101-117, and_decide/or_decide/not_decide 196-228, flow_le types.mn:1176-1180 (total 3-level lattice), refinement_admits verify.mn:137/141 called from synth_proposer.mn:137, the single subst_self call at infer.mn:3313 reached from all three R2 homes (422 fn-return, 1121/1203 call-arg, 3293 let).
  fixes    : 1.
