LEAD WITH THE PARSER — name it `Hβ.parser.where-on-compound-base`. parse_type_decl (parser.mn:687-693) must decide the refinement branch on the RHS type's own extent, not a fixed `p2 + 1` probe: parse the base FIRST (parse_type_ty for a type expr, parse_record_type_fields for the brace branch), THEN check for `where` at the position the base parse RETURNED. This deletes the dead `base_val` binding (parser.mn:687 — a read whose only purpose was the one-token probe, never used; the branch re-parses via parse_type_ty(tokens, p2) at line 690) and collapses all three RHS shapes onto one "parse the base, then optionally refine" spine — LESS code, and it makes SYNTAX.md's own `type NonEmpty = [Int] where len(self) > 0` compile. This is the actual unblocker for Hβ.refine.buffer-invariant and Hβ.dsp.hz-ceiling-ambient-sample-rate. Must be marched (it changes what parses); no `where`-on-compound-base exists in src/**, so plausibly Law-7-additive — assert nothing until march.sh says so.

2. DELETE subst_self; DO NOT DEEPEN IT. A decider walking a tree needs a BINDING, not a substitution. Thread `self_h` through the decider: predicate_decide(pred, self_h) -> compare_decide(op, l, r, self_h) -> node_const(h, self_h), whose VarRef("self") arm reads value_handle LIVE and whose FieldAccess arm projects a field off the live record node. verify.mn:101-117 (subst_self + subst_self_operand, 17 lines) is DELETED; one param threads through four fns; the row stays GraphRead; zero nodes minted. That is Law 1 verbatim — carry the handle, read live, the fix is LESS code — where the design's path-copy mints fabricated no-source-correspondence nodes into the monotonic never-freed bump image (PLAN §6) on the discharge path of every `span: ValidSpan` param in the wheel, the exact churn §5.O names as the constant-factor amplifier. The self handle rides on the verify op (a coordinated arity change — a representation change, unpatchability-theorem-blessed, not a patch).

3. KEEP part (2), but not as gate 2's proof. The node_to_predicate/predicate_of_bool_expr consolidation + the missing BNe arm (normalize to PNot(PCompare(BEq,l,r)) — litval_compare (verify.mn:159) already has BEq, so no new decision procedure) is real and CONFIRMED. Its payoff is the NARROWING weave's precision (lower.mn:4743's list_index_proven elision), NOT refinement discharge. Gate it there: an `if i < len(xs) { xs[i] }` fixture whose WAT contains `list_index_proven` — mechanically checkable by grep, and prove it RED today before claiming the delta (PBoolNode and PCompare both yield a handle to narrowing_pred_handle at infer.mn:4421-4423, so the win must be demonstrated, not assumed).

4. RE-SCOPE BOTH GATES. Gate 1 becomes `expect_executable` on a list- or record-based refined alias AFTER the parser lands. Gate 2's honest form is dropped and replaced by a positively-named peer for the capability it actually tests: `Hβ.verify.const-binding-chase` — node_const chases a VarRef to its let-bound literal (constant propagation). That is currently unnamed and unbuilt, and it is what `let x = 5; let y: Positive = x` needs. Naming it is the difference between an honest gap and drift.

5. NAME THE RIGHT GATE HELPER. The design says "same expect_surfaced/expect_refusal shape". Wrong: gate 1A and gate 2 need `expect_executable`, which already asserts zero debt (`! grep -Eq '(^|: )(E_|V_?Pending)'`, tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh); `expect_surfaced` asserts the diagnostic IS present — the opposite of "zero V_Pending". Gate 1B (count=5) needs `expect_refusal` with 'E_RefinementRejected'.

6. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS. The item says nothing about m2 == m3, the 71 micros, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, or crown 5/5. The parser change and any row widening of refinement_admits both touch the wheel's own compile; "Law-7-additive" is a march result, never a design claim.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment
  Name the actual decidable fragment as literal-constant folding under self-substitution, then widen it in two structural moves — recursive substitution through field/call/binop trees, and one shared predicate-decomposer feeding both construction paths — rather than reaching for a general arithmetic solver.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact-state diagnosis is accurate (verify.mn:85-91, 113-117; parser.mn:2473-2487's missing BNe; infer.mn:4414's stub all check out), but the mechanism re-derives a folder the e-graph already runs (egraph.mn:207/212/222), both gate legs fail as specified (no narrowing→discharge edge exists at infer.mn:4276→lower.mn:4743; node_const has no FieldExpr arm), part (2) silently moves emitted bytes through narrowing_pred_handle (infer.mn:4421) into lower's list_index_proven choice, and "Blocked on nothing" is false — it proposes reusing a sharing helper from the unbuilt peer Hβ.infer.instantiate-shares-never-clones while subst_ty (infer.mn:3099-3149) clones every compound.
  objection: The design builds a SECOND constant folder in verify.mn parallel to the one the graph already runs — the Carried-Truth Law violated at the exact site the design claims to honor, and it falsifies the novelty verdict's central claim.

The design's part (1) says: "Pair it with a small, NAMED, closed whitelist in node_const for structure-only reads on a literal operand — starting with `len` on a literal sequence... extend only when a concrete refinement needs the next primitive." Its novelty verdict rests on: "the decider is a graph read, not a translation to a separate VC-IR, so there is no second representation to keep synchronized (Liquid's and Dafny's VC-IRs both need their own soundness argument relative to source; Mentl's doesn't, because it has no second IR)."

Mentl already has the folder. egraph.mn:207 `fn const_int(handle) with GraphRead = match graph_node_body(handle) { NExpr(LitInt(n)) => Some(n), _ => None }` — byte-for-byte the same shape as verify.mn:85 `node_const`. egraph.mn:212 `fn fold_int(op, a, b)` folds BAdd/BSub/BMul/BDiv/BMod. egraph.mn:222 `rw_const_fold` reads operands through `egraph_extract` "so a folded subterm (already ≡ a literal) folds transitively (the congruence payoff for fold)" (egraph.mn:204-205), refuses div/mod-by-zero because "the trap is the program's meaning, not a constant" (egraph.mn:205-206), and mints idempotently. This is the e-graph engine PLAN §7 records as live in lower. So "literal-constant folding under self-substitution" — the design's own name for its fragment — is a capability the graph already computes, and the design re-derives it in a narrower hand-rolled walk that grows one arm per refinement. Two folders, one truth: drift-7 at the fold layer, and the second representation the novelty verdict swears Mentl doesn't have.

The whitelist is also strictly weaker than the thing it duplicates. `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) has no arithmetic arm, so after part (1) recursively mints a substituted spine, `self % 2 != 0` on a literal 3 still returns None — while `rw_const_fold` + `fold_int(BMod, 3, 2)` already decides it. The design's own gate leg 1 proves the point: `type Buffer = {data: List, count: Int} where self.count <= len(self.data)` needs `node_const(FieldExpr(record_handle, "count"))` to project a field out of a record literal, and the design names only a `len` entry — so gate leg 1 FAILS as specified.

Gate leg 2 fails harder, and self-contradictorily. `mn-refine-narrowing-decidable.mn` claims to prove "the consolidation in part (2) actually threads narrowing into refinement discharge." It cannot. The narrowing weave's ONLY consumer is infer.mn:4276 `if ph != 0 { graph_narrow_set(ih, ph) }`, on INDEX nodes only, read at lower.mn:4743 `let callee = if graph_narrow_at(ih) != 0 { "list_index_proven" } else { "list_index" }`. There is no edge from it to `apply_refinement_constraint` (infer.mn:3302); consolidating a predicate CONSTRUCTOR threads nothing. And even with an edge, `let y: Positive = x` passes the VarRef node for `x` as value_handle, `node_const` returns None on it (verify.mn:91 `_ => None`), and deciding it from `x > 0` in scope is predicate implication over a non-literal variable — precisely what the design's own novelty verdict disclaims: "genuine Presburger/interval reasoning over non-literal-but-bounded variables... That is a larger, separate gap; naming it honestly here rather than implying this item closes it." The gate demands the capability the scope refuses. Both legs fail; under attack 4 that alone refutes.

I tried to save the design on the second-folder charge by arguing the e-graph runs too late to reuse — and that is true (`saturate_pass` is "a `|>` stage between frontend and lower", egraph.mn:272-275, while refinement discharge runs during inference at infer.mn:318/422/1121/1203). But that disproves the design's "SEQUENCING: Blocked on nothing," it does not license a second folder: the ordering is a question about where the ONE fold is read, and the design never mentions the e-graph at all, so it did not weigh this and route around it — it duplicated by not looking.
  fixes    : The diagnosis survives; the mechanism, the gate, and the sequencing do not. Five corrections the design must absorb.

**1. `node_const` must read the FOLD that exists, not grow a second one.** Delete the "small, NAMED, closed whitelist ... starting with `len` ... extend only when a concrete refinement needs the next primitive" — that is a per-refinement growth path for a folder Mentl already has. The native form: `node_const(h)` reads `egraph_extract(h)` (egraph.mn:40) instead of `graph_node_body(h)` raw (verify.mn:85), so every operand the e-graph has already proven equivalent to a literal decides for free — including the arithmetic the whitelist cannot reach (`self % 2 != 0` on a literal is `rw_const_fold` + `fold_int(BMod, 3, 2)`, egraph.mn:212-218, already built and already refusing to fold div/mod-by-zero for the right reason). `len`-on-a-literal-sequence becomes a RULE beside `rw_const_fold` (egraph.mn:222); field-projection-over-a-record-literal becomes another. One folder, one home, rules added where every other rule lives. This costs the design its "extend the whitelist" tail and buys transitive folding via congruence — `egraph_extract` already reads operands through itself so a folded subterm folds transitively (egraph.mn:198-206 comment).

**2. Name the ordering blocker the design missed — it is the real design question.** `saturate_pass` is "a `|>` stage between frontend and lower in the pipeline spine" (egraph.mn:272-275); `apply_refinement_constraint` runs DURING inference (infer.mn:318, 422, 1121, 1203). So at verify time `graph_canon_at` has drawn no edges and `egraph_extract` returns the handle unchanged. Either the refinement discharge moves to a post-saturate stage, or saturation gains an inference-time entry. That is a sequencing question about ONE folder — never a license for two. "Blocked on nothing" is false: this is the actual block, and it is load-bearing.

**3. Delete gate leg 2 (`mn-refine-narrowing-decidable.mn`).** Its stated purpose — "proving the consolidation in part (2) actually threads narrowing into refinement discharge" — is unreachable and self-contradictory. Replace with a leg testing what part (2) actually buys: `type NotFive = Int where self != 5`, literal 5 must emit E_RefinementRejected, literal 4 must carry zero V_Pending. (The BNe arm normalized to `PNot(PCompare(BEq,l,r))` is sound and genuinely missing — keep that.) Gate leg 1 (`mn-refine-field-projection.mn`) is the right shape but needs correction 1 to pass: after part (1) mints `FieldExpr(value_handle, "count")`, `node_const` has no FieldExpr arm and returns None.

**4. Part (2) is an EMIT change — declare it or sequence it with R5.** `narrowing_pred_handle` (infer.mn:4421-4435) dispatches on predicate SHAPE, and `predicate_of_bool_expr` today returns ONLY `PBoolNode(h)`. Substituting node_to_predicate's match re-points the recorded representative: `PTrue => 0`, `PNot(_) => 0`, `POr(_,_) => 0` (narrowing dropped, elision lost), `PCompare(_, lh, _) => lh` (the LEFT operand — for source `i > 0`, parser.mn:2479 normalizes to `PCompare(BLt, h(0), h(i))`, so the recorded "proof that the index is in range" becomes the literal zero's handle). That flips lower.mn:4743's `list_index_proven` vs `list_index` choice — the byte fixpoint moves, march.sh rules TRANSITION, and the R5 latent-OOB landmine gets a more arbitrary input while the design calls itself a no-op consolidation. Land part (2) WITH the elision gated on real discharge, not before it.

**5. Drop the "reuse instantiate's sharing helper" claim.** `subst_ty` (infer.mn:3099-3149) rebuilds every compound unconditionally — `TList(elem) => TList(subst_ty(elem, mapping))`, `TName(name, args) => TName(name, subst_list(args, mapping))` — with no share-if-unchanged anywhere. `Hβ.infer.instantiate-shares-never-clones` is the peer that would BUILD that helper (PLAN §5.O layer 2); the design reads an open peer as landed substrate. Either part (1) grows the independent walker the design promises it won't, or it sequences behind that peer. Also fix the framing: `node_to_predicate` (parser.mn:2473, where-clause → predicate_decide) and `predicate_of_bool_expr` (infer.mn:4414, if-condition → narrowing_pred_handle → graph_narrow_set → lower's elision) are not "the same operation performed twice" — they are two operations with different consumers and different downstream contracts, which is exactly why swapping one for the other moves emitted bytes.

**What survives intact:** part (1)'s diagnosis (`subst_self_operand` at verify.mn:113-117 matches only a top-level `VarRef("self")`, so `self.count <= len(self.data)` can never decide) is correct and the recursive substitution is the right mechanism. The missing BNe arm is real and the `PNot(PCompare(BEq,l,r))` normalization is sound. The scope disclaimer (no Presburger over non-literal variables) is honest — the gate just contradicts it. Rename the item to what it now is: `Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment` = recursive self-substitution + the discharge reading the e-graph's fold, with `Hβ.verify.narrowing-elision-gated-on-discharge` (R5) as the named peer part (2) must land beside, and `Hβ.egraph.structure-read-rules` (len/field-projection over literals) as the named home for the folding arms the whitelist wanted to own.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.own.region-return-transfer
  The landed transfer rule ('a value tagged with my own top region re-tags to my PARENT's region at return') is proven correct for statically nested declarations read off own.mn's own lexical stack; its remaining scope is generalizing 'parent' from a fixed stack-depth read to the caller's instantiated region variable at an arbitrary call site — exactly what region polymorphism supplies.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design proposes to change how `parent` is computed, but I built two variant compilers and measured that `parent` is a write no consumer reads (delete the rule, or set `parent := 999`: gate micro still exits 5, WAT byte-identical, 71/71 micros, census 2266 = baseline exactly), and on the wheel's own self-compile the expression the design proposes to replace (`list_index(region_stack, depth-2)`, own.mn:225) never executes at all — all 23 transfers fire at depth=1 → parent=0, while 2,096/2,119 returns (98.9%) are never checked and E_RegionEscape has fired 0 times.
  objection: The design's load-bearing factual claim — "proven correct for statically nested declarations ... Proven by tests/micros/mn-region-transfer.mn (expected exit 5)" — is false, and I disproved it by construction rather than by argument.

I built three compilers from the pinned boot and ran the design's own gate through each:
  (1) baseline: PASS exit=5 diags=0
  (2) own.mn:223-227 (the whole transfer branch) DELETED: PASS exit=5 diags=0, emitted WAT BYTE-IDENTICAL to baseline, 71/71 micros, census errors 2266 = verify-baseline.txt's census_errors_max exactly, 0 RegionEscape on the wheel's own 40,738-line self-compile.
  (3) own.mn:224-225 replaced by `let parent = 999` (a region id never live): PASS exit=5 diags=0, WAT BYTE-IDENTICAL, 71/71 micros, census 2266.
The design changes exactly one thing — the source of `parent`. No gate in the repo can observe `parent` at all. "Proven" is not weakly supported; it is measurably empty.

Then the census (instrumented check_escape, compiling the wheel) reframes the whole item:
  2,096 (98.9%) V0_UNTAGGED — own.mn:213 `lookup_tag(region_index, value_handle)` keys the RAW handle, and a block body's handle is a union-find class-mate of its tail, not the tail's handle, so lookup misses and the arm at own.mn:215 resumes with NO CHECK. Confirmed directly: `fn bare() = (1,2)` → V1_TRANSFER; `fn blocky() = { let t = (3,4); t }` — a region-local value returned, the escape-shaped case — → V0_UNTAGGED, never examined.
  23 (1.1%) V1_TRANSFER, ALL at depth=1 → own.mn:224 `if depth < 2 { 0 }` → parent = 0, the global sentinel. `list_index(region_stack, depth - 2)` — the design's target expression, the thing it calls "reading the caller's region off the STATIC stack" — is NEVER EVALUATED on the wheel. It executes exactly once in the repo: inside the gate micro.
  0 V2_OUTER_LIVE, 0 V3_DEAD_ESCAPE. E_RegionEscape (own.mn:231) has never fired.
Structurally it cannot: check_escape's only caller is infer.mn:434, on `body_handle`, run before region_exit at infer.mn:435 — and a fn's own body node can only be tag_alloc'd while its own region is top. So the tag is always top_rid, verdict 1 always, verdicts 2 and 3 unreachable. PLAN §7's "Hylo-quiet on the wheel (0 escapes)" and commit 82a7e42's "ZERO escapes ... every legal return transfers, no false flags" are the arithmetic of an unreachable branch, not evidence of quiet inference — and the design leans on that measurement ("the 'zero escapes, Hylo-quiet' claim is a claim about that case").

Three further refutations:

DRIFT — the re-tag at own.mn:227 is a WRITE WITH NO READER, the write-only side-ledger PLAN §5.U names "the textbook Carried-Truth violation" (resume_kinds, zero readers, dissolved 7b72790). region_index has exactly two readers: own.mn:213 (each fn body checked once) and own.mn:282 join_field_regions (FIELD handles only — and infer.mn:529-541 `call_is_alloc_returning` tags only ConstructorScheme/RecordSchemeKind, so cross-fn flow rides untagged CALL nodes and a callee's body handle never becomes a caller's field handle). Proven by variant (3). The design polishes the input to a write nobody reads.

DRIFT — the design says "check_escape's region_id == top_rid branch (own.mn:223-227) keeps its shape" and "the transfer semantics do not change." That is Law 2 violated. Once stage 2 makes the result region a call-site-instantiated parameter, the ultimate form is tag_alloc stamping ρ_ret at CONSTRUCTION — the value is born in the caller's region — and the branch DISSOLVES; check_escape drops to two verdicts. The fix is LESS code (delete 223-227). Keeping the rule and swapping its input is the patch.

DRIFT/SOUNDNESS — own.mn:206-209 claims the transfer "is what makes the region drop safe as the layer-3 arena reset: everything still tagged with the dying region is provably transient." A re-tag moves the LEDGER ENTRY, not the BYTES. Under §5.O layer 3 the region drop IS the arena reset; the returned value's bytes sit in the dying slab while its tag says "parent" — in fact "0 = global lifetime" at all 23 wheel sites, the strongest possible lie. The design propagates it verbatim ("the transfer semantics do not change"), scheduling a use-after-free INTO the arena swap the item exists to unblock.

NOVELTY — an overclaim against a named tool. The design says "MLKit's region-polymorphic functions re-derive the whole relationship per instantiation inside its own separate machinery" and that no other system "build[s] the rule once and generalize[s] its input source afterward." Tofte-Talpin/MLKit derive a region-polymorphic type ONCE at the definition (`letrec f[ρ1..ρn](x) = e`) and instantiate the region parameters per application — that IS build-once-instantiate-per-call, the property claimed as uniquely Mentl's. And MLKit has no transfer rule to separate: allocation is annotated `e at ρ`, and a result outliving the callee is allocated into a region parameter the caller passes in. That is literally "the result-region-as-parameter form" — the name own.mn:205 and commit 82a7e42 both claim while the code does a post-hoc re-tag off a lexical stack. The comparison is inverted: MLKit's form is stronger (it cannot lie about where bytes are); Mentl's is the weaker post-hoc relabel.

GATE — vacuous in both legs, proven. Leg 1 ("mn-region-transfer.mn must keep passing byte-for-byte") passes with the rule deleted and with parent:=999. Leg 2 ("a new call-boundary micro must also pass") cannot prove a generalization fired — a micro that must PASS has no falsifying power, and the call-boundary shape compiles clean today (I ran two: closure-capturing-a-nested-region-value and lambda-returning-tuple — 0 diags each, before stage 2 exists).

What I tried that did NOT refute the design, in fairness: the line cites are exact (own.mn:223 is `} else if region_id == top_rid {`; 224-225 are the parent binding; 227 is the re-tag), the mechanism description is accurate, the claim that region_stack depth at infer_fn's recursive call tracks the lexical chain is true (infer.mn:393/434/435), and the identified gap is real — infer.mn:529-541 confirms ordinary calls are untagged, so there genuinely is no mechanism for cross-call flow. The design correctly names a hole. It is the diagnosis of that hole's shape, the "proven" claim, the novelty verdict, and the gate that fail.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE "proven correct" and "Proven by tests/micros/mn-region-transfer.mn." Measured: the transfer branch deleted → micro PASS exit=5, WAT byte-identical, 71/71 micros, census 2266 = baseline; `parent := 999` → identical. No artifact in the repo distinguishes a correct parent from no rule at all. Say instead: "the transfer rule is currently unobservable — no gate reads its output."

2. STATE THE REAL ARTIFACT STATE (replacing "reading the caller's region off the STATIC stack"): on the wheel's own self-compile, check_escape fires 2,119 times — 2,096 (98.9%) hit own.mn:215 (untagged, NO CHECK), 23 hit the transfer, ALL at depth=1 → own.mn:224 `if depth < 2 { 0 }` → parent = 0. `list_index(region_stack, depth-2)` NEVER EXECUTES on the wheel; it fires exactly once in the repo, inside the gate micro. V2_OUTER_LIVE and V3_DEAD_ESCAPE fire 0 times; E_RegionEscape (own.mn:231) is structurally unreachable, because check_escape's only caller (infer.mn:434, before region_exit at 435) passes `body_handle`, whose tag can only ever be the fn's own region. Correct PLAN §7 and commit 82a7e42: "Hylo-quiet on the wheel (0 escapes)" measures an unreachable branch, not quiet inference.

3. RESCOPE THE REMAINING WORK. It is NOT "generalize parent's source." It is two things the design's framing cannot see, both already named in the repo: (a) class-aware tag lookup — own.mn:213 keys the RAW handle while a block body's handle is a union-find class-mate of its tail, blinding the check to 98.9% of returns (PLAN §11 col 3 stage 1; commit 82a7e42's own "the class-aware tag lookup ... is the arena increment's named burden"); (b) tagging cross-fn value flow — infer.mn:529-541 tags only ConstructorScheme/RecordSchemeKind, so ordinary calls carry no region. Fixing `parent` fixes neither. Until (a) lands, `parent` is unreachable for ~99% of the wheel and cannot be the cursor.

4. RESTRUCTURE, DON'T PATCH. Cut "check_escape's region_id == top_rid branch (own.mn:223-227) keeps its shape" and "the transfer semantics do not change." With the result region as a call-site-instantiated parameter, tag_alloc reads ρ_ret LIVE at CONSTRUCTION — the value is born in the caller's region — and own.mn:223-227 is DELETED; check_escape drops to two verdicts (on-stack → ok, dead → report). The fix is LESS code. This item is not "the same rule over a richer input"; it is the deletion of a re-derivation once the edge exists.

5. RETRACT OR CORRECT THE MLKit COMPARISON. Tofte-Talpin/MLKit derive the region-polymorphic type once at the definition and instantiate region parameters per application — exactly the "build once, generalize the input source" property the design claims no other system has. MLKit needs no transfer rule because `e at ρ` allocates into a caller-passed result region: the value is born correct. That IS the "result-region-as-parameter form" own.mn:205 and 82a7e42 name while doing a post-hoc re-tag. Name MLKit as the form to reach, not the one surpassed.

6. NAME THE ARENA UNSOUNDNESS IN POSITIVE FORM (new peer, e.g. Hβ.own.transfer-allocates-not-retags, or fold into the result-region-as-parameter peer): a re-tag moves the ledger entry, not the bytes. own.mn:206-209's "everything still tagged with the dying region is provably transient" is false the moment the region drop becomes the arena reset (§5.O layer 3) — the returned bytes sit in the reset slab while the tag reads "0 = global." A hidden gap is drift; this one is currently hidden inside a comment that claims the opposite.

7. REPLACE THE GATE. "Must keep passing" is not a gate — both legs pass today, with the rule deleted, and with parent:=999. The honest gate is red-to-green: a crucible carrying a value that genuinely outlives its region, REFUSED with E_RegionEscape at both spans (V3_DEAD_ESCAPE reachable for the first time), failing before the design and passing after. Pair it with a verdict-census ratchet: V0_UNTAGGED must fall from 2,096 as class-aware lookup lands, and V1_TRANSFER at depth>=2 must become nonzero on the wheel — the first mechanical evidence the lexical-parent read is live at all.

8. STATE BLAST RADIUS HONESTLY (the design says nothing). Today the rule moves no gate (measured: 71/71 and census 2266 with it deleted). Stage 2 changes infer's own inference, so m2 != m3 — a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3, per march.sh's own arbitration. The real risk is unmasking: PLAN §11 col 3 already warns "before any mutation-site check — a naive store-check false-floods the ref-buffer idiom," and the 2,096 untagged returns are precisely the mask hiding whether that flood exists. No gate covers it.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.own.region-return-transfer
  The landed transfer rule ('a value tagged with my own top region re-tags to my PARENT's region at return') is proven correct for statically nested declarations read off own.mn's own lexical stack; its remaining scope is generalizing 'parent' from a fixed stack-depth read to the caller's instantiated region variable at an arbitrary call site — exactly what region polymorphism supplies.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is exactly right and survived every attack I made on it, but the design fails on GATE and DRIFT: its gate is mechanically incapable of failing (regions are analysis-only, E_RegionEscape is non-fatal, the harness gates on exit code alone), and it preserves a re-tag branch that region polymorphism's own named form — "result-region-as-parameter", own.mn:206 — deletes rather than re-sources.
  objection: THE GATE CANNOT FAIL — proven, not argued. The design stakes itself on "tests/micros/mn-region-transfer.mn must keep passing byte-for-byte (exit 5)". That micro is blind to the rule it allegedly gates:

1. Regions produce exactly ONE observable: `report(ERegionEscape(...))` on stderr (own.mn:231). Non-fatal — the only `proc_exit(1)` in the pipeline is the hole gate (pipeline.mn:125,119-130).
2. The tag reaches nothing else. `lookup_tag` / `region_index_get` have ZERO readers outside own.mn (grepped src/ lib/); `current_region()` exists only as a decl at types.mn:996 with no performer. `emit_memory_arena` is DORMANT — pipeline.mn:69,148,190 install `emit_memory_bump` at every site — so no region fact reaches codegen.
3. tools/run-micro.sh gates on compile_exit / wat2wasm / run_exit. It computes `diags=$(grep -c '^[EW]_' ...)`, PRINTS it, and never compares it. verify.sh:64-67 tests only `[[ "$out" == PASS* ]]`.

Therefore mn-region-transfer.mn exits 5 whether the transfer fires, is deleted (bare `resume()`), or re-tags to garbage. Measured: `PASS tests/micros/mn-region-transfer.mn: exit=5 (expected 5) diags=0`. The census ratchet — the one gate the design did NOT cite — fails only on a RISE (`"$errors" -gt "$max"`, verify.sh:96), so a silently under-firing transfer passes that too. m2==m3 is invariant (regions never reach emit). The design's proposed NEW call-boundary micro is vacuous by the identical mechanism: it would pass BEFORE stage 2 lands.

Nothing in tests/ asserts E_RegionEscape ever fires (only mn-region-transfer.mn's docstring mentions it). The arm has never been observed to fire — "ZERO escapes on ~37k lines" is a measurement of an arm no test can reach. The repo already holds the correct precedent the design ignores: tools/crown-gate.sh:33 — `smuggle-*) want="reject"; ok=$([ "$n" -ge 1 ]...)` — a diagnostic-count gate that fails at pre-fix boot and passes against the fix (PLAN §4③).

SECOND OBJECTION (DRIFT — patch, not restructure). The design: "check_escape's region_id == top_rid branch (own.mn:223-227) keeps its shape." But own.mn:206 — the code's OWN comment — names this "the result-region-as-parameter form". Under that form (Tofte-Talpin; MLKit), the result region IS a parameter, so the callee allocates directly into the caller's region: the value is BORN in the right region and there is no return re-tag, because there was never a wrong tag to fix. The design's own analogy refutes it — it invokes infer.mn:399, `unify(body_handle, ret_handle, span, FnReturn(name, Inferred("body")))`, and ordinary return typing does NOT re-tag the body's type to the parent's at return; it UNIFIES once. Region polymorphism DELETES the branch; it does not re-source its input. "Same rule, swapped input" is Law 2 (a fix that fits in a patch means the architecture is wrong) and Law 1 (the fix is always LESS code) — this fix is the same code reading a different slot.

THIRD (NOVELTY OVERCLAIM against a named tool). "MLKit's region-polymorphic functions re-derive the whole relationship per instantiation inside its own separate machinery" is false: MLKit instantiates region parameters by substitution at region application (`f[ρ1..ρn]`), which is not a re-derivation. MLKit has no "transfer rule" to separate from "parent identification" because it never needed one. The design claims novelty for a SEPARATION that exists only because Mentl currently reads a lexical stack as a stand-in for the missing region parameter — celebrating the scaffold as the invention.

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTACK (I tried to refute the factual claim and could not): own.mn:223 is exactly `} else if region_id == top_rid {`; 224-225 are exactly the `list_index(region_stack, depth - 2)` parent binding; 216-233 is the three-verdict Some(tag) body as described. Commit 82a7e42's message independently confirms the framing verbatim. The scoping claim is correct too: I traced check_escape's SINGLE call site (infer.mn:434, on body_handle) through the micro — inner's `(1,2)` is a MakeTupleExpr tagged at infer.mn:717, transfers at depth 2 to outer's region; outer's body is a BlockExpr so its own check_escape hits `None` (the "class-aware tag lookup" burden 82a7e42 names). One transfer, lexical, exactly as claimed. Minor slip, not load-bearing: "outer subsequently builds two more tuples" — outer builds ONE tuple literal (mn-region-transfer.mn:12); `let (a, b) = inner()` is a pattern, not a construction.
  fixes    : 1. REPLACE THE GATE — it must be able to fail. Delete the claim that mn-region-transfer=5 gates anything: it is insensitive to the rule (proven above). Adopt the crown precedent (tools/crown-gate.sh:33): a diagnostic-count crucible pair — an `escape-*` case that MUST report E_RegionEscape (`n >= 1`) and a `transfer-*` case that MUST NOT (`n == 0`), each asserted on the grep count in m2.err, not on the exit code. The gate must FAIL against the pre-stage-2 boot and PASS against stage 2. If no program can currently make the arm fire (my search found none, and the wheel's ~37k lines produce zero), that is itself the finding to state: the escape verdict has never been observed to execute, so "sound for the static-nesting case, measured Hylo-quiet" rests on an arm no test reaches. Say that, and gate it, before generalizing it.

2. THE DESIGN MUST SAY THE BRANCH DISSOLVES, NOT THAT IT "KEEPS ITS SHAPE". Under result-region-as-parameter — the form own.mn:206 already names — tag_alloc stamps the value with the return's region VARIABLE (a handle), the call site unifies that variable with the caller's ambient region, and the value is born in the caller's region. There is nothing to re-tag at return. check_escape's three verdicts collapse to one live read of the resolved region variable, mirroring infer.mn:399's single `unify(body_handle, ret_handle, ...)` — which the design cites as its analogy while drawing the opposite conclusion. Stage 2 is a DELETION (own.mn:221-233 collapses; the `depth < 2 → 0` widening-to-global stand-in goes with it), not an input swap. LESS code, per Law 1. Reframe the item as `Hβ.own.region-return-transfer` DISSOLVING INTO `Hβ.infer.region-polymorphism`, not surviving inside it.

3. DROP THE MLKit CLAIM. It misstates what MLKit does (region application is substitution, not per-instantiation re-derivation) and claims novelty for a separation that is an artifact of the current stand-in. The honest novelty statement, if one is wanted: Mentl's region variable can ride the SAME union-find the type variables ride (infer.mn:399), so region polymorphism needs no separate region-inference machinery — that is a real structural claim about one graph with two operations, and it is the argument that the transfer branch was scaffolding.

4. NAME THE OBSERVABILITY GAP POSITIVELY. The re-tag currently feeds no consumer (zero readers outside own.mn; emit_memory_arena dormant at pipeline.mn:69,148,190). Until either the arena swap lands or a projection surfaces the region, no gate on this analysis can be anything but vacuous. Name it: `Hβ.own.region-projection-observable` (the cursor projects a value's resolved region, so the analysis has a reader and a gate has something to assert) — sequenced BEFORE or WITH stage 2, since without it stage 2 lands unfalsifiable. Also worth naming while the file is open: region_index_add (own.mn:249-253) PREPENDS and region_bucket_scan (own.mn:261-267) returns the first match, so a re-tag SHADOWS rather than replaces — the bucket grows per re-tag and never shrinks, a side-ledger accumulating a fact the node could carry (PLAN §5.O already banks this as the region waypoint into Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive).
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.verify.ledger-soundness
  verify.mn itself never assumes true anywhere — confirmed by reading every arm — but two OTHER sites do: a dormant oracle stub and a live gradient-suggestion path that never calls the refinement check that already exists elsewhere.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the fix the design tells you to land NOW is a no-op against a variant that is never constructed, while the same forty lines it audited contain a live gate that cannot fail and that the design never sees.
  objection: Fix (2) changes nothing, targets dead code, and the audit walked past the real bug at the site it was reading.

(a) THE TARGET IS DEAD. `ARefined` has ZERO construction sites. `grep -rn "ARefined(" src/ lib/ tests/` returns exactly the ADT declaration (mentl.mn:37) and seven pattern-match arms (:197, :221, :265, :332, :432, :528, :570). No code mints one. Both callers of `try_each_annotation` enumerate the identical six-variant set with no `ARefined`: gradient_next builds `[APure, ANotAlloc, ANotIO, ANotNetwork] ++ ownership_candidates(params)` (mentl.mn:155-157, where ownership_candidates yields only AOwn/ARef, :387-394), and `gradient_candidates_at` builds the same list (oracle.mn:234-235). The design defers fix (1) precisely because race is unreachable, then asserts "This fix is live and reachable — land it without waiting on anything else" for a violation that is MORE thoroughly unreachable — race at least has an import chain; ARefined has no constructor. Identical dormancy, opposite verdicts.

(b) THE FIX IS A NO-OP. The design says to call `refinement_admits(narrowed_ty, handle)`. At this site `narrowed_ty` is a TFun — `narrow_row` builds `TFun(params, ret, narrow)` (mentl.mn:353). `refinement_admits` matches TAlias / TRefined / `_ => true` (verify.mn:136-145); a TFun hits `_ => true`. The proposed gate evaluates to `true && true`. It is also the wrong mechanism for the site: `refinement_admits(ty, value_handle)` needs a VALUE node — `subst_self` → `node_const` → `graph_node_body(h)` expecting `NExpr(LitInt(..))` (verify.mn:85-91). At synth_proposer.mn:137 `h` is exactly that (`let N(_, _, h) = node`, :136, after `infer_expr(node)` runs at :135). At mentl.mn:249 `handle` is a TYPE handle bound to `NBound(TFun(...))`. The mechanism does not transport; "no new mechanism, just apply the existing one at a second site" is false.

(c) THE REAL BUG, LIVE, AT THE AUDITED LINES. `verify_after_apply(handle)` (mentl.mn:249) CANNOT return false. Proof: both callers invoke `try_each_annotation` only inside `match kind { NBound(ty) => ... }` (mentl.mn:152-158; oracle.mn:216-219), so the handle enters NBound. `apply_annotation_tentatively`'s eight arms (mentl.mn:260-294) reach only `narrow_row` → `graph_bind`, a bare `()`, or `verify(...)` (which resumes without mutating the graph — verify.mn:35-45). `graph_bind` (graph.mn:271-289) sets `NBound(ty)` unconditionally; its ONLY rejection is `occurs_in`, and that arm does `report(EOccursCheck(...)); resume()` with NO state change — leaving the node's prior NBound intact. mentl.mn contains zero calls to `infer_expr`/`unify`/`row_subsumes`/`resolve_row`/`effs_at` (grep returns empty), so nothing can ever record NErrorHole here. Therefore `verify_after_apply` reads `NBound(_) => true` (mentl.mn:379) on every path, for every variant. The `filter` at mentl.mn:253 is the identity. mentl.mn:140's own comment — "Only PROVEN annotations reach the user" — is false: `gradient_next` will propose `with Pure` on a function that performs IO. This is live via mentl.mn:121 (`resume(gradient_next(handle))`), the Propose/Teach op that PLAN §7 records as working.

(d) THE GATE IS UNWRITABLE OR VACUOUS. "a micro test proposing `where self > 0` against a handle already bound to a literal -1" cannot be built: no enumeration produces an ARefined, so the fixture must hand-inject one into a path no caller reaches; and the handle cannot be both the fn's TFun type handle (what the site requires) and a literal -1 value node (what `node_const` requires). Hand-inject anyway and `refinement_admits(TFun, h)` returns true — the gate passes without the fix. Refuted on attack 4 either way.

(e) THE NOVELTY VERDICT REFUTES ITSELF. It credits greppability: "Finding both violations took one targeted grep plus reading two call chains." The grep found the cosmetic literal (`= { true` at combinators.mn:289) and missed the structural one, because mentl.mn:249's unsoundness is a COMPOSITION fact — graph_bind cannot fail, plus nothing re-infers, therefore NBound always — with no `true` token to match. The audit method the design credits is the method that produced its central error.

Minor factual slips: `try_handler_with_rollback` (combinators.mn:297-304) IGNORES its `checkpoint` parameter — the body is just `h` — so "obligations accrued since the fork's own checkpoint (already threaded by try_handler_with_rollback)" describes a window that does not exist; there is no fork. And "verify.mn never assumes true anywhere — confirmed by reading every arm" is not exact: `refinement_admits`' TAlias arm returns `true` on typed identity without discharging the predicate (verify.mn:136-138). That one is defended by a coherent layering comment (:129-135) and I do not count it against the design, but the sweeping claim is not what reading every arm shows.
  fixes    : WHAT SURVIVES. All three verify.mn soundness claims are true, verified line by line: None accrues debt (:42-44), SmtUnknown accrues debt rather than resuming clean (:297-299), an unencodable operand renders the UNSAT-forcing "false" (:376-379). `outcome_verified` is a literal unconditional true (combinators.mn:288-290). Race is dormant: combinators' only importer is oracle.mn:42; main.mn:61 imports oracle and calls nothing from it. Fix (1)'s deferral to Band K's oracle-search activation — named as a required fixture in that wiring commit rather than patched now — is correct and should be kept verbatim.

CORRECTION 1 — restate site (2) honestly. `try_each_annotation` is live; the ARefined branch inside it is not. Say: "mentl.mn:265-270's ARefined arm is a dead branch of a live filter — `ARefined` has no constructor anywhere in src/, lib/, or tests/, and neither enumeration (mentl.mn:155-157, oracle.mn:234-235) produces one." Then apply the design's OWN standard, the one it got right for race: this is a precondition on whichever commit first mints an ARefined candidate, not a standalone landing.

CORRECTION 2 — the peer this cluster actually owns is a different, live one. Name it `Hβ.gradient.candidate-gate-reads-live-row`: try_each_annotation's checkpoint → apply → verify → rollback proves nothing, because `verify_after_apply` reads back the binding `narrow_row` just wrote. That is the Carried-Truth Law in its purest form — not "a consumer assumed a fact" (the design's framing) but a consumer FABRICATING a fact, writing it to the graph, reading its own write, and calling the echo proof. The tell is on the page at mentl.mn:154: `TFun(params, _ret, _row)` destructures the inferred row and DISCARDS it, then asks "can this fn be Pure?" by writing a fabricated EfPure and reading it back. The fact that answers the question is in hand, underscore-prefixed, thrown away.

CORRECTION 3 — the ultimate form is LESS code, not a second conjunct. A candidate survives iff the crown's own gate says so: `row_subsumes(inferred_row, candidate_row)` — effects.mn:376, the by-name negation gate that landed at 29df478 and that tools/crown-gate.sh holds at 5/5. Read `_row` live at mentl.mn:154 instead of discarding it; ask row_subsumes directly. Then DELETE narrow_row's speculative bind, DELETE the checkpoint/rollback bracket around it (nothing is written, so nothing needs rolling back), and DELETE verify_after_apply's use at this site. The whole apply/rollback dance at mentl.mn:244-255 exists only to manufacture a write it then reads; the row algebra answers with no write at all. Fix = less code, exactly as Law 1 predicts. This also makes the design's INTERCONNECTION observation land correctly: widening `predicate_decide` is irrelevant to a gate that never consults a predicate, but row_subsumes is the gate the decidable fragment composes with.

CORRECTION 4 — the ownership half is a distinct read; name it, do not silently inherit it. AOwn/ARef take the `() => ()` arms (mentl.mn:271-272) and pass the gate having done nothing at all. Their honest gate is the use-count (arm 5), not the row — `Hβ.gradient.ownership-candidate-from-use-profile`, riding the `Hβ.infer.use-profile` peer PLAN §5.R band N already names (count_uses → grade + use-positions + escape-bit). Landing the row half without naming this leaves half the live candidate set still passing vacuously; a hidden gap is drift.

CORRECTION 5 — the gate that would actually fail. Compile a function whose body performs IO, project the teach verb over it, assert `gradient_next` does NOT return `APure`. That fails at HEAD today and passes only once the live row is read. It needs no ARefined, no hand-injection, no fixture that contradicts the site's own types. Blast radius: the compile pipeline never calls gradient_next (main.mn:61 imports oracle but invokes nothing from it), so m2 == m3 and the 71 micros are untouched; frontier 47/0 and proof-exactness 9/9 exercise the synth gate at synth_proposer.mn:137, which is the site that already does this correctly and is not being changed. The design says nothing about blast radius — add this.

CORRECTION 6 — the novelty verdict. Drop the greppability claim; this exercise refutes it. The Mentl-specific fact worth claiming is the opposite and stronger one: a proof gate that reads back its own write is invisible to grep and visible to the Universal Audit's first question — "is this fact computed, copied, snapshotted, or re-derived anywhere it could be read live?" — which answers itself at mentl.mn:154 in one read. The audit found the literal `true` and missed the tautology; the interrogation finds the tautology and does not care about the literal.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.verify.ledger-soundness
  verify.mn itself never assumes true anywhere — confirmed by reading every arm — but two OTHER sites do: a dormant oracle stub and a live gradient-suggestion path that never calls the refinement check that already exists elsewhere.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's headline asymmetry ("(1) dormant, (2) LIVE, land it now") is false: `ARefined` is never constructed anywhere in the repo, so fix (2)'s target arm is exactly as dead as fix (1)'s stub; and both proposed mechanisms are incoherent against the artifact (fix (1) checks a debt-entry class that is empty by construction; fix (2) passes a `narrowed_ty` that does not exist in scope).
  objection: FIX (2) IS NOT LIVE — the claim that ARefined is a reachable gradient path is refuted by grep. `ARefined` occurs 8 times repo-wide: mentl.mn:37 (the ADT declaration `| ARefined(String, Predicate, Option)`) and seven destructures (mentl.mn:197, 221, 265, 332, 432, 528, 570). ZERO constructions. Both callers of try_each_annotation build ARefined-free candidate sets: gradient_next at mentl.mn:155-157 (`[APure(None), ANotAlloc(None), ANotIO(None), ANotNetwork(None)] ++ ownership_candidates(params)`, and ownership_candidates at mentl.mn:387-394 emits only `[AOwn(name, None), ARef(name, None)]`); candidates_at (oracle.mn:213-221) routes through gradient_candidates_at (oracle.mn:232-238), which is the byte-identical set. So apply_annotation_tentatively's ARefined arm (mentl.mn:265-270) is unreachable dead code. The design's entire sequencing ("Fix (2) now, no dependency"; "This fix is live and reachable") rests on this misread, and by the design's OWN rule — "no gate lands until race is wired live (gating dead code teaches nothing)" — fix (2) is equally a precondition on an ARefined producer.

Three further independent kills, each fatal on its own:

(a) FIX (1)'s CHECK IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION. The design proposes "an outcome is verified iff no debt entry decided false during its window." The ledger NEVER records a false decision: verify.mn:38-41 is `Some(false) => { report_refinement_rejected(predicate, span, reason); resume() }` — reports and resumes CLEAN, no accrual; only `None` accrues (verify.mn:42-44). Debt holds undecidable obligations exclusively. "A debt entry that decided false" is the empty set, so the check returns true for every outcome — identical to the `true` stub it replaces. The design cites :42-44 correctly and then builds a fix contradicting the line it cites. Per the task's gate criterion, a check that passes vacuously is refuted.

(b) FIX (1)'s CITED WINDOW DOES NOT EXIST. The design says the fork window is "already threaded by try_handler_with_rollback, combinators.mn:297-303". Read it (combinators.mn:297-304): `fn try_handler_with_rollback(h, checkpoint) with GraphRead + GraphWrite = { ... h }` — the `checkpoint` param is accepted and never read; the body is literally `h` (line 303). No fork, no per-fork checkpoint, no window, and the "outcome" IS the handler value, carrying no proof state. `replay_winner` (combinators.mn:311-313) is likewise `winner`. race is a pass-through skeleton.

(c) FIX (2) DOES NOT TYPE-CHECK AND ITS ANALOGY IS BROKEN. The design says "call `refinement_admits(narrowed_ty, handle)`". try_each_annotation's scope (mentl.mn:244-255) holds exactly `handle`, `ann`, `base_ty` — there is no `narrowed_ty`, and the ARefined arm computes none (it returns verify()'s result, not a type). The claimed precedent synth_proposer.mn:137 (`verify_after_apply(h) && refinement_admits(target_ty, h)`) has different variable roles: there `target_ty` is the HOLE's type and `h` is the CANDIDATE VALUE's node handle. In try_each_annotation, `handle` is the FUNCTION node — NBound(TFun(...)) is the precondition to even reach the loop (mentl.mn:152-154). Passing base_ty hits verify.mn:145 `_ => true` (TFun is neither TAlias nor TRefined) → vacuous; passing a pred-bearing type runs subst_self(pred, fn_handle) → node_const(fn_handle) → None → verify.mn:143 `None => false` → rejects everything. Neither is the described check. "No new mechanism" conceals that the mechanism has no argument to receive.
  fixes    : 1. BOTH SITES ARE DORMANT — drop the asymmetry. ARefined has zero constructions (mentl.mn:37 decl + 7 destructures); both callers emit ARefined-free sets (mentl.mn:155-157 + :387-394; oracle.mn:232-238). Fix (2) is a PRECONDITION on an ARefined producer exactly as fix (1) is a precondition on Band K's oracle activation. Delete "Fix (2) now, no dependency" and "live and reachable".

2. WITHDRAW FIX (1)'s MECHANISM. Debt never records a false decision (verify.mn:38-41 reports and resumes clean; only :42-44's None arm accrues), so the proposed check is empty-set-vacuous. And there is no fork window (combinators.mn:297-304 discards `checkpoint`, returns `h`). The honest precondition on Band K is not "replace outcome_verified with a debt check" — it is "race was never built: try_handler_with_rollback returns h (:303), replay_winner returns winner (:312), and eight projections fabricate their answers. outcome_verified is one symptom of a skeleton."

3. FIX (2) IS A RESTRUCTURE, NOT AN ADDED CONJUNCT (Law 2). The real Carried-Truth defect the design missed: ARefined is the ONLY arm of apply_annotation_tentatively that does not BIND — every sibling routes through narrow_row → graph_bind (mentl.mn:261-264, :288). ARefined instead PERFORMS verify() into HANDLER state (mentl.mn:269). Handler state is not graph state: graph_rollback(cp) at mentl.mn:250 reverts the trail, NOT verify_ledger's `debt` (verify.mn:33, evolved via `resume() with debt = ...`). So the arm LEAKS obligations permanently past the rollback — falsifying both mentl.mn:267's "Ledger captures; rollback removes" and mentl.mn:374-375's "the trail-based rollback restores whatever state the handle held... so this check is non-destructive." The design's fix leaves the verify() call in place, preserving the leak. The form: bind TRefined(base, pred) like every sibling, delete the speculative verify(), and let verify_after_apply + refinement_admits read the bound type live. Then refinement_admits receives a real refined type and a real value handle. Less code, one mechanism.

4. THE GATE IS UNREACHABLE AS SPECIFIED. "a handle already bound to a literal -1" is not NBound(TFun(...)), so gradient_next falls to `_ => None` (mentl.mn:153-154, :167-169) and gradient_candidates_at returns [] (oracle.mn:237) — try_each_annotation never runs. The gate must be a REQUIRED fixture on the commit that first constructs an ARefined candidate, symmetric with what the design already concedes for (1).

5. INVERT THE NOVELTY VERDICT. "Exhaustively GREPPABLE... one targeted grep plus reading two call chains" is falsified by this audit: it missed `intent_matches` (combinators.mn:191-193) — a SECOND literal silent-`true` in the very file it audited, gating tiebreak_declared_intent (:84-87) — plus six more fabrications that are the same Carried-Truth violation at non-`true` types (`EfPure` :151, `Inferred("race-survivor")` :155, `Span(0,0,0,0)` :159, `""` :163, `1` :185, `0` :321), plus the two pass-through no-ops (:303, :312). A grep keyed on the literal `true` structurally cannot see a fabricated Span or a fabricated Reason. Also factually wrong: "combinators.mn is imported only by oracle.mn" — importers are lib/runtime/threading.mn, crucibles/crucible_oracle.mn, lib/runtime/search.mn, src/oracle.mn. (The dormancy conclusion survives via a stronger fact the design never checked: `race` has zero callers repo-wide.) The native form is not the grep — it is Hβ.audit.carried-truth-projection (PLAN band L): the medium projecting "this fn fabricates its answer" from the graph, which no text search can do. Name that peer.

6. FIX THE FOUNDATIONAL CLAIM. "verify.mn itself never assumes true anywhere — confirmed by reading every arm" is not confirmed. verify.mn:274 declares `handler verify_smt with witnesses = []` — no `debt` state — yet its SmtUnknown arm does `resume() with debt = [(span, predicate, reason)] ++ debt` (:299) and `verify_debt() => resume(verify_residual(debt, witnesses))` (:309). Grep confirms `debt` is bound only at verify.mn:33 as verify_ledger's state. Per SYNTAX.md's handler form (state follows `with`; the `with` clause on resume lists state updates by field name), verify_smt's `debt` is unbound. Cite this as an open defect (name it Hβ.verify.smt-handler-debt-state-unbound) rather than as the evidence that verify.mn is sound. Note also that verify_smt is never installed — only verify_ledger is (main.mn:278/341/504; pipeline.mn:74/153/169/195/213/239/265).

7. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS (the design is silent). Both sites are dead, so neither fix moves m2==m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, or the 71 micros. But fix (2) as literally written references `narrowed_ty`, an identifier not in scope at mentl.mn:244-255 — it would fail the build outright.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.verify.proof-incrementality-cached-cursor
  No caching exists today, and for the current literal-folding fragment that's fine; caching becomes load-bearing only once an SMT tier is real, where re-running a subprocess or module-call query on every keystroke would violate the O(1)-per-operation law at the one site meant to be interactive.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's key ("operand epoch") reads a per-node epoch that does not exist (graph.mn has ONE global bind-counter; types.mn:440-445's only per-node epoch is NFree's birth-stamp, destroyed at bind and read as `_` everywhere), it keys on the `nodes` weave while the operands live in the epoch-silent `program` weave, and its own gate would FAIL against the design correctly landed because verify is raised from inside inference where every bind bumps that global epoch.
  objection: THE PER-OPERAND EPOCH DOES NOT EXIST — the design reads a field that is not in the ADT, and its novelty verdict rests entirely on that misread.

The design's mechanism: "if neither operand's epoch has advanced since the last decide, the cached Some(true)/Some(false)/None is still valid." Its novelty verdict: "the graph already versions every node by epoch for exactly this purpose, as a KERNEL primitive ... this item is Verify finally using a primitive that already exists, not inventing one." Both are false against the artifact.

1. THE EPOCH IS ONE GLOBAL SCALAR, NOT PER-NODE.
   - graph.mn:58 — `epoch = 0` is a single field on graph_handler's state.
   - graph.mn:93-95 — `graph_epoch() => { resume(epoch) }` is the ONLY read op. There is no `graph_epoch_at(handle)`.
   - graph.mn:279 (graph_bind), :306 (graph_bind_row), :260 (graph_boundary_set) — `let new_epoch = epoch + 1`, bumped GLOBALLY on every bind.
   - types.mn:440-445 — `type NodeKind = NBound(Ty) | NFree(Int) | NRowBound(EffRow) | NRowFree(Int) | NErrorHole(Reason)`. `NBound` (a resolved node) carries NO epoch. The only per-node epoch is `NFree(Int)` — types.mn:442 comments it "epoch at allocation time": a BIRTH stamp on an UNRESOLVED node, destroyed by graph_bind (graph.mn:277 replaces `GNode(NFree(epoch), reason)` with `GNode(NBound(ty), reason)`), and pattern-matched as `_` at every one of its consumers (query.mn:99,101,391; mentl.mn:381,383; cursor.mn:557,559; pipeline.mn:564,566; eight_loop.mn:169,170; cursor_transport.mn:383). It is a stamp nothing reads.
   Implementing this design therefore requires ADDING a per-node last-modified epoch to GNode/NodeKind — new structure in the kernel's most load-bearing ADT. PLAN §2's "the IC cursor memoizes live reads by epoch" is the kernel's stated INTENT; the artifact implements a global bind-counter. The design read the prose and asserted the mechanism.

2. THE EPOCH TRACKS A DIFFERENT WEAVE THAN THE CACHE DEPENDS ON.
   - verify.mn:61 → :74 → :85 — `predicate_decide` → `compare_decide` → `node_const(handle) with GraphRead = match graph_node_body(handle)`.
   - graph.mn:111-113 — `graph_node_body(handle) => resume(if handle < len(program) { list_index(program, handle) } else { NHole(0) })` — reads the `program` weave (state field, graph.mn:67).
   - graph.mn:173-178 — `graph_register_node`, the program weave's writer, bumps NO epoch and writes NO trail (its own comment, :171-172: "No trail: the weave only grows at parse").
   The epoch counts binds in `nodes` (the union-find type cells). The design's soundness sentence — "invalidation is exactly 'the string changed', which only happens on an epoch advance" — is FALSE in the direction that kills a proof gate: the term a predicate reads can change with no epoch advance. And `graph_register_node` is not parse-only — egraph.mn:247-252 `mint_fold` calls it post-infer during saturation (`graph_fresh_ty` → `graph_register_node(h, NExpr(LitInt(v)))` → `graph_bind` → `graph_canon_set`). Epoch-silent graph writers, complete list: `graph_fresh_ty` (:126-144 — no `epoch =` in its resume-with clause, and it performs `graph_mutated(epoch, ...)` with the OLD epoch), `graph_fresh_row` (:146), `graph_register_node` (:173), `graph_attach_comment` (:188), `graph_canon_set` (:210), `graph_narrow_set` (:235). "Graph state changed ⟹ epoch advanced" is not an invariant this artifact holds.

3. THE GATE FAILS AGAINST THE DESIGN LANDED (not vacuous — contradictory).
   - infer.mn:3313 — `TRefined(_, pred) => verify(span, subst_self(pred, value_handle), Refinement(pred, PTrue))`, inside `apply_refinement_constraint`. Obligations are raised from INSIDE the inference walk.
   - Inference is the one writer; every unification's `graph_bind` does `epoch = epoch + 1` (graph.mn:279).
   So between any two verify raises in one compile the global epoch HAS advanced. A cache keyed on `(predicate identity, operand epoch = graph_epoch())` misses on the second lookup; the gate's counter increments TWICE; the gate fails even when the design is implemented exactly as written. Separately, there is no `smt_solve` handler in the repo to instrument: verify.mn:270-272 declares `effect SmtSolver { smt_solve(String) -> SmtResult }`, verify.mn:284 performs it, and nothing handles it — the gate instruments an arm that does not exist.

4. ARTIFACT_STATE MISREAD AT THE CITED LINE. The design claims "verify_ledger/verify_smt's debt/witnesses state (verify.mn:33, :274)". verify.mn:33 is `handler verify_ledger with debt = [] {` — correct. verify.mn:274 is `handler verify_smt with witnesses = [] {` — it declares ONLY `witnesses`, yet its arms reference an undeclared `debt` at :299 (`resume() with debt = [(span, predicate, reason)] ++ debt`) and :309 (`resume(verify_residual(debt, witnesses))`). No top-level `debt` binding exists (the only other occurrence is `debt_by_kind`'s parameter, :249). verify_smt has an undeclared-state bug, latent only because it is installed nowhere (every install site is `verify_ledger`: pipeline.mn:74,153,169,195,213,239,265; main.mn:278,341,504).

5. THE PEER'S OWN CANONICAL TEXT SPECIFIES THE OPPOSITE MECHANISM. docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md:194 — the full-detail home PLAN §5.R names for the 95 peers — defines this item as: "Cache-invalidation SOUNDNESS proven: a changed node invalidates EXACTLY the obligations whose witness depended on it (trail-backed, no stale-proof leak)." That is a dependency EDGE from obligation → the nodes its verdict read, invalidated by the trail's mutation record (graph.mn:37 "trail — flat mutation buffer"; :278 `trail_append(trail, trail_len, MSetNode(handle, old_node))`). The design replaces the edge with a stamp COMPARISON — re-deriving "did my operands change?" from a version number instead of following the edge the trail already draws. That is the Carried-Truth violation the design accuses its alternative of, and it silently contradicts the peer's own text.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE THE ITEM: the problem statement holds. `grep cache|memo src/verify.mn` returns zero hits; `debt = []` is genuinely fresh at all 10 install sites; `predicate_decide` genuinely re-runs from scratch; verify_smt is genuinely uninstalled and `smt_solve` genuinely unhandled, so the sequencing ("depends on smt-handler-swap to matter", "does not block smt-handler-swap shipping uncached") is honest and survives. The item deserves to exist. Its mechanism does not.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the epoch-comparison key. The design must say: the graph has ONE global epoch (graph.mn:58, bumped at :279/:306/:260), no per-node epoch (types.mn:440-445 — NBound carries none; NFree(Int) is a birth-stamp destroyed at bind and read as `_` at all 12 consumers), and no `graph_epoch_at(handle)` op. "(predicate identity, operand epoch)" is unimplementable as written, and keying on the global epoch means zero hits — verify is raised from inside inference (infer.mn:3313) where every graph_bind bumps it.

2. NAME THE REAL MECHANISM: the trail-backed dependency edge, as this peer's own canonical text already specifies (post-first-light-roadmap.md:194 — "a changed node invalidates EXACTLY the obligations whose witness depended on it (trail-backed, no stale-proof leak)"). At decide time, draw an edge from the obligation to the operand handles its verdict actually read; invalidate by walking the trail's own mutation record (graph.mn:37; `MSetNode(handle, old_node)` at :278), which already names exactly which handles changed. Follow the edge, do not re-read a stamp. The design must state this as its mechanism and drop the stamp comparison.

3. RETRACT the novelty verdict. "Verify finally using a primitive that already exists, not inventing one" is false: the per-node epoch does not exist, and building it is new structure in GNode/NodeKind. Worse, both the rejected borrowed form ("a bespoke memoization table keyed by ad-hoc predicate hashing") and the proposed replacement are the SAME shape — version-stamp memoization, i.e. Salsa's red-green scheme, which graph.mn:13 already cites by name ("Salsa 3.0 pattern: flat array + epoch + per-module overlay"). The design waves at the borrowed shape and keeps its mechanism. The genuinely native form is the trail edge — Mentl already logs every mutation for rollback, so proof-invalidation is the SAME log read in a different mode (the cursor in a different traversal mode, Anchor 3), and that argument — not "the epoch IS the cache-validity signal, by definition" — is the one that holds.

4. FIX THE WEAVE. State that `predicate_decide`'s operands are read via `graph_node_body` (verify.mn:85) → the `program` weave (graph.mn:111-113), whose only writer `graph_register_node` (graph.mn:173-178) bumps no epoch and writes no trail — and that it is NOT parse-only (egraph.mn:249 `mint_fold` writes it post-infer). So the validity signal for THIS cache is not the epoch at all; if the design keeps a trail-backed edge it must also say what makes the epoch-silent program/canon/narrow weaves invalidate, or the proof gate leaks a stale verdict on a changed term. Companion peer, positive form: Hβ.graph.program-weave-trailed-and-epoched (the co-indexed weaves join the one version discipline the `nodes` weave has).

5. FIX THE GATE. As written it fails against a correctly-landed design (two verify raises in one compile always straddle an epoch bump) and instruments an arm that does not exist (no SmtSolver handler anywhere; verify.mn:270-272 declares, :284 performs, nobody handles). The honest gate for a trail-backed edge is a SOUNDNESS gate, not a call-count: mutate one operand node, assert exactly the obligations whose verdict read that handle are invalidated and re-decided, and assert every other cached verdict survives — a crucible that FAILS at baseline (no cache) and FAILS louder on a stale-proof leak. The call-count check is the second leg, and it belongs after smt-handler-swap lands a real answerer.

6. CORRECT THE ARTIFACT_STATE at verify.mn:274. verify_smt declares `with witnesses = []` ONLY; the `debt` its arms reference at :299 and :309 is undeclared, and there is no top-level `debt` binding. Cite it as a latent bug in dead code (verify_smt is installed nowhere — all 10 install sites are verify_ledger), not as existing state this item would cache. Positive-form peer: Hβ.verify.smt-handler-debt-state-declared (the SmtUnknown residual needs a `debt` state field on verify_smt, or the two handlers' shared ledger needs one home) — it is a prerequisite of smt-handler-swap, and it should be named there, not discovered by this item.

7. STALE INTERCONNECTION. `Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive` has LANDED: lib/runtime/imap.mn ("imap — the ONE indexed-map primitive (Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive, §5.O)"), a str_hash-bucketed String→value `smap`, imported by src/infer.mn and src/lower.mn. So "ideally after indexed-map-primitive lands" is stale. But do NOT rekey onto smap: PLAN §11's own named-peer audit already ruled the smap-first sequencing INVERTED ("str_hash-keyed maps re-key by handle when interning lands — the migration paid twice; intern-at-lex leads, the maps land handle-keyed once"). A new smap-keyed proof cache is that migration paid a seventh time. The obligation→handle edge is handle-keyed from birth and needs no map at all.

8. BLAST RADIUS, unstated and required. A trail-backed proof-invalidation edge is written during inference, which the oracle runs under checkpoints (graph.mn:73-81, :115-118 `graph_push_checkpoint`) — so the edge must trail and roll back with the type nodes it names, exactly as `graph_boundary_set` does (graph.mn:255-269, whose comment names precisely this constraint) and unlike the untrailed canon/narrow weaves. Say so. Then say what the design says nowhere: whether it holds m2 == m3 (a cache that changes which obligations are re-decided must not change which diagnostics are emitted or in what order — `report_verify_debt` at verify.mn:239-244 prints a COUNT, so a double-accrual or a dropped accrual moves the emitted diagnostic and the fixpoint), and whether proof-exactness 9/9 (hole refuses / debt surfaces / suspension runs) still holds when debt is served from a cache.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.verify.proof-incrementality-cached-cursor
  No caching exists today, and for the current literal-folding fragment that's fine; caching becomes load-bearing only once an SMT tier is real, where re-running a subprocess or module-call query on every keystroke would violate the O(1)-per-operation law at the one site meant to be interactive.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design keys the cache on a per-operand epoch that does not exist, and the one global epoch that does exist (graph.mn:58, bumped only at :260/:279/:306) versions the `nodes` weave, not the `program` weave `predicate_decide` actually reads; the key is therefore simultaneously useless (over-invalidating) and unsound (under-invalidating) in a proof gate.
  objection: THE CACHE KEY IS VERSIONED BY A COUNTER THAT DOES NOT OBSERVE THE DATA THE CACHED FUNCTION READS.

Trace the read. `predicate_decide` (verify.mn:61) -> `compare_decide` (:74) -> `node_const` (:85) -> `graph_node_body(handle)` -> graph.mn:112, which reads `program`, NOT `nodes`. `program` is written by `graph_register_node` (graph.mn:173-178): no epoch bump, no trail, no `graph_mutated`. The epoch bumps at exactly three sites — graph.mn:260 (`graph_boundary_set`), :279 (`graph_bind`), :306 (`graph_bind_row`) — every one a write to `nodes`/`boundaries`, a DIFFERENT co-indexed weave. Two fatal consequences:
- OVER-INVALIDATING: the global epoch advances once per type bind (millions per self-compile) while the weave the verifier reads is static after parse. Every entry dies on every bind; the cache hits ~never and every probe adds work. It cannot deliver the latency it exists for.
- UNDER-INVALIDATING (unsound, in a PROOF GATE): `program` (graph.mn:173), `canon` (:210), and `narrowing` (:235) are ALL written with the epoch unchanged — and `narrowing` is what verify.mn:67-68 says "the SMT tier resolves live" for PBoolNode/PPatternMatch. So a cached `Some(true)` can outlive a change to the operand it proved on. That is the silent assume-true `Hβ.verify.ledger-soundness` exists to refuse (PLAN §5, the Dafny `{:axiom}` cautionary) — introduced by an item filed under proof.

The novelty verdict's central artifact claim is false: "the graph already versions every node by epoch" — it does not. There is ONE global counter (graph.mn:58 `epoch = 0`). No node carries a version. `NFree(epoch)` (:130) stamps a FREE node's birth epoch and that stamp is destroyed at bind (`GNode(NBound(ty), reason)`, :277). "Neither operand's epoch has advanced" is unimplementable as written.

Four more that independently refute:

1. THE SMT STRING KEY RE-DERIVES THE WORK IT CLAIMS TO SAVE, at the site the design says must be O(1). `predicate_to_smt_lib` (verify.mn:325) -> `predicate_to_smt` (:329) is a full recursive walk: `handle_to_smt` (:356) -> `graph_node_body` -> `body_to_smt` (:367), recursing through CallExpr args via a fold (`smt_arg_terms`, :400), BinOp (:406), UnaryOp (:412) — row `GraphRead + Memory + Alloc`. To look up by that string you must first BUILD it: whole-tree graph walk + O(size) string alloc + str-hash/str_eq. "(already computed)" is exactly backwards — the string is already computed only on the MISS path (as smt_solve's argument, :284); on the HIT path, the only path the cache exists for, you pay the full render to discover you didn't need it. The design's own motivating sentence invokes "the O(1)-per-operation law at the one site meant to be interactive" and then proposes an O(predicate-size) allocating key. PLAN §5.O: "a name is a HANDLE, not a byte-sequence... every table is handle-keyed (O(1) index, never a name scan)"; PLAN §11 col 3: "never str_hash-keyed twice". Worse, the render is not pure: `body_to_smt` (:376-379) and `smt_call` (:390-393) `report(EVerifyUnencodable(span_zero()))` — every probe on an unencodable predicate re-emits a diagnostic. A key that emits diagnostics is not a key.

2. THE GATE PASSES VACUOUSLY. "assert the counter increments once" after two verifies cannot distinguish the hypothesis from the null — counter==1 is equally consistent with "the cache worked" and with "the second verify never happened". Nothing establishes the same predicate is verified twice in one compile (`verify` is performed at construction sites: infer.mn:3313, infer.mn:4523, mentl.mn:269). And there is no arm to instrument: grep across src/lib/tools/tests finds `smt_solve` ONLY at verify.mn:271 (effect decl) and :284 (the perform) — zero handlers for SmtSolver. Finally, "instrument ... (removed after)" is not a gate: the repo's gates are `tools/*-gate.sh` that FAIL mechanically and run per-commit (PLAN §11 col 5, tools/ci/run-board.sh). A counter added-then-deleted is a probe.

3. NOVELTY IS A SLOGAN — the rejected shape IS the proposed shape. The design names the borrowed form ("a bespoke memoization table keyed by ad-hoc predicate hashing") then proposes a memoization table keyed by (identity, version), relabeled `Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive`. Only the label changed. The artifact's own native answer is a CO-INDEXED WEAVE, used five times already: `program`, `comments`, `canon`, `narrowing`, `boundaries` (graph.mn:67-71, :167, :188, :199, :228, :247), each "co-indexed by handle exactly like `nodes`", each O(1), each stating "No side-ledger" in its own comment (:206-208: "an already-rooted `from` overwrites its one cell, so saturation never grows an O(N²) pair-list"). PLAN §5.O layer 2 names this exact move twice for peers (`Hβ.lower.esc-row-on-node` — "a FIELD on the fn's node read live, the side-ledger deleted"; `Hβ.lower.reach-edge-on-node`), and PLAN §11's peer-audit verdict (1) says the str_hash-map waypoint sequencing is INVERTED — "the maps land handle-keyed once". Citing indexed-map-primitive adds a sixth consumer to a waypoint the audit says is paid twice. Band F already names the native home: `Hβ.verify.reason-edge-pcc-certificate` (PLAN:848).

4. INTERROGATION 8 DESCRIBES CODE THAT DOES NOT EXIST. "a cache hit must attach the SAME Reason node as the original decide" — `predicate_decide` (verify.mn:61-70) mints no Reason; it returns `Option(Bool)`. The Reason is a PARAMETER of the op (types.mn:1718 `verify(Span, Predicate, Reason) -> ()`) supplied by the caller. There is no decide-minted Reason to re-attach. Written from the text, not the artifact.

BLAST RADIUS — silent, and real. `predicate_decide` is emitted (11 hits in m2.wat) and `verify_ledger` is installed on every route (pipeline.mn:74/153/169/195/213/239/265; main.mn:278/341/504). Adding cache state changes the handler-state record arity — a representation change, so bytes shift: a TRANSITION (re-pin from m3), never Law-7 byte-identical. And verify_ledger already snocs `[(span, predicate, reason)] ++ debt` (:44) on the undecidable path ONLY; a cache puts a `++` on EVERY verify — the `acc ++ [X]` O(N²) class PLAN §9 names by name. A performance item that can measurably slow the compile. The design says none of this.

WHAT I TRIED THAT FAILED TO REFUTE: the observation itself holds. I grepped verify.mn for any memo — nothing (the only hits are `verify_residual`/`predicate_to_smt*`, no cache state). No cross-compile or cross-keystroke cache exists; verify_ledger's `debt` (:33) and verify_smt's `witnesses` (:274) are handler-local. The peer name is real (PLAN:848, band F). The sequencing-behind-smt-handler-swap is honest. And epoch-memoization as a PATTERN is genuinely live — oracle.mn:154-167 does exactly it (`with last_epoch = -1`; `if epoch == last_epoch`), which is sound THERE because the project queue is a whole-graph projection that any bind may change. It does not transfer to a per-predicate cache over a different weave.

ARTIFACT WRINKLE bearing on the cited state: the design says "verify_ledger/verify_smt's debt/witnesses state (verify.mn:33, :274)". verify_smt declares ONLY `with witnesses = []` (:274) — `debt` is not one of its fields, yet its arms reference it at :299 (`with debt = [...] ++ debt`) and :309 (`verify_residual(debt, witnesses)`). verify_smt has no debt state; it is never installed (only verify_ledger is), so it never surfaces — but the SMT handler this item is sequenced behind is incoherent as written.
  fixes    : The name survives; the mechanism must be replaced whole (Law 2 — restructure, not patch).

1. DELETE the epoch key. There is no per-operand epoch (graph.mn:58 is one global counter; `NFree(epoch)` at :130 dies at bind, :277). The design must NOT say "if neither operand's epoch has advanced" and must NOT say "the graph already versions every node by epoch". It must say: the graph carries ONE monotonic counter over the `nodes`/`boundaries` weave, and `predicate_decide` reads the `program` weave (graph.mn:112) — a weave the epoch does not observe (`graph_register_node`, :173-178, bumps nothing).

2. DELETE the SMT-string key. It is the byte-sequence key §5.O is deleting, it costs a full allocating graph walk per probe (verify.mn:329-428) at the site the design itself says must be O(1), and it re-emits `EVerifyUnencodable` (:377, :391) on every probe. If a solver round-trip is keyed at all, it is keyed by the predicate's HANDLE, after `Hβ.perf.name-is-handle` — never by rendered text.

3. THE NATIVE FORM: the verdict is an EDGE ON THE NODE, not an entry in a map. Follow `canon`/`narrowing` (graph.mn:199-235) exactly: a co-indexed verdict cell at the predicate's handle, `list_extend_to |> list_set`, read O(1) by `graph_*_at(handle)`, default 0 = undecided, "no side-ledger, one cell per handle (idempotent)". There is then NO key, NO render, NO hash, and NO invalidation logic — the fact is rewritten by the same write that changes the node. This is the move PLAN §5.O layer 2 already names twice (`Hβ.lower.esc-row-on-node`, `Hβ.lower.reach-edge-on-node`) and it is LESS code, not more. Do not cite `Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive` as the home; PLAN §11's peer audit says the map waypoint is paid twice, and a verdict-per-handle needs no map at all.

4. MERGE, don't multiply. `Hβ.verify.reason-edge-pcc-certificate` (PLAN:848) already names the proof-evidence edge. A discharge verdict IS that certificate's cheapest instance. State the interconnection as a MERGE candidate — one edge carrying (verdict, witness/Reason) — not as a cache depending on a map primitive.

5. FIX THE GATE or drop the item to a named-only peer. The stated gate cannot fail: counter==1 after two verifies is indistinguishable from counter==1 after one. It must be DIFFERENTIAL and script-resident in `tools/` (the repo's gate shape: frontier-gate.sh / proof-exactness-gate.sh / crown-gate.sh, run by tools/ci/run-board.sh per PLAN §11 col 5): same input, assert N solver calls without the edge and N-k with it, or count HITS directly. Instrumentation "removed after" is a probe, never a gate.

6. STATE BLAST RADIUS honestly: verify_ledger is installed on every route (pipeline.mn:74/153/169/195/213/239/265; main.mn:278/341/504) and `predicate_decide` is emitted (11 hits in m2.wat). Any state-arity change to verify_ledger is a TRANSITION (m2 != m3, re-pin from m3), not Law-7 byte-identical. And any accumulation must not put `++` on the decidable path — that is PLAN §9's `acc ++ [X]` O(N²) class.

7. FIX interrogation 8: `predicate_decide` mints no Reason (verify.mn:61-70); the Reason is the op's parameter (types.mn:1718), supplied by infer.mn:3313/:4523 and mentl.mn:269. The correct arm-8 answer is that the verdict edge CARRIES the Reason, which is why it is the PCC certificate and not a cache.

8. NAME, positively, what is NOT being built: `Hβ.verify.discharge-verdict-on-node` (the co-indexed verdict cell, sequenced after `Hβ.verify.smt-handler-swap` and merged with `.reason-edge-pcc-certificate`). And name the artifact debt this pass exposed, since a hidden gap is drift: `Hβ.verify.smt-handler-debt-state` (verify_smt references an undeclared `debt` at verify.mn:299/:309 while declaring only `witnesses` at :274 — wire or delete before any item is sequenced behind it), and `Hβ.verify.node-const-reads-raw-weave` (`node_const`, verify.mn:85, reads `program` directly and never follows the `canon` edge that egraph's `mint_fold` (egraph.mn:247-253) already drew, so a saturation-folded constant still reads undecidable — a live Carried-Truth violation worth more than any cache).
