REFUTED: Hβ.infer.instantiate-shares-never-clones
  instantiate stops re-scanning an allocating filter per type-variable leaf and, in its larger and separately-sequenced half, stops rebuilding tree structure no quantified variable ever touches — on a handle domain (type-variable graph handles) already Int and therefore independent of items 1 and 2.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the subst_ty half snapshots at Ty-construction a fact `subst_ty` reads live via graph_chase (infer.mn:3111-3112), which the artifact's own comments (3105-3110, 3129-3134) record as an already-fixed let-poly bug, and the find_mapping half rests on an invariant ("at most one can ever match") that free_in_ty's non-deduping multiset (2976-2991 -> 2920 -> 3077) contradicts outright.
  objection: THE SUBST_TY HALF CACHES, AT CONSTRUCTION, A FACT THE GRAPH HOLDS LIVE — the Carried-Truth Law violated by the item whose banner is the Carried-Truth Law.

`subst_ty`'s TVar arm reads the graph LIVE (src/infer.mn:3111-3112):
    match graph_chase(id) {
      GNode(NBound(inner), _) => subst_ty(inner, mapping),
A `TVar(id)` node whose construction-time reachable set is {id} reaches {j,k} at substitution time once unification binds `id` to a type containing j and k. The Ty tree is immutable; its POINTEES are not. So the design's O(1) intersection test against a construction-time set computes "empty intersection", shares the subtree, and SKIPS a substitution that must happen — j and k never freshen, and every instantiation of the scheme shares vars that must be distinct. Let-polymorphism breaks silently.

This is not speculative. The artifact records it as a bug that already happened, twice, in comments at the exact arms the design proposes to change:
  - infer.mn:3105-3110: "Read the LIVE node, not the raw captured TVar (union-find / Carried-Truth). A var free at generalize but BOUND after (a handler config-param the arm-walk linked to the payload) must substitute its RESOLVED type, so the payload freshens WITH it. ... Was: find_mapping on the raw id — left bound/aliased vars stale, breaking the config↔payload link."
  - infer.mn:3129-3134: "Dropping v here shared the SAME row tail across every instantiation of an open-record scheme — a proven let-poly asymmetry (generalize quantifies v; instantiate must not strand it). Carried-Truth: the free_in_ty <-> subst_ty symmetry was broken at this arm."
And `generalize` itself must `chase_deep(ty)` (infer.mn:2919) before `free_in_ty` — the artifact stating outright that a stored Ty tree's var content is not determined by its shape.

The design's Interrogation-1 answer inverts this: it says the graph "does not yet carry that fact anywhere, so it must be added at construction." The artifact's answer is that the graph carries it live via graph_chase, and the reason it is not cached is that the cache would be WRONG. The design read the absence of a snapshot as an absence of the fact. Salvaging it by chasing each set member at test time costs O(|set|) and re-unions through bindings — the walk it claims to delete. The O(1) claim dies with the soundness.

SECOND, INDEPENDENT, AND FACTUAL — the stated invariant "at most one can ever match" is FALSE:
  - free_in_ty (infer.mn:2976-2991) does not dedup: TVar(id) => [id], aggregates ++/flatten.
  - generalize (infer.mn:2920): Forall(free_in_ty(body_ty), body_ty) — no dedup.
  - build_inst_mapping (infer.mn:3077-3088): map over qs — no dedup.
So qs is a MULTISET. `fn id(x) = x` : TFun([a], a, eff) yields qs = [a, a] -> mapping [(root_a, fresh1), (root_a, fresh2)]: same key, two different fresh vars. `map(f: a -> b, xs: [a]) -> [b]` yields qs = [a, b, a, b] — four mints, two of them orphaned graph nodes no reader can ever reach. The design's proposed walk happens to preserve behavior only because head(xs) = xs[0] (lib/prelude.mn:206) and filter_collector is order-preserving (lib/prelude.mn:100-109) — i.e. the mechanism survives by accident while the justification is backwards, in a codebase whose own SYNTAX.md ruling records that its list primitives read the SNOC end and that the borrowed cons-vocabulary already cost one real bug.

THIRD — GATE VACUITY, and it is worse than "passes today." `verify.sh` + `march.sh` are green on the current tree, so they cannot fail for want of this change. They also cannot catch the one error the false invariant invites: a last-match walk still returns a fresh var, both generations still compile the same source with the same emit, and m2 == m3 still holds (PLAN §7's io precedent: "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation"). The gate is blind in exactly the direction the design is wrong.

FOURTH — the novelty citation argues AGAINST the design. Rémy-style level-based generalization tracks a single INT per type VARIABLE, updated at UNIFICATION (the write), to optimize GENERALIZE. It is not a per-node free-variable SET frozen at CONSTRUCTION to optimize SUBSTITUTION. The design cites it as "kept, not reinvented" while proposing the one variant the precedent specifically is not — and the difference (write-time maintenance vs. construction-time snapshot) is precisely what makes the design unsound.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE THE DESIGN: its rejection of graph_bind-ing the mapping holds — one scheme is instantiated at many sites needing different fresh vars, so a permanent binding is sound for exactly one instantiation, ever. (Its other premise for that rejection, "the graph is append-only and monotonic (PLAN §6)", is a misread — §6 says the BUMP ALLOCATOR is monotonic and never frees; nodes are bound after creation, which is what graph_chase is for. Right conclusion, one bad premise — and the same bad premise is what makes the construction-time set look safe.) Its priority honesty also holds and matches PLAN §5.O's own corrected lesson. And its correction of PLAN's stale line numbers (§5.O cites infer.mn:2687/2840; the live sites are 3063/3218) is accurate — that is PLAN's drift, not the design's.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE THE MULTISET FIRST — this is the "fix is always LESS code" move the design walked past. `free_in_ty` (infer.mn:2976-2991) returns a multiset; nothing dedups it through `generalize` (infer.mn:2920) or `build_inst_mapping` (infer.mn:3077-3088). A free-variable collection IS a set, and PLAN §4① already rules that the ordered-keyed-set implementations unify into ONE operation over sequence/product. Make `free_in_ty` return a root-keyed SET and four things fall out at once: "at most one can ever match" becomes TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION (the design's invariant, earned instead of assumed); `build_inst_mapping` stops minting orphan graph nodes (2 of 4 for `map`, 1 of 2 for `id`) — a mint whose result no reader can ever reach is a fabrication, deleted not wrapped (Law 2); the scan shrinks at the domain rather than at the mechanism; and find_mapping's ordering question dissolves rather than being answered. Positive-form peer: Hβ.infer.free-vars-is-a-root-set. Sequence this BEFORE find_mapping's mechanism — it is the Carried-Truth read (the graph holds roots; free_in_ty re-derives them as a duplicated list), where the filter swap is only the allocation.

2. IF find_mapping's walk lands before the set: it must be FIRST-match, and the design must say WHY — `head(xs) = xs[0]` (lib/prelude.mn:206) and `filter_collector` appends at `count` then slices 0..count (lib/prelude.mn:100-109), so today's result is the first match over a duplicated domain. Not "at most one can ever match." The distinction is load-bearing in exactly this codebase: SYNTAX.md's vocabulary ruling records that Mentl's own list primitives read the SNOC end and that the borrowed cons-vocabulary already billed one real bug (the env-orientation truncation). A last-match walk is the natural error here and the design's stated invariant invites it.

3. THE SUBST_TY SHARING FACT IS MAINTAINED AT THE WRITER, NEVER AT CONSTRUCTION. The graph has exactly one writer (inference). A fact about which vars a subtree reaches changes when `graph_bind` binds a var — so it is maintained at the bind, or it is read live, and there is no third option. Construction-time is the one placement that is unsound. Two sound forms, both already named by the docs: (a) maintain it at unification the way Rémy's levels actually do — an int per VARIABLE updated at bind, not a set per NODE frozen at construction (this is the design's own cited precedent, read correctly, and it argues against the design's mechanism); or (b) PLAN §2's cached cursor — an EPOCH-versioned memo, where "read live is the semantics and cached is the mechanism, never hand-rolled." Note (b) is weak during inference: every bind bumps the epoch, so the memo invalidates constantly — which is itself the artifact's answer to why no cache exists here. Positive-form peer for the honest version: Hβ.infer.reach-set-maintained-at-bind.

4. PLAN §6's monotonicity premise does not say what the design uses it to say. §6: "Bump allocator, monotonic, never frees (determinism = fixpoint)" — that is ALLOCATION monotonicity. Node binding (NFree → NBound) is mutation of the pointee behind a stable handle; the whole union-find substrate depends on it. The design's REJECTION of graph_bind-ing the mapping is nonetheless CORRECT — keep it — but on its second premise only: one scheme is instantiated at many sites needing different fresh vars, so a permanent binding is sound for exactly one instantiation. Drop the monotonicity premise; it is the same misread that makes the construction-time set look safe.

5. THE GATE MUST MEASURE, NOT PASS. `verify.sh` + `march.sh` are green on the current tree, so they cannot fail for want of this change — that is the no-regression signal PLAN §9.7 says to KEEP, not the gate it says to WRITE. Worse, it is blind to the specific error the design's false invariant invites: a last-match walk still yields a fresh var, both generations still compile the same source with the same emit, and m2 == m3 still holds (the PLAN §7 io precedent: "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation"). Use the design's own subst_ty instrument for BOTH halves — the §8 perf recipe, asserting allocation volume, plus an assertion that the mapping domain contains no duplicate root once correction 1 lands (that one CAN fail today, which is what makes it a gate).

6. KEEP, unchanged: the priority honesty. "This item's architectural validity is confirmed by reading, its current dominance is not, and must be measured before it is prioritized ahead of items 1-2" is exactly PLAN §5.O's own corrected lesson — the 8-agent code-read named instantiate/find_mapping and then MISSED every dominant cost; the seven perf iterations (classifier, region tracker, esc write, esc read, reachability, iterate) never touched instantiate. The design is right to refuse to sequence itself on a code-reading estimate.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.perf.name-is-handle
  Every compiler-internal identifier becomes one dense Int handle, interned exactly once at lex, so every downstream identity question — env lookup, effect-row membership, esc/base/reach/summary map keys — becomes a direct i32 comparison or array read instead of a str_hash/str_eq scan.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design names one gate as the thing it "stands or falls on" (146 false diagnoses → 0) and the artifact proves that gate unreachable by this mechanism: of the 161 live residual diagnoses, ZERO are name-identity failures, so content-addressed interning (extensionally identical to the str_eq already at every leaf) cannot move the number — and the repo's own 8-agent audit already overturned this exact framing in cc487f87's commit message.
  objection: THE GATE IS UNREACHABLE BY THE MECHANISM, AND THE REPO ALREADY MEASURED THIS.

I compiled the wheel through the pinned boot (boot/mentl.wasm, 40,743 lines, exit=0) and counted:
- LIVE residual: 161 (95 E_EffectMismatch + 66 E_PurityViolated) — NOT the cited 146 (85+61). That figure is stale prose from 2026-07-14 (cc487f87 / PLAN §7), presented in ARTIFACT STATE as "Measured residual on the wheel's own self-compile".
- IDENTITY FAILURES (declared == inferred textually yet still failing): **0 of 161**. Not one.
- 127/161 (79%) carry an UNRESOLVED ROW VARIABLE, e.g. `E_PurityViolated ... expected Pure but found effects: Memory + r109526@e22332`. A row VAR is not a name; interning is a structural no-op on it.
- The remaining 34 are genuine set-membership failures where the shared names ALREADY MATCH: `GraphWrite + GraphRead vs GraphWrite + GraphRead + Memory + r` (17×), `GraphRead vs Alloc + Memory + GraphRead + r` (7×), `EnvRead vs EnvRead + Alloc + Memory + WasmOut` (3×). `GraphRead` matching `GraphRead` across the gate is POSITIVE PROOF that by-name identity works today. The failure is the extra Memory/Alloc/WasmOut — the builtin-state-init pollution class (the project's own memory: "hoeffect ROOT PINNED — builtin state init: Memory/Alloc in handler STATE INIT pollutes the residual row") plus the unclosed open tail.

WHY THE MECHANISM CANNOT MOVE IT (structural, not empirical): interning is content-addressed, so intern(a) == intern(b) IFF bytes(a) == bytes(b). That is EXTENSIONALLY IDENTICAL to `str_eq(eff_name_str(a), eff_name_str(b))` already sitting at every comparison leaf — effects.mn:892 (name_set_contains_loop), :765 (name_set_prefix_contains_loop), :373 (name_in_forbidden) — and :690/:691 route list_subset_str/list_disjoint through those same leaves, so BOTH the positive and negative arms of row_subsumes already bottom out in content equality. Interning changes the comparison's SPEED, never its ANSWER. cc487f8 went 598→146 precisely BECAUSE it harvested the entire identity class; the residue is definitionally what is left once identity is already correct.

THE REPO RULED ON THIS TWICE, IN WRITING, AND THE DESIGN ENGAGES NEITHER:
- `git log -1 cc487f87`: "An 8-agent adversarial audit OVERTURNED the destiny-audit framing that names-are-handles (R1) clears the 594 self-compile false effect-mismatches. Measured: ~80% were an unresolved-row-var class dying at row_subsumes' EfClosed-gate vs EfOpen-body => _ => false arm." My 127/161 = 79% reproduces that measurement exactly.
- `git log -1 672a9e42`: "EffName-is-a-handle DROPS NO CENSUS, so value-proof won per-effort... bank... EffName-is-a-handle as the exact-edit, BYTE-IDENTICAL perf-cleanup remainder."

This is CLAUDE.md ⊕ corollary 1 violated verbatim: "A probe disproved you? Do NOT crown the next thing you see as the root." The probe (cc487f8) disproved names-are-handles as the mismatch root; the design re-crowns it.

WHAT I TRIED AND COULD NOT REFUTE (the design is strong here): every artifact-state citation verified TRUE — types.mn:402-408 (EffName ADT), effects.mn:419-438 (EfNeg arm), :872-894 (str_eq leaf), :888-891 (names its own destination verbatim), lib/runtime/imap.mn (smap str_hash bucket), own.mn:239-264 (region_index buckets i32_and(handle,65535) — NO string hash), wasm.mn:191-232 (the seventh copy), pipeline.mn:399-436 (env bespoke), lexer.mn:214-222 (TIdent arm), parser.mn:152-157 (ident_at's `""` fabrication). Commit order confirmed: `git log --reverse` shows 9feb727b (string interner) precedes 2c1e9b56 (smap). The REGION_INDEX CORRECTION IS A GENUINE CATCH and PLAN §5.O is wrong exactly where the design says: it lists "six hand-rolled copies (summary/region/env/base/esc/reach)" as str_hash waypoints, but own.mn:239-264 buckets by handle with no str_hash — region_index is the exemplar, not a member. The seventh-copy find (wasm.mn's string_index, never retrofitted onto smap) is real. The mechanism (Name = transparent-alias Int, intern at lex, one atomic edit mirroring the TCont arity change) is sound and IS PLAN §5.O layer 1. I refute the PAYOFF, the GATE, the BLAST-RADIUS CAUSE and the NOVELTY DIFFERENTIATOR — not the mechanism.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the correctness claim; restate as PERF + CONSOLIDATION. The design must say: this closes ZERO of the 161 residual diagnoses. Evidence: 0/161 are identity failures; 127/161 carry an unresolved row var; the other 34 are Memory/Alloc/WasmOut set-membership failures whose shared names already match. Re-file the 161 under its true root — the builtin-state-init row pollution + the unclosed EfOpen tail (cc487f87's "EfClosed-gate vs EfOpen-body => _ => false" arm) — as SEPARATE named peers, e.g. Hβ.infer.builtin-state-init-row-pollution and Hβ.effects.open-tail-closure-at-callsite. Neither is this item.

2. INVERT THE GATE to byte-identity. GATE currently says "expect m2 != m3 (the residual-146 closure changes which fns pass their own declared row, hence emitted evidence-threading), REQUIRE m3 == m4, re-pin boot from m3." The stated CAUSE is false: no closure occurs, so no fn changes which declared row it passes, so evidence-threading is unchanged. 672a9e42 banked this exact edit as "byte-identical". The correct gate: **m2 == m3 MUST HOLD**; the residual count MUST stay at its live baseline (161, re-measured, not 146); crown 5/5, 71 micros, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9 unchanged. As written the design pre-authorizes m2 != m3 as expected and instructs re-pinning boot on it — converting the sharpest correctness signal the repo owns into expected noise, and re-opening the bless-the-wrong-generation surface Hβ.tools.march-transition-native exists to close. (PLAN §5.O does flag a possible handle-order TRANSITION at layer 1 — if that is the expectation, say THAT, and prove the shift is order-only; never attribute m2 != m3 to a closure that cannot occur.)

3. FIX THE NOVELTY VERDICT — its differentiator is the refuted claim. "Interning here is correctness-load-bearing, not merely fast: it is what makes the crown's !E negation sound... no mainstream compiler's interning carries that role" is FALSE. The crown is ALREADY sound by-name (29df478, crown 5/5) and stays sound under interning by extensional identity. So interning here IS merely fast — exactly the textbook role (sys.intern, LLVM StringPool) the design claims to transcend. The honest novelty residue, which survives and is worth keeping: (a) the three-way map-shape dispatch derived from each domain's proven boundedness (dense-bounded Name array / sparse-unbounded handle bucket / unbounded content hash) as "the proof becomes the dispatch" applied to map shape; (b) the region_index correction to PLAN §5.O; (c) the seventh-copy find. Drop the "surpasses mainstream interning" frame.

4. UNBUNDLE THE FALSE-DEPENDENCY DELETION. The design credits interning with collapsing forbidden_names_disjoint/name_in_forbidden into "an exact duplicate of name_set_disjoint/name_set_contains — deleted, not patched." They are ALREADY exact duplicates TODAY: forbidden_names_disjoint (effects.mn:363-369) loops the body applying eff_name_str and calls name_in_forbidden (:371-374, `str_eq(eff_name_str(list_index(forbidden,i)), s)`); name_set_disjoint (:857-864) loops and calls name_set_contains (:872-893, `str_eq(eff_name_str(...), eff_name_str(...))`). Same predicate; they differ only in WHERE eff_name_str is applied. That Carried-Truth deletion needs no interning and no atomic edit — it is available now, and belongs to its own landing. Removing it from this item's payoff shrinks the "LESS code" claim to what interning actually earns.

5. RESOLVE THE env/nmap GAP the design names then walks past. ARTIFACT STATE correctly says env stays bespoke "because smap's plain Option can't carry the pos<count liveness re-check scope_exit needs" — then DESIGN migrates env onto nmap, "a true flat array indexed directly by the handle, zero collision scanning, strictly stronger than any bucket." The artifact refuses that: env_index_add (pipeline.mn:407-410) PREPENDS (name,pos) per binding, so the bucket is a per-name SHADOWING STACK; env_bucket_pos (:413-421) returns the first entry with `pos < count` (the liveness filter); env_resolve (:425-434) re-validates against the buffer and falls back to env_find_flat because "scope_exit can reuse a position and stale a bucket entry". One slot per Name cannot represent shadowing. nmap works only if its VALUE is the per-name chain (a real win: no hash, no cross-name collisions) — but then "zero collision scanning" is FALSE (the per-name liveness scan is inherent to shadowing, not to hashing) and "strictly stronger than any bucket" is an overclaim for env. State nmap's value type, and say whether the stale-entry linear fallback survives.

6. RE-MEASURE THE BASELINE, DON'T CITE IT. Replace "Measured residual... 146 (85+61)" with the live number (161 = 95+66 on this tree) obtained by running the compile. The design cited 2026-07-14 prose as a measurement — the exact drift PLAN §5.O's own correction warns against ("perf the artifact, never the estimate").

7. ENUMERATE THE LITERAL-MINTED EffName SITES. "Intern at lex" covers identifiers born in the lexed source, but any EffName minted from a compiler-source string literal (a builtin like Memory/Alloc) never passes lex_from. Those mint sites need intern_name at the mint. HM will catch them (String vs Int is a type error), so the atomic-edit discipline holds — but the grep-inventory rule should name this class explicitly rather than leave it to the typechecker to discover.

REFUTED: Hβ.verify.smt-handler-swap
  The SMT rendering pipeline is complete and sound; the entirely missing piece is a real answerer, and under today's WASI-only substrate that answerer cannot be "shell out to z3" — it has to be a linked WASM module or a host-mediated two-pass bridge, named as target and fallback respectively.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's premise ("the SMT rendering pipeline is complete and sound; the entirely missing piece is a real answerer") is false in four independent ways: the SAT/UNSAT polarity is inverted so a real answerer would silently discharge unproven obligations; `verify_smt` does not resolve its own state (`debt` is a free variable, and the wheel's own census says so twice); the `false` sentinel is a subterm that `PNot` flips into a fabricated proof; and the rendered SMT-LIB carries no declarations, so every string that reaches `smt_solve` would make a real solver error rather than answer.
  objection: THE POLARITY IS INVERTED — the pipeline is unsound, and an answerer wired to it silently discharges unproven obligations.

`predicate_to_smt_lib` (verify.mn:325-327) renders `(assert P)\n(check-sat)`, which asks *is P satisfiable* (∃M. M⊨P). The handler maps `SmtSat(witness) => resume()` (verify.mn:285-287 — the discharge path, no report) and `SmtUnsat => report_refinement_rejected` (verify.mn:288-291). A verification obligation asks *is P valid* (∀M. M⊨P), proven by checking `(assert (not P))` UNSAT. Satisfiability does not imply validity.

Failure scenario, end to end: `type ValidPort = Int where self >= 1024 && self <= 65535`; `let p: ValidPort = user_input`. `subst_self` (verify.mn:101-117) swaps `self` for the `user_input` VarRef node; `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) returns None; `compare_decide` (verify.mn:74-80) returns None; the None arm reaches `smt_solve`. Rendered: `(assert (and (>= user_input 1024) (<= user_input 65535)))\n(check-sat)`. Z3 answers **sat**, model `user_input=1024`. Handler: SmtSat → `resume()` → obligation DISCHARGED, with "proved by SMT witness: user_input=1024" (types.mn:2124) attached as a walkable Reason. `user_input` may be 80. This is `Hβ.verify.ledger-soundness` violated — "no silent assume-true; the Dafny `{:axiom}` cautionary" (PLAN §5.R band F) — by the very handler verify.mn:292-296 comments as refusing it.

Proof this is a bug and not a deliberate reading: the SAME handler's decidable arm (verify.mn:277-282) calls `predicate_decide`, which answers *is P true*, and maps `Some(true)→resume` / `Some(false)→reject`. Two arms of one handler, answering validity and satisfiability, mapped to identical outcomes.

THREE MORE, each independently fatal to "complete and sound":

(1) `verify_smt` does not compile — and the medium already said so. verify.mn:274 declares `handler verify_smt with witnesses = []`; no `debt` field. verify.mn:299 (`with debt = [...] ++ debt`) and verify.mn:309 (`resume(verify_residual(debt, witnesses))`) reference `debt`. verify_ledger's `debt` (verify.mn:33) is a *different handler declaration's* state record field — handler state is per-declaration, never shared. `.build/m2cache/m2.err` carries exactly two `infer: E_MissingVariable error: debt`, at wheel lines 32862:58-62 and 32872:28-32, which I mapped byte-identically to verify.mn:299 and verify.mn:309 (cached wheel region diffs clean against live source; verify.mn is at HEAD). The design believed the comment at verify.mn:295-296 ("It lives in the SAME debt ledger verify_ledger keeps") instead of reading the state declaration 25 lines above it. This kills the design's central claim outright: "Installation is trivial once an answerer exists: `~> verify_smt` ... source unchanged" is false — source must change first.

(2) "Never a fabricated `true`, confirmed by reading every arm" is refuted by composition. The `_` arm (verify.mn:376-379) renders the string `"false"` for a SUBTERM, and `predicate_to_smt` is compositional: `PNot(inner) => "(not {predicate_to_smt(inner)})"` (verify.mn:334), `PBoolNode(n) => handle_to_smt(n)` (verify.mn:335). `body_to_smt` enumerates only LitInt/LitFloat/LitString/LitBool/VarRef/CallExpr/BinOpExpr/UnaryOpExpr — so `IfExpr`, `MatchExpr`, `IndexExpr`, `BlockExpr`, `MakeRecordExpr`, `NamedRecordExpr`, `RecordUpdateExpr`, `LambdaExpr`, `LitUnit` (all types.mn `type Expr`) hit `_`. Then `type T = Int where !(if self > 100 { true } else { false })` renders `(not false)` = `true` → `(assert (not false))` → **sat** → DISCHARGED for self=200. At even negation depth the same sentinel fabricates a REFUTATION: bare `PBoolNode(IfExpr)` → `(assert false)` → unsat → `E_RefinementRejected` on a program whose refinement HOLDS, which PLAN §0's sound-and-incomplete stance forbids (unencodable must be `V_Pending`, never a verdict in either direction). The `_ => "true"` fabrication that PLAN §7 and tests/micros/mn-refine-narrow.mn:17 record as DELETED was not deleted — it was relocated one operator away and made parity-dependent. The design's own confidence phrase names the method that failed: the arms are individually clean; the composition is not. Separately, `smt_call` (verify.mn:386-396) splices the same `"false"` into an application head — `(false arg1 arg2)` — ill-sorted, so a real solver errors rather than answering unsat. Not unsat-forcing either.

(3) The rendered SMT-LIB is not a well-formed script for exactly the class that reaches it. `predicate_to_smt_lib` emits no `(set-logic …)`, no `(declare-const …)`/`(declare-fun …)`, no `(get-model)`. Free symbols reach the renderer via `NExpr(VarRef(name)) => name` (verify.mn:372) and uninterpreted callees via `smt_call`. But `compare_decide` sends only the NON-constant case to None — so every string reaching `smt_solve` carries at least one undeclared symbol, and Z3 answers `(error "unknown constant …")`: neither SmtSat nor SmtUnsat nor SmtUnknown. With no `(get-model)`, `SmtSat(String)`'s witness payload has no source.

GATE — the design's own real-swap gate cannot pass, even with a perfect answerer. It demands "zero V_Pending and a VerifyWitness Reason attached". But `verify_residual` (verify.mn:318-323) returns `debt ++ map(w → (span, pred, VerifyWitness(model)), witnesses)`, and its consumer is `report_verify_debt(verify_debt())` (pipeline.mn:120) → `report(VPending(len(debt)))` (verify.mn:239-244). Every witness IS an element of the list whose length is the V_Pending count, so zero V_Pending ⟺ zero witnesses. The design did not trace the residual to its consumer.

NOVELTY — "absorbs Hβ.verify.reason-edge-pcc-certificate entirely" is a category error, and self-contradictory inside the design. PCC emits a checkable proof object (unsat core, resolution/LFSC term, Z3 `(get-proof)`) that a third party re-checks WITHOUT trusting the solver. The design states, correctly and creditably, that an answerer "adds Z3 ... to the trust base". PCC exists precisely to remove it. A SAT model is not a certificate — it is what you get when the proof fails; under correct polarity it is a counterexample attached to a REJECTION, so it cannot be the discharge's certificate. The peer is untouched, and it is the one item that would answer the trust-base cost the design honestly names. Lesser overclaim: "different pipeline stages run different proof strength from the SAME source — Dafny/F* each have one monolithic verifier" omits Why3, whose entire architecture routes each verification condition to any of dozens of provers; Dafny also swaps solvers (`/prover:`) and scopes per-method (`{:verify false}`, `{:timeLimit}`).

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTEMPT TO REFUTE IT: the install-site census is exactly right — `~> verify_ledger` at main.mn:278/341/504 and pipeline.mn:74/153/169/195/213/239/265 (seven sites, as claimed); `~> verify_smt` appears nowhere; nothing answers `smt_solve` (decl verify.mn:270-272, call verify.mn:284); z3/cvc5/bitwuzla only in comments (verify.mn:8, types.mn:1100); VerifyWitness at types.mn:617 (render is 2124, design said 2125 — immaterial). The substrate argument is TRUE and is the design's best contribution: WASI preview1 has no fork/exec, and tools/wt-env.sh's `WT_RUN_FLAGS=(-W threads=y -W shared-memory=y -W tail-call=y -S threads=y)` carries no process capability, so "the answerer cannot be shell-out-to-z3 from inside the guest" is correct; the argument that a WASM-to-WASM crossing needs no host import and is therefore strictly weaker than Band N's native-syscall seam — and so must not sequence behind the native backend — is sound. The trust-base correction to PLAN §0 ("bounded, standard, reversible, paid only where installed") is right and better than PLAN's own framing, which makes "absorbs PCC entirely" two paragraphs later the sharper contradiction.
  fixes    : 1. INVERT THE PREMISE. Strike "the SMT rendering pipeline is complete and sound; the entirely missing piece is a real answerer." The pipeline is unsound (polarity), incomplete (no declarations/logic/get-model), compositionally unsafe (the `false` sentinel), and the handler does not resolve its own state. The answerer is the LEAST urgent piece: wiring one to today's pipeline produces the worst outcome the repo admits — silent discharge of unproven obligations, dressed in a Reason edge that says "proved by SMT witness". Correct framing: the pipeline is a SKETCH that has never been executed by anything, and its errors are exactly the errors an unexecuted proof gate accumulates.

2. INVERT THE SEQUENCING. Pipeline correctness is the prerequisite, not the follow-on, and all of it is landable NOW with no answerer, no WASM-Z3, no host bridge:
   - `handler verify_smt with debt = [], witnesses = []` — declare the state the arms already assume (deletes both E_MissingVariable).
   - Polarity: render `(assert (not P))`; `SmtUnsat => resume()` (discharged); `SmtSat(model) => report_refinement_rejected` with the model as the counterexample Reason; `SmtUnknown => debt`. This also makes `VerifyWitness` mean what types.mn:619 already calls it — a counterexample — and attaches it to the rejection where it belongs.
   - The unencodable sentinel: DELETE it, do not relocate it (Law 2 — a surrender-fallback is deleted, never wrapped). `body_to_smt` must not return a String for a node it cannot encode. Render as `Option(String)`, or thread the refusal in the row, so an unencodable subterm short-circuits the WHOLE obligation to SmtUnknown → debt. That is LESS code — two sentinel arms and two `report(EVerifyUnencodable)` splices deleted — and it makes the parity bug unsayable rather than patched.
   - `verify_residual`: stop folding witnesses into the list `report_verify_debt` counts. Debt and evidence are two projections; conflating them into one list is the side-ledger drift, and it is what makes the design's own gate unpassable.
   - The declaration preamble is Carried-Truth-POSITIVE, not new machinery: the free symbols are already HANDLES in the graph with types the inference proved (`lookup_ty`), so `(declare-const <name> <sort>)` is a projection of the predicate's own operand nodes, read live. Follow the edge; do not re-derive a symbol table.

3. REPLACE THE GATE. The proposed render-correctness unit test is too weak (it would pass against an inverted polarity), and the proposed real-swap gate is unpassable. Three gates that FAIL TODAY and need no answerer:
   - STATE: `grep -c 'E_MissingVariable error: debt' .build/m2cache/m2.err` must be 0. It is 2 today. Mechanical, immediate.
   - POLARITY: assert the rendered script for a known predicate is `(assert (not …))`, and that a STUB answerer returning SmtSat REJECTS while SmtUnsat DISCHARGES. A stub suffices — and the stub IS the handler-swap thesis demonstrating itself, which is a stronger novelty argument than the design's current one.
   - PARITY: `PNot(PBoolNode(<IfExpr node>))` must reach SmtUnknown/debt, and must NOT render `(not false)`. Fails today.

4. NAME THE BLAST RADIUS — the design names none. verify.mn is compiled into the wheel, so m2 bytes change; verify_smt is installed nowhere, so no runtime behavior changes, and per PLAN §6 a source-only change holds m2 == m3 in one generation (the `Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc` precedent, PLAN §5.R band D). Fixing the free `debt` LOWERS the census by 2, which the ratchet permits (tools/verify.sh:98 blocks rises only) and which tools/verify-baseline.txt can then tighten. Note what this means: the census — "2,266 claims the medium makes about itself and does not believe" (tools/verify-baseline.txt) — has been carrying the receipt for this item's refutation the whole time. The medium already told us verify_smt is broken; the design read the comments instead of the census.

5. FIX THE NOVELTY VERDICT. Drop "absorbs Hβ.verify.reason-edge-pcc-certificate entirely" — it is the opposite of true, and it contradicts the design's own (correct) trust-base admission. Name the peer positively instead: `Hβ.verify.reason-edge-pcc-certificate` is the answer to the trust-base cost — Z3's `(get-proof)`/unsat-core rendered as a Reason edge a third party re-checks WITHOUT trusting Z3. That is the genuinely Mentl-native claim available here (a certificate as a walkable graph edge rather than a separate dump), and it is strictly stronger than the model-as-witness story the design currently tells. Soften "surpasses Dafny/F*" to the claim that survives: the swap is a `~>` install edge on the graph, so proof strength is a fact read live at the install site rather than a CLI flag — Why3 already routes per-goal to many provers, so the novelty is the edge, not the routing.

6. WHAT TO KEEP VERBATIM: the WASI no-fork/exec substrate argument, the WASM-to-WASM linkage as target with the host two-pass bridge as named fallback, the refusal to sequence behind Band N's native backend, and the trust-base correction to PLAN §0. Those four are correct, verified, and are the item's real contribution — they just belong to a piece of work that comes AFTER the pipeline can answer honestly.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.hz-ceiling-ambient-sample-rate
  The Nyquist ceiling is hardcoded where it should read the currently-installed Sample(rate) effect's own argument — the one item in this cluster that needs a genuinely new primitive: an inference-time read of an ambient effect's parameter from inside a refinement predicate.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design reads the sample rate out of a channel it does not live in (`effect Sample`, lib/dsp/clock.mn:41, is UNPARAMETERIZED; the rate is handler STATE at clock.mn:101/134), and the "wholly new mechanism ... no existing skeleton to extend" it calls the one greenfield piece of the cluster is already running at infer.mn:1497.
  objection: The design's central claim — "a genuinely new primitive: an inference-time read of an ambient effect's parameter", "the one genuinely greenfield piece in this entire cluster, with no existing skeleton to extend" — is contradicted three independent ways by the artifact.

(a) THE RATE IS NOT AN EFFECT PARAMETER. `effect Sample { sample_rate() -> Int; advance_sample() -> (); current_sample() -> Int }` (lib/dsp/clock.mn:41-45) takes no argument. `grep -rhE '^effect [A-Za-z_]+\(' lib/ src/` returns EMPTY — ZERO parameterized effect declarations exist repo-wide. And none can: `parse_effect_decl` (src/parser.mn:783-789) does `expect(tokens, skip_ws(tokens, pos + 1), TLBrace)` — TLBrace IMMEDIATELY after the name, no parameter list — and the AST node is `EffectDeclStmt(String, List)` (src/types.mn:1053), name + ops, no params. `effect Sample(rate: Int)` is SYNTAX.md lathe-lag, not code. The rate actually lives as handler state: `handler sample_real with rate = 44100, count = 0` (clock.mn:101) and `handler sample_test with rate = 48000` (clock.mn:134). So "read live from the SAME row the crown's by-name !E gate already chases" would read NOTHING for Sample — the EffArg/EANode channel is real (types.mn:388-400, 404-421) but nothing flows through it for this effect. The artifact_state's "A program running under `~> Sample(48000)` ... still refines Hz against a 44.1kHz-derived 22050.0 ceiling — silently wrong for its own declared ambient context" describes a program that CANNOT BE WRITTEN: Sample takes no argument, and no handler named Sample exists (only `sample_real`/`sample_test`). The design misread feedback.mn's doc-comments ("with Sample(rate)", :38/:64/:92) as artifact state.

(b) THE INFER-TIME AMBIENT READ EXISTS AND RUNS. src/infer.mn:1497: `if iter_context() { ... } else { report(EFeedbackNoContext(span)) }` — inference PERFORMS an effect op to read the ambient handler stack and decide a compile-time question (is `<~` legal here?). The op is `effect IterContext { iter_context() -> Bool }` (clock.mn:63-65), provided by `handler iterate_context { iter_context() => resume(True) }` (clock.mn:78-80), with `no_iter_context` (clock.mn:72-74) as the identity default. Same file, same DSP domain, sibling feature. The design asserts this mechanism does not exist anywhere.

(c) THE OP FOR THIS EXACT VALUE IS DORMANT SUBSTRATE. `sample_rate() -> Int` (clock.mn:42) is declared, provided by BOTH handlers (clock.mn:102, 135 — `sample_rate() => resume(rate)`), and has ZERO callers. The design steps over a declared, provided, uncalled op returning exactly the Int the Nyquist ceiling needs, to invent an `ambient_rate()` intrinsic — a SECOND name for one truth (drift-7), implemented as a name-keyed compiler recognizer (drift-6/drift-8), the exact class PLAN §9 bills as costing hours ("the seed's name-keyed intrinsic table (bootstrap/src/infer/walk_expr.wat)"). Audit dormant substrate first: defined-but-uncalled IS the slot for what you are about to build.

Also: `subst_self` (src/verify.mn:101-118, called at infer.mn:3313 and verify.mn:141) is precisely the "no existing skeleton" — a predicate-operand substitution walk that binds a free marker to a live node handle PER CHECK SITE by reading the operand's body live (`NExpr(VarRef(name)) => if name == "self"`, verify.mn:114-115), rebuilding the Predicate rather than mutating a node. That rebuild is exactly what lets one shared Hz predicate node fold to 22050 under one install and 24000 under another while `node_const(handle)` stays a pure function of the handle. The design needed that property, declared the mechanism absent, and reached for a new primitive instead of a second binder. Carried-Truth: the fix is LESS code.

GATE REFUTED: `~> Sample(96000)` installs a handler named Sample; none exists. `with Sample(44100)` declares a row arg on an effect that takes none. The gate's two legs mix a row declaration with a nonexistent install and cannot be written against the artifact.

BLAST RADIUS UNSTATED: tools/march.sh:70-71 concatenates `find lib -name '*.mn' -not -path '*/tutorial/*'` into the wheel's own compile input, so lib/dsp/feedback.mn IS wheel source and editing `type Hz` edits the self-compile. The design says nothing about m2 == m3 or the green board.

WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE: the hardcode itself (feedback.mn:66 and the :195 admission are real); the `resolve_in_stack` characterization (lower.mn:1730 is emit-time, op_name→handler target — correctly described, and correctly excluded); the decidable-fragment dependency (`node_const`, verify.mn:85-91, matches only `NExpr(LitInt/LitFloat/LitString/LitBool)` → `rate / 2.0` is None today, so the dependency is honestly stated); and the Reason (arm 8) point that the Why chain must walk to the INSTALL site rather than the type declaration — that survives intact and is the design's best contribution.
  fixes    : 1. STATE THE ARTIFACT CORRECTLY. `effect Sample` (lib/dsp/clock.mn:41-45) is unparameterized; ZERO parameterized effect decls exist and none parse (parser.mn:786; `EffectDeclStmt(String, List)`, types.mn:1053). The ambient rate is handler state: `sample_real with rate = 44100` (clock.mn:101), `sample_test with rate = 48000` (clock.mn:134). The design's route needs a parser surface + AST arity change it never names — not "a new, small infer-time projection".

2. DELETE `ambient_rate()`. `sample_rate() -> Int` (clock.mn:42) already IS that op, already provided by both handlers (clock.mn:102/135), with zero callers. A compiler-recognized intrinsic beside it is a second home for one truth (drift-7) via a name-keyed recognizer (drift-6/drift-8) — the table PLAN §9 already bills as a bug class. Read the op; do not mint a twin.

3. THE PREDICATE READS THE INSTALL, NOT THE ROW. Rewrite Hz as `type Hz = Float where 0.0 < self && self < sample_rate() / 2.0` — an ordinary op call, no intrinsic, no EffArg, no parser change. `iter_context()` (infer.mn:1497) is the precedent: infer performs the op, the ambient handler answers, the `~>` edge is followed and the live node read. Note this also settles a question the design left blurred: the ceiling comes from the INSTALL that answers, not from the declared row.

4. THE ACTUAL MISSING PIECE — NAME IT HONESTLY. `iterate_context` has ZERO installers; only `no_iter_context` is ever installed, and only by the compiler's own pipeline (src/main.mn:308, 371, 384, 427, 441, 472, 634). So `iter_context()` is ALWAYS False today — infer reads the COMPILER's handler stack, not the user program's. The one thing to build is the installer walk: infer mirroring the user's `~>` chain onto its own stack as it descends. That single mechanism serves BOTH `iter_context()` (curing the always-False degeneracy, which is a live latent bug — `<~` under a genuine Sample handler reports E_FeedbackNoContext today) AND `sample_rate()` (the Hz ceiling). One mechanism, two features, less code. Name it `Hβ.infer.ambient-install-walk`, not a Hz-specific intrinsic.

5. GENERALIZE `subst_self`, DO NOT INVENT. verify.mn:101-118 already rebuilds the predicate per check site, binding a free marker to a live node handle. Bind the op's resolved node the same way. Its per-site rebuild is what keeps `node_const` pure while one Hz predicate folds to two ceilings — the property the design needed and did not have.

6. REBUILD THE GATE ON REAL INSTALLS. `~> Sample(96000)` cannot resolve. Use the artifact's pair: `~> sample_real` (44100 → Nyquist 22050) and `~> sample_test` (48000 → Nyquist 24000). The discriminating literal must lie in (22050, 24000) — 30000.0 is rejected under BOTH and proves nothing. Hz at 23000.0 must E_RefinementRejected under `sample_real` and discharge clean under `sample_test`. Today it is rejected under both (hardcoded 22050), so the gate fails pre-landing and passes post — mechanically checkable, non-vacuous.

7. NAME THE UNADDRESSED CONSEQUENCE. A predicate that performs `sample_rate()` makes Hz effect-carrying: every Hz construction site then needs Sample in its row. That is arguably the correct answer (an Hz is meaningless without a rate) and is the row arm speaking, but it is a real semantic change to wheel source (march.sh:70-71 puts lib/dsp/feedback.mn in the self-compile input). State it, and state the m2 == m3 expectation: lib-source-only changes hold the fixpoint in one generation.

8. KEEP THE `no_iter_context` PRECEDENT AS THE IDENTITY. A `no_sample_rate` default handler makes "no ambient rate installed" an honest V_Pending or diagnostic rather than a fabricated 22050 — the sound-incomplete choice, not a surrender-fallback.

9. SEQUENCING CORRECTED. This is NOT "the one genuinely greenfield piece with no existing skeleton". It is: generalize subst_self's binder + build the ambient-install walk (which pays for the `<~` context bug at the same time) + inherit arithmetic folding from Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment. The novelty verdict's core survives — no refinement-types-without-effects language (Liquid/Dafny/F*) can read an ambient capability value, and the alternative really is threading the rate as an explicit parameter — but the honest form of the claim is that Mentl reads it through an ORDINARY op on an ORDINARY handler, which is a stronger statement than the intrinsic the design proposed, and it costs no new primitive.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.refine.buffer-invariant
  A direct, mechanical application of the decidable-fragment extension — the invariant is already stated in prose at the type's own declaration site; write the `where` clause once the extension lands and it discharges at every literal construction.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's single prescribed action ("write the clause verbatim") is a hard compile failure today and stays one after R3 lands, because the blocker sits in `parse_type_decl` (src/parser.mn:687-691), not in `subst_self`; landing it into lib/runtime/buffer.mn zeroes the wheel's WAT.
  objection: THE DESIGN'S ONE ACTION DOES NOT PARSE, AND R3 CANNOT FIX THAT.

`parse_type_decl` (src/parser.mn:687-689) probes for the refinement exactly one token past the RHS start:
    let probe = skip_ws(tokens, p2 + 1)
    if at(tokens, probe, TWhere) { ... RefineStmt ... }
    else if at(tokens, p2, TLBrace) { ... nominal record ... }
For a record RHS, p2 = `{` and p2+1 = `data` — never TWhere. The record arm (parser.mn:691) wins, the `where` is never consumed, and the predicate text falls out as garbage top-level tokens. Measured, boot/mentl.wasm, the design's declaration verbatim:
    parser: P_UnexpectedToken Warning: unexpected token: where at 1:40-1:45
    infer:  E_MissingVariable error: self at 1:46-1:50
    infer:  E_MissingVariable error: len at 1:60-1:63
    graph:  E_OccursCheck error: infinite type: 4 occurs in t4@e0
    verify: E_UnresolvedHole error: unresolved value hole (handle 2)
    compiler exit=1, WAT BYTES EMITTED: 0
`Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment` touches subst_self/node_const in src/verify.mn. It cannot make parse_type_decl see a `where` after a `}`. So "Depends entirely on Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment; contributes nothing new to it" is false, and "no new mechanism" is false. SYNTAX.md §«Type aliases» confirms the surface side: it defines transparent alias, refined alias `type X = Y where pred`, repr-pinned alias, and nominal record `type X = {…}` — there is NO `type X = {record} where pred` form. The design's action is unspecified at the authority doc.

The design picked the one spelling that cannot parse. The adjacent form works today — measured, exit=0, zero diagnostics, 2476 WAT bytes:
    type BufRec = {data: List, count: Int}
    type Buffer = BufRec where self.count <= len(self.data)

FOUR MORE INDEPENDENT REFUTATIONS.

(1) The gate fixture does not type. `data: List` is `TName("List",[])` — no `type List` declaration exists anywhere in the repo — while `[1,2,3]` is `TList(TInt)` (parser.mn:2325-2331; types.mn:39 `TList(Ty)`). Measured on the design's own fixture: `infer: E_TypeMismatch error: List(Int) vs List at 3:19-3:52`. Buffer's `data` field is uninhabitable by any list value. The gate fails before a refinement is ever decided.

(2) Row is the blocker, not a "confirming check". `fn len(xs) with Memory = load_i32(xs)` (lib/runtime/lists.mn:38). So the comment's own target predicate performs Memory, and buffer.mn:65-66's `fn buf_invariant(b: Buffer) -> Bool with Pure` is a live row lie. Measured: `effects: E_PurityViolated error: expected Pure but found effects: Memory + r2598@e2475 at 627:4-628:18` (memory.mn+lists.mn = 562 lines ⇒ buffer.mn:65-66). Reproduced minimally, which also yields `E_TypeMismatch: List vs List(t251@e229)` on `len(buf_data(b))` — the comment's predicate does not type either.

(3) "The comment already states the exact target" is false. buffer.mn:48 says `where buf_invariant(self)`; buffer.mn:127-130 says `buf_count(self) <= len(buf_data(self))`; the design writes a third predicate, `self.count <= len(self.data)`, and calls it verbatim. The difference is load-bearing: `buf_invariant(self)` hits node_to_predicate's `_ => PBoolNode(node_handle(node))` (parser.mn:2486) → `PBoolNode(_) => None` (verify.mn:69) → V_Pending forever, and needs `Hβ.infer.predicate-from-bool-expression` (PLAN.md:848; roadmap:206 "infer.mn:3423 returns the PBoolNode stub today") — a real named peer the design never cites. `buf_invariant(b: Buffer)` is also self-referential: the predicate over the refined type takes the refined type as its parameter.

(4) The mechanism is under-specified and breaks the one-writer law. node_const (verify.mn:85-91) folds only LitInt/LitFloat/LitString/LitBool, and LitValue (types.mn:1093-1097) is LVInt|LVFloat|LVString|LVBool — there is NO sequence value, so a "`len`-on-literal-sequence whitelist arm" has nothing to produce into; and the required FieldAccess-on-RecordExpr fold (to get `<record>.count` → 3) is never named. Worse: `fn subst_self(pred, value_handle) with GraphRead` (verify.mn:101). "subst_self correctly mints a substituted FieldAccess/CallExpr chain" makes verify a second writer into the graph, against PLAN §2/L0 "Only inference writes."

VACUOUS GATE + UNSTATED TOTAL BLAST RADIUS. Buffer has ZERO callers: grep for buf_make|buf_push|buf_freeze|buf_count(|buf_data(|Buffer{ outside lib/runtime/buffer.mn returns only src/infer.mn:3834 (a comment); nothing imports runtime/buffer. So "it discharges at every literal construction" describes an EMPTY set. The only two constructions in the repo — buf_make (buffer.mn:74, `Buffer{data: make_list(0), count: 0}`) and buf_push (buffer.mn:81, `count: buf.count + 1`) — are both non-literal, both V_Pending. Net effect on the wheel: +2 V_Pending, 0 discharged. And buffer.mn IS in the wheel input (`find lib` position 9), so the measured exit=1 / 0 WAT is the wheel's own compile: no m2, no march, every gate (frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros) dead. The design does not mention blast radius at all.

WHAT DISPROVED MY OWN FIRST GUESS: I expected the refined nominal record to silently degrade Buffer's brand (TName → TRefined(TAlias)) — a soundness drift. It does not; the `where` is simply never reached, so the failure is loud, not silent. Productive-under-error is working. That is the only thing here that held.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE "Depends entirely on Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment; contributes nothing new to it" and "same-day mechanical follow-through, not a design task." The item is blocked on three things R3 does not touch, each of which must be stated:
   (a) SURFACE. SYNTAX.md defines no `type X = {record} where pred` form, and parse_type_decl (parser.mn:687-691) structurally cannot reach the refine arm from a `{`-opened RHS. Either amend SYNTAX.md with the refined-nominal-record form and turn the lathe to it (SYNTAX.md is the wheel, the parser is the lathe), or write the two-decl form that already parses and is measured green: `type BufRec = {data: List, count: Int}` + `type Buffer = BufRec where …`. Say which, and say why the brand survives (or does not) when a nominal `TName("X",[],TRecord)` meets a refinement that produces `TRefined(TAlias("X", Y), pred)`.
   (b) TYPE. `data: List` is an uninhabitable phantom — no `type List` declaration exists; `List` reads as `TName("List",[])` while every list literal is `TList(elem)`. The field must be `[a]`, exactly as buffer.mn:43's own comment already says. Fix the field before writing any predicate over it.
   (c) ROW. `len` is `with Memory` (lists.mn:38). The comment's target predicate performs Memory, and buffer.mn:65's `with Pure` is a measured E_PurityViolated. Arm 4 is the design lever here, not "a confirming check" — a refinement predicate discharged by a `with GraphRead` decision procedure cannot perform Memory. Either the predicate reads the sequence's count structurally from the graph node (no `len` call), or the item needs a Pure length projection that does not exist.

2. Name the predicate you are actually writing, and stop claiming verbatim fidelity. The artifact specifies TWO forms (buffer.mn:48 `where buf_invariant(self)`; buffer.mn:127-130 `buf_count(self) <= len(buf_data(self))`); the design writes a THIRD. Both artifact forms route to CallExpr/PBoolNode operands, i.e. to `Hβ.infer.predicate-from-bool-expression` (PLAN.md:848; infer.mn:3423 stub) — cite it as a co-dependency or explicitly retire the `buf_invariant(self)` form from buffer.mn:48. Also note the self-referential regress: `buf_invariant(b: Buffer)` takes the refined type as its own parameter.

3. Specify the two node_const arms the design needs and never names: FieldAccess-on-RecordExpr → the field's literal, and the sequence-length fold. State that LitValue (types.mn:1093-1097) has no sequence constructor, so "len-on-literal-sequence" needs either a new LitValue arm or a structural read that never produces a LitValue. And resolve the writer question: subst_self is `with GraphRead` (verify.mn:101) — either substitution is a pure structural fold that reads through the record node without minting, or it moves to the inference side. Verify minting nodes violates PLAN §2/L0 "Only inference writes."

4. Replace the gate. The current one is refuted twice: the fixture `{data: [1,2,3], count: 3}` yields E_TypeMismatch (List(Int) vs List), and even if it typed, it would prove nothing about the repo — the invariant discharges at ZERO sites. A gate that only passes on a synthetic fixture for a type with no callers passes vacuously. State the honest arithmetic: +2 V_Pending (buf_make buffer.mn:74, buf_push buffer.mn:81), 0 discharged.

5. State the blast radius. Measured: the clause as written, as a dead lib decl, gives compiler exit=1 and 0 WAT bytes. buffer.mn is in the wheel input at `find lib` position 9. This does not merely fail to help — it kills m2, the march, and every green gate.

6. Fix the novelty verdict. "Nothing novel to audit" is wrong twice over. The borrowed shape is the Eiffel/Dafny CLASS INVARIANT — a predicate attached to a type and re-checked at every method boundary — and buffer.mn:65's self-referential `buf_invariant(b: Buffer)` is that regress in Mentl clothing. Reject it by name: Mentl's native form is not a contract re-checked per method; it is an edge the cursor reads live at the construction node, discharged once where the value is minted (verify.mn:141 refinement_admits, infer.mn:3313), with the undecidable residue accruing as honest V_Pending. Say that, or say the borrowed shape is kept and why.

7. Run the Law-2 read the design skips. buffer.mn has zero callers; `name_seen_at` (src/backends/wasm.mn:1158) hand-rolls the exact flat-buffer membership scan the file was built to supply and never imports it; PLAN §5.O already names the real home — `Hβ.emit.flat-accumulator-dedup` → a handle-set bit, and `Hβ.runtime.indexed-map-primitive`. Buffer is a side-ledger of a primitive that landed elsewhere, with an uninhabitable field type and a lying effect row that nothing ever exercised. The Carried-Truth move on a dead, broken type is DELETION, not decoration — the fix is LESS code. Either re-scope this item onto a refined type that has callers, or convert it to the positive-form peer for the removal: `Hβ.runtime.buffer-dissolves-into-indexed-map`.

8. Minor: `Hβ.verify.decidable-fragment` is not a named peer anywhere in PLAN.md or docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md — R3 exists only as prose (PLAN.md:806, PLAN.md:1354). If the cluster is minting the handle, mint it in band F explicitly.

REFUTED: Hβ.effects.parameterized-negation-instance
  The negation gate compares parameterized-effect ARGS, not just names, so `!Sample(44100)` forbids only that instance and admits `Sample(48000)` — built now, independent of EffName-is-a-handle, by extending the one low-frequency call site the by-name fix already isolated.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the body's row never carries an instance (`!Sample(44100) vs Sample`, measured), so the design's arg-compare never fires on the case it exists to fix; its one firing path admits on an unverified annotation, converting a sound-but-over-broad gate into an under-forbidding one.
  objection: **The design fixes the reader of a fact nobody writes, and its only firing path is unsound.**

I ran the design's own gate fixture through the pinned boot:

    effect Sample(rate: Int) { tick() -> () }
    fn good() with !Sample(44100) = tick()
    → E_EffectMismatch: effect row mismatch: **!Sample(44100) vs Sample**

The body's row entry is bare `Sample` — no args. That is forced by infer.mn:3506-3513: `eff_vars = unique(effect_type_vars(qops))`; `eff_name = if len(eff_vars) == 0 { ENamed(effect_name) } else { EParameterized(effect_name, map((v) => EAType(TVar(v)), eff_vars)) }`, and register_one_op sets the op's row to `mk_ef_closed([eff_name])` (infer.mn:3556). For SYNTAX.md's own `effect Sample(rate: Int)`, the ops `tick()`/`current_sample() -> Float` carry no lowercase type-var leaves, so `eff_vars = []` → `ENamed("Sample")`. `EParameterized`'s args on the perform side are the effect's TYPE parameters (`EAType(TVar(v))`) — a different axis entirely from install-time VALUE args. The ADT is overloaded across two meanings and the design conflates them.

Under the design's rule ("if both sides are `EParameterized`…"), forbidden=`EParameterized`, candidate=`ENamed` → not both → name-match → forbid. **E_EffectMismatch is still emitted; the gate fails after the fix.** The design's fixture ("a fn whose body performs `Sample(48000)`") is unbuildable — you perform `tick()`, not `Sample(48000)`.

The only shape where the compare fires is annotation-vs-annotation via a callee's authored row (`fn inner() with Sample(48000) = tick()` → `!Sample(44100) vs Sample(48000)`, measured mismatch=1). And that annotation is verified against nothing — measured:

    fn inner() with Sample(99999) = tick()   → E_EffectMismatch = 0

`with Sample(99999)` over a body performing bare `Sample` compiles clean. The sole `EAInt` producer is parser.mn:637, inside `parse_eff_args` ← `parse_one_effect` ← the authored `with` clause. So the design would ADMIT `outer() with !Sample(44100)` on the strength of a fiction, while the handler actually installed may be `sample_h(44100)`. That is an UNDER-FORBID — a new silent-wrong class in the column chartered to eliminate them, and the exact inverse of the design's own claim "sound (never under-forbids)".

**GATE, separately refuted as unstable.** Two fixtures differing only by the presence of `handler sample_h(rate: Int) { tick() => resume() }`: with it, mismatch=0; without it, mismatch=1 — same `!Sample(44100)` vs `Sample(48000)` relationship. crown-gate.sh compiles each crucible solo, so a naturally-written Sample fixture (with a handler) already passes today: **vacuous before the fix**. The design's "it currently DOES get blocked" holds only for the handler-less shape, which the fix does not repair.

**Layering/row violation in the EAType arm.** effects.mn imports only `types` + `runtime/strings` (effects.mn:16-17); infer.mn imports `graph, effects, types` (infer.mn:27-29) — so calling `unify_types` (infer.mn:1795, no declared row) from effects.mn inverts the layer and closes a cycle. `graph_push_checkpoint()` is a `GraphRead` op (types.mn:1220/1227) and `graph_rollback(Int)` a `GraphWrite` op (types.mn:1246); performing them inside `name_in_forbidden` (effects.mn:371), `forbidden_names_disjoint` (:363), `row_subsumes` (:376) — all `with !Mutate` — injects GraphRead+GraphWrite into the file whose header reads "Pure functions on data. No handlers, no state, no effects" (effects.mn:3-4), propagating to every caller (infer.mn:480/488, egraph.mn:72, gradient_delta.mn:284, synth_proposer.mn:236). Worse, types.mn:414-416 already states EAType is "unified, not value-compared… Flows performer→handler at the install", and infer.mn:3496-3498 states "the install unifies the whole instance per-position" — so probing unifiability at the gate RE-DERIVES what the install already wrote into the union-find. Carried-Truth violated by the design that invokes it.

**What I tried that failed to refute.** The perf argument survives: 608 authored negations across 25 files hit `row_subsumes`'s EfNeg arm, but the EAType probe only fires when both sides are EParameterized-with-EAType, which is rare — the timeout analogy to the abandoned structural `name_set_contains` does not carry. The artifact_state is also largely accurate: eff_name_str's arg discard (effects.mn:351-355), the exclusivity of `forbidden_names_disjoint`/`name_in_forbidden` to effects.mn:425/433 (grep-confirmed; `eff_name_str` has two further callers at :765/:892, which the design correctly leaves alone), and types.mn:386-421's unimplemented intent all check out. The design's SEQUENCING refutation of the EffName-is-a-handle dependency is also correct as far as it goes — interning does nothing for an `EAInt`. The design fails not on its reading of the gate, but on its inference about what reaches the gate.
  fixes    : **1. State the real gap, one layer down.** The design says "args are discarded at the gate". The truth: the install-time VALUE argument never enters ANY row. `eff_name_str` discards nothing on the body side because there is nothing there (`!Sample(44100) vs Sample`, measured; infer.mn:3508-3512). The only `EAInt` in the system is born at parser.mn:637 from an authored `with` clause. Instance-precision at the gate would refine annotation-against-annotation, never a proof about what the program performs.

**2. Re-root the item: make the instance PROVEN, not annotated.** The ultimate form is the one infer.mn:3496-3498 already names for the type axis — "the install unifies the whole instance per-position" — extended so the `~> sample_h(44100)` install unifies its VALUE argument into the op's row, the way `EAType` payloads already flow performer→handler (types.mn:414-416). Then `!Sample(44100)` gates a fact the graph wrote, and an arg comparison at the gate finally means something. Name it positively: `Hβ.effects.install-instance-unifies-value-args` — the true blocker this item should depend on, and the one it must not land ahead of. The current item is the reader; that is the writer.

**3. Until the instance is proven, by-name IS the ultimate form here.** PLAN §4③ already records it: "bare `!E` by-name is sound and conservative." A gate that over-forbids refuses a program the medium cannot yet prove safe; a gate that admits on an unverified annotation lies. The design trades the first for the second. Keep by-name.

**4. Delete parser.mn:650-653 before any arg compare.** `_ => { report(EEffArgNotLiteral(span_at(tokens, pos))); (EAInt(0), pos + 1) }` is a surrender-fallback (Law 2). Under the design, `EAInt(44100)` vs a fabricated `EAInt(0)` reads as "provably different" → ADMIT: a reported parse error becomes an effect-gate admission.

**5. Keep `unify_types` / `graph_push_checkpoint` / `graph_rollback` out of effects.mn.** Layer inversion (effects.mn:16-17 vs infer.mn:27-29) plus an effect-row violation against the `with !Mutate` declared on effects.mn:363/366/371/376 and the header's "no effects" claim (effects.mn:3-4). The synth_proposer.mn:152/549 precedent does not transfer: those sites sit in the proposer, which is built ON the algebra, not inside it.

**6. Re-specify the gate.** As written it fails after the fix (handler-less: candidate is bare `ENamed`) or passes before it (handler-declared: measured mismatch=0 today). A gate for the re-rooted item would assert the install/perform instance is carried at all — e.g. that a body under `~> sample_h(48000)` reports its row as `Sample(48000)`, not `Sample` — which is checkable against the diagnostic text the crown harness already greps, and which fails loudly today.

**7. Honest blast radius the design omits.** Landing arg-sensitivity into `row_subsumes`'s EfNeg arm changes the verdict for every `with !E` in the wheel (608 authored negations across 25 files). The design asserts no fixpoint impact; it should say so explicitly and gate it, since a verdict flip on the wheel's own `!Mutate`/`!Alloc` declarations moves emitted diagnostics and can move bytes.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.runtime.abort-catch-option-wrap
  `try_with_abort_catch`'s success path must wrap its own return value in `Some(...)` before installing `catch_abort` — the library never does this today, so `backtrack`'s general-purpose promise ("N-queens, SAT, parser combinators — only the element type differs") is false for the overwhelmingly common case of a non-Option-shaped search payload.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's central mechanical premise ("`abort()`'s `-> !` bottom type short-circuits before `Some(...)` is ever constructed") is contradicted by name at src/infer.mn:4041 and measured false three ways; applying the design's fix verbatim to its own gate yields exit 134, not the predicted 30.
  objection: THE FIX DOES NOT FIX ITS OWN GATE — MEASURED. I copied lib/runtime/search.mn to scratch, applied the design's exact one-liner (`let attempted = (Some(try_fn())) ~> catch_abort`), and ran the design's own gate micro. Predicted 30. Measured 134. Unchanged from pre-fix.

THE ROOT — the design's premise is false BY NAME in the artifact. src/infer.mn:4036-4043:
  fn arm_disc_of(grade, ret) = match grade {
    UZero => match ret {
      TVar(_) => OneShot,   // bottom: `fail -> a`
      TUnit   => OneShot,   // `abort -> !` (parsed TUnit) / unit — no value to abandon
      _       => Abandon    // value-returning UZero (`abort -> Option`): the None must reach the install, not the perform site
    }
  }
`catch_abort` (src/own.mn:31-33, arm `abort() => None`, zero resume sites → UZero) is an arm of `abort() -> !` (src/types.mn:1625-1627). `!` hits the TUnit arm → **OneShot, not Abandon**. src/types.mn:1619-1624 documents it as deliberate: "`abort` is inferred OneShot despite `-> !`". So `abort()` returns the arm's `None` TO THE PERFORM SITE and execution CONTINUES straight into the `Some(...)` wrap. The design's interrogation — "catch_abort is an Abandon-discipline handler" — is factually wrong.

MEASURED, THREE WAYS (boot/mentl.wasm, tools/run-micro.sh, RTLIBS per tools/verify.sh:24):
1. tests/micros/mn-option-protocol.mn verbatim except `-> Option` → `-> !`: control exits **42** (Abandon, catch runs); the `-> !` twin exits **0** — catch never runs. One character apart. The micro's own header already documents this exact failure: "the Some-wrap carries 0 and the catch never runs. TODAY: exit 0."
2. The design's wrap in the pure combinator shape (no backtrack, no multi-shot): predicted 42, measured **0**. `attempt(10)` returns `None` to the perform site → `Some(None)` → the match takes `Some(value) => value` → `None`. `catch_fn()` never runs.
3. In backtrack that means `catch_fn` — `graph_rollback(checkpoint); try_each(i+1)` (search.mn:142-145) — never runs. The fix makes backtrack return None on the FIRST dead-end and never try option 2: silently wrong instead of loudly wrong. Strictly worse than the trap it replaces.

THE GATE IS INDEPENDENTLY REFUTED. The gate micro compiles with `abort`, `graph_push_checkpoint`, and `graph_rollback` ALL UNBOUND — 3 unbound-name floors in the emitted WAT ("unbound name abort — infer proved it missing; the lowering will not guess it into a global"). `effect Abort` is at src/types.mn:1625 and the graph fns are nowhere in lib/; run-micro.sh:21 CATs a lib list and cannot see src/. The 134 fires in `body` as an unbound-`abort` floor, NOT the diagnosed `scrut_tmp >= 4096` tag-check. The gate fails identically pre- and post-fix for an unrelated reason: it can never reach 30, so it gates nothing.

WHAT DID NOT REFUTE — the bug is REAL. Unfixed combinator, `abort() -> !`, raw Int payload 30 on the SUCCESS path: exit **134**. `try_with_abort_catch`'s Some/None match against an unwrapped value really does trap, exactly as claimed. The design found a real defect, misdiagnosed the mechanism, and proposed an inert fix.
  fixes    : 1. NAME THE CONTRADICTION THE DESIGN MISSED. docs/SYNTAX.md:1072-1074 (authority on surface, superseding all other docs) says `abort() -> !` is "the control cut the **Abandon** discipline reads". src/infer.mn:4041 classifies it **OneShot**. Surface authority vs. artifact, in direct contradiction. SYNTAX.md wins by the three-doc contract. That contradiction — not the missing wrap — is the silent-wrong class, and it is larger than the one the design found.

2. THE ROOT IS CARRIED-TRUTH, TEXTBOOK. `!` is not a type: SYNTAX.md:1075-1077 names this exact lathe-lag ("the parser currently has no `TBang` arm in the type position, so `-> !` rides unexpected-token recovery (typed as unit, with a spurious `P_UnexpectedToken`)"). Measured: `parser: P_UnexpectedToken Warning: unexpected token in type position — expected a type`. `grep TBang src/types.mn src/parser.mn` returns only the TOKEN, no Ty constructor. So: the decl at types.mn:1626 PROVED "abort never returns"; the parser DISCARDED the proof; `arm_disc_of` RE-DERIVED the discipline from a fabricated TUnit and got the opposite answer. infer.mn:4041's own comment concedes it in parentheses — "(parsed TUnit)" — encoding the fabrication AS the rule. That is a surrender-fallback to DELETE, not to encode.

3. THE ULTIMATE FORM, IN ORDER (less code, one fact read live):
   a. `TBang` in the Ty ADT + a `parse_type_atom` arm. Not new scope — SYNTAX.md's standing obligation ("the parser is the lathe turned to SYNTAX.md; where they disagree, the parser is wrong").
   b. `arm_disc_of` gains `TBang => Abandon`. A never-returning op's zero-resume arm abandons BY CONSTRUCTION — there is no perform site to return to; that is what `!` MEANS. The `TUnit => OneShot` arm shrinks to its honest remainder: genuine unit, already named at infer.mn:4041 as `Hβ.lower.uzero-unit-abandon`.
   c. `!` unifies with any consumer (bottom), so `if v > 25 { v } else { abort() }` types as Int with no TUnit mismatch.
   d. THEN the Some-wrap is FORCED BY INFERENCE (via the companion handler-abandon-result-unify: catch_abort's arm value type must unify with the install's body type), not hand-added by convention. The design's one-liner is the right EDIT as a consequent — never the fix.

4. THE SEQUENCING CLAIM IS INVERTED. Design: "Depends on nothing... the gate above passes with this fix alone." Measured false (134 → 134). It depends on BOTH root fixes. Landing the one-liner alone changes nothing observable and buys a false green.

5. THE NOVELTY VERDICT IS DISHONEST BY THE BRIEF'S OWN TEST. It says "not a borrowed pattern needing replacement" and cites Haskell's `try` / Rust's `catch_unwind` as "the unremarkable, standard functional idiom". That IS the borrowed shape, waved at and kept — the exact failure the novelty audit exists to catch. Those languages wrap the Ok path BECAUSE THEY HAVE NO EFFECT HANDLERS. src/types.mn:1618-1619 states Mentl's own purpose in one line: "Universal short-circuit / early return. **Flattens the 'Option Monad' into the topological pipeline.**" Mentl exists to NOT need the wrap. The native form is already built and measured: the **Abandon discipline** (types.mn:274, landed M4 2026-07-13, pin 67e44c9c, `micro:option-protocol=42`) delivers the arm's value AT THE INSTALL and abandons the continuation — what `catch_unwind` simulates with a wrap and what `!` promises by type. Strictly stronger: the wrap is an allocation plus a match the caller must not forget; Abandon is a type-directed control cut the compiler proves. The design keeps the borrowed shape at the exact site where Mentl's native form was already built.

6. REWRITE THE GATE. Drop `import runtime/search` — run-micro.sh CATs a lib list and cannot reach src/types.mn or the graph fns. The honest gate is mn-option-protocol.mn's twin with `abort() -> !`: today **exit 0** (catch never runs, measured), after the root fix **exit 42** (catch runs) — the same 0→42 flip M4 already performed for `-> Option`. Register `micro:abort-bang-abandon=42`. It fails today for the RIGHT reason and goes green only if the design lands. A backtrack end-to-end gate is still worth having, but it must run the real wheel path (a frontier-gate fixture using the compiler's own lib resolution), never the cat harness.

7. BLAST RADIUS — the design says NOTHING, and there is real radius. (a) lib/runtime/search.mn IS in the wheel compile input (tools/march.sh:70-71: `find lib -name '*.mn' -not -path '*/tutorial/*' | sort | xargs cat`) — "imported by nothing" is NOT "not compiled". The one-liner alone is likely fixpoint-safe per PLAN §5.R band D's measured law (a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in one generation). (b) The ROOT fix is not safe by default: src/voice.mn:911 (`unimplemented_arm`) makes the WHEEL ITSELF perform `abort()`, and infer.mn:3769-3772 warns in terms — "They stay OneShot — so **NO wheel op changes disc** (Law 7 wheel-wide: m2 == m3, the abandon forms are added arms exercised only by out-of-wheel crucibles)." Flipping `-> !` to Abandon flips the wheel's own `abort` discipline → emit changes → **m2 ≠ m3 is EXPECTED-when-correct: a TRANSITION, re-pin from m3** (PLAN §6; march.sh runs the m4 leg itself and rules TRANSITION vs BROKEN). (c) `micro:effect-identity=81` must be RE-MEASURED, not assumed: in that micro the perform is the whole body, so abandon and return-to-perform-site are observationally identical — but that is a claim until the binary says so. `mn-uzero-through-frames=33` and `mn-resume-across-install` declare `-> Option` and are untouched.
