REFUTED: Hβ.types.resume-world-mismatch-value-gate
  Wire it: `EResumeWorldMismatchWorld` is a fully-built, fully-projected diagnostic with zero reachable call sites because the ONE place it belongs — persist's runtime rehydrate check — cannot reach `report(...)` (a compile-time-only mechanism); the fix is teaching `Fail` to carry a `DiagKind` payload instead of a bare `String`, a four-site change, not a program-wide redesign.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the diagnostic-side facts are all exactly right, but the design's load-bearing claim ("only 2 real call sites exist repo-wide... a four-site change, not a program-wide redesign") is false by grep and false by execution: it misses a gated call site, breaks the entire 71-micro battery, and routes the compile-time inference graph (GraphRead) into the runtime failure path.
  objection: The design inverts its own best evidence, and the artifact proves it three ways.

(1) FACTUAL — the enumeration the design says was "confirmed by repo-wide grep" is wrong. There are THREE `fail(` call sites and TWO handler arms, not two and one: the design misses `tests/micros/mn-effect-identity.mn:15` (`fn failing() with Fail = fail("failure")` — a String call site) and `:8` (`fail(_) => 7` — a second arm). Both are gated by `tools/effect-identity-gate.sh:39-46`, which runs that micro expecting 81 and is on the board. `fail(msg: String)` -> `fail(d: DiagKind)` makes `fail("failure")` a type error and takes effect-identity RED. Every diagnostic-side claim checks out exactly (types.mn:1339/1401/1452/1503/1615, zero report sites; sibling wired at infer.mn:1946 + lower.mn:4006; persist.mn:84; prelude.mn:362-363; io.mn:132-138) — the design read the diagnostic correctly and mis-read the blast radius it built the "four-site" claim on.

(2) BLAST RADIUS — proven by execution, not argued. `tools/verify.sh:24` sets `RTLIBS=(lib/runtime/memory.mn lib/runtime/strings.mn lib/runtime/lists.mn lib/prelude.mn)`; `run-micro.sh:21` cats them (no import resolution from a stdin stream). Every micro links prelude.mn; none links src/types.mn. I ran the design's own shape: `effect Fail2 { fail2(d: DiagKind) -> a }` + `fail2(EResumeWorldMismatchWorld(1,2,span_zero()))` -> `infer: E_MissingVariable error: EResumeWorldMismatchWorld at 2030:34` and `E_MissingVariable error: span_zero at 2030:66`, run exit 134 (trap). The identical micro with `d: String` -> PASS exit=7. The design's step "update unwrap's call site (prelude.mn:366) to raise EUnwrapOnNone(span_zero())" plants those unresolvable names in the one file all 71 micros link. Its own proposed gate cannot run either: `tools/frontier-gate.sh:82` PERSIST_RTLIBS = RTLIBS + io.mn + persist.mn, still no src/types.mn.

(3) ARCHITECTURAL — "reusing the EXISTING diag_message projection... this handler becomes strictly more general for free" is false, and Row? is the interrogation that kills it (the design's own INTERROGATIONS section skips it: "Row? — irrelevant directly"). `src/types.mn:1568: fn diag_message(d) with GraphRead + Memory + Alloc + !Mutate`, and `src/types.mn:1220: effect GraphRead { graph_chase(Int) -> GNode, graph_chase_handle, graph_epoch, graph_next, graph_reason_edge, graph_snapshot, graph_push_checkpoint, graph_node_body }` — that is the compile-time inference graph (union-find chase, epoch, snapshot). Rows are per-function, not per-arm: even though the EResumeWorldMismatchWorld arm needs only int_to_str, diag_message carries GraphRead because sibling arms (ETypeMismatch(Ty,Ty,_) -> show_type) chase handles. Putting diag_message in `fail_exit` (io.mn:133, the host-boundary runtime policy) gives every program's failure path a GraphRead requirement against a graph that does not exist at runtime.

The novelty verdict reads its own tell backwards. It observes correctly that "two bare Int world-TAGS, not two EffRows" prove the constructor was built for what row-unification cannot reach — then concludes the compile-time catalog belongs at runtime. The bare Ints prove the opposite: at runtime there is no graph to carry a row. A compile-time diagnostic IS a projection of the live graph (that is exactly why diag_message needs GraphRead); a runtime failure has none. Not "one vocabulary, different spans." And the unification is structurally blocked, not inconvenient: DiagKind is welded to the metaschema — ETypeMismatch(Ty,Ty,Span) (1302), EResumeWorldMismatch(ResumeDiscipline,...) (1312), TOverDeclared(EffRow,EffRow,String,Span) (1321), EEffectMismatch(EffRow,EffRow,Span) (1322), EPurityViolated(EffRow,Span) (1323), ERefinementRejected(Predicate,Span) (1325). DiagKind cannot reach lib/prelude.mn without dragging Ty + EffRow + Predicate + ResumeDiscipline — 2,161 lines of compiler internals — into the stdlib every user program links. The claim to "collapse a split other languages never even attempt to close" overclaims: rustc's E-codes and a binary's panic! are separate because the compiler's diagnostic carries the compiler's IR and a compiled binary has no rustc in it. Same layering fact, not fluency.

What I tried that failed to save it: I checked whether the resolved-import path rescues the design, since lib/runtime/io.mn:35 already does `import types` (so lib->src is not a clean wall). It does not — (a) verify.sh, effect-identity-gate.sh, and frontier-gate.sh all use cat-mode RTLIBS, so the battery breaks regardless of resolution, and (b) even fully resolved, GraphRead in fail_exit stays unsatisfiable at runtime and DiagKind still drags the metaschema into the stdlib.
  fixes    : 1. DROP the false enumeration. It is 3 call sites + 2 handler arms, not 2 + 1: persist.mn:84, prelude.mn:366, tests/micros/mn-effect-identity.mn:15, with arms at io.mn:133 and mn-effect-identity.mn:8 — the latter two under tools/effect-identity-gate.sh, a board gate. "Four-site coordinated edit... the same shape and scale as the TCont arity precedent" must be struck: the TCont arity change was ~14 destructure sites inside the compiler, where the metaschema is in scope. This one crosses the compiler/stdlib boundary, which is a different kind of edit, not a bigger one.

2. STATE THE MEASURED BLAST RADIUS. The design must say: this breaks all 71 micros (verify.sh:24 RTLIBS links prelude.mn, never src/types.mn), plus effect-identity, frontier, march-gate. Reproduce it: a micro with `fail2(d: DiagKind)` and `fail2(EResumeWorldMismatchWorld(1,2,span_zero()))` on the gate's lib-set gives `E_MissingVariable: EResumeWorldMismatchWorld` + `E_MissingVariable: span_zero`, exit 134; the `d: String` control exits 7.

3. KILL the "free" claim about diag_message. It costs GraphRead + Memory + Alloc (types.mn:1568) in every program's failure path, and GraphRead (types.mn:1220) is graph_chase/graph_epoch/graph_snapshot — the compile-time inference graph. Answer interrogation 4 honestly instead of "Row? — irrelevant directly"; the row is the refutation.

4. RESTORE THE OPTION THE DESIGN DELETED. PLAN.md:1353 reads "`E_ResumeWorldMismatchWorld` wire-or-DELETE (band B)". The design assumes "wire" and never argues against delete. The gap is real — five projections (types.mn:1401/1452/1503/1615 + the decl at 1339), zero raise sites, textbook drift-9 dead substrate — but "wire" is not the resolved half.

5. THE EVIDENCE FAVORS DELETE-FOR-NOW, and the design's SEQUENCING section waves away the reason. It says capture-at-reify is "assumed correct here, not re-designed by this item." persist.mn:10-13 says otherwise, in the artifact's own words: "`world_tag` is likewise a compatibility fingerprint, not a capability proof. The current lowerer folds effect names into 32 bits, omits parameter arguments and open row tails, and does not identify handler installs. Collisions can admit the wrong world." Wiring a wheel-side catalog diagnostic whose payload is an admittedly colliding 32-bit fold canonizes the lossy fingerprint permanently (CLAUDE.md filter 1: a band-aid in the WHEEL is never fine). A diagnostic that can only fire where the graph does not exist, carrying a hash the artifact calls unsound, is not waiting to be wired.

6. THE ULTIMATE FORM points the other way. persist.mn:79-84 already refuses (`fail`, never a fabricated zero continuation) — the refusal is not the gap. The gap is that the world is a folded Int instead of the EffRow that PLAN §4③(b)/§5.U put on `TCont(R, S, ResumeDiscipline, EffRow)`. Fix the world's REPRESENTATION at `Hβ.infer.tcont-world-capture-at-reify` and the runtime comparison either disappears (proved at compile time, where the graph exists and the diagnostic already has a home) or carries a real row. That is the Carried-Truth reading: the Int is the re-derivation of a row the graph already holds.

7. IF a structured runtime failure is genuinely wanted, the payload must be a type the STDLIB owns, not the compiler's DiagKind. Name it positively — `Hβ.runtime.failure-payload-is-stdlib-adt` — and note it needs the graph-dependent/graph-free partition of the catalog. That is a real design; it is not this item, and it is not four sites.

REFUTED: Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name
  Two top-level declarations of the same name — of ANY kind, not just `type` — shadow silently today via env_extend's blind append-and-read-most-recent; the fix is one collision check at the single shared pre-registration dispatch point, which also discharges SYNTAX.md's own already-specified but never-implemented E_ImportNameCollision for free.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the check is placed at a site that reads a name which, for ADTs, is never an env entry, so the design's own title case (two `type Shape` decls) stays silent after the fix; its repro passes only through the nominal-record guard `ctor_name == type_name` at infer.mn:3452, and it generalized that coincidence into a claim of covering "any kind".
  objection: THE DISPATCH POINT DOES NOT HOLD THE NAME THAT ENTERS THE ENV.

For `NStmt(TypeDefStmt(name, variants))` (infer.mn:211), `name` is never registered. `register_type_constructors` (infer.mn:3319-3333) only calls `register_one_variant` per variant; `register_one_variant` (infer.mn:3443-3468) has exactly three `env_extend` calls and all three key on `ctor_name`, never `type_name`. Every other `TypeDefStmt` arm in the walk is a no-op (infer.mn:327 `=> ()`, 3970 `=> acc`, 4111 `=> UZero`, 4315 `=> ()`). So `env_lookup("Shape")` returns None, and the proposed `match env_lookup(name)` cannot fire.

Proven, not argued (tools/run-micro.sh, pinned boot):
- `type Shape = Circle(Int) | Square(Int)` twice -> exit=5, diags=0. The design's TITLE case. Its check is blind to it.
- `type Foo = A | B` / `type Bar = A | C`, then `match A {...}` -> exit=42, diags=0. This is the exact class SYNTAX.md's cited law describes ("two declarations claiming one constructor name shadow silently and mis-unify — the 2026-06-09 'expected Ty, found TokenKind' x95 class"). The design quotes that sentence as its justification, then checks "Foo"/"Bar" and never sees "A".
- `type Widget = {count: Int}` twice -> exit=1, diags=0, reproducing the claimed state exactly. But it only works because infer.mn:3450-3457's nominal-record branch is GUARDED by `total == 1 && ctor_name == type_name && is_record_ty(...)`. The type name lands in the env by coincidence, not by design. The whole generalization rests on that guard.

WORSE: THE MECHANISM IS MISDIAGNOSED. For ADT type names the failure is not "blind append + read-most-recent" — there is no env entry to shadow. Both decls' ctors CO-EXIST and both mint `TName(name, ...)`, which the type layer unifies BY NAME. Two structurally distinct types MERGE. Proven: `type Spec = Alpha(Int)|Beta(Int)` and `type Spec = Gamma(Int)|Delta(Int)`, then `fn takes_spec(s: Spec) = match s { Alpha(n) => n, Beta(n) => n+1 }` called as `takes_spec(Gamma(42))` -> exit=42, diags=0. A foreign type's ctor matched the `Alpha` arm via tag_id 0 aliasing. That is a silent-WRONG-VALUE, strictly worse than shadowing (shadowing discards one decl; this keeps both and fuses them) — and it is precisely the class this column-2 item exists to close.

BLAST RADIUS THE DESIGN NEVER ASKS: the canonical wheel input (march.sh:70-71, `-not -path '*/tutorial/*'`; my first scan wrongly included lib/tutorial and I corrected it) contains exactly one duplicate top-level name — `type FeedbackSpec` at lib/prelude.mn:387 (`Delay|Accumulate|FilterSpec`) and src/types.mn:956 (`FbState|FbDelay|FbFilter`) — two decls of ONE concept (both comments say "the RHS shape of `<~`", src/types.mn:951, lib/prelude.mn:380), disjoint ctor sets, silently merged under one TName today. It is an ADT, so the design's check will not fire on it: the single live duplicate `type` in the medium's own source is invisible to the gate built to find duplicate types. I verified 0 duplicate fn / handler / effect names in the canonical wheel, so the fn/effect/alias half is clean and would not fire.

GATE REFUTED AS MIS-CALIBRATED: `smuggle-duplicate-type.mn` uses Widget — a nominal RECORD, the one shape the check accidentally covers. It goes green while the ADT class stays broken. A gate calibrated on the coincidence certifies the miss.

NOVELTY OVERCLAIM: "a Rust/OCaml-style partitioned design could never unify [redeclaration and import collision] — they'd need two separate checks, one per axis." rustc funnels both `fn f` and `use ...::f` through one resolver define/try_define into a per-namespace binding table and reports E0428 vs E0252 as different messages off the SAME collision mechanism. The claim is false about the tool it names. It is also moot: Mentl's env is not one flat namespace across kinds. It holds value-level names (fns, ctors, effect decls, ops, handlers, aliases); nominal ADT type names live only in `TName(name, args)` and resolve by name-comparison in the type layer — a second namespace with no collision check and, unlike the env, no shadowing at all. "Constructors share ONE namespace" is a sentence about constructors; the design used it to motivate a check placed where constructors are invisible.

Confirmed TRUE (attacked, survived): env_extend appends blindly (pipeline.mn:448-457); latest-wins holds via both paths (env_index_add prepends, pipeline.mn:407-409; env_bucket_pos takes first live, 414-419; env_find_flat scans backward, 473-480); pre_register_stmt's one caller is infer.mn:174 via 204; handlers fall to `_ => ()`; register_handler's two extends (~3662 pre-arms, ~3717 re-store) match the design's reading; E_ImportNameCollision has zero implementation repo-wide; `report(DiagKind)` exists (types.mn:1346).
  fixes    : 1. SPLIT THE CLASS — it is two, not one.

(a) ENV-LEVEL COLLISION (fn / effect-name / op / ctor / alias / refine / handler): real shadow-and-discard, latest-wins. The correct site is the ONE funnel every registration already flows through — `env_extend` (pipeline.mn:448) — gated on the live scope depth. `len(scopes) == 0` iff top-level, and `scopes` is state the env handler ALREADY holds (env_scope_enter/exit, pipeline.mn:460-471). Read that fact live instead of re-deriving "is this a top-level decl?" by enumerating five NStmt arms and getting the name wrong in one of them. The design's stated reason for rejecting env_extend ("also used for lexical bindings where shadowing is correct") is exactly the fact `scopes` answers — it discarded a fact the graph holds and rebuilt a worse proxy for it. Only this site sees ctors and ops, so only this site catches the x95 class and two effects both declaring `print`.

Sharp edge the correction must own, not wave at: register_handler's second `env_extend(hname, ...)` (infer.mn:3717) legitimately re-stores the same name at the same depth. A scope-gated check false-fires there. The honest read is that this re-store is itself a second edge into one name carrying the arms' unifications — the Carried-Truth smell the gate exposes — so it is either distinguished (a re-store of the binding just drawn) or dissolved. Do not paper it with an exemption flag.

(b) NOMINAL TYPE-NAME COLLISION (ADTs): not an env question at all. The type name has no binding; two decls merge under one `TName`. Closing it requires the type layer to own nominal identity — a decl handle per `type`, so `TName` carries the handle it was minted from and two decls of one name are two handles that refuse to unify. That is the ultimate form (identity is an edge, not a string), and it is strictly bigger than one check. Say so; do not fold it into (a).

2. RETRACT "of ANY kind, not just `type`". The honest scope of the proposed site is: duplicate fn, duplicate alias/refine, duplicate effect NAME, duplicate nominal-record name, duplicate handler name. `type` (ADT) — the headline — is the one kind it structurally cannot cover.

3. RETRACT "Depends on nothing in this cluster" / "zero additional cost". `type FeedbackSpec` x2 (lib/prelude.mn:387, src/types.mn:956) is a real prerequisite: one home must win before any nominal-type gate can go green on the self-compile. Two decls of one concept is itself the Carried-Truth violation; resolving it is part of the work, named.

4. RETRACT "discharges E_ImportNameCollision for free". It falls out only for value names. The realistic import collision — two modules exporting `Some` — is a ctor collision, which the proposed site cannot reach. It falls out of correction (1a), not from the dispatch point.

5. RE-CALIBRATE THE GATE. Required fixtures, each MUST fail pre-fix: an ADT duplicate (`type Shape = Circle(Int) | Square(Int)` x2 — currently exit=5, 0 diags); a cross-type ctor collision (`type Foo = A|B` / `type Bar = A|C` — currently exit=42, 0 diags, the x95 shape); and the nominal merge (`takes_spec(s: Spec)` matching Alpha/Beta fed `Gamma(42)` — currently exit=42, 0 diags, a live silent-wrong-value). The Widget nominal-record fixture is insufficient and must not stand alone: it passes through the ctor_name==type_name guard and would certify a fix that misses the class. Keep `sound-shadowed-let.mn` as the must-not-fire control.

6. RE-STATE THE NOVELTY VERDICT without the false Rust claim. rustc reports duplicate-definition and duplicate-import off one collision mechanism in its resolver. The native argument that actually holds is different and stronger: Mentl's env handler carries `scopes` live, so "is this binding top-level?" is a graph read, not a symbol-table property to model — which is why the gate belongs at the one edge-drawing funnel rather than at N decl sites. Make that the claim; it survives.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.infer.handler-abandon-result-unify
  `~>`'s type rule never unifies an Abandon-discipline handler's arm-result type against the installed body's own type — `infer_pipe_tee` blindly binds the whole expression's type to `TVar(lh)` (the body's type alone), so ANY Abandon handler whose arm produces a different type than the body's natural completion silently compiles and traps (or worse) at runtime instead of raising E_TypeMismatch.
  verdict  : REFUTED — `catch_abort` is **not** an Abandon handler (`abort() -> !` types as `TUnit`, and `arm_disc_of` maps `UZero`×`TUnit` → **OneShot**, infer.mn:4041), and `(5) ~> catch_abort` does **not** trap: I ran it through the pinned boot and it **exits 5**, correctly. The item's name, thesis, worked example, gate fixture and interconnection all rest on two false artifact claims, and its gate's leg 1 fails against its own fix.
  objection: **The item's own empirical claim is false, and I disproved it with the pinned boot compiler.**

artifact_state asserts: "Confirmed empirically: `(5) ~> catch_abort` compiles with zero diagnostics and traps at runtime." I compiled a self-contained fixture (`effect Abort { abort() -> ! }` + `handler catch_abort { abort() => None }` + `fn main() = { let x = (5) ~> catch_abort  x }`) against `boot/mentl.wasm` with the canonical `wt_run`/`wt_asm` flags:

- compile_exit=0; the **only** diagnostic on the fixture's own lines is `parser: P_UnexpectedToken Warning: unexpected token in type position — expected a type` at the `!` of `abort() -> !`. **Zero diagnostics at the install site.**
- assembled OK; **RUN EXIT=5.** It does not trap.

Exit 5 is the *correct* answer, not a bug: the body `5` never performs `abort`, so the arm never fires and the value is the body's own. `graph_bind(handle, TVar(lh), …)` (infer.mn:1305) typing it `Int` is right, and the runtime agrees. **The proposed fix would reject a program that compiles and runs correctly** (`unify_types(TVar(lh)=Int, Option(a))` → E_TypeMismatch). I also ran the abort-taken path (`fn risky(n) with Abort = if n > 3 { abort() } else { n }`; `(risky(9)) ~> catch_abort` matched Some/None) → **exit 42**, correct.

**And the taxonomy is refuted at the artifact.** types.mn:1625-1627 declares `effect Abort { abort() -> ! }`. SYNTAX.md names the `TBang` lathe-lag (`-> !` "rides unexpected-token recovery (typed as unit)") — which my run confirms via that exact P_UnexpectedToken. infer.mn:4039-4043 then reads:
```
UZero => match ret {
  TVar(_) => OneShot,   // bottom: `fail -> a` …
  TUnit   => OneShot,   // `abort -> !` (parsed TUnit) / unit — no value to abandon
  _       => Abandon    // value-returning UZero (`abort -> Option`) …
}
```
So `catch_abort` classifies **OneShot**. infer.mn:3766-3779 states it as the M4 design record: "`abort() -> !` … are BOTTOM … They stay OneShot — so **NO wheel op changes disc**", and Abandon is reserved for an op declared `-> Option`. There are **zero Abandon handlers in the wheel**, so this cannot be a wheel correctness-spine item as claimed.

**The safety argument's mechanism is fabricated.** "for resuming handlers it is a provably redundant no-op (the transitive link through TCont already made them equal)". `TCont(op_ret_ty, TVar(result_h), …)` (infer.mn:2806-2810) and `unify(abh, result_h, …)` (infer.mn:2815) both name `result_h` = `s_h`. That is a **tautology inside the handler-decl frame**; neither touches the install's `lh`. No union-find link between `s_h` and `lh` exists today — which is exactly why the design must add one. The real no-op property (which the design never states) comes from `generalize` (infer.mn:2914-2921): `chase_deep(ty)` then `Forall(free_in_ty(body_ty), body_ty)` — `s_h` is quantified **only while still free**. Two wheel handlers bind it concretely: `catch_abort` (`abort() => None` → `Option(a)`) and `fail_exit` (lib/runtime/io.mn:132-137, arm ends `proc_exit(1)`, never resumes) — for the latter `free_in_ty` of a concrete type is empty, so **nothing is quantified and every install is pinned**.

**The gate contradicts the fix.** Leg 1 (`let x: Int = (5) ~> catch_abort` "should compile and run fine") unifies `Int` with `Option(a)` under the design → E_TypeMismatch. Leg 1 **fails against the fix it certifies**. Leg 2's `String` is irrelevant: I proved `let x: String = 5` raises **nothing** today (no diagnostic on that line; the plain let-annotation isn't enforced on this path), so both legs collapse to the same `Int ~ Option(a)` unification while asserting opposite outcomes.

**The edit does not compile.** `s_h` is minted at infer.mn:3696; `handler_ty = TName("Handler", [inst_ty])` is at infer.mn:3633 — 63 lines **earlier**. `TVar(s_h)` at 3633 is an unbound name.

What survives: infer.mn:1305 does bind the whole `~>` expr to the body's type with zero read of arm results; `register_handler`'s scheme (3633) carries only `inst_ty`, never `s_h`; the `"Handler"` grep is exactly 2 sites (infer.mn:1403, 3633) and no user-facing `Handler(...)` annotation exists, so a 2-ary change is contained. A real gap exists — it is just not the one described.
  fixes    : **1. Rename and re-scope — the Abandon framing is dead.** The wheel has zero Abandon handlers (infer.mn:3771-3773: "They stay OneShot — so NO wheel op changes disc"). Drop `Hβ.infer.handler-abandon-result-unify`. The true gap is discipline-independent: `infer_pipe_tee` never relates the handler's answer type to the install. Name it `Hβ.infer.handler-answer-type-at-install`.

**2. Delete both fabricated claims.** "traps at runtime" → **runs, exit 5** (measured). "catch_abort is Abandon" → **OneShot** (infer.mn:4041). "provably redundant no-op via the TCont link" → there is **no** s_h↔lh link; the no-op holds only while `s_h` is free, per `generalize`'s `chase_deep` → `free_in_ty` (infer.mn:2919-2920). State the real predicate: **the constraint is vacuous iff every arm ends in `resume(…)`; it bites the moment an arm binds `s_h` concretely** (`catch_abort` → `Option(a)`; `fail_exit` → `proc_exit`'s type, quantifying nothing).

**3. Keep digging — the root reduces further (CLAUDE.md ⟲: "do NOT crown the next thing you see as the root").** The silent-wrong generator here is the **missing bottom type**, not `infer_pipe_tee`. `abort() -> !` degrades to `TUnit` through unexpected-token recovery, and I watched it poison downstream: `E_TypeMismatch: () vs Int` at `fn risky(n) with Abort = if n > 3 { abort() } else { n }`, then `() vs Option(t13342)` at the match. This is SYNTAX.md's own named lathe-lag (no `TBang` arm in `parse_type_atom`). Land `Hβ.syntax.bang-bottom-type` first: with a real `⊥` that unifies with any consumer, `risky` types as `Int`, `arm_disc_of`'s `TVar(_)/TUnit => OneShot` arms get a principled `TBottom => OneShot` peer, and the `-> !` P_UnexpectedToken (present in **every** run above) disappears.

**4. Answer the design question the item never asks (interrogation 2 — Handler).** Mentl handlers have **no return clause** (SYNTAX.md §Handler declarations: config params, state, op arms — nothing else), so a handler structurally *cannot* wrap the body's normal completion. `catch_abort`'s `Option` answer is therefore unreachable from an `Int` body by construction. Decide explicitly: (a) the return-clause-less rule the item's unification accidentally encodes — answer type ≡ body type, forcing callers to pre-wrap (which is exactly what search.mn:109's `match attempted { Some(value) … None … }` already demands of `try_fn`); or (b) a distinct answer type carried on the handler, requiring a return clause. The item picks (a) by accident while believing it fixes Abandon. Pick it *on purpose*, or reject it.

**5. Fix the edit site.** `s_h` must be minted before line 3633 (currently 3696), or `handler_ty` constructed after it. State this; the literal edit as written does not compile.

**6. Replace the gate.** Both legs collapse to one unification and assert opposite outcomes. A real gate must (i) use an op whose declared return is a **concrete TName** (`abort() -> Option`) — the only shape `arm_disc_of` routes to `Abandon` (infer.mn:4042) — since that is the case the existing crucibles test; (ii) assert the mismatch fixture raises E_TypeMismatch where it raises nothing today (verified: it does not today); (iii) assert `(5) ~> catch_abort` **still exits 5** if the design intends to preserve it, or state honestly that it is now rejected; (iv) run the full board (`tools/state.sh`) — 102 handler decls across src/lib are in the blast radius and the design assesses none of them. Note in the item's favour: the high-install handlers I checked (`diagnostics_handler`, `mutate_sink`, `arm_state_ctx`, `summaries_frozen`, `fresh_for_inference`, and every prelude collector) end their arms in `resume(…)`, so `s_h` stays free and the fixpoint likely survives — but that is a measurement the design must make, not inherit from me.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: The oracle-fuzz loop (PLAN S11 col. 2; graduates to Hβ.synth.oracle-fuzz-loop)
  The oracle-fuzz loop is enumerate_typed and enumerate_vocabulary_calls generalized from filling one authored hole to composing whole programs over the wheel's own vocabulary and the Ty/EffRow grammar itself, walked enumeratively to a ratcheted depth and judged by three oracle legs that need no external reference compiler.
  verdict  : REFUTED — Stage 1's gate is vacuous by construction (`enumerate_inhabitants` never consults the vocabulary, and `ty = TUnit` yields exactly one effect-free candidate, so `E_EffectMismatch` can never fire at any depth), and `fuzz_rows -> [EffRow]` fuzzes inference's OUTPUT representation, for which the AST has no slot.
  objection: THE GATE PASSES VACUOUSLY, AND CANNOT DO OTHERWISE.

The design's Stage 1: "fix ty = TUnit, vary only fuzz_rows and vocabulary/handler-chain composition — ... calls the EXISTING enumerate_inhabitants(ty, row, top_level_context) per pair". Both halves are impossible against the arm it names.

1. `enumerate_inhabitants` never touches the vocabulary. Its whole body (synth_proposer.mn:140-143) is `let enriched = enumerate_typed(...); yield_inhabitants(...)`. Only `synth_propose` (synth_proposer.mn:114-115) calls `enumerate_vocabulary_calls` and concats. So "vary ... vocabulary composition" via `enumerate_inhabitants` is unreachable.

2. `ty = TUnit` collapses the yield to one candidate. `enumerate_typed`'s TUnit arm (synth_proposer.mn:190) is `one_unit_candidate(context)`, which returns a 1-element buffer holding `LitUnit` (synth_proposer.mn:443-453). Stage 1 therefore generates exactly one program per row — `fn main() with <row> = ()` — which performs nothing. No row is violable; the oracle expects no mismatch and always gets none. Green at every depth, forever, whether or not the crown is sound. That is the definition of a gate that passes vacuously.

3. The topologies it claims to generalize are structurally out of reach. tests/crown/smuggle-transitive.mn needs a multi-decl chain (`fn a() = op(); fn b() = a(); fn bad() with !E = b()`); smuggle-higher-order.mn needs `fn run(f) = f()` plus a lambda argument. The vocabulary enumerator admits ONLY zero-arg fns (`if len(params) == 0`, synth_proposer.mn:233) and EXCLUDES any name whose free-name closure reaches the enclosing decl (`!reaches_decl(name, enclosing, [])`, line 235). Arg-bearing fns are the file's own NAMED OPEN peer, `Hβ.synth.vocabulary-arg-holes` (synth_proposer.mn:221-223) — which the design neither cites nor sequences. So "direct/transitive/higher-order" is not generable from this substrate.

THE SECOND FATAL ERROR — the design fuzzes the wrong representation, and would become a second writer.

`FnStmt(String, List, Node, List, Node)` (types.mn:1049) carries its authored clause as `[(EffName, negated: Bool, EConn)]` — parser.mn:559-568: "Each entry is (EffName, negated, EConn); infer folds the list applying each connective ... the parser names the topology, infer reads the algebra live." `build_declared_row` (infer.mn:573-589) destructures exactly that: `let (name, negated, conn) = eff`. `EffRow` is inference's OUTPUT — types.mn:375-376: "After normalization every row is one of EfPure / EfClosed / EfOpen. EfNeg / EfSub / EfInter are intermediate forms that reduce away." So `fuzz_rows(depth) -> [EffRow]` produces values with NO slot in the AST, and "wraps every survivor as `fn main() with row = <candidate>`, minted through the same synth_node/mint_node fabric" is not constructible. Minting the algebra's normalized output straight into the weave inverts the artifact's own division and makes the fuzzer a SECOND WRITER (PLAN §3 L0: "Only inference writes").

AND THE ADT COUNT IS WRONG. "recurses over EffRow's 5 constructors (types.mn:378-384)" — there are SIX: EfPure, EfClosed, EfOpen, EfNeg, EfSub, EfInter (verified: `sed -n '378,384p' src/types.mn | grep -cE '^\s*[=|]\s*Ef'` = 6; `show_effrow` matches all six at types.mn:2020-2031). The design never says which it drops. The entire novelty verdict rests on "exhaustive-at-depth-N is a PROOF of absence up to that bound" — a generator that miscounts the constructor set at design time cannot ground that proof, and if the dropped arm is EfNeg, Stage 1 fuzzes the crown's topology with the crown's own operator absent.

"ZERO NEW DEPENDENCY" IS FALSE ON BOTH LEGS.

- `enumerate_inhabitants` has ZERO callers (grep across src/ lib/ tests/ tools/: only the decl at mentl.mn:108, the arm at synth_proposer.mn:140, and comments). Both proven `??` workflows go through `synth_propose` — the OneShot op (cursor.mn:641, mentl.mn:493). Stage 1 would be the first-ever invocation of the MultiShot yield path (`resume([c.node])` then recurse, synth_proposer.mn:156-157), and PLAN §5.R band N names two OPEN peers exactly there: `Hβ.lower.multishot-uzero-abort` ("Mentl currently tail-resumes a zero-resume arm") and `Hβ.lower.config-fn-evidence-in-arm`. "Depends on nothing not already landed" is an overclaim about dormant substrate.
- `format_at_handle` / `format_program` / `format_chain` / `format_default` have ZERO callers outside format.mn — the Format effect is declared, bodied, and never installed. The design quotes destiny-audit:17 and then routes its whole harness through two of the exact engines that audit names as unwired ("Synth, the Why engine, persist, Verify ... every one is a real engine with no live loop calling it", destiny-audit:10-14).

THE PROJECTION CHANNEL IS A FABRICATION FLOOR (a real latent finding, worth filing regardless). `format_at_handle` is not a round-tripping graph→source projection: `render_expr_tokens` ends `_ => "<expr>"` (format.mn:348), `render_stmt_tokens` `_ => "<stmt>"` (357), `render_pat_tokens` `_ => "<pat>"` (364) — every NodeBody outside the 17 named arms renders as literal `<expr>`, which is not Mentl source (it lexes as a comparison chain). Worse for Stage 1 specifically: `render_with_clause` (format.mn:396-399) calls `build_row_from_effs(effs) = EfClosed(effs)` (402), wrapping the parser's 3-tuple list in EfClosed — whose payload is documented `[EffName]` (types.mn:380). `show_effrow` → `show_list(names, show_eff_name, " + ")` → `show_eff_name` on a 3-tuple hits its display-totality floor `_ => "«invalid-effect»"` (types.mn:2047). The clause renders as `with «invalid-effect»` and the `negated` bit is DROPPED — the exact bit Stage 1 fuzzes, destroyed at projection. It is dead code today, which is why it survived; the design would make it live without noticing. Those three `_ =>` floors are also, verbatim, CLAUDE.md's red-flag surrender-fallback.

THE ORACLE PROBLEM IS UNNAMED. "judged by three oracle legs that need no external reference compiler" does not survive. Leg 1's "trivial oracle (E_EffectMismatch presence matches the topology's own shape)" requires computing the expected verdict from the generated program — which past a direct call of a known-effectful op IS the row algebra (`row_subsumes` / `forbidden_names_disjoint`), the thing under test. Either the fuzzer reuses the wheel's `row_subsumes` (tautological — the compiler agrees with itself, vacuous a second way) or hand-writes a second row model (a second home for the algebra = Carried-Truth violated, and a new !Outside — the "external reference" the headline denies needing). Csmith answers this with differential testing across N independent compilers; the design rejects that and substitutes nothing. Leg 3 (boot vs fresh, frontier-gate.sh:57-61/409-443) is real plumbing but only detects divergence between two generations of ONE wheel — a fixpoint check; two generations sharing a bug agree. Leg 2 is deferred to an unlanded item. So the trio is: tautological-or-external, deferred, and self-agreement. Not a proof of absence.

WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE (these held): Ty has exactly 14 constructors (types.mn:34-48) and `enumerate_typed` matches all 14 exhaustively (synth_proposer.mn:184-199) — CONFIRMED. `row_subsumes` gates the vocabulary with rejections kept as Reasons (synth_proposer.mn:236-241) — CONFIRMED. `propose_at` (cursor.mn:638) and `teach_synthesize` (mentl.mn:488) both derive target_ty from a real hole (`ty_of_kind(h,k)` / `graph_chase(hole_handle)` → `NBound(ty)`); neither walks the Ty grammar — CONFIRMED. frontier-gate.sh:296-390 holds the three workflows; 42-70/409-443 is the `both` dual-invocation plumbing — CONFIRMED. destiny-audit:17 quotes synth_proposer.mn:570's "the loop is closed" — CONFIRMED (though the anchor has itself drifted; that text is now near line 710). PLAN.md:1257-1259 asserts the trust trio with oracle-fuzz feeding both — CONFIRMED. crown-gate.sh is 41 lines — CONFIRMED. wasm-tools 1.252.0 installed and unused — CONFIRMED. The PRNG rejection is genuinely sound on reproducibility grounds, and the ratchet analogy to tools/verify.sh:84 ("errors may never RISE") is fair and native. I could not break any of these.

MINOR STALENESS (calibration, not refutation): "beside the five gates already there" — state.sh runs SIX (`grep -c '^  gate ' tools/state.sh` = 6; PHANTOMS/comment-ratchet.sh landed today, 7e47e850). "grep for fuzz/shrink ... returns nothing but one unrelated English-verb comment (frontier-gate.sh:201)" — there are five: infer.mn:4203, effects.mn:300, types.mn:513, types.mn:2044, frontier-gate.sh:201 (the substantive conclusion is right; the grep was not run). "each gated by verify_candidate's checkpoint->infer->refinement_admits->rollback" — `verify_candidate` (synth_proposer.mn:120-138) does NOT checkpoint or roll back; its CALLERS bracket it (lines 152-154, 547-549). Three careless reads in one brief, and they cluster with the 5-vs-6 miscount.
  fixes    : 1. FUZZ THE AUTHORED CLAUSE, NEVER THE ROW. Delete `fuzz_rows(depth) -> [EffRow]`. The generable object is the surface clause the AST actually carries: `[(EffName, negated: Bool, EConn)]` (parser.mn:559-568; FnStmt's 4th slot, types.mn:1049), where EffName comes from the live env's EffectOpScheme entries (types.mn:348), `negated` is Bool, and EConn is the finite `EcUnion | EcSub | EcInter` (types.mn:432-434). Let `build_declared_row` (infer.mn:573-589) project it. The artifact states the division the design must adopt: "the parser names the topology, infer reads the algebra live" (parser.mn:560-562). This is smaller than the design, it IS the design's own stated principle ("the type system read backwards") applied correctly, and it removes the second-writer violation of PLAN §3 L0.

2. STATE THE COUNT CORRECTLY OR DROP THE PROOF CLAIM. EffRow has SIX constructors (types.mn:378-384). Under correction 1 the count is moot for the generator; but if any exhaustive-at-depth-N claim survives, it must enumerate the authored clause's finite product (EffName × Bool × EConn) and say so, and must not claim "PROOF of absence" beyond the subset the oracle actually DECIDES.

3. NAME THE ORACLE FORK, OR THE HEADLINE FALLS. "Three oracle legs that need no external reference compiler" must either (a) restrict leg 1 to generate-with-known-answer over the topologies the generator constructs by shape — an honest, modest claim, not a proof of absence; or (b) name the second row model as a NEW !Outside and justify it against Carried-Truth. Reusing the wheel's `row_subsumes` as the expected-verdict computer is tautological and must be rejected by name. Leg 3 must be re-described honestly: boot-vs-fresh differential is a FIXPOINT check, not a soundness check — two generations of one wheel share their bugs and agree.

4. STAGE 1 MUST NOT PIN ty = TUnit, AND MUST NOT ROUTE THROUGH enumerate_inhabitants. `TUnit` yields one effect-free candidate (synth_proposer.mn:190, 443-453) and `enumerate_inhabitants` ignores the vocabulary entirely (140-143). To reach a leak the generator needs an effect-PERFORMING body, i.e. the vocabulary path — which today means `synth_propose` (OneShot, returns Proposals) — and to reach transitive/higher-order it needs arg-bearing calls and lambdas, which is the file's own named-open peer `Hβ.synth.vocabulary-arg-holes` (synth_proposer.mn:221-223). Sequence that peer FIRST and say so, or state positively that Stage 1 covers only the DIRECT topology and cannot express transitive/higher-order — do not claim tests/crown's five fixtures "generalized" when three of the five shapes are ungenerable.

5. DELETE format_at_handle FROM THE HARNESS. It has zero callers, its handler is never installed, `render_expr/stmt/pat_tokens` carry three `_ => "<expr>"/"<stmt>"/"<pat>"` surrender floors (format.mn:348/357/364), and `render_with_clause` → `build_row_from_effs(effs) = EfClosed(effs)` (396-402) mis-wraps the parser's 3-tuples so every clause renders `with «invalid-effect»` via show_eff_name's floor (types.mn:2047) with the `negated` bit dropped. Either generate SOURCE TEXT directly (the fixture is text; the compiler is the consumer; no graph→source projection needed at all — strictly less code) or name `Hβ.format.source-projection-total` as a positive-form prerequisite and land it first. Independently: file the `build_row_from_effs` type bug and the three surrender floors as findings against format.mn — they are real, and dormancy is why they survived.

6. RETRACT "zero new dependency" / "depends on nothing not already landed." Both `enumerate_inhabitants` and the whole Format effect have ZERO callers. Say the true thing: this is the first invocation of two dormant engines, one of them the MultiShot path with two open band-N peers against it (`Hβ.lower.multishot-uzero-abort`, `Hβ.lower.config-fn-evidence-in-arm`). That is the destiny audit's own "machinery real, wiring absent" verdict applying to this design's own foundation — the design cites the audit and then stands on what it indicts.

7. FIX THE STALE READS: state.sh runs six gates, not five (comment-ratchet.sh, 7e47e850). `verify_candidate` does not checkpoint/rollback — its callers do (synth_proposer.mn:152-154, 547-549). `enumerate_vocabulary_calls` takes NO `env_snapshot()` read: it destructures `Context(scope_bindings, _active, enclosing)` (synth_proposer.mn:226) and its row is `GraphRead + GraphWrite + !Mutate` with no EnvRead; the live env reads are at cursor.mn:652 and synth_proposer.mn:323. Consequently `fuzz_rows` cannot be "pure" while reading the env (`env_snapshot() -> Env` is an EnvRead op, types.mn:1200-1203 — `with Pure` means the row is literally empty, SYNTAX.md), and `fuzz_programs(depth) with Synth + GraphRead + GraphWrite + !Mutate` omits the EnvRead it needs. Project all EIGHT interrogations, not four: the brief skips Row, Ownership, Refinement, and Gradient — and Row is exactly where it breaks.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.closure.correctness-oracle-internal
  The wheel's own Verify becomes the correctness oracle through a third peer handler on the already-documented swap surface — verify_witness compiles one refinement predicate both symbolically and executably, so a statically-proven claim that fails at runtime is a self-detected miscompile with no reference compiler needed.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the witness is a structural tautology. `predicate_decide` returns `Some(true)` if and only if the predicate is already proven true from the graph's own literals (verify.mn:61-91), so lowering *that* predicate to a runtime expression emits a guard that evaluates to true by construction; the program's value is provably absent from it, and the "oracle" therefore cannot detect the miscompiles it claims to.
  objection: THE MECHANISM CANNOT FIRE ON WHAT IT CLAIMS TO DETECT — it lowers only predicates already reduced to `true`.

`predicate_decide` (verify.mn:61-70) reaches `Some(true)` through exactly three doors, and every one of them means "proven true":
  - `PTrue` (verify.mn:62) — a literal true.
  - `PCompare` -> `compare_decide` (verify.mn:74-80), which returns non-None only when BOTH operands resolve through `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) to a bare `LitInt/LitFloat/LitString/LitBool`. Both operands are literal constants.
  - `PAnd/POr/PNot` recursing on the above (`and_decide` verify.mn:196-208 yields Some(true) only from Some(true)+Some(true); `or_decide` verify.mn:210-222 is the sole door admitting an undecided subterm — `or_decide(None, Some(true)) => Some(true)`).
  - `PFlowLe` (verify.mn:69) — always decidable, discussed below.

So the design's own trigger ("whenever predicate_decide(subst_self(pred, value_handle)) returns Some(true), it ALSO lowers the same post-substitution pred") fires precisely on predicates that are closed over literals. The repo's only fixture that reaches this door is tests/micros/mn-refine-let.mn — `type Sample = Float where -1.0 <= self && self <= 1.0` / `let ok: Sample = 0.5`. After subst_self the predicate is `PAnd(PCompare(BLe, LitFloat(-1.0), LitFloat(0.5)), PCompare(BLe, LitFloat(0.5), LitFloat(1.0)))`. The design's `predicate_to_expr` emits `if !(-1.0 <= 0.5 && 0.5 <= 1.0) { trap }`. That is `if !true { trap }`. The binding `ok` never appears in it. Even the `or_decide` escape hatch yields `h || true` — still a tautology, h irrelevant.

The design's Carried-Truth boast IS the refutation, stated in its own words: "predicate_to_expr reads the identical Predicate node predicate_decide already reads — zero re-derivation, one AST, two consumers, the Carried-Truth audit passed by construction". A differential oracle REQUIRES two independent derivations. The design proudly has one. Two consumers of one node cannot disagree about that node; they can only disagree about the fold (`litval_le`, verify.mn:184-189, vs the emitted `f64.le`), on constant operands, evaluated by the same engine. Law 1 is the right law for a compiler (never re-derive) and exactly the wrong law for an oracle (an oracle IS a second derivation). That is why band J's `Hβ.closure.diverse-double-compilation` — which the design waves off as "a different leg with a different threat model" — supplies the independence this leg entirely lacks and never notices it needs.

Residual yield, measured: across 147 fixtures (tests/micros 127, frontier 12, crown 5, native-cont 3) only 8 carry a refinement at all; of those, mn-refine.mn never constructs one, mn-refine-reject.mn is refused at compile, mn-proof-debt-surfaced.mn is the `None` case the design excludes. Ceiling: ~2 fixtures (mn-refine-let.mn, mn-refined-cmp.mn's `vcmp(4200, 5000)` -> `if !(0 <= 4200) { trap }`), both tautologies. The only bug a tautology guard can catch is an emit that mangles a literal's value in an order-breaking way at a refined construction site — and the witness's own constants ride the same emit path, so even the historical `every float lexed collapsed to 0.0` class (PLAN §7, 2026-07-10) passes it silently.

FOUR further refutations, each independently sufficient:

1. THE GATE IS VACUOUS ON BOTH LEGS, TODAY, UNLANDED. "Every tests/fuzz/*.mn and tests/crown/*.mn fixture whose target type carries TRefined" — `tests/fuzz/` DOES NOT EXIST (tests/ holds only crown, frontier, micros, native-cont), and 0 of the 5 tests/crown/*.mn fixtures contain `where` (they are effect-negation crucibles: smuggle-direct/higher-order/transitive, sound-empty, sound-higher-order). Both quantifiers range over the empty set. The gate passes vacuously right now.

2. THE SCOPING ARGUMENT IS REFUTED BY A LIVE GATE FIXTURE. The design claims "refinement_admits's existing behavior does the pre-filtering for free — only candidates already proven Some(true) reach a program at all." `refinement_admits` has exactly ONE non-recursive caller: synth_proposer.mn:137. It gates SYNTH CANDIDATES, nothing else. The gate on what reaches a program is `verify_ledger`'s verify arm (verify.mn:35-45), reached from infer.mn:3313, and it RESUMES on `None`, accruing debt. tests/frontier/mn-proof-debt-surfaced.mn is the counterexample in the repo's own battery — `let claimed: Positive = x` compiles, exits 0, and RUNS with V_Pending debt; its own comment reads "The runtime value here even satisfies the predicate (unchecked(1)) — refusing it would refuse a correct program." tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh:109-147 enforces this as leg 2. The design read synth vocabulary ("candidates") as the program-wide gate.

3. PFlowLe IS UNLOWERABLE, AND THE DESIGN'S ONE SENTENCE ON IT IS FALSE. "PPatternMatch/PFlowLe are already decidable or already expression-shaped and lower the same way." `PFlowLe(FlowLabel, FlowLabel)` (types.mn:1110) carries FlowLabels, NOT node handles — `FlowLabel = Public | Tainted | Secret` (types.mn:1152-1155) is a compile-time-only lattice with `flow_le` `with Pure` (types.mn:1176-1180). Labels are erased; there is no runtime FlowLabel to test. And it IS reachable: infer.mn:4523 constructs `verify(fspan, PFlowLe(label, Public), ...)`, and verify.mn:69 decides it `Some(flow_le(a,b))` — Some(true) whenever label is Public. So the witness path fires with nothing to lower to. The only exits are fabricating `LitBool(true)` — which verify.mn:364-366 names by hand as "the most dangerous silent fallback in the compiler ... a `true` fallback in a PROOF GATE claims proof of the unprovable" — or a `_ =>` surrender. Both are Law 2. (The sibling `predicate_to_smt` verify.mn:337 renders it as the literal `"true"`, which is correct for a compile-time formula and is exactly the fabrication when the target is a runtime witness.)

4. IT IS A FIFTH HAND-WALK, AND THE LOWERING IT CALLS ABSENT ALREADY EXISTS. "No verify_witness-shaped handler and no Predicate -> Expr lowering exists anywhere in src/ (grep confirms)" is true only on the literal string "Expr". `predicate_to_smt` / `handle_to_smt` / `body_to_smt` / `smt_call` / `smt_binop` / `smt_binop_sym` (verify.mn:329-428) IS a complete, exhaustive Predicate -> checkable-form lowering, inside `handler verify_smt` (verify.mn:274) — the "sibling handler" the header already names. The design's INTERCONNECTION says R3 "needs a Predicate -> checkable-form lowering" without noticing one is already written. Predicate already has four hand-walks (predicate_decide:61, subst_self:101, classify_predicate:254, predicate_to_smt:329) plus query.mn:280 and infer.mn:4425 — the design proposes a fifth. And types.mn:1116-1119 already killed the copy this would reintroduce: "PExpr is GONE: its leaves (PSelf/PInt/PEVar/PApp) were a lossy COPY of the expression the AST node already holds ... carry the handle, read live, delete the copy." PCompare/PBoolNode/PPatternMatch already carry node HANDLES (types.mn:1104-1109). Band F's first item, `Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr`, is the ultimate form and it dissolves `predicate_to_expr` rather than building it.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE (the design's honest ground): the ARTIFACT STATE block is largely accurate. predicate_decide is live at 61-70 and IS narrower than its stale header (which still names PInt/PFloat/PString, PExpr leaves types.mn:1116 deleted). subst_self/subst_self_operand at 101-117 are correct and R2 IS landed and wired — commit 730dfe8b exists ("verify: discharge refinements against the live value (value-proof R2)"), and infer.mn:3313 calls `verify(span, subst_self(pred, value_handle), ...)`; the design's correction of the same-day destiny-audit prose against the tree is right and is the discipline working. refinement_admits:141-144 does refuse on None as hard as on Some(false). `synth_node` exists (synth_proposer.mn, 14 sites). And the design's best observation is TRUE and worth keeping: tools/verify.sh:61 certifies every micro by `micro:NAME=EXPECTED_EXIT` — a human-pinned integer (mn-effect-identity.mn pins 81 = 7*10+11) — so the battery IS an external oracle. That observation is correct; the mechanism proposed to answer it is not.
  fixes    : FOUR corrections survive. The first two are the design; the last two are its gate and its build.

1. POINT THE WITNESS AT `None`, NOT `Some(true)` — AND RENAME THE PEER. The design aims the mechanism at the set where it is a tautology and away from the only set where it has teeth: the `None` arm (verify.mn:43-44), where a real refinement over a live non-literal value accrues V_Pending debt and goes UNCHECKED at runtime. tests/frontier/mn-proof-debt-surfaced.mn is exactly that program: `let claimed: Positive = x` runs today with `0 < x` never tested. A witness THERE — `if !(0 < claimed) { trap }` — references the live value, can genuinely fire, and converts honest-but-unchecked debt into an enforced obligation. It also composes with R3 in the correct direction: R3 widens `predicate_decide`'s decidable set, which SHRINKS the witness set (the witness is the residue R3 has not reached), rather than the design's inverted "R3 widens Some(true) -> widens verify_witness's coverage" (widening coverage of tautologies). But this is NOT the correctness oracle and must not carry that name: it detects BAD PROGRAMS, never a MISCOMPILE, and subsumes zero of the 71 micros (none carry a refinement; 8 of 147 fixtures carry one at all). Name it in positive form as its own band-F peer beside `Hβ.verify.ledger-soundness`: `Hβ.verify.pending-debt-runtime-witness` — the honest sound-incomplete cash-out, gated on SYNTAX's proof-debt law (an executable with V_Pending SURFACES and compiles), and it must stay a handler swap, never a default, or it taxes every refined construction with a runtime check the gradient exists to erase.

2. THE CORRECTNESS ORACLE NEEDS A SECOND DERIVATION — AND THE MEDIUM ALREADY HAS THE NATIVE ONE. Carried-Truth guarantees the two consumers AGREE; that is the wrong law here. The independence must come from somewhere, and the design's "no reference compiler needed" is the overclaim: it removed the reference without replacing what the reference supplied. Mentl's own structure supplies it: PLAN §2 makes emit "the projected cursor ... the handler IS the backend", so TWO backend handlers on ONE LowIR are two independent projections of one graph — `><`, diffed. A miscompile surfaces as a projection disagreement, both derivations Mentl's own, `!Outside`-clean — strictly stronger than diverse-double-compilation's second seed (band J), which buys independence at the price of an external artifact and a foreign toolchain. That is what `Hβ.closure.correctness-oracle-internal` should mean, and it sequences behind R6's `~> Backend` emit seam and band N's native backend rather than being buildable now. Until then the honest statement stands unchanged: the micro battery is an external oracle and PLAN §11 col 5's named residual !Outside is correct as written.

3. THE GATE MUST QUANTIFY OVER A NON-EMPTY SET AND MUST NOT REQUIRE THE COMPILER TO STAY BAD. `tests/fuzz/` does not exist and 0/5 crown fixtures carry TRefined — both legs are vacuous today. Worse, the "confirmed present in the emitted WAT (grep for its trap instruction) rather than dead-code-eliminated" clause is anti-correlated with the medium's own improvement: `rw_const_fold` (egraph.mn:221-245) fires in the saturation loop (egraph.mn:297) and today spares the guard only because `fold_int` (egraph.mn:212-219) folds BAdd/BSub/BMul/BDiv/BMod and returns None on comparisons. The instant band G completes const-fold over BLe/BLt/BEq — the obvious ultimate direction — every tautology guard folds to `if !true { trap }`, dies to DCE, and the design's own gate reports failure on every fixture. A gate that demands the e-graph stay incomplete is a gate against the medium. Under correction 1 the gate becomes real and non-vacuous: name the fixtures that carry V_Pending debt (mn-proof-debt-surfaced.mn today), assert the witness is present AND that a deliberately-violating sibling fixture TRAPS at runtime while its ledger-compiled twin exits 0. That gate fails if the design is not landed.

4. DO NOT BUILD `predicate_to_expr`. It is the copy types.mn:1116-1119 already deleted by name ("PExpr is GONE: its leaves were a lossy COPY of the expression the AST node already holds ... carry the handle, read live, delete the copy") and a fifth hand-walk over an 8-constructor ADT that already has four (verify.mn:61, 101, 254, 329) — the direction PLAN §5.U names for retirement (four fold generators -> one `fold(ty, leaf)`), inverted. The ultimate form is band F's first item, `Hβ.types.predicate-is-expr`, completed: PCompare/PBoolNode/PPatternMatch already carry node handles (types.mn:1104-1109), so a Predicate whose leaves ARE Expr handles needs NO lowering at all — the witness is the operand handle, spliced. PFlowLe is the one constructor that is not an expression and never can be (a compile-time-only lattice, types.mn:1152-1180, with no runtime label); it must be excluded structurally, not fabricated to `LitBool(true)` — verify.mn:364-366 already names that fabrication as the most dangerous silent fallback in the compiler. And the design should state its blast radius, which it never mentions: new wheel source in src/verify.mn with no emit change holds m2 == m3 in one generation (PLAN §7, 2026-07-10 — "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation ... the transition form is for EMIT changes only"), so the fixpoint is safe; silence on the march is an omission, not a hazard.

REFUTED: PLAN S11 col.5 — license + public-repo readiness (the CLAUDE.md leak)
  The repo is public (`gh repo view` -> isPrivate:false) and is currently serving the full internal CLAUDE.md plus two agent-skill definitions plus a vscode-extension binary — all six paths explicitly gitignored with the stated intent to keep them private, tracked anyway since before that intent was recorded, and never actually removed from the index.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the finding is real (6 gitignored-yet-tracked files are live on a public remote), but the design's causal core is a misread of the artifact: `git rm` at this exact path was run twice and defeated twice, so the proposed remedy is the failed remedy and its stated rationale ("stops FUTURE commits from re-exposing edits") is falsified by the very commit the design cites as evidence.
  objection: The design's causal core is false, and it is the sentence the whole remedy rests on: "commit ba298e53 ... deleted its 235 lines of content ... but never ran git rm --cached, so the path stayed tracked."

`git show --name-status ba298e53` returns `D	CLAUDE.md`, and `git ls-tree ba298e53 -- CLAUDE.md` returns empty. The untrack SUCCEEDED; the path left the tree. The real history (`git log --diff-filter=AD --name-status --reverse -- CLAUDE.md`, no --follow) is a defeat cycle, not a never-ran:
  1985aad8 (2026-03-16) A  <- the ACTUAL first commit
  ba298e53 (2026-03-19) D  <- untrack #1 SUCCEEDS, ignore rule added
  dc4352c7 (2026-03-19) A  <- re-added the SAME DAY
  81d7a397 (2026-03-23) D  <- untrack #2 ("cargo fmt", -378 lines, +3 .agents/workflows deleted)
  806ec82c (2026-03-24) A  <- re-added; survives 116 commits to f7a1b3a5

At BOTH re-adds the ignore rule was already live: `git show dc4352c7:.gitignore` line 3 = `CLAUDE.md`; `git show 806ec82c:.gitignore` line 4 = `CLAUDE.md` (plus `.agents/` at line 3). Git refuses `git add` on an ignored untracked path without `-f`. I tried to exonerate the design with the innocent explanation — a merge replaying an old branch bypasses .gitignore harmlessly — and it is ruled out: `git log --merges --oneline | wc -l` = 0 across `git rev-list --count HEAD` = 1550, and all four commits are single-parent. So the writer force-added or rebase-replayed; both bypass .gitignore by construction.

Therefore the design re-applies the twice-failed remedy (`git rm --cached` + an ignore line) and asserts "This stops FUTURE commits from re-exposing edits" — a claim the repo's own history disproves. It diagnoses an ABSENT control where the artifact shows a DEFEATED control. Per Law 2 that is a patch, not a restructure; per Law 5 it is an assertion the tool contradicts.

Two more factual errors, same shape:
- "first committed at 806ec82c (the pre-rename Inka era)" is false. First commit is 1985aad8 (2026-03-16); `git merge-base --is-ancestor ba298e53 806ec82c` returns true, so 806ec82c is a DESCENDANT of the untrack — the third add, not the first. The design read a `--follow`-distorted log.
- "add one line for stray tools/editor/ binaries so a future extension rebuild doesn't re-track" — the rule ALREADY EXISTS at `tools/editor/vscode-mentl/.gitignore:3` (`*.vsix`), confirmed by `git check-ignore -v --no-index tools/editor/vscode-mentl/mentl-0.1.0.vsix`. It too was defeated. Three for three, the design mistakes a defeated control for a missing one.

Where I was disproved: I expected the secrets scan to find something that would force step (2). It did not. Scanning every historical blob of CLAUDE.md across all 126 commits touching the leaked paths for `sk-*`/`ghp_*`/`github_pat_*`/`AKIA*`/PEM headers/`api_key=` and for any email address returns zero hits. The design's "no credentials or PII, only internal-discipline prose" holds, and holds more strongly than it claimed (it only read current content; the full history is clean). The license half is also fully verified: LICENSE-MIT + LICENSE-APACHE added at 75e59999 (2026-03-20), referenced at README.md:597 exactly. The gate is non-vacuous: `git ls-files -i -c --exclude-standard` returns exactly the 6 claimed paths today, and origin/main carries all 6 with `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...main` = 0/0 and `git diff origin/main -- CLAUDE.md` = 0 bytes at 427 lines.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the causal claim. The design must NOT say "never ran git rm --cached, so the path stayed tracked." It must say: the untrack was run twice (ba298e53 `D CLAUDE.md`; 81d7a397 `D CLAUDE.md`, -378 lines) and defeated twice by a writer that re-drew the edge (dc4352c7, 806ec82c) while the ignore rule was live. Fix the two other misreads: first commit is 1985aad8 (2026-03-16), not 806ec82c (which merge-base proves is a descendant of the untrack); and `tools/editor/vscode-mentl/.gitignore:3` already carries `*.vsix` — the proposed new ignore line buys nothing.

2. INVERT the Carried-Truth reading. The design says ".gitignore ASSERTED these paths are untracked, the index silently carried a different fact, and nothing ever compared the two." Backwards — after ba298e53 the index AGREED. Two operations: draw an edge, project. Something DREW this edge three times and nothing was watching. The design never asks who, and that identification is the missing work. The tooling embodies the trap precisely: `git check-ignore -v tools/editor/vscode-mentl/mentl-0.1.0.vsix` reports NOT IGNORED because check-ignore skips tracked paths — the assertion-shaped tool lies by omission exactly where the defect lives; only the comparison (`ls-files -i -c`) sees it.

3. The remedy is necessary but NOT sufficient, and the sufficiency claim must go. `git rm --cached` on the 6 paths should still land — untracking is right. But drop "This stops FUTURE commits from re-exposing edits." It demonstrably does not; it did not in March, twice, at this path. What binds is a gate that RUNS.

4. WIRE THE GATE — this is the correction that makes the item real, and it is where the design's own novelty verdict fails. "No borrowed-vs-native axis applies" is not honest: the design reaches for `.gitignore`-as-control, the conventional shape, when the artifact proves that shape does not bind here. PLAN §11's own novelty audit already names the native form — "the green board (tools/state.sh) IS the release gate." So: put `git ls-files -i -c --exclude-standard` in `tools/state.sh` as a board rung. Today `grep -cn 'ls-files\|check-ignore' tools/state.sh` = 0, `grep -rn 'ls-files' tools/` finds nothing, `.github/workflows/` does not exist, and `tools/ci/run-board.sh` (24 lines) only shells `tools/state.sh` — so the "REQUIRED" CI transport PLAN §11 col 5 names has no home and the gate is prose. A hand-run check at a path with a documented history of defeating hand-run remedies is PLAN §0's exact failure: discipline written down cannot enforce itself. On the board it is red today, ratchets red->green when the untrack lands, and can never silently regress. Name honestly that adding it turns THE BOARD red until step (1) commits — that is the correct ratchet direction, not a regression.

5. ADD the missing Mentl-specific blast radius to step (2). The design names filter-repo's cost as "invalidates every existing clone/fork" and stops there. It omits the cost that actually matters here: `boot/PROVENANCE.md` carries 28 SHA-shaped tokens (26bfe90a, 94e449dc, 4b7f998f, 701c7024, cf479f9d, c323a40f, 7b188a31, 9c8b23ba, ...), and PLAN §11 designates that chain as the release itself — "the pin IS the release (boot + the PROVENANCE chain of self-confirmed fixpoints)." A history rewrite rewrites every commit SHA and orphans all 28 pins, breaking the one artifact Mentl uses as its release identity. Given the clean secrets scan, step (2) has no justification and a large named cost: recommend AGAINST it, rather than leaving it as an open call.

6. NAME the peer positively. The unidentified writer is the open work: `Hβ.ops.tracked-index-writer-identified` — pin whether the re-adds came from `git add -f` or a rebase replay, because if it is rebase replay, any future rebase across the 116 commits that carry CLAUDE.md re-adds it a third time and the untrack alone cannot hold. Sibling: `Hβ.ops.board-carries-index-hygiene` — the gate as a state.sh rung, which is what makes the wrong move unsayable instead of merely discouraged.

7. Blast radius on the compile is correctly stated and I confirm it: `git rm --cached` touches no `.mn` source, so m2 == m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, and the 71 micros are untouched.

REFUTED: Hβ.ci.board-dissolves-to-verify-verb
  state.sh/verify.sh collapse into a first-class `mentl verify` verb in three independently landable layers — a CLI-surface gap (no such verb exists), an exit-code-trust gap (owned by the already-named Hβ.diag.refusal-law-per-class), and an execution-wiring gap (two already-stubbed handlers) — with the verdict's epistemic strength staying bounded by item 4 regardless of how completely the other two layers land.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is near-exact and the analysis is sound, but the item's only buildable deliverable (Layer A's VVerify verb) is a redundant second name for `mentl check`, which its own delegated peer already fixes by name, and its full-item gate passes vacuously because the "NPipeExpr-class" bug is E_MissingVariable (census 118), a class the peer's ratchet cannot gate.
  objection: LAYER A IS A REDUNDANT FORM ITS OWN DELEGATED PEER DISSOLVES — and the item names the correct structure, then builds something else.

The item's one buildable deliverable is "add VVerify(target) to the EntryVerb ADT and a 'verify' arm to parse_cli_args, dispatching to verify_run, which composes pipeline_check with [the] ratchet read". But two independent homes in the artifact already name `mentl check` — not a new verb — as the surface whose exit code becomes truthful:

  - PLAN.md:1293-1295 states the peer's defect AS check's exit code: "the exit code is a projection of the diagnostic ledger, never a fabricated 0 (`mentl check <missing>` printed E_MissingModule and exited 0)".
  - tools/verify.sh:86-87: "the endpoint is `mentl check` on the wheel exiting 0, at which point emit can refuse on any error and this counter DISSOLVES".

So Layer B, landing, makes `mentl check src` exit nonzero. At that moment verify_run == check_run (src/main.mn:437-444) with a second name — exactly the "redundant second name for an edge already drawn" SYNTAX rejects (Governing Principle 2; the `import X as Y` precedent). Nothing principled forces check to exit 0: productive-under-error is about continuing to PRODUCE diagnostics, not about the exit code.

The item's own INTERROGATIONS paragraph names the right structure: "diagnostics_handler is a handler that resumes past exactly the fact its own installed position (outermost, 'least trusted... sandbox boundary' per main.mn:174) should be enforcing." I verified that position — src/pipeline.mn:576-585 has no `with` clause (no state) and its report arm eprint_strings then resume()s, and it is installed at all 21 live sites (main.mn:235,279,309,342,372,385,428,442,473,505,520,565,587,635; pipeline.mn:83,162,175,204,219,247,273). Put the refusal THERE and all 14 verbs inherit a truthful exit code from one handler gaining state plus one read at the boundary it already owns. That is LESS code and strictly more reach than a 15th verb. The item states this and then adds the verb anyway.

Compounding it: Layer A cannot be built as specified. PLAN.md:1309-1311 fixes the peer's mechanism as "`diag_refuses` ... + the count on diagnostics_handler's own state + ONE LIVE READ AT executable_gate". executable_gate (pipeline.mn:119-130) is installed at exactly 3 compile-shaped sites (pipeline.mn:64,143,186) and takes a LOWERED tree. pipeline_check (pipeline.mn:164-176) -> driver_check (driver.mn:185, not 178-184 as cited — that is its comment block) never lowers. So "composes pipeline_check with whatever the peer exposes" composes with nothing that exists; "whatever ... exposes" is where the design stops designing.

(Verified, not assumed: I ran the pinned boot on a ghost-constructor fixture — 4 E_MissingVariable + 3 E_OccursCheck, EXIT=0. The item's central artifact claim is TRUE. It is the design built on top of it that fails.)
  fixes    : 1. DELETE Layer A. No VVerify, no EntryVerb constructor, no verify_run. The refusal read goes on diagnostics_handler (pipeline.mn:576-585) — already outermost at all 21 sites, already the declared sandbox boundary (main.mn:174), already the peer's chosen home for the count (PLAN.md:1310-1311). One handler gains state + one read; all 14 verbs inherit a truthful exit code at once. The verb is `mentl check` — PLAN.md:1295 and verify.sh:86-87 both already say so.

2. RE-TARGET THE GATE — the item's two gate clauses contradict each other. The "NPipeExpr-class" bug is E_MissingVariable: infer.mn:2486-2489 reports EMissingVariable("constructor: {name}", span) on an unresolved PCon; confirmed live ("infer: E_MissingVariable error: constructor: NCircle at 5:14-5:19"). Its wheel census is 118 (PLAN.md:1306; re-derived from .build/m2cache/m2.err), so the peer's ratchet — "each when the wheel's own census of that class reaches zero" (PLAN.md:1299-1301) — cannot gate it. `mentl verify src` would exit 0 on that bug after Layer A lands and stay 0 until 118 diagnostics are fixed: a gate that passes vacuously. Delete the second gate clause; the FIRST one (expect_refusal missing-module ... 'E_MissingModule', census zero per PLAN.md:1303-1304) is correct and is the whole gate.

3. RETITLE — the item claims state.sh, delivers verify.sh. tools/state.sh:38-46 runs verify.sh PLUS six more gates: march.sh (the m2==m3 byte-fixpoint over two full self-compiles), frontier-gate, proof-exactness-gate, crown-gate, effect-identity-gate, comment-ratchet. Layers A+B+C touch verify.sh's two sections only (census -> Layer B; micro battery -> Layer C). march.sh is categorically not a `check` and is named by no layer. Claim "verify.sh dissolves into `mentl check` + `mentl test`", not "state.sh/verify.sh collapse into `mentl verify`".

4. FIX TWO CITATIONS, AND TAKE THE DRIFT THE SECOND ONE EXPOSES. (a) driver_check is driver.mn:185; 178-184 is its comment block (substance intact — it never installs executable_gate and its walk is pure inference). (b) "a get_results() nothing calls" is false: get_results IS called at lib/test.mn:84 (under ~> test_counter) and lib/test.mn:110 (under ~> suite_counter). The true claim is narrower — nothing performs Test's ops UNDER assert_reporter's install, because pipeline_check's walk is pure inference. That exposes a drift the item missed and inverts its Layer C: assert_reporter (main.mn:483-492) is a THIRD (passed, failed) counter duplicating test_counter (lib/test.mn:42-53) and suite_counter (lib/test.mn:55-67). Layer C must DELETE assert_reporter into test_counter — which the item's own Layer C already names as the landing handler — not wire it.

5. RUN THE NOVELTY AUDIT ON THE DELIVERABLE, NOT THE DELEGATION. The novelty_verdict is honest but inherited wholesale from PLAN.md:1314-1322 (the ratchet-earns-what-rustc/tsc-assume argument, which is the peer's, correctly attributed). The item's OWN novel contribution is a `verify` verb — a borrowed shape (cargo/go vet/npm audit) that the audit should have killed and never examined. Applied to the deliverable, the audit rejects it: the medium already draws the edge as `check`, and a second name for it is the alias form.

6. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS. The item is silent. Honest answer: Layer A as specified is src/-source-only, so m2 == m3 should hold in one generation and the green board (frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros) is untouched — which is also the tell that Layer A changes nothing that matters. Under correction 1, the diagnostics_handler change gains handler state, which IS an emit change (record layout) — a TRANSITION per PLAN §7's march arbitration, re-pin from m3. Name it.

WHAT SURVIVES AND SHOULD BE KEPT: the artifact_state (near-exact, and its central claim — `mentl check` exits 0 on every non-hole error class — I confirmed empirically). The delegation of Layer B to its real owner rather than redesigning it. The explicit trap flag against re-deriving the fleet-killed blanket severity-to-exit fix. And the SEQUENCING conclusion, which is the item's real value and is correct: until the refusal law lands class-by-class, the bash-and-grep board is the only trustworthy signal and must not be deleted. That analysis stands on its own without a verb attached to it.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.bandpass-biquad
  A real bandpass filter, center-frequency-and-Q parameterized, computing coefficients for lib/dsp/processors.mn's existing general biquad handler — the phase-band and amplitude-band isolators every downstream CFC stage filters through first.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's two cited precedents (the `biquad` handler and `lowpass_iir`) have never been emitted and do not assemble today; I compiled both shapes through the pinned boot and both fail at wat2wasm, so the design "mirrors the shape" of dead source and its gate dies before reaching its RMS assertion.
  objection: The design is built on two pieces of code that have never run, and it misreads that as working precedent. I tried to confirm the artifact_state and the probes disproved the design twice.

(1) FLOAT `<~` DOES NOT ASSEMBLE. The design's `bandpass` is "a `<~`-based per-sample step mirroring `lowpass_iir`'s shape (feedback.mn:104-107)". I compiled exactly that shape (`((prev) => input + 0.5 * prev) <~ delay(1)` over floats) through boot/mentl.wasm with verify.sh's RTLIBS:
    FAIL(wat): type mismatch in f64.mul, expected [f64, f64] but got [... f64, i32]
Root, pinned: src/backends/wasm.mn:2156 hard-codes the feedback locals `["__fb_prev_<h> i32", "__fb_<h> i32"]`, and wasm.mn:787-793 hard-codes `(global $s<n> (mut i32) (i32.const 0))`. The sibling line 2145 IMMEDIATELY ABOVE already reads `repr_wat(repr_of(lookup_ty(h)))` for `__hres_<h>` — the LFeedback locals are the one site the repr gradient never reached. Control: the shipped int gate tests/micros/mn-feedback-iir.mn PASSES exit=30. Its own header (line 10) says "Integer IIR accumulator (float arithmetic is the seed's separate gap, STEP 1)" — the int-only scope was documented; the design read past it. So `lowpass_iir`, `highpass_iir`, `dc_blocker`, `envelope_follower`, `audio_chain`, `stereo_chain` are all unexercised source, and feedback.mn:25-27's claim ("now NUMERICALLY correct IIR filters, surpassing Faust's untyped `~`") is false against the artifact.

(2) FLOAT HANDLER STATE DOES NOT ASSEMBLE — so the biquad the design computes coefficients FOR is equally dead. Probing the handler path:
    FAIL(wat): undefined local variable "$__resume_upd_state_6464"
Isolated cleanly — INT state + config param PASSES (exit 10); FLOAT state, no config param FAILS. So it is float state, not config params. The emitted WAT names the root exactly:
    2266: (local $__resume_upd_state_6464.f64 f64)   ; decl — repr-mangled
    2275: (local.set $__resume_upd_state_6464.f64)   ; set  — mangled
    2277: (local.get $__resume_upd_state_6464)       ; read — UNMANGLED
`LLet` decl (wasm.mn:2069-2076) and store (wasm.mn:2866-2871) both read `llet_repr(h, inner)` = `repr_join(repr_of(lookup_ty(h)), tail_expr_repr(inner))` (wasm.mn:2321) — the f64 INIT drives it to RF64 → `.f64`. `LLocal` (wasm.mn:2843-2850) reads only `repr_of(lookup_ty(h))`, and `resume_commit_prefix` (lower.mn:4327-4336) hands it the RESUME SITE's handle → RI32 → bare name (`local_wat_name` returns `name` unchanged for RI32, wasm.mn:2332 — which is exactly why int hides the bug and float exposes it). Every handler in processors.mn has float state (peak_tracker, lowpass, biquad, envelope, warm_filter, bright_filter): none can be emitted. The ARTIFACT STATE's "a fully general direct-form-I biquad" is false in its material implication.

Why this was invisible: nothing imports dsp/feedback or dsp/processors (feedback.mn:58-59 imports only dsp/signal + dsp/clock), and `grep -rln "lowpass_iir|process_biquad|dc_blocker|audio_chain|bandpass" tests/` returns NONE. lib/dsp IS concatenated into the wheel (march.sh:71 `find lib -name '*.mn'`) but reachability==emission prunes it, so the board stays green over source that cannot assemble.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE GATE (attack 4): the gate is refuted — not merely wrong, but incapable of running. mn-bandpass-biquad.mn would be the FIRST caller to make either path reachable, and run-micro.sh dies at wt_asm ("FAIL(wat)") before the RMS assertion is ever evaluated. A gate that fails at assembly tests nothing about bandpass.

CARRIED-TRUTH (attack 2): the design contradicts itself and re-derives state the graph already holds. Its first sentence computes coefficients FOR the biquad handler — which ALREADY carries y1,y2,x1,x2, the exact four cells a 2nd-order section needs — then abandons it to build a SECOND memory via `<~`, needing an unbuilt ring buffer. That is MORE code for a fact already carried. Worse, `LFeedback(h, body, _spec)` (wasm.mn:3434) discards the spec, so `delay(2)` emits identical WAT to `delay(1)`: a silent 1st-order filter — the silent-wrong-value class §11 col 2 forbids. The design names LF.1 as unbuilt ("this is that demand") and writes code requiring it without landing it (drift-9), and never names the two f64 repr gaps at all.

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTEMPT TO REFUTE IT: three things held. The literal citations are accurate (processors.mn:45-53, feedback.mn:66-77/87/104-107, processors.mn:38-39, no bandpass anywhere, `cos` genuinely absent — the design names that dependency honestly). The RBJ formula KEPT as scaffold-tier is the right call and its HIP-emission analogy is sound. And I suspected the biquad's `with y1 = y, y2 = y1` was a sequential-update bug — I probed it (2-cell handler, `a = x, b = a`) and was DISPROVED: it returned 9 = 0+1+3+5, proving simultaneous update reading the OLD value. The ordering is correct; I withdraw that objection.
  fixes    : The design must say the following instead.

1. LEAD WITH TWO REQUIRED SUBSTRATE LANDINGS, not background. Neither is optional; neither is named today.
   - `Hβ.emit.llocal-repr-carries-the-proven-width` — `LLet` decl+store read `llet_repr(h, inner)` (wasm.mn:2321 = repr_join of the handle's repr with the INIT's tail repr → RF64 → `$name.f64`); `LLocal` reads only `repr_of(lookup_ty(h))` (wasm.mn:2846), and `resume_commit_prefix` hands it the RESUME SITE's handle (i32, lower.mn:4335) → bare `$name`. Two projections of one local's repr. The fix is Carried-Truth and is LESS code: one projection — carry the init's handle (or the proven repr) into the LLocal rather than re-deriving from the resume handle. This single fix unblocks EVERY processor in processors.mn (all six have float state). The comment at wasm.mn:2327 ("Decl and every use read THIS one projection") must be corrected — LLocal cannot reach the init and does not read it.
