   - `Hβ.emit.feedback-local-repr` — wasm.mn:2156 hard-codes `__fb_prev_<h> i32` / `__fb_<h> i32`; wasm.mn:787-793 hard-codes `(global $s<n> (mut i32))`. The sibling at wasm.mn:2145 already reads `repr_wat(repr_of(lookup_ty(h)))` for `__hres_<h>`. Same one-projection fix.

2. DELETE the `<~`-based per-sample `bandpass` and `bandpass_buffer`'s map (Carried-Truth). The biquad handler ALREADY carries y1,y2,x1,x2 — the exact four cells a 2nd-order section needs; the design's own first sentence targets that handler and then abandons it for a SECOND memory needing an unbuilt ring buffer. The whole item is:

     fn compute_bandpass_coeffs(center: Hz, q: Float, sr: Int) -> (Float,Float,Float,Float,Float) with Pure + !Alloc = ...
     fn bandpass_buffer(xs, center, q, sr) = {
       let (b0,b1,b2,a1,a2) = compute_bandpass_coeffs(center, q, sr)
       process_biquad(b0, b1, b2, a1, a2, xs)
     }

   `process_biquad` already exists (processors.mn:52-53). This needs NEITHER LF.1 NOR the feedback-local repr fix — only fix 1. It is strictly less code and it is what the graph already knows. (The `with y1 = y, y2 = y1` ordering is SOUND — I verified simultaneous update empirically, 2-cell probe returned 9 = 0+1+3+5. Do not "fix" it.)

3. IF the `<~` path is kept anyway, LF.1 must LAND in the same cut: `LFeedback(h, body, _spec)` (wasm.mn:3434) DISCARDS the spec, so `delay(2)` emits WAT byte-identical to `delay(1)` and silently yields a 1st-order filter. That is a silent-wrong-value class, which PLAN §11 col 2 ("no known silent-wrong class") forbids outright. Naming LF.1 as "the demand" while writing code that requires it is drift-9, deferred-by-omission.

4. THE GATE MUST SPECIFY Q AND A THRESHOLD CONSISTENT WITH IT. For the RBJ constant-0dB BPF, |H| at w = 4·w0 is (w/Q)/sqrt((1-w^2)^2+(w/Q)^2): Q=0.707 → 0.353; Q=1 → 0.258; Q=1.4 → 0.187; Q=2 → 0.132. The stated "<0.2x at 4x center" FAILS a perfectly CORRECT filter at the common Q=0.707 and Q=1 — the threshold silently constrains Q, which the gate never states. Specify Q=2 (0.132, comfortable margin) or relax the threshold. (Bilinear warping shifts these slightly, which strengthens the point.)

5. THE GATE MUST NAME ITS LIBS, and feedback.mn must `import runtime/math`. verify.sh's RTLIBS are memory/strings/lists/prelude only — `sin`/`cos`/`two_pi` live in lib/runtime/math.mn and would be missing. Per §11 "the imports ARE the manifest", feedback.mn:58-59 imports only dsp/signal + dsp/clock while compute_alpha:89 ALREADY calls `exp` undeclared — it resolves only because the wheel cats all of lib into one namespace. Declare the edge.

6. CORRECT THE FALSE PROSE the design inherits. feedback.mn:25-27 asserts "The filters below are now NUMERICALLY correct IIR filters, surpassing Faust's untyped `~`". The artifact says they do not assemble. Prose drifts; artifacts do not. Fix the prose in the same cut.

7. The novelty verdict's KEEP of RBJ as scaffold-tier is correct and should stand — a closed-form filter-design formula is not a language pattern, and the HIP-text-emission analogy holds. But drop the JUCE/Faust "stronger" claim until the mechanism runs: you cannot claim durable-persistable, thread-portable handler state for state that cannot be emitted. Re-make the claim after fix 1 lands, when a probe can back it.

Secondary: `bandpass(x: Sample) -> Sample` types the output with `-1.0 <= self <= 1.0`, but a resonant BPF transient overshoots unity even when steady-state |H(w0)| = 1. That is a refinement obligation the design waves through as "honestly V_Pending"; at high Q it is decidable-FALSE territory, not pending. Name it.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.stft-complex-substrate
  A Complex record, a Hann window, and a direct DFT/STFT — the time-frequency engine item 4 reads phase and amplitude bins from, landed correctness-first at O(N²) with radix-2 FFT named as the explicit performance follow-on.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is accurate, but the GATE is vacuous (a sign-flipped DFT, a bin-reversed DFT, and a non-transform all pass it at |err|≈8.9e-16), the novelty verdict's central claim ("composes for free with row-polymorphic functions accepting {re: Float, ...}") is empirically false — it traps the compiler or silently returns the imaginary part for the real one — and the gate does not compile as written.
  objection: THE GATE IS VACUOUS, AND VACUOUS IN THE ONE DIRECTION THAT DESTROYS THE DELIVERABLE. Parseval is an ENERGY identity; it holds for any scaled-orthogonal transform, so it does not pin the DFT. I ran it (N=8, fixed frame) against four candidates under the design's own tolerance `abs(time_energy - freq_energy) < 0.001`:
  correct DFT (e^-i)      |err|=8.88e-16 -> PASSES
  SIGN-FLIPPED DFT (e^+i) |err|=8.88e-16 -> PASSES
  BIN-REVERSED DFT        |err|=8.88e-16 -> PASSES
  IDENTITY * sqrt(N)      |err|=8.88e-16 -> PASSES
The gate passes with NO DFT IMPLEMENTED AT ALL: `fn dft(frame) = map(scale_by_sqrt_n, frame)` is green. The brief's test — "would it actually FAIL if the design were not landed?" — answers no. Worse: Parseval is magnitude-only and structurally BLIND TO PHASE, and INTERCONNECTION says "Item 4 reads stft's output bins directly for phase and amplitude." The sign-flipped twiddle (the single most common DFT bug) negates every phase (bin 1: +2.7718 vs correct -2.7718 rad) and the gate green-lights it — silently corrupting a phase-amplitude comodulogram, i.e. the exact research half of the bar. The design's own justification is the tell: "independent of any specific input" is precisely the property that makes it not pin the transform.

ARTIFACT-ANCHORED TWIN — the novelty verdict's central claim is false. It asserts Complex "composes for free with row-polymorphic functions accepting {re: Float, ...}". Tested through the pinned boot:
  A) nominal `Complex{re,im}` -> `fn f(c: {re: Float, ...})` = COMPILER TRAP (`wasm trap: unreachable` in compile_stdin_run), zero diagnostics.
  B) bare structural `{re,im}` -> same param = SILENT WRONG VALUE (exit 0, expected 42, diags=0).
  C) nominal -> nominal param = PASS.  D) nominal -> closed structural param = PASS.  H) closed record, last field = PASS.
Root pinned at src/lower.mn:2993:
  `TRecordOpen(fields, _) => field_byte_offset(fields, field_name, 0, len(fields)),`
The row variable — which unification ALREADY bound to the rest (src/infer.mn:1898-1906) — is DISCARDED (`_`), and the offset is re-derived from the open prefix. The TName arm three lines below (src/lower.mn:2997-3007) correctly chases to the live RecordSchemeKind; the open-row arm does not. The comment at src/lower.mn:2982 states the wrong assumption outright ("field's index in `TRecord(fields)` / `TRecordOpen(fields, _)` maps directly to `4 * idx`"). This is THE CARRIED-TRUTH LAW verbatim (the graph proved the layout; a consumer discarded it and re-derived), and it is a CLASS, not a mole — the same discard recurs at src/lower.mn:4828 (`record_fields_of_handle`) and src/lower.mn:3267. Pinned mechanically: fields sort alphabetically (SYNTAX.md), so `{a,z}` lays out a@0/z@8 (Float=RF64, width 8); reading `a` via `{a,...}` PASSES (offset 0 both) while reading `z` via `{z,...}` returns `a` — asymmetry exactly as predicted. For `Complex = {re, im}` the sort is im@0, re@8, so `c.re` through `{re: Float, ...}` RETURNS THE IMAGINARY PART. Silently. Inside a DFT. This is an UNNAMED silent-wrong class (no peer names it — greps over src/lib/PLAN.md find none), which PLAN §11 column 2 requires to be empty ("no known silent-wrong class"). It deserves a name: Hβ.lower.open-row-offset-reads-live-record.

BLAST RADIUS THE DESIGN NEVER STATES: `lib/dsp/*.mn` enters the wheel's OWN self-compile (tools/march.sh:70-71, `find lib -name '*.mn' -not -path '*/tutorial/*'`; spectral.mn sits at .build/wheel.mn:31428). So any `{re: Float, ...}` the design writes traps the compiler compiling itself. Lib-source-only changes do hold m2==m3 in one generation (the 2026-07-10 Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc precedent) — but the design asserts nothing about it.

WHAT I TRIED THAT FAILED TO REFUTE (the design's skeleton survives): the artifact_state is TRUE on every clause — no complex/FFT/DFT/STFT code exists (only tools/editor/vscode-mentl/samples/showcase.mn:14 and a comment in tests/micros/mn-float-gate.mn:9); lib/dsp/spectral.mn is the spectral-flux-distortion file; `estimate_flux` is at lines 62-68 exactly and is an envelope-difference proxy. `Alloc` really does exist (lib/runtime/memory.mn:56), so `with Alloc` is honest. `cos` genuinely does not exist while `sin`/`two_pi`/`float_of_int` do (lib/runtime/math.mn:107/34, used at :41) — the item-1 dependency is stated correctly. And Complex-as-a-record needing no new node-kind is CORRECT and is a real §4① datum. I also tried to refute the O(N²)-first staging as lowered-target drift and could NOT: §5.O's "O(1) only" is defined as "read the edge, never re-scan" ≡ Law 1, which governs re-derivation of graph facts, not the asymptotics of a numerical kernel; PLAN §5's REAL aspect plus a positive-form named peer is the sanctioned move, and Hβ.dsp.radix2-fft is well-formed. The staging stands.
  fixes    : 1. REPLACE THE GATE (load-bearing; the design's current gate is refuted). Parseval cannot be the primary check. Use a KNOWN-ANSWER test, which pins twiddle sign, bin order, scaling AND phase in one frame: the shifted impulse. For x = δ[n-1], the unnormalized forward DFT gives exactly X[k] = e^(-2πik/N); at N=4, X[1] = (re=0, im=-1). A sign-flipped twiddle yields (0, +1) and FAILS. Add a second frame — a pure tone at bin k0 — asserting energy lands in bins k0 and N-k0 (the amplitude/leakage half). Keep Parseval only as a cheap secondary invariant, never as the proof. The gate must also assert PHASE, because phase is what item 4 consumes; a magnitude-only gate cannot verify the deliverable.

2. THE GATE DOES NOT COMPILE AS WRITTEN. `abs(time_energy - freq_energy)` binds prelude `abs` (lib/prelude.mn:260: `fn abs(x) with Pure = if x < 0 { 0 - x } else { x }`); `LitInt` binds concrete `TInt` (src/infer.mn:613), so `abs : Int -> Int`. Measured: `type mismatch in call_indirect, expected [i32, i32] but got [i32, f64]` — and notably with ZERO compiler diagnostics; the fault surfaced only at wat2wasm. A control with a hand-rolled `fn fabs(x: Float) -> Float` passes (exit 42). The design must use a Float abs and name it. Two residues worth naming positively: Hβ.prelude.abs-numeric-polymorphic (the Int-pinned abs) and Hβ.diag.float-arity-mismatch-teaches (a call-shape mismatch reaching wat2wasm instead of E_TypeMismatch is productive-under-error failing, CLAUDE.md ⟲).

3. "a plain two-field structural record" IS A MISREAD OF SYNTAX.md. `type X = {f1: T1, ...}` is the NOMINAL record form — "Creates `TName("X", [], TRecord([...]))`. The record's name brands its identity." Construction is `Complex{re: 0.5, im: 0.25}`, not `{re: 0.5, im: 0.25}` (verified: tests C and D pass only with the branded form). The design must say NOMINAL and show the construction syntax. The nominal brand is arguably the RIGHT choice here (a Complex must not unify with an unrelated {re,im}) — but then the row-polymorphism selling point is not available, and the novelty verdict must drop it.

4. DELETE THE ROW-POLYMORPHISM CLAIM, OR FIX lower.mn:2993 FIRST. As it stands the claim is false in the worst way (trap / silent wrong field). Either (a) drop it from the novelty verdict, or (b) land the Carried-Truth fix first: the open-row arm must chase the row variable to the record unification already bound and read the offset from the LIVE concrete layout — the same chase the TName arm at src/lower.mn:2997-3007 already performs. That fix is LESS code (one chase replacing a re-derivation) and dissolves all three discard sites (2993, 4828, 3267) as one class. Name it Hβ.lower.open-row-offset-reads-live-record.

5. "STRICTLY STRONGER THAN PYTHON'S complex" IS AN OVERCLAIM. Python's `complex` carries `+ - * / abs() ** .conjugate()`. Mentl has no operator overloading (no traits/typeclasses; `+` is word arithmetic, `==` is the structural derivation, `++` is seq_concat), so Complex arithmetic must be written `c_add(a,b)` / `c_mul(a,b)`. On arithmetic ergonomics the record is WEAKER. The honest claim — which is real and sufficient — is narrower: the record is stronger on INSPECTABILITY and EFFECT-ROW VISIBILITY (the row is a checked claim the same compiler verifies, not a hidden C-library detail), and it needs no new node-kind. Drop the word "strictly"; keep the effect-row argument, which survives.

6. HANN IS THE WRONG WINDOW. `0.5 - 0.5*cos(two_pi()*n/(size-1))` is the SYMMETRIC window (filter design). STFT analysis needs the PERIODIC window — denominator `size`, not `size - 1`; scipy.signal defaults to sym=False for exactly this reason. Measured COLA at 50% hop, N=256: symmetric ripple 6.12e-03 vs periodic 6.66e-16. CFC reads the AMPLITUDE ENVELOPE, so a 0.6% hop-rate ripple is a periodic amplitude artifact injected straight into the comodulogram — an artifact shaped like coupling. This is a domain error, not a nit, given the deliverable.

7. STAGE LAW VIOLATION. `fn stft(xs: [Sample], window_size: Int, hop: Int)` puts the DATUM FIRST. SYNTAX.md's Stage Law is explicit: "a stage-shaped fn declares its CONFIGURATION parameters first and the flowing DATUM last" (`map(f, xs)`, `take(n, xs)`). Correct form: `fn stft(window_size: Int, hop: Int, xs: [Sample])`, so `xs |> stft(1024, 512)` completes by hole-fill. As written, `xs |> stft(1024, 512)` binds 1024 to xs. (Related pre-existing rot the design should not copy: lib/dsp/signal.mn:6-9 still documents the DELETED prepend convention — "signal is always the first argument" — and `clip(x, threshold)` is genuinely wrong under hole-completion: `x |> clip(0.95)` becomes `clip(0.95, x)`. Name it Hβ.dsp.signal-stage-law-sweep.)

8. RUN THE OTHER FIVE INTERROGATIONS. The design projects three (Graph, Row, Verb) and its Verb answer is the shallow half. Missing and load-bearing: (7) GRADIENT — `{re: Float, im: Float}` is two RF64 fields (16 bytes/Complex); PLAN §5.U's repr pin (`repr f32`) halves it and is the precondition for the v128 lane cash-out (4 complex f32 pairs per lane). (6) REFINEMENT — `stft(xs: [Sample], ...)` uses Sample (`Float where -1.0 <= self <= 1.0`), but DFT bins reach magnitude N and are NOT Samples; the design is right to return Complex but never states why, and that is exactly the refinement boundary Verify would discharge. (3) VERB, generatively — radix-2 Cooley-Tukey's decimation-in-time IS `<|` (split even/odd) + `><` (the two half-transforms are independent pipelines); the butterfly is Mentl's own topology, and PFanout + `~> Schedule` (§5.U STEP 4) would then thread it for free. That is where the generative pass lands and it should shape how Hβ.dsp.radix2-fft is named, even though the O(N²)-first staging itself stands.

9. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS. Say plainly that lib/dsp/stft.mn enters the wheel's own compile (tools/march.sh:70-71), that a lib-source-only change is expected to hold m2 == m3 in one generation (Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc precedent, 2026-07-10), and that the march arbitrates it. Silence here is the omission the brief asks about.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.instantaneous-phase-amplitude
  Bandpass-then-STFT-bin-read turns a raw recording into phase(t) and amplitude(t) per band — one `|>` chain that makes the CFC field's own known ordering bug structurally unsayable, and the one place this pipeline states the !Alloc real-time/offline row split explicitly.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's primary route (Route A: STFT-bin phase) cannot deliver CFC phase at its own stated parameters (window 128, hop 32: exactly 1 of 63 bins has unaliased phase), its gate is built on the one input that hides this and fails a correct implementation at bin ≥ 4 as literally written, and its central novelty claim ("the |> chain makes the ordering bug structurally unsayable") is false — the ordering lives inside the fn body exactly as it would in Python, and nothing in the types distinguishes a bandpassed [Sample] from a raw one.
  objection: THE HEADLINE (pure arithmetic from the design's own numbers — window N=128, hop=32):

The design reads phase from "the bin nearest the band's center frequency." Phase is produced once per hop, so the phase time series is sampled at sr/hop. Phase advance per hop at bin k = 2π·f_k·hop/sr = 2π·k·hop/N = k·π/2. Unambiguous phase requires |advance| < π → k < 2. Verified:
  k=1 → π/2 per hop (4 samples/cycle, the bare floor)
  k=2 → π exactly (Nyquist; +π and −π indistinguishable)
  k=3 → 3π/2, wraps to −π/2 (phase appears to rotate BACKWARD)
  k=4 → 2π, wraps to 0 (phase appears STATIONARY)
  k≥4 → fully aliased
For N=128 real input the usable bins are 1..63. Exactly ONE (k=1) yields trackable phase. The design's "read the bin nearest the band's center" is meaningful for 1 of 63 bins.

This is not incidental — it is CFC's defining constraint. CFC spans a 10×–100× frequency ratio by definition; the phase band needs a LONG window (frequency resolution), the amplitude band needs a SHORT one (time resolution, to track amplitude at the phase band's timescale). The design applies ONE window to BOTH and calls that unification its justification for Route A as primary ("one mechanism, not two"). The unification IS the bug. For the actual research target — Physarum contraction oscillations, ~0.005–0.01 Hz (memory: project_research_intent_cfc.md) — no 128-sample window resolves a 0.01 Hz phase band (needs ≥100 s of data) and any meaningfully faster amplitude band simultaneously.

THE GATE FAILS BOTH HORNS (refutes attack 4 outright):
- "phase(t+hop)-phase(t) mod 2π must track two_pi()*f*hop/sr". At k=4 the RHS is exactly 2π (verified: 6.283185307179586) while the LHS, reduced mod 2π, reads ~0. The gate FAILS a perfectly correct implementation. Reduce the RHS mod 2π too and the k=4 expectation becomes 0 — the gate then PASSES an implementation returning a CONSTANT phase.
- "amplitude(t) ... within 5% of the sine's true amplitude" against the design's own formula `sqrt(bin.re²+bin.im²)`: an unnormalized DFT bin magnitude for A·sin at exact bin center is A·N/2 = 64·A. Off by 64×. The formula carries no 2/N normalization and no window coherent-gain factor, and the design never names a window SHAPE at all — only length and hop.
- "a pure sine at a band's EXACT center frequency" is the single input under which bin-read is exact; the gate's own expected value 2π·f·hop/sr only equals the observed advance 2π·k·hop/N when f is at bin center. The degenerate case is baked into the gate's math, so it cannot generalize to the off-center tones that dominate any real recording.

THE NOVELTY CLAIM IS FALSE, TWICE:
1. The API is `fn phase_amplitude_series(xs: [Sample], band, q, sr, window_size, hop)` — it takes RAW [Sample] and bandpasses INSIDE its own body. The ordering is enforced by being inside one function, which is exactly what `def phase_amplitude_series(xs, ...): return hilbert(filtfilt(b, a, xs))` achieves. Nesting two Python calls is the identical data-dependency edge. `|>` buys nothing here.
2. Nothing in the types prevents skipping the bandpass: `bandpass_buffer : [Sample] -> [Sample]` is type-identical to the identity on [Sample], so `xs |> stft(window_size, hop) |> map(bin_to_phase_amp)` typechecks perfectly. The medium cannot distinguish a bandpassed [Sample] from a raw one. The design's own words — "not a convention two disconnected library calls might violate" — describe a property it does not deliver.

FAUST OVERCLAIM, contradicted by PLAN's own text: "Faust proves real-time by forbidding allocation entirely, so it cannot express the STFT stage in the same language at all." PLAN.md:200 says "Faust has the verbs and no effects"; PLAN.md:355 says "`~>` (handler/effects) is the arm Faust lacks: Mentl = Faust's topology + handlers." PLAN's claim is about the handler arm, not expressiveness. Faust does not forbid allocation — it statically allocates delay lines and rwtables at compile time, and ships spectral/FFT processing. The design escalates PLAN's careful true claim into a false one it never needed.

ARTIFACT STATE — half confirmed, half laundered:
- CONFIRMED: lib/dsp/feedback.mn:131-135 is exact; the row `with Sample + Pure + !Alloc` is at :132; the recurrence `env[n] = α·|x[n]| + (1-α)·env[n-1]` via `<~ delay(1)` is at :133-135.
- REFUTED: "a real, working ... per-sample amplitude tracker." `grep -n release lib/dsp/feedback.mn` returns exactly two hits — line 129 (a comment asserting "Refinement keeps attack + release as positive Hz values") and line 131 (the signature). `release: Hz` is DECLARED AND NEVER USED. The body computes one `alpha = compute_alpha(attack, sr)` (feedback.mn:87-89) and applies it in both directions. A real envelope follower branches (attack when mag > prev, release otherwise). This one has no branch: it is a one-pole magnitude smoother whose signature advertises an asymmetry it does not implement and whose comment asserts the dead parameter is load-bearing. Under the Carried-Truth Law that is a signature claiming a fact the body doesn't carry. The design launders it as "real, working" and hangs `Hβ.dsp.envelope-follower-amplitude-fastpath` on it, propagating the defect.
- Internal contradiction: "ARTIFACT STATE ... (depends on items 2, 3)" vs "SEQUENCING: Depends on items 1, 2, 3." Item 1 is where the design's own formulas live ("both item 1") and its contents are wholly absent: `grep -rn -E "\b(cos|sqrt|atan|atan2|float_sqrt)\s*\(" --include="*.mn" .` returns NOTHING. lib/runtime/math.mn provides exactly ln2/pi/two_pi/float_floor/float_pow2/exp/log/sin/tanh plus series helpers — no `cos` (item 3's STFT twiddle needs it), no `sqrt`, no `atan2`. math.mn:3 notes WASM has an f64.sqrt instruction; nothing in src/ exposes it.

GATE MECHANICS — cannot run as specified: verify.sh:24 sets `RTLIBS=(lib/runtime/memory.mn lib/runtime/strings.mn lib/runtime/lists.mn lib/prelude.mn)` — no math.mn, no lib/dsp — and verify.sh:64 feeds the SAME RTLIBS to every battery micro while run-micro.sh cats libs+micro into one namespace (no import resolution). `mn-phase-amplitude-tone.mn` cannot reach `two_pi()`/`sin`, let alone the unbuilt cos/sqrt/atan2 or lib/dsp. Either RTLIBS grows globally (feeding math+dsp to all 71 micros) or the micro inlines the pipeline and gates nothing in lib/dsp. The design specifies neither.

BLAST RADIUS — silent. march.sh:70-71 builds the wheel as `find src -name '*.mn' | sort | xargs cat; find lib -name '*.mn' -not -path '*/tutorial/*' | sort | xargs cat`. Only */tutorial/* is excluded, so lib/dsp/** IS compiled INTO the compiler; every fn this peer adds lengthens every self-compile and moves m2/m3 bytes. The design says nothing about m2==m3, the 71-micro battery, or the census ratchet that verify-baseline.txt gates. PLAN §5.R band D holds the mitigating precedent it should have cited ("a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation").

WHAT I TRIED THAT DIDN'T REFUTE THE DESIGN: the file:line and the effect row on envelope_follower are exactly as claimed; `two_pi()` really is at lib/runtime/math.mn:34; the micro battery really is 71 (`grep -c "^micro:" tools/verify-baseline.txt` = 71), matching PLAN §7; the `<~ delay(1)` per-sample idiom is real and working across feedback.mn:105-135. The design's read of the ONE line it cites is honest. Everything built on top of it is not.
  fixes    : 1. INVERT THE ROUTES — Route A's justification is factually wrong. The design picks STFT as primary because "phase has no `<~`-only equivalent to envelope_follower; a continuous instantaneous-phase tracker needs the analytic signal." False. A complex one-pole resonator — y[n] = x[n] + r·e^{jω}·y[n-1], i.e. a pair of real recurrences — IS the analytic signal per-sample: |y| is the band amplitude, arg(y) is the band phase. It is the SAME `<~ delay(1)` shape already built at feedback.mn:105-115 (lowpass_iir/highpass_iir) with a complex coefficient, sin/cos evaluated ONCE at setup, not per sample. So the PRIMARY route is a per-sample complex resonator under `with Sample + Pure + !Alloc` — no STFT, no hop latency, no bin quantization, no aliasing table above. STFT becomes the OFFLINE cross-check, not the spine. This strictly strengthens the peer's own headline claim: instead of "half the pipeline is !Alloc, half is Alloc," the whole CFC front-end becomes real-time-provable, and the `Alloc` row appears only where it is genuinely earned (the comodulogram accumulation in item 5).

2. DELETE (128, 32) — do not restate it, do not tune it. Any fixed single window for both bands is the error. State the CFC parameter law as a REFINEMENT that Verify discharges, not a comment: the phase band's window must span ≥2 periods of f_phase (N ≥ 2·sr/f_p), the amplitude envelope must be sampled well above the phase band (sr/hop ≫ f_p), and the phase bin must satisfy k·hop/N < 1/2 or its phase aliases. Under Route A those three are jointly unsatisfiable for a real CFC ratio — which is the proof that Route A is not the primary.

3. MOVE THE NOVELTY CLAIM FROM ARM 3 TO ARM 6 — this is the one real correction. The design runs 2 of 8 interrogations (Row, Reason) and skips Refinement, which is exactly where its own thesis lives. Aru et al. 2015's central warning is the BANDWIDTH criterion: the amplitude band's filter bandwidth must be ≥ 2× the phase frequency, or the phase-driven sidebands (f_A ± f_P) are filtered out and the CFC you measure is an artifact of your own filter. That is a predicate over two parameters — statable as `type AmpBand = Hz where bandwidth(self) >= 2.0 * phase_band_center`, discharged at the construction site by Verify, and genuinely unsayable in scipy/MNE/JAX/Faust type systems. The design cites the paper for "filter-bandwidth and ordering artifacts" and then builds a mechanism for ordering only — the half `|>` cannot actually enforce — while the half Mentl can uniquely prove goes unbuilt. Drop the ordering claim entirely; it is not a Mentl property.

4. IF the ordering claim is kept in any form, it must be earned by a TYPE, not a verb: a nominal or refined band-passed sequence (`type Banded = {samples: [Sample], band: Hz, q: Float}`) so that `stft` cannot accept raw `[Sample]`. Only then is "was this bandpassed before its phase was read" a structural fact. Note the honest cost: this is a brand, and it must not become a side-ledger of the band parameters that the graph already connects.

5. FIX THE FORMULAS. `amplitude = sqrt(re²+im²)` needs the normalization (2/N) and the window's coherent-gain divisor, and the design must NAME the window shape. An implicit rectangular window's −13 dB sidelobes are the dominant real error in CFC — a weak fast-band amplitude read next to a strong slow-band component drowns in the slow band's leakage. Silence on the window shape is the gap.

6. REBUILD THE GATE so it can fail. A tone at exact bin center tests the DFT, not the pipeline. The non-vacuous gate is a SYNTHETIC WITH KNOWN CFC: a carrier at f_A whose amplitude is modulated by a slow f_P (a[n] = (1 + m·cos(2π f_P n/sr))·sin(2π f_A n/sr)), asserting the recovered phase-amplitude relationship matches the planted modulation index m and phase offset, plus a NEGATIVE control (unmodulated carrier → recovered coupling ≈ 0). Add an off-bin-center tone. As written, the gate's expected value 2π·f·hop/sr is only valid at bin center and fails a correct implementation at bin ≥ 4; state whether the RHS is reduced mod 2π and accept that doing so makes the k=4 case pass on a constant.

7. CORRECT THE ARTIFACT STATE. Say: item 1's contents are wholly absent — no `cos` (item 3's STFT twiddle needs it), no `sqrt`, no `atan2` anywhere in the repo; lib/runtime/math.mn stops at exp/log/sin/tanh. Fix "depends on items 2, 3" to "depends on items 1, 2, 3", matching its own SEQUENCING line. And stop calling envelope_follower "real, working": `release: Hz` at feedback.mn:131 is dead, and the comment at :129 asserts it is load-bearing. Either implement the attack/release branch or delete the parameter and the comment's claim — do not name `Hβ.dsp.envelope-follower-amplitude-fastpath` on top of a signature that lies. Under Route B's demotion (correction 1) the honest peer is `Hβ.dsp.envelope-follower-attack-release-branch`.

8. DROP THE FAUST OVERCLAIM. Use PLAN's own true claim (PLAN.md:200, :355): Faust has the topology and no effect row, so it cannot NAME `!Alloc` as a proposition or swap the schedule as a `~>` handler. That is sufficient, defensible, and already the project's position. "Cannot express the STFT stage in the same language at all" is false and contradicts PLAN.

9. STATE THE BLAST RADIUS. lib/dsp/** is in the wheel (march.sh:70-71 excludes only */tutorial/*), so this peer's code compiles INTO the compiler. Cite PLAN §5.R band D — a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in one generation — and say so. Name the battery change: verify.sh:24's RTLIBS must gain lib/runtime/math.mn (and the dsp libs) for the gate to reach its own dependencies, which feeds them to all 71 micros; or the micro inlines the pipeline and gates nothing in lib/dsp. Pick one and say which.

10. RUN THE REMAINING SIX INTERROGATIONS. Graph (what edge carries the band parameters to the Reason chain — the design asserts the Reason walk-back but names no edge), Handler (which Iterate-class handler drives the per-sample route — clock.mn:78 `iterate_context` / :101 `sample_real` exist and the design never mentions the driver it needs), Ownership (the buffer's use-count across the two bands — `<|` borrows one input to two branches, which is the actual topology of "phase band and amplitude band from one recording", not the `|>` the design draws), Refinement (correction 3), Gradient, Verb.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.tort-modulation-index
  The per-band-pair coupling scalar — Tort's KL-divergence modulation index over binned phase — turns two time series into the one number a comodulogram cell holds, refined to a provable [0,1] range no existing CFC toolkit's output type states, and honestly undischargeable under the substrate as it stands today.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's central data structure, the `[Float]` per-bin accumulator via list_index/list_set, traps on the live compiler (exit 134, `indirect call type mismatch`) with zero diagnostics; the declared row omits `Memory`; the refinement interrogation is inverted (output refined-but-inert, input precondition untyped); and the gate is vacuous with respect to the design's sole novelty claim.
  objection: THE `[Float]` ACCUMULATOR DOES NOT EXIST — the design's core mechanism, refuted by the artifact.

Probe (the design's exact pattern): `make_list(4) |> list_set(??, 0, 0.5) |> list_index(??, 0)` → **exit 134, `wasm trap: indirect call type mismatch`, 0 diagnostics** through the pinned boot. Two controls isolate it perfectly: identical micro with literal `5` instead of `0.5` → PASS 42; `0.25 + 0.25 == 0.5` outside any list → PASS 42. Float scalars work. Lists work. Floats IN lists trap.

The substrate names the root: `make_list` allocates `8 + count * 4` (lib/runtime/lists.mn:28) — 4-byte slots; `list_set` is `store_i32(l + 8 + idx * 4, val)` (lists.mn:47); `list_index` is `load_i32(list + 8 + i * 4)` (lists.mn:93). Word-strided i32 throughout; there is no `store_f64`/`load_f64` anywhere in lists.mn. An f64 is 8 bytes. This is §5.U STEP 1's representation gradient never having reached the sequence node-kind — the sibling of the already-named `Hβ.emit.variant-payload-repr-width` (wasm.mn:4913, the sum kind) and `Hβ.emit.f64-closure-capture-box` (PLAN §7, closure captures). tests/micros/mn-distort.mn's own header says it out loud: "the real lib uses [Float] waveshaping... this gate uses Int scalars... the float waveshaping is STEP 1's separate representation gap."

The cited precedent does not transfer, and the design misread it: clock.mn:157-165 IS the trace-accumulation handler (citation correct), but it accumulates `Instant(Int)` (clock.mn:26) from `host_clock_ns() -> Int` (clock.mn:86), APPENDED at a monotonic counter (`list_set(??, trace_len, now)`). The design generalizes an Int append-at-counter into a Float write-at-computed-index and calls it "matching clock.mn's own record/replay trace-accumulation idiom." It is neither the same element type nor the same access pattern.

The trap is itself worth naming: 0 diagnostics plus a runtime call_indirect trap is productive-under-error FAILING (CLAUDE.md ⟲ — broken input yields a diagnostic, never a silent trap). `list_set`'s `val` param is untyped, so nothing refuses the f64 at the call site.

Everything else I checked HELD, and I tried hard to break it: math.mn:40 IS `float_floor`; math.mn:77 IS `log`; math.mn:78-79 IS the non-positive guard returning 0.0 (so `0*log(0)=0` genuinely does fall out); verify.mn:61-70 IS `predicate_decide`; verify.mn:85-91 IS `node_const` with four literal arms and no arithmetic arm (so the V_Pending conclusion is CORRECT); feedback.mn:77 IS `type Gain = Float where 0.0 <= self`. The design's factual reading of the verification substrate is accurate. It is the value substrate it never probed. (One path slip: `math.mn` is `lib/runtime/math.mn`, not `lib/dsp/math.mn` as the bare citations alongside a `lib/dsp/pac.mn` placement imply.)
  fixes    : 1. BLOCK on a named peer; do not build this item. `Hβ.repr.float-in-flat-list` — the flat list's 4-byte stride (make_list's `8 + count*4`, lists.mn:28) vs f64's 8. Ultimate form: `make_list` sizes by `repr_width(repr_of(elem_ty))` and `list_set`/`list_index` project `store_f64`/`load_f64` from the same `repr_of(lookup_ty(h))` read `emit_binop_for` already does — §5.U STEP 1's gradient reaching the sequence node-kind exactly as it reached the word. Peer `Hβ.lists.elem-repr-refusal`: `list_set` typed against the list's element repr so a width mismatch is `E_TypeMismatch` at the call site, not a call_indirect trap. Do NOT box the float — PLAN §5.U names alloc-per-op "the OCaml disease, fatal to DSP" and makes it unsayable.

2. ROW IS WRONG. `with Alloc` omits `Memory`. `list_set`/`list_index` are `with Memory` (lists.mn:46, 90); `make_list`/`list_extend_to` are `with Memory + Alloc` (lists.mn:28, 67); `Memory` (memory.mn:20) and `Alloc` (memory.mn:56) are distinct effects. Declared must subsume inferred → `E_EffectMismatch`. Correct row: `with Memory + Alloc`.

3. INVERT THE REFINEMENT — the design's arm-6 is backwards. It refines the OUTPUT (undischargeable, V_Pending by its own correct reading of node_const) and leaves the INPUT bare `[(Float, Float)]`. Tort's MI requires an ENVELOPE (|analytic signal|, non-negative by construction). A negative amplitude → negative bin-mean → negative p[i] → `log(negative)` returns 0.0 SILENTLY (math.mn:78) → a wrong MI with no diagnostic. The [0,1] bound is therefore NOT guaranteed by the math as specified. Native form: `type Envelope = Float where 0.0 <= self` — exactly the `Gain` shape at feedback.mn:77 that the design names as "the closest precedent" and then does not follow. Refine the input; the bound then FOLLOWS from the math instead of being asserted about a value nothing checks.

4. DELETE THE log-GUARD DEPENDENCY; DO NOT COMMENT IT. The design builds on a fabricated value (`log(x<=0) = 0.0`), calls it "a coincidence of the existing guard, not a designed-in convention," and proposes to name it in a comment. That is decorating a silent surrender-fallback — CLAUDE.md red-flag: "`_ => <fabricated value>` — DELETE it, don't wrap"; Law 8: "no bolts onto non-ultimate forms." A comment is not a proof, and a design that depends on a coincidence has made the coincidence load-bearing. With p[i] >= 0 proven (correction 3), the only residue is p[i]==0, and the fold should skip empty bins STRUCTURALLY — Tort's convention stated as topology, not inherited from a guard's accident.

5. NOVELTY VERDICT OVERCLAIMS — drop "instead of a silent NaN." SYNTAX.md:1002: "The predicate is a compile-time obligation; at gradient-top it erases entirely (no runtime check)." Undischargeable (the design's own correct finding) + no runtime check = INERT. It catches nothing at runtime. A Python toolkit's `assert 0 <= mi <= 1` would actually catch the empty-bin corruption; the undischarged refinement does not — so for THIS value TODAY the borrowed runtime assert is strictly stronger, the exact opposite of the claim. The V_Pending ledger says "this refinement is pending," never "this MI is out of range." The honest and still-real claim: the refinement is the one machine-readable HOME of the bound that R3/SMT will discharge — a claim about what is named, not about what is caught.

6. THE GATE IS VACUOUS FOR THE NOVELTY. mn-tort-mi-bounds checks MI < 0.05 and MI > 0.9 — the ARITHMETIC. It passes identically whether `ModulationIndex` carries the refinement or is bare `Float`, because the predicate erases with no runtime check. The design's sole novelty claim has NO gate. Add a leg through tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh (the existing executable contract for "debt surfaces") asserting the ModulationIndex construction site surfaces exactly one V_Pending obligation — that bites when the refinement is deleted. Also: the gate cannot run as written (the accumulator traps), and MI is a Float while exit codes are Int — the float→int projection is unstated.

7. BLAST RADIUS UNSTATED (minor, benign). lib/ is wheel input (PLAN §6: `find src` + `find lib`), so lib/dsp/pac.mn joins the self-compile. Band D's io.mn precedent (f0089a3) measured that a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation. Likely benign — but the design should say so, and it does not.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.comodulogram-fanout
  The grid of (phase-band, amplitude-band) pairs, computed sequentially via map today and in K-way parallel via a static ><+~>Threaded split, with the genuinely dynamic schedule-aware map this pipeline actually wants named as the concrete real consumer of PLAN's own open Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee.
  verdict  : REFUTED — every ARTIFACT STATE claim verifies (I checked all nine), but the design's destination is unreachable by the peer it names: `map` contains no `><` (lib/prelude.mn:150), so `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` — defined by PLAN band E as propagating a caller's Schedule to a callee's *internal* `><` — has nothing to specialize, and landing it in full leaves `map ~> Threaded` exactly as sequential as today; the gate is vacuous by construction and `~> Threaded` is not a writable install.
  objection: The design names the wrong peer as its own ultimate form, and wants that wrong name canonized in a wheel comment.

`fn map(f, xs) = { iterate(xs); result() } ~> map_collector(f)` (lib/prelude.mn:150). map is a handler-driven iterate loop. It contains zero PFanout nodes — `grep "><" lib/prelude.mn` returns nothing. And `lower_fanout_schedule()` is read at exactly ONE site: `run_fanout_thunks` (src/lower.mn:2781), on the PFanout lowering path (`lower_pipe`, src/lower.mn:2501). No PFanout → the schedule is never read.

So `cross(pb, ab) |> map(compute_pac_for_pair) ~> Threaded` cannot parallelize, today or ever, via the named peer. PLAN §5.R band E scopes `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` as "whether a reusable fn's INTERNAL `><`/`<|` should EVER inherit a caller-installed `Schedule` across a call boundary." map has no internal `><`. That peer landed in full changes nothing here. The design's INTERCONNECTION section ("Name the comodulogram as that concrete consumer") is therefore false: the comodulogram is not a consumer of schedule-specialized-callee, because it never reaches a fanout node.

Three corroborating refutations, each independently sufficient:

1. `~> Threaded` IS NOT WRITABLE. `Threaded` (src/lower.mn:1766) is a nullary constructor of the compiler-internal `Schedule` ADT in src/lower.mn — the value `schedule_in_stack` RETURNS, not a handler user code installs. The install is `~> parallel_compose` (lib/runtime/threading.mn:119), matched because it covers `effect Thread` (threading.mn:62) via `handler_covers_effect(stack_entry_name(e), "Thread")` (lower.mn:1794). The design writes `~> Threaded` in its interim code, its gate, and the comment it wants written into lib/dsp/signal.mn.

2. THE GATE IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION. `parallel_compose` is installed NOWHERE in the repo (grep, excluding its own definition). All six cited `mn-scheduled-fanout-*` peers install `sequential_compose` (or `persist_to_disk`), and they use `<|`, not `><` — e.g. `let (a, b) = (2 <| (widen, widen)) ~> sequential_compose` (tests/frontier/mn-scheduled-fanout-float.mn:8). `sequential_compose` runs `let result = task()` synchronously (threading.mn:139). A gate "matching the existing six peers' shape" therefore asserts that a synchronously-evaluated fanout equals a sequential map — true by construction, proving nothing. The design's own cited property (threading.mn:109-118, "output tuple order preserved regardless of completion order") is untestable when nothing completes out of order.

3. THE PARALLEL PATH RESTS ON A FABRICATED ZERO. Installing `parallel_compose` instead cannot pass: `ffi_spawn(ref task)` (threading.mn:187) calls `closure_pointer(task)` → `closure_pointer_intrinsic(ref task) with Pure = 0` (threading.mn:230). It is NOT an emit intrinsic — `grep -rn "closure_pointer" src/` returns zero hits; the seed's name-keyed intrinsic table died with the seed (7401c4b). So real threading spawns on a NULL closure pointer and joins by reading `closure_done_addr(0)` = `load_i32(4)` (threading.mn:211). The design proposes building its interim parallel form directly on the exact surrender-fallback Law 2 says to DELETE, not build on.

4. DRIFT, NAMED. "Land the parallel form second, honestly as the INTERIM, not the destination... State in the code's own comment that the ultimate form is [X]" is CLAUDE.md ⟐ filter 1 verbatim: "A band-aid in the WHEEL (.mn) → never fine; it is canonized forever." The seed is deleted; lib/dsp/signal.mn IS the wheel — there is no disposable layer left to absorb an interim. K=4 as "a compile-time constant" approximating a core count is drift-8 at the topology layer: the chunk boundary is a side-ledger of a partitioning fact the `~> Schedule` handler is supposed to own, and `ffi_num_cores()` (threading.mn:226) already exists to prove the constant redundant.

WHAT DISPROVED MY OWN ATTACKS: I tried to refute the artifact state and failed on every claim. src/lower.mn:1764-1769 Schedule ADT ✓; lower.mn:1789-1799 schedule_in_stack with the frame fence ✓; threading.mn:119/137 handlers ✓; `bash tools/frontier-gate.sh --compiler boot` → "frontier: 47 pass / 0 red" ✓ (ran it); types.mn:947 `PFanout(FanOwnership)` ✓; types.mn:93 `TFloat => RF64` ✓; no repr arm in parse_type_atom ✓; no Simd handler in lib/ (only a comment at threading.mn:301) ✓; LSimdLanes/f32x4 real in wasm.mn (682, 1064, 1476, 1893, 2114) ✓; no `cross` in prelude/lists ✓; `type Hz` real (lib/dsp/feedback.mn:66) ✓. The SIMD novelty paragraph is honest — tests/micros/mn-fanout-simd.mn states in its own header that it proves "the lane-pack + lane-op CONTROL SHAPE arithmetically", exactly as the design says. I also tried to refute "`><` arity is compile-time-fixed" and could not: `lower_pipe(kind, left, right, handle)` is binary-chained from the parse tree; the arity is syntactic. The design's facts are good. Its inference from them is not.
  fixes    : 1. STRIKE the K-way interim from the wheel. Land the sequential comodulogram alone. Not "sequenced first" — the parallel form as specified is unbuildable (`~> Threaded` is not a handler) and, corrected to `~> parallel_compose`, runs on `closure_pointer_intrinsic = 0`. There is no interim to land; the fork it presents ("sequential now, K-way chunk as INTERIM, generic map as destination") is itself the drift the red-flag table names.

2. FIX THE PEER — the design names one of two required halves and calls it the whole. `map ~> Threaded` needs BOTH:
   (a) `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` — the FRAME FENCE half. `schedule_in_stack` returns Seq at `field_name_eq(stack_entry_name(e), "") { Seq }` (lower.mn:1793), so a caller-installed schedule provably cannot cross into a callee. Necessary. Not sufficient.
   (b) A peer that does not exist in PLAN today — the LOOP DRIVER half. Name it positively: `Hβ.prelude.iterate-driver-reads-schedule`. This is the genuinely native form the design missed, and it is stronger than everything the design proposed: `map` is ALREADY `{ iterate(xs); result() } ~> map_collector(f)` — a handler-driven loop whose per-element `f(x)` is ALREADY a thunk, and §5.U already rules that a branch thunk = closure = continuation = one contiguous record. So data parallelism over a RUNTIME-LENGTH sequence is a HANDLER SWAP ON THE LOOP DRIVER: map_collector reads the live schedule and forks per-element thunks. No `><` arity. No code-generated 225-tuple. No K constant. No Cartesian chunking. The 225 pairs never need to become branches — they are already thunks in a loop the medium already drives with a handler. The design reached for `><` because JAX reaches for a meshgrid; Mentl's iterate-as-handler makes the fanout node unnecessary. That is the answer to "the comodulogram is the concrete consumer" — it is a consumer of (b), which nothing has named.

3. `cross` should reuse what exists: `fn cross(xs, ys) = flat_map((x) => map((y) => (x, y), ys), xs)` — `flat_map` is already at lib/prelude.mn:210, `flatten` at :209. The design's `xs |> map(...) |> flatten` re-derives flat_map inline. Fix = LESS code (Law 1).

4. THE GATE MUST CHANGE TARGET. A scheduling gate is unreachable while threading.mn:230 fabricates 0, and the six cited peers cannot be its shape template (they all install `sequential_compose`, so element-identity holds by construction). The honest gate for THIS cluster is the sequential comodulogram's NUMERICAL correctness against a hand-computed MI on a synthetic coupled signal — real, mechanically checkable, and it fails if the design is not landed. A scheduling gate belongs to peer (b) above, gated behind the fabricated-zero deletion.

5. NAME THE FABRICATED ZERO. `Hβ.runtime.closure-pointer-fabricated-zero` — `closure_pointer_intrinsic(ref task) with Pure = 0` (lib/runtime/threading.mn:230) is a live surrender-fallback in the WHEEL, and it is why `parallel_compose` is installed nowhere. Every green gate stays green because nothing exercises it. Per Law 2 it is DELETED, not built upon; the real form is the closure pointer read from the graph (the record LMakeClosure already emitted — lower knows the handle). This is upstream of any parallel comodulogram and the design should say so instead of citing parallel_compose as "the real Thread-covering handler" — it is real in shape and inoperative in fact.

6. BLAST RADIUS, unstated by the design: adding `cross` to lib/prelude.mn is lib-source-only, and reachability==emission (dcac3b8) means an unreferenced `cross` emits nothing — m2 == m3 holds in one generation (the precedent is `Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc`, band D). The design should assert this rather than omit it.

7. The JAX novelty argument survives and is the design's best part — `><`'s ownership discipline making race-freedom a proven fact where vmap cannot is real, and correctly attributes JAX "the handlers" per PLAN §1. Keep it. But it currently defends a parallel form the design should not build; re-aim it at peer (b), where it is a stronger claim: JAX bakes the schedule at trace time, and a schedule-aware iterate driver keeps it a live `~>` edge over a runtime-length sequence — which is what feedback.mn:164-165 already claims for `><` and which (b) would make true for `map`.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.cfc-groundtruth-crucible
  crucibles/crucible_cfc.mn — a synthetic phase-amplitude-coupled test signal with known ground truth, validated the way the CFC literature itself validates PAC estimators, followed by one real recording — the executable proof this cluster's gate demands.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the gate cannot work: `mentl run`'s exit code is a hardcoded literal `resume(0)` (src/main.mn:411), so both stages' assertions are structurally unobservable, and the design models itself on `crucibles/`, which the repo's own drift ratchet classifies as out-of-band archaeology and which has never passed its own acceptance criteria (163 error-severity diagnostics, exit 0).
  objection: THE GATE PASSES VACUOUSLY — proven at the source and empirically.

The design: "Crucibles run via `mentl check` then `mentl run`... Stage 1's exit code is the numeric ground-truth check; Stage 2's exit code is completion-only."

src/main.mn:404-413:
    handler process_wasmtime {
      process_exec(entry) => {
        eprint_string("mentl run: dispatched {entry} to process boundary\n")
        resume(0)
      }
    }
`resume(0)` is a HARDCODED LITERAL. `mentl run` never spawns wasmtime; the real wire is the named-but-unbuilt peer `Hβ.cli.process-exec-wire` (main.mn:365, 398) — honest sequencing for the compiler, fatal here. `mentl run`'s exit code is incapable of being anything but 0.

Empirically, this session:
- `mentl run crucibles/crucible_dsp.mn` → EXIT 0, 577,977 bytes of WAT on stdout, stderr "mentl run: dispatched crucibles/crucible_dsp to process boundary". The program never executed.
- `mentl check crucibles/crucible_dsp.mn` → EXIT 0 with 163 error-severity diagnostics: 70 E_MissingVariable, 66 E_OccursCheck, 10 E_TypeMismatch, 10 E_OwnershipViolation, 4 E_PurityViolated, 3 E_EffectMismatch.

So Stage 1's "argmax within one bin of (10 Hz, 80 Hz) AND MI > 3x median, both encoded into one deterministic exit code" is unobservable, and Stage 2's "assert only that it COMPLETES (no trap, no E_UnresolvedHole)" is satisfied by a file that does not type-check. Both legs are green on garbage.

The design rejected the working harness BY NAME ("not tools/run-micro.sh"). tools/run-micro.sh:3 reads: "every exit code is captured directly (never through a pipe)"; its usage is `run-micro.sh <micro.mn> [expected_exit] [lib...]`. I ran the live path this session — `cat RTLIBS tests/frontier/mn-partial-hole-executable.mn | wt_run boot/mentl.wasm > t.wat; wt_asm t.wat t.wasm; wt_run t.wasm` → REAL exit code 42. The correct mechanism exists, works, and was discarded for one that structurally cannot work.

Compounding (FACTUAL): "crucible_dsp.mn is the exact precedent" is a misread. tools/drift-patterns.tsv:90 — the repo's own words — "Out-of-band archaeology (crucibles/, tools/editor samples, bootstrap/test repros) may still carry these — the ratchet flags them on touch; the wheel (src/ lib/ tests/) is clean." No crucible is on tools/state.sh's board (its one "crucibles" mention, line 44, is the CROWN gate over tests/crown/). crucible_dsp.mn carries 17 `///` lines = mode-40 dissolved syntax (drift-patterns.tsv:93). ROADMAP.md:616 rates the six crucibles "seeded, not fully demonstrated" — and ROADMAP.md is itself superseded archaeology outside the three-doc contract. The design's own hedge — "verify crucible_dsp.mn's own board membership as part of landing this, to match convention" — defers the single check that refutes its chosen home: there is no board membership and no convention. That deferral is the Drift-9 tell.
  fixes    : 1. GATE — replace `mentl check` + `mentl run` entirely. Use tools/run-micro.sh (rejected by name; it is exactly right: `run-micro.sh <micro.mn> [expected_exit] [lib...]`, "every exit code is captured directly (never through a pipe)"), or a tools/cfc-gate.sh built on frontier-gate.sh's run_program() shape: compile via stdin (`cat "${RTLIBS[@]}" "$source" | wt_run "$compiler" > "$wat" 2> "$cerr"`), count diagnostics (`grep -cE '(^|: )[EWVTP]_'`), normalize + subtract the inherited shadow (`comm -23 "$normalized" "$RUNTIME_SHADOW"`), require rc==0 AND errors==0 (frontier-gate.sh:174-181 makes rc==0-with-errors an explicit FAIL branch — the exact case crucible_dsp.mn hits), then `wt_asm`, then `wt_run "$wasm"` and compare the REAL exit to expected. Proven live: exit 42.

2. THE EXPECTED VALUE HAS ONE HOME, and it is not a comment. tools/verify-baseline.txt holds `micro:NAME=EXPECTED_EXIT` (71 entries: `micro:ev2=57`, `micro:ev5=21`, …); frontier passes `$expected` into run_program(). The design cites tests/frontier/*.mn's "Expected exit: N" comments as establishing the convention — those are human echoes, not the mechanism. Encoding the assertion in prose while reading `mentl run`'s exit code puts the truth in the wrong home and re-derives what the baseline already holds — Carried-Truth violated at the tooling layer.

3. RTLIBS (frontier-gate.sh:72-78) = memory/strings/lists/threading/prelude. It does NOT include lib/runtime/math.mn, which the Stage-1 formula requires (sin, two_pi), nor any lib/dsp/*. The CFC gate must extend the lib set and PIN the resulting inherited runtime shadow the way run_program() does — otherwise the added libs' baseline errors either mask a real regression or fail the gate spuriously.

4. HOME — not crucibles/. That directory is out-of-band archaeology (drift-patterns.tsv:90) whose ratchet "flags them on touch"; adding crucible_cfc.mn is that touch. The live home is tests/frontier/ (or tests/micros/ plus a verify-baseline.txt line). Board membership follows automatically from the existing gate, rather than requiring a new state.sh row for a directory the repo excluded from the wheel.

5. Do not reproduce crucible_dsp.mn's shape: its `import types` (line 19) pulls the COMPILER's own src/types.mn into a user program — that is the 163-error cascade (E_OwnershipViolation "ref binding 'ty' escapes its scope" at types.mn:165, E_MissingVariable len at 181, E_OccursCheck "infinite type"). Its `///` comments are dissolved syntax. Its citations (INSIGHTS L302+, CRU walkthrough §1a, DM-dsp-ml-rewrite.md) are dead docs. "Matching crucible_dsp.mn's documented style" prescribes reproducing all of it.

6. Stage 1's "3x the median" is an invented constant presented as the numerical check. Tort et al. 2010 establishes MI significance with surrogate/shuffled distributions, not a fixed ratio. Either name it as a sanity heuristic in positive form (the design half-does: "ruling out a flat, uninformative grid") or use surrogates and say so.

7. NOVELTY overclaim: "not a Jupyter notebook someone eyeballs — the actual state of validation in most published CFC pipelines" is a strawman. tensorpac, pactools (Dupré la Tour et al.), and MNE-Python all ship automated pytest suites including synthetic-PAC ground-truth tests. The honest native-form argument is narrower and still real: the assertion rides the SAME one-command board (tools/state.sh) as every other claim the medium makes about itself, with the expected value in one home. Drop the field-disparagement; keep the board-membership point.

WHAT SURVIVES (do not rebuild these): the no-CFC/no-comodulogram and no-recording-data (.wav/.csv/.npy) claims — verified. lib/dsp/signal.mn is 1490 bytes with clip/gain/mix/soft_clip/db_to_linear only — genuinely greenfield, as framed. The synthetic-AM-first methodology is correctly kept as scientific method rather than a language pattern; that novelty verdict is honest. The Stage-1 formula is buildable: two_pi() (math.mn:34) and sin() (math.mn:107) exist, and sin IS range-reduced (math.mn:108) — I attacked the formula on precision grounds (argument ≈5027 at 80 Hz × 10 s would wreck a naive series) and the artifact disproved me; residual error ~1e-13 is irrelevant to a comodulogram. Stage 2's "ground truth on a real recording is honestly unknown, assert completion only" is sound epistemics — it just needs a gate that can observe completion.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.felt.lsp-transport-projection
  The LSP server becomes a thin turn-recording wrapper over the SAME cursor_at/propose_at eight-aspect projection mentl edit already runs, instead of a second, sparser proposal engine that never re-infers on keystroke.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the diagnosis (sparse `propose` vs real `propose_at`; didChange discarding contentChanges) is TRUE and worth keeping, but the prescription is refuted: fix #2 hand-rolls an op that already exists and is fully implemented (`edit(FileHandle, Patch)`, voice.mn:1643-1660), fix #1 does not typecheck against `Proposals`/`Cursor`/`CaretTarget`, the chain edit is understated by five handlers, and the gate cannot fail for the headline item.
  objection: FATAL: the design re-derives an edge that already connects the fact — its own Law 1. Fix #2 says handle_did_change "must apply params.contentChanges[].text to the cached FileHandle's buffer ... and then re-run `frontend |> infer_program`". But `Interact.edit(FileHandle, Patch) -> EditOutcome` (voice.mn:359) is FULLY IMPLEMENTED at voice.mn:1643-1660 and already does precisely that: `replace_span_in_text(old_text, span, replacement)` → `update_file_text(handles_table, ...)` → `driver_check(path_to_module_name(s))` → `EditAccepted`. The design accepted lsp.mn:433-434's STALE comment ("edit substrate gates on MV.2.e.P.edit peer sub-handle which lands separately") at face value and never read the arm. The correct edit is: project contentChanges → Patch, call `edit(fh, patch)`. The design's version is MORE code in the WRONG home, and it makes lsp.mn the only transport that knows how to re-infer — precisely the "second engine" its own novelty verdict calls structurally impossible.

FATAL: fix #1 does not typecheck, three ways. (a) `Interact.propose(CaretTarget) -> VoiceLine` (voice.mn:374), but CursorView's propose field is `Proposals(List, List)` (types.mn:704-705, mentl.mn:72-73) — survivors/rejected pairs, not a VoiceLine. (b) "or `cursor_argmax`/`cursor_pinned` per CaretTarget variant ... read the `propose` field off the returned CursorView" is incoherent: both return `Cursor(Handle, Reason, Float)` (types.mn:652-653), which has NO propose field; only `cursor_at(Span) -> CursorView` (cursor.mn:66) returns one. (c) `span_of(target)` is not total — `CaretTarget = TargetHandle(Int) | TargetSpan(Span) | TargetIdent(String)` (voice.mn:257-260); only TargetSpan carries a span, and `resolve_cursor_target` maps TargetIdent → current_handle unresolved (voice.mn:952).

FATAL: "zero change to lsp.mn" contradicts "deletes the sparse Situation composition". lsp.mn:306/322 destructure `VoiceLine(_, _, slots, _)` and feed `completion_items_from_slots` / `code_actions_from_slots`. Only `render_for_tentacle(situation)` (voice.mn:791) produces those slots. Delete the Situation and nothing produces the VoiceLine lsp.mn consumes. Also `silence_predicate` has exactly ONE caller (voice.mn:1317) — deleting it drops the silence law while lsp.mn:305/321 still match `Silence`.

CARRIED-TRUTH VIOLATION IN THE FIX ITSELF: the propose arm ALREADY holds `target_handle` from `resolve_cursor_target` (voice.mn:1314). Routing through `cursor_at(span_of(target))` goes handle → re-derived span → `handle_at_span` (cursor.mn:102) → `scan_for_span`, an O(n) forward scan over the whole graph (cursor.mn:525-542, its own comment: "Today this is a forward scan"). Handle → span → scan → the same handle. That is Law 1 and PLAN §5.O's O(1)-only law violated by the remedy.

MISREAD CITATION: "not re-reading from disk (matching `driver_check_module`'s existing per-file honesty, driver.mn:137-148)" — driver_check_module's FIRST action is `fs_read_file(path)` (driver.mn:147). It re-reads from disk. The design cites it as the model for the exact opposite of what it does. (Also: handles_table is in voice.mn:1539/1567, not "lsp.mn:456-465" — that range is a comment block.)

BLAST RADIUS UNDERSTATED 6×: "adding `~> cursor_default` to serve_run's chain is the concrete edit" is false. serve_run (main.mn:296-312) installs lsp_adapter / mentl_voice_filesystem / mentl_voice_default / query_default / env_handler / wasi_filesystem / no_iter_context / diagnostics_handler / graph_handler / mutate_sink — and NONE of cursor_default, synth_default, interrogate_default, mentl_default, delta_default, verify_ledger. `propose_at` needs `Synth` (cursor.mn:638); teach_gradient/teach_why need mentl_default; verify_pending_at needs `Verify`. edit_run's own comment (main.mn:264-271) is explicit that a missing install means "the first perform met the strict ev-scan miss" and that `cursor_argmax` is AMBIGUOUS against ic_fixpoint_handler. That is 5-6 installs in a load-bearing order, plus cursor_default must sit OUTSIDE mentl_voice_default whose arm would now perform cursor_at — a chain reorder, not an append.

GATE VACUOUS FOR THE HEADLINE ITEM: the gate hovers. `handle_hover` (lsp.mn:278-292) routes through `consult(QTypeAt(span))` — never `propose`. The design itself says consult is already correct ("no drift"). So the gate cannot fail for the propose/propose_at merge; the design's PRIMARY item ships ungated. The gate is also fragile: `ensure_doc_open` re-opens from disk unconditionally (lsp.mn:463-470, "every ensure_doc_open performs open_file unconditionally"), so a harness that writes source B to disk gets `Bool` with zero fix applied — passing vacuously. The design never states that disk must be pinned at source A.

What I tried and failed to refute: the two-engines diagnosis is REAL. I grepped every `propose` call site — only lsp.mn:304 and lsp.mn:320 — while `cursor_at` (cursor.mn:109) fans to `propose_at`, so the terminal transport and LSP genuinely run different proposal engines. And `compose_propose_situation` is `with Pure` (voice.mn:1021), which PROVES it cannot read the graph: the fabricated `env_snapshot: []` / `row_at_topic: None` / `refinements: []` / `gradient_next: None` / `facts.is_hole: false` are a surrender-fallback, not merely "sparse". The rust-analyzer characterization is also fair (it IS a deliberately separate salsa engine from rustc). The diagnosis survives; only the prescription is refuted.
  fixes    : The design must say this instead:

1. FIX #2 IS ONE CALL, NOT A NEW ENGINE. handle_did_change projects `params.contentChanges[].text` → `Patch(whole_doc_span, text)` (textDocumentSync=1=Full, lsp.mn:258, so the span is the whole document) and calls the EXISTING `edit(fh, patch)` (voice.mn:359, arm at 1643-1660). Delete lsp.mn:433-434's stale "edit substrate ... lands separately" comment — the substrate landed. No `frontend |> infer_program` in lsp.mn; the edit arm's `driver_check` already re-projects. Less code, one home, shared with the terminal transport.

2. NAME THE REAL SUBSTRATE GAP THE DESIGN MISSED — it is the interesting one. `edit`'s arm force-persists (`fs_write_file`, voice.mn:1653) BECAUSE `driver_check_module` re-derives the text from disk (`fs_read_file(path)`, driver.mn:147) instead of reading the live buffer `handles_table` already holds. So calling `edit` on an unsaved LSP keystroke would WRITE THE USER'S DISK on every character — wrong semantics for a dirty buffer, and the reason didChange cannot simply reuse edit today. The Carried-Truth fix at the ROOT: the check reads the LIVE buffer when handles_table holds one, disk only when it does not — which simultaneously frees `edit` from its forced disk write, makes didChange correct without touching disk, and deletes a re-derivation. Name it `Hβ.driver.check-reads-live-buffer`; it is the actual blocker and it gates fix #2.

3. FIX #1 IS "STOP FABRICATING THE SITUATION", NOT "DELETE IT". Keep `silence_predicate` (voice.mn:410) and `render_for_tentacle` (voice.mn:791 — it has a second live caller at voice.mn:870, fold_queue_items, so it never goes dead); they are the voice's render and the silence law, and the VoiceLine contract lsp.mn:306/322 consumes. The Carried-Truth violation is that `compose_propose_situation` is `with Pure` and therefore FABRICATES the seven fields the CursorView already carries live. The fix: the propose arm reads the eight-aspect view ONCE at the handle it already resolved, then fills `env_snapshot` / `row_at_topic` / `row_declared` / `ownership_at_topic` / `refinements` / `gradient_next` / `facts.is_hole` from that view (CursorView's query/propose/row/trace/verify/teach fields), deleting each fabricated `[]`/`None`/`false`. The fabrications are the drift; the render is not.

4. READ BY HANDLE, NEVER ROUND-TRIP THROUGH A SPAN. Do not call `cursor_at(span_of(target))` — the arm holds `target_handle` from `resolve_cursor_target` (voice.mn:1314), and `cursor_at` re-scans it back with `handle_at_span` (O(n), cursor.mn:525-542). The honest form is a handle-addressed view read, with `cursor_at(Span)` becoming the span-resolving projection ON TOP of it. Name it `Hβ.cursor.view-at-handle`; it also fixes the same existing round-trip in cursor_transport.mn:251 and is a §5.O O(1) waypoint.

5. THE CHAIN EDIT IS A REORDER OF SIX INSTALLS, NOT AN APPEND. State honestly: serve_run gains cursor_default + synth_default + interrogate_default + mentl_default + verify_ledger (+ delta_default for the cursor_argmax ambiguity main.mn:268-271 names), with cursor_default OUTSIDE mentl_voice_default (whose propose arm now performs cursor_at) and the Synth/Teach/Verify catchers outside that. Cite main.mn:264-271's ev-scan-miss warning as the known sharp edge.

6. THE GATE MUST EXERCISE THE HEADLINE ITEM. Two legs, not one. (a) PROPOSE leg: a `textDocument/completion` request at a `??` hole must return items derived from the live vocabulary; today the sparse Situation's fabricated `is_hole: false` + empty env_snapshot make it structurally impossible, so it fails at baseline and passes only against the fix. Hover cannot serve here — handle_hover uses `consult`, which the design itself certifies as already correct. (b) DIDCHANGE leg: pin disk at source A, send source B ONLY over the wire, assert hover reports `Bool` — and assert the on-disk file is STILL source A afterward, which is what proves the buffer path is live rather than a disk round-trip and catches the fs_write_file regression from correction #2.

7. BLAST RADIUS, stated: fix #1 touches voice.mn + cursor.mn + main.mn's serve_run chain; it changes no emitted wheel semantics on the compile path, so m2 == m3 should hold, but the serve_run chain reorder is the risk surface (an ev-scan miss is a runtime trap, not a compile error) and must be marched, not asserted. The design's "no hard blocker; can start immediately on the propose/propose_at merge (self-contained, cursor.mn + voice.mn only)" is false — it needs the main.mn chain, and fix #2 needs Hβ.driver.check-reads-live-buffer first.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.dsp.recording-ingest
  Two working-today ingestion paths — 16-bit PCM WAV and CSV/text — plus raw float32/float64 named and gated on item 7, turning fs_read_file's raw bytes into [Sample] for any real recording format a slime-mold, bat, electric-fish, or plant electrode rig actually exports.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's return type `[Sample]` does not compile (a bare `[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]` literal fails to assemble because lists.mn hardcodes a 4-byte i32 payload while repr_width(RF64)=8 sits live in types.mn:130), Path A silently returns 0.0 for every CSV token (parse_float is view-blind while lines() returns views) and then traps 134 anyway (HOF-over-Float is an i32-only boundary), and Path B's fixed data-offset 44 is wrong by 34 bytes for ffmpeg-produced pcm_s16le — the exact format it targets.
  objection: The item's own return type does not exist. `[Sample]` is `[Float]` (lib/dsp/feedback.mn:72), and a bare float list literal does not assemble through the pinned boot:

  $ echo 'fn main(q) = len([1.0, 2.0, 3.0])' | tools/run-micro.sh ...
  type mismatch in call, expected [i32, i32, i32, i32] but got [i32, i32, i32, f64]

Every `[Float]` form fails — literal, `++`, `list_set` (exit 134) — while every Int control passes (exit 2/3). Root, read live: lib/runtime/lists.mn:29 `alloc(8 + count * 4)`; lists.mn:47 `store_i32(l + 8 + idx * 4, val)`; lists.mn:91 `load_i32(list + 8 + i * 4)`. The sequence node-kind's payload is a hardcoded 4-byte i32 slot. Meanwhile src/types.mn:130 `repr_width(RF64) => 8` is live, and src/backends/wasm.mn:1409-1597 (`ft_name` / `emit_distinct_repr_ft_types` / `call_ft_name`) already keys the ft table on the interned repr-vector. So the graph proves the element's width and the container re-derives it as the constant `4` at three sites — the Carried-Truth Law violated verbatim, and PLAN §4①'s "ONE sequence type, many element types" is true on the surface and false in the artifact. The repo already knows: tests/micros/mn-distort.mn:11-14 — "the real lib uses [Float] waveshaping ... the float waveshaping is STEP 1's separate representation gap"; tests/micros/mn-autodiff.mn:8 routes around the same hole. The design's ARTIFACT STATE ("No WAV header parser, PCM decoder, or CSV-to-[Sample] reader exists anywhere") is true but names the wrong absence: what is missing is not the reader, it is the `[Sample]` the reader must return.

Three independent refutations underneath it, each tool-proven:

(a) PATH A RETURNS ZEROS. `split` (strings.mn:279) returns zero-copy VIEWS via `str_slice` (strings.mn:260-271: `store_i32(view, 0 - 1)`), and `lines(s) = split(s, "\n")` (prelude.mn:292). `parse_float` (strings.mn:715) reads raw `load_i32(s)` and `load_i8(s + 4 + i)` — it never touches `byte_len`/`byte_at`, so on a view it sees slen = -1 and every guard `i >= slen` fires immediately. Measured, exit=3: `parse_float(lines("1.5\n2.5")[0]) == 0.0` AND `parse_float(lines("-42.0")[0]) == 0.0`, exactly, while `parse_float("1.5")` on a flat literal returns 1.5 and `byte_len` on the same view correctly returns 3. The design cites parse_float and parse_int as equally available; they are opposites — `parse_int` is at lib/prelude.mn:336 (NOT strings.mn:701, which is the private `parse_int_digits`) and reads through `byte_at`, so it works on views. The design's cited line names the wrong function and its Path A composes the one primitive of the pair that is broken.

(b) PATH A DOES NOT RUN. The design's signature verbatim → `wasm trap: indirect call type mismatch`, exit 134, diags=0 (silent miscompile). Pinned to a general floor, not the pipe: `map(parse_float, ls)` traps; `map((t) => parse_float(t), ls)` traps; `apply(f, x) = f(x)` over Float traps (exit 134) while the Int control passes (exit 6); `fold(0.0, (a,x) => a+x, xs)` won't assemble. A function reaching Mentl through a *parameter* is pinned to an all-i32 `call_indirect` signature. Direct lambda call `((x) => x*2.0)(2.0)` works (exit 2) — it is a direct `call`, no ft table.

(c) OFFSET 44 IS WRONG FOR THE DESIGN'S OWN TARGET. Measured against ffmpeg, the tool nearly every rig's audio passes through: `-c:a pcm_s16le` (the design's exact format) writes a `LIST` chunk between `fmt ` and `data` → data payload at **78, not 44** — off by 34, an ODD offset, so every 2-byte sample straddles two real samples, silently, no trap. `-c:a pcm_f32le` → fmt size 18 plus a `fact` chunk plus LIST → data at **92**. Only python's minimal `wave` writer lands at 44. Sample-rate at absolute byte 24 does survive on both. "for the common uncompressed case" is both the lowered-target tell AND factually false of the common case.

The gate is not vacuous in principle — `mn-csv-decode.mn` would correctly fail today — but `mn-wav-pcm16-decode.mn` as specified builds its own header with `store_i8`, which bakes in the 44 assumption and goes GREEN while every ffmpeg-produced recording decodes garbage. A gate that exercises only the writer shape the design assumes is the gate not doing its job.

What I tried and failed to break: Path B's decode kernel. I ran it — `byte_at` → `i32_or(lo, i32_shl(hi, 8))` → sign-extend at 32768 → `/32768.0` — and it is CORRECT: exit=3, 0x4000 → +0.5, 0x8000 → -1.0. `load_i8` really does lower to `i32.load8_u` (src/backends/wasm.mn:3524), so the unsigned assumption holds; `byte_at` (strings.mn:71) really is view/flat-transparent, so the WAV path would have survived the view bug that kills Path A. The arithmetic is right. It has nowhere to put its results.
  fixes    : The design must say, instead:

1. THE ITEM IS BLOCKED, NOT INDEPENDENT. Delete "Path A/B independent of every other item" and "needs nothing new". The real prerequisite is a named peer that does not exist yet in PLAN §5.R: **Hβ.runtime.sequence-element-repr-stride** — lists.mn reads `repr_width(repr_of(element_ty))` (types.mn:130 — RF64 => 8, live today) at `make_list`/`list_set`/`list_index` instead of fabricating the constant `4` (lists.mn:29, 47, 91). This is PLAN §5.U STEP 1's unfinished half: the ft table got the repr-vector (wasm.mn:1409-1597); the sequence node-kind did not — so PLAN §4①'s "ONE sequence type, many element types" is true on the surface and false in the container. Band D names the SUM-payload sibling (`Hβ.emit.variant-payload-repr-width`) and no one names the sequence one. Nothing in this cluster — item 3's FFT, item 5, item 9's CFC crucible — compiles until this lands, because every DSP stage is `[Float] -> [Float]`.

2. PATH A IS DELETED, NOT SHIPPED. `bytes |> lines |> map(parse_float)` is a fabricated-zero generator; Law 2 says a surrender-fallback is deleted, not wrapped. Its prerequisite is **Hβ.runtime.parse-family-view-blind**: six fns (parse_int_digits:701, parse_float:715, parse_frac_digits:739, parse_int_sign:752, find_char:762, find_char_either:768) re-derive the flat layout by hand while `byte_len`/`byte_at` (strings.mn:63/71) are the projection that already dispatches both shapes. `parse_int` (prelude.mn:336) is the correct form sitting in the same repo — the fix is deleting the hand-rolled offsets at six sites and reading live. Fix = LESS code, exactly per Law 1. Then Path A also needs the HOF-over-Float floor (`map(f,xs)`/`fold`/`apply(f,x)=f(x)` all fail on f64 while their Int controls pass) — name it **Hβ.emit.hof-param-repr-signature**.

3. WAV DATA IS FOUND BY WALKING THE CHUNK CHAIN, NOT AT 44. `fn find_chunk(bytes, id) -> (offset, size)` — from byte 12, read `[4-byte id][4-byte le size]`, skip `8 + size + (size & 1)`, repeat. This handles fmt=16/18/40 (WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE), LIST, fact, and bext with one loop and zero special cases. Keep the sample-rate read, but read it at `fmt_payload + 4`, not absolute 24 — same reason. Delete "for the common uncompressed case": it is the lowered-target tell AND it is false of the common case (ffmpeg pcm_s16le → data at 78).

4. THE ROW IS `with Memory + Alloc`. Not `with Alloc` (DESIGN) and not `with Filesystem + Alloc` (INTERROGATIONS). `byte_at` is `with Memory` (strings.mn:71); `parse_float` is `with Memory + Alloc` (strings.mn:715). Filesystem is never performed — the fn takes `bytes: String` already read; claiming it is T_OverDeclared.

5. THE RETURN TYPE IS `[Float]`, NOT `[Sample]`. `type Sample = Float where 0.0 - 1.0 <= self && self <= 1.0` (lib/dsp/feedback.mn:72). A slime-mold/plant electrode trace exports microvolts (50.0, -120.0) — outside the refinement. `samples_from_csv -> [Sample]` is a type lie for the exact research use the ITEM headline invokes. Return the recording's own units as `[Float]`; make normalization an explicit named stage whose refinement discharge is honest. Path B's `/32768.0` genuinely lands in [-1,1] and may claim `[Sample]`; the CSV path may not.

6. THE GATE MUST INGEST A REAL FILE. A `store_i8`-built header tests only the writer shape the design already assumes and goes green while ffmpeg files decode garbage. Check in a fixture produced by `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i sine=... -c:a pcm_s16le` (it carries a LIST chunk; data at 78) and read it through `fs_read_file` — that is the only gate that fails if the chunk walk is absent. Keep `mn-csv-decode.mn`: it correctly fails today, which is the finding.

7. BLAST RADIUS, STATED HONESTLY: repr-aware sequence payload is a lib+emit change that shifts emitted bytes — a march TRANSITION (m2 ≠ m3, m3 == m4, re-pin boot from m3) per PLAN §8, not a Law-7 no-op. The design's zero-blast-radius claim is wrong at the root.

8. THE NOVELTY VERDICT INVERTS ITSELF AND MUST BE REWRITTEN. It rejects scipy.io.wavfile on memory-safety grounds and then adopts a form strictly weaker than what it rejected: every serious WAV reader walks the chunk chain; the design hardcodes 44. RIFF declares data's location as an edge chain ([id][size][payload]); reading 44 fabricates it — the Carried-Truth violation the same design invokes as its justification. The honest native argument is the one the design skipped: the chunk walk IS the graph-native form (follow the edge the format draws), it is LESS code than a per-format offset table, and Mentl's bump-allocated image is a genuine (if secondary) bonus. Separately, the "C library via FFI" foil may be misidentified — scipy.io.wavfile is, to my knowledge, pure Python doing a real chunk walk, which would make the CVE-class argument aimed at a target that isn't there. I could NOT verify this with a tool (scipy is not installed on this host), so I do not rest the refutation on it and neither should the rewrite.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.felt.ide-persistent-session
  The browser IDE stops cold-booting the whole compiler on every keystroke; it becomes one persistent WASM instance driven by an exported edit-step function, riding the same Surface effect terminal_transport already implements.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's load-bearing safety argument is inverted, and the artifact disproves it in 15 calls: I ran the design's exact shape (one instance, many calls) against ide/mentl-ide.wasm and it died at keystroke 15 with `memory access out of bounds`. The never-free bump allocator is the HAZARD, not "exactly the property that makes this safe"; the per-keystroke `WebAssembly.instantiate` the design calls "the defect" is the medium's only reclaim mechanism today.
  objection: THE SAFETY ARGUMENT IS EXACTLY BACKWARDS, AND IT IS MEASURABLE.

The design states: "the graph populated by the first edit is still live (the bump allocator is monotonic and never frees, PLAN §6 — exactly the property that makes this safe) when the second edit arrives."

I ran it. Probe: instantiate ide/mentl-ide.wasm ONCE, call the entry repeatedly with IDE-shaped input (the linked RT — lib/runtime/{memory,strings,lists}.mn + lib/prelude.mn, 2022 lines, exactly what index.html:646-649,667 sends per keystroke). `_start` is a faithful stand-in for the proposed `mentl_ide_step`: `$heap_ptr` is a global initialized at INSTANTIATE (src/backends/wasm.mn:1768 `(global $heap_ptr (mut i32) (i32.const 1048576))`), not per call, so the memory behavior is identical for either export shape.

  keystroke  1-14 | exit 0 | WAT lines 1146
  keystroke 15 | TRAP: memory access out of bounds | WAT lines 0
  >>> DIED AT KEYSTROKE 15 ON THE PERSISTENT INSTANCE.

Two independent measurements agree on why: 511 MiB usable / 14 survived = 36.5 MiB per keystroke; RSS delta (70,696 KB with RT − 34,740 KB empty) = 35.1 MiB. The shipped ide/mentl-ide.wasm declares `MemoryType { shared: true, initial: 8192, maximum: Some(65536) }` — 512 MiB initial — and src/backends/wasm.mn:1756 states plainly: "the bump allocator never grows memory." lib/runtime/memory.mn:49: "No free. No GC."
