The wheel's own comment already documents this exact failure and the design walks past it (src/backends/wasm.mn:1754-1761): "32768 initial silently starved a wheel-scale compile: the bump allocator never grows memory, so the first record allocated past 2GB 'succeeded' arithmetically and the first READ of it trapped OOB — m3's m4-generation died in reach's str_eq with structurally-perfect operands (2026-07-09; three probe cycles found no corruption because there was none)." The design proposes re-introducing that debugging hell at the human's keystroke boundary — and this time there is no escape hatch, because the current emit is already at `(memory (export "memory") 65536 65536 shared)` (wasm.mn:1762) and 65536 pages IS the wasm32 ceiling. At the measured 36.5 MiB/keystroke, a 4 GiB build buys 112 keystrokes; at cursor_transport.mn:115's `default_cadence() = CadDebounced(250)` that is minutes of real editing, and there is no larger number to purchase.

FOUR SUPPORTING REFUTATIONS:

1. FACTUAL MISREAD. "there is no structured CursorView anywhere in this path" is false. ide/index.html:544-590 `projectAspects` IS an eight-aspect CursorView — labeled graph/handler/verb/row/own-ref/refine/gradient/why — built from `surfaceParse` (index.html:486) and `tokenize` (index.html:394), driven by `projectCursor` (857) on independent `["click","keyup"]` listeners (900). That is ~200 lines of JS re-deriving the lexer, the parser, AND the eight-aspect projection from raw text — the largest Carried-Truth violation in the file and the actual heart of this item. The design targets only `parseDiags` (699-714, 16 lines) and scopes its JS rewrite to `render()` (721-737), which is not where the aspects are computed.

2. MISSED DORMANT PRIMITIVE. `emit_memory_arena` (src/backends/wasm.mn:139) already exists, uncalled, and its own comment (130-138) names the exact reclaim the persistent session needs: "on scope exit, the pointer resets to the entry snapshot, reclaiming the whole region in O(1) ... pipeline can swap `~> emit_memory_bump` for `~> emit_memory_arena` with zero source changes." The design never mentions it. "Defined but uncalled" IS the slot for what it is about to build.

3. THE REACTOR SHAPE EXPELS `<~`. The novelty verdict claims "no kernel semantics ride on the choice of which export shape the host calls." False — the export shape decides whether the feedback loop lives in the medium or in JavaScript. `cursor_session` (cursor_transport.mn:235-240) OWNS the `<~ accumulate`; a call-in `mentl_ide_step` returning `view_ptr` means JS drives the loop and `TransportState` has no home across calls (the design never says where it lives). cursor_transport.mn:228-233 names this precisely: "The former hand-rolled tail-recursion ... was the `<~` verb un-named — the exact drift the prime directive forbids." The native form is available and unmentioned: the page is already `crossOriginIsolated` (index.html:643) and the memory is already `shared` (confirmed in the shipped module), so a Worker + `Atomics.wait` lets `surface_action()` genuinely BLOCK exactly like the terminal's stdin read — the `<~` stays in the medium and browser_transport becomes a true peer of terminal_transport (cursor_transport.mn:148) instead of an inversion of it.

4. THE GATE IS UNRUNNABLE AND TESTS THE WRONG PROPERTY. "using wasmtime directly ... instantiate the ide-entry wasm ONCE, call the exported step function ... then call it AGAIN on the SAME instance" — `wasmtime run --invoke <FUNCTION>` (verified, wasmtime 43.0.1) runs ONE function once and exits. The wasmtime CLI cannot do this; the real host is ide/test-shim.mjs (Node), already in the repo and unnamed by the design. Worse, the gate asserts PERSISTENCE, which the broken form satisfies — it passes at keystroke 2 and ships a session that dies at 15. It is the gate the design cannot write because it believes the ceiling is a feature.

BLAST RADIUS, ABSENT: the design says "only the `Surface` handler implementation is new." There are exactly three export sites in the entire emit, all hardcoded string literals (wasm.mn:1762 memory, 2605 `_start`, 2647 `wasi_thread_start`) and no general export mechanism. Minting `mentl_ide_step` is an EMIT change — the TRANSITION case (m2 != m3, re-pin from m3) per PLAN §7 / `Hβ.tools.march-transition-native`. The design states nothing about the fixpoint.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE (the design did read the code): serve.mn is a pure static server, all 113 lines, no compile loop — TRUE. `boot()` compiles MODULE once (index.html:650) — TRUE. `compile()` instantiates fresh per keystroke (673) — TRUE. `args_sizes_get` returns argc=0 (621-623) — TRUE. `fd_read` fake stdin (609-619) — TRUE. Surface effect with three ops (cursor_transport.mn:73-87) — TRUE. terminal_transport at 148, cursor_session/cursor_step at 235-270 as a real `<~` loop — TRUE. edit_run at main.mn:254-286 installing terminal_transport at 264 — TRUE. serve_run/mentl_lsp_session at 300-318 — TRUE. The artifact_state is accurate throughout except the one claim that decides the design.
  fixes    : The item survives, but only in a form that inverts its own premise and its sequencing. Six corrections:

1. DELETE THE SAFETY ARGUMENT; IT IS THE HAZARD. Strike "the bump allocator is monotonic and never frees, PLAN §6 — exactly the property that makes this safe." The correct sentence: the per-keystroke `WebAssembly.instantiate` is the medium's ONLY reclaim today (a fresh memory IS a bump reset), and deleting it without a replacement trades a bounded per-keystroke budget for a monotonically-consumed one against a ceiling that cannot move. Cite the measurement: 36.5 MiB per keystroke, dead at 15 on the shipped 512 MiB module, 112 on a 4 GiB one, and 65536 pages is the wasm32 ceiling.

2. THE SEQUENCING IS WRONG: THERE IS A HARD DEPENDENCY. Strike "No hard dependency; buildable now." The dependency is the image/scratch allocator split and the arena reset — already named at PLAN §5.O layer 3 (`Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena`, "the region drop IS the arena reset (O(1))... Activates the dormant emit_memory_arena swap (wasm.mn:139)") and PLAN §11 column 3 ("the image/scratch allocator split → the emit_memory_arena swap"). PLAN §7 records regions LIVE (82a7e42) and §11 marks the arc "unblocked 2026-07-16", so this is a real, named, now-buildable dependency — not an absence. This item lands AFTER it, not before item 1.

3. THE IMAGE/SCRATCH SPLIT IS NOT A PREREQUISITE BOLTED ON — IT IS THIS ITEM'S ACTUAL CONTENT. The persistent session's whole question is precisely which bytes persist: the GRAPH (image — the thing "still live when the second edit arrives") versus the per-step compile garbage (scratch — 36.5 MiB that must die at the step boundary). That IS the image/scratch distinction, and `emit_memory_arena` (wasm.mn:139) is the dormant handler swap that draws it: "on scope exit, the pointer resets to the entry snapshot, reclaiming the whole region in O(1)." One edit step = one region. Read the dormant substrate first; the slot for what this item builds already exists, uncalled.

4. KEEP `<~` IN THE MEDIUM: THE BLOCKING WORKER, NOT THE REACTOR CALL-IN. Replace the `mentl_ide_step(action_ptr, action_len) -> view_ptr` reactor with a Worker running `cursor_session`'s `<~` loop, where `browser_transport`'s `surface_action()` blocks on `Atomics.wait` over the already-`shared` memory and the main thread posts keystrokes with `Atomics.notify`. The substrate is already there: index.html:643 checks `crossOriginIsolated`, serve.mn:69 already ships the COOP/COEP pair, and the module's memory is already `shared`. This makes `surface_action()` block exactly like the terminal's stdin read, so browser_transport becomes a genuine peer of terminal_transport (cursor_transport.mn:148) — the "handler IS the backend" swap the design claims but its own shape prevents. The reactor form hoists the `<~` into the JS event loop and leaves `TransportState` homeless across calls; cursor_transport.mn:228-233 already names that the forbidden drift. Correct the novelty verdict accordingly: the reactor is NOT semantics-free scaffold — it costs a kernel verb, so it is rejected, not adopted.

5. NAME THE REAL CARRIED-TRUTH TARGET IN index.html. It is not `parseDiags` (699-714, 16 lines). It is `tokenize` (394) + `surfaceParse` (486) + `tokenAt` (534) + `enclosingDecl` (539) + `projectAspects` (544-590) + `projectCursor` (857) — ~200 lines of JavaScript second-compiler re-deriving the lexer, the parser, and the eight-aspect CursorView from raw text, wired to its own `["click","keyup"]` listeners (900) that fire independently of `compile()`. Those ~200 lines are what `surface_render` writing a real CursorView DELETES. That deletion is the item's payoff and its honest blast radius. The fix is LESS code — state the line count.

6. THE GATE MUST BOUND THE HIGH-WATER, RUN UNDER NODE, AND FAIL TODAY. Host it on ide/test-shim.mjs (already in the repo), not `wasmtime --invoke` (which runs one function once and exits, verified on wasmtime 43.0.1). Assert the property that actually decides soundness: N steps (N >= 50) on ONE instance with the memory high-water BOUNDED — i.e. step k's post-step bump pointer is within a constant of step k+1's, proving the scratch region resets while the graph persists. Export the bump pointer so the gate can read it (it is a private global today, wasm.mn:1768 — that read is itself the honest requirement). That gate fails today at keystroke 15 with `memory access out of bounds`, and it cannot pass by accident: a design that persists the instance without the arena reset fails it at exactly the ceiling. Drop the "compile time consistent with an incremental read" assertion entirely — PLAN §5.O records ±14% run-variance, so timing is not a mechanical check.

ALSO: use the name the codebase already carries. src/main.mn:252 already reads "Named follow-up: Hμ.cursor.transport.browser-wasm" for exactly this work. Minting `Hβ.felt.ide-persistent-session` as a second name for one truth is the one-home violation the three-doc contract forbids. And state the blast radius honestly: minting any new export requires a fourth hardcoded `wat_emit` beside wasm.mn:1762/2605/2647 (there is no general export mechanism), which is an EMIT change — the m2 != m3 TRANSITION case, re-pin from m3.

REFUTED: Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection
  The DiagKind ADT and its seven projection functions are fully built and correct; the gap is that exactly one consumer reads them, so "one graph fact, many renderers" is proven in the type layer but not yet in practice.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the diagnosis is sound and understated, but the prescribed mechanism is refuted on the artifact: Mentl has no handler return-clause, so "a stateful handler is the entire fix; no new effect" cannot work; as specified it lands a zero-reader accumulator — the exact write-only side-ledger PLAN §5.U names as the textbook Carried-Truth violation — and its two `with` requirements are mutually unparseable at parser.mn:893.
  objection: The design's central claim — INTERROGATIONS: "a genuinely stateful diagnostics_handler ... is the entire fix; no new effect, no new ADT field" — is false against the artifact, and landing it as written reproduces a drift the project already paid to delete.

Mentl has NO handler return-clause. `HandlerDeclStmt(String, String, List, List({init: Node, name: String}), List)` (src/types.mn:1057) = name, "", config, state, arms — no return slot. `parse_handler_decl` (src/parser.mn:876-908) parses config, an optional `with`, then arms, and constructs exactly that node. Nothing projects a handler's final state to its install site's value.

The ONE mechanism by which handler state escapes an install is a SECOND OP answered by `resume(state)`. This is the prelude's universal, proven pattern: `handler fold_handler(f, init) with acc = init { yield(elem) => resume() with acc = f(acc, elem), result() => resume(acc) }` (lib/prelude.mn:163-165), invoked as `{ iterate(xs); result() } ~> fold_handler(f, init)` — the body PERFORMS `result()` before the install scope closes. Identical shape at `collector` (prelude.mn:139-145), `any_handler` (172), `all_handler` (177), `find_handler` (182), `count_handler` (190), and the `{ iterate(xs); result() } ~>` call form at prelude.mn:150-153.

`effect Diagnostic { report(DiagKind) -> () }` (src/types.mn:1345-1347) declares exactly one op — which the design itself stresses as a virtue. So a `with diags = []` accumulator on diagnostics_handler has ZERO READERS. That is precisely what PLAN §5.U names by name: "The write-only `resume_kinds` side-ledger (zero readers) is the textbook Carried-Truth violation" (dissolved at 7b72790). The design either lands that drift, or needs `result() -> [DiagKind]` on the Diagnostic effect — contradicting its own "no new effect" and making the design incomplete rather than merely mis-worded, because that op must then be performed by each route body at 21 install sites (14 in src/main.mn, 7 in src/pipeline.mn) which NEST: main.mn:235 wraps the whole verb dispatch, pipeline.mn:83 installs another INSIDE the compile route, so by the `~>` row rule the inner absorbs every report and the outer install never sees the compile's diagnostics at all.

The knockout: the design wants BOTH `with !Diagnostic` preserved AND `with diags = []` state on the same handler. parser.mn:889-904 makes `with` an EITHER/OR — `if at(tokens, p_with, TBang) { ([], skip_to_lbrace(tokens, p_with)) }` returns EMPTY STATE and skips to the arms. A handler cannot carry both a row guard and state; the failure is SILENT (state dropped to `[]`).

And the Row interrogation is doubly fictional. Design: "`!Diagnostic` on diagnostics_handler's own arms (pipeline.mn:576's structural gate, noted in its comment) already proves the handler cannot recursively report." src/pipeline.mn:576 is bare `handler diagnostics_handler {`. The ONLY two occurrences of `!Diagnostic` in all of src/ and lib/ are COMMENTS (types.mn:1343, pipeline.mn:573). The design's own phrase "noted in its comment" is the tell — it cited stale prose as the artifact (CLAUDE.md ⟲: a label is a HYPOTHESIS until the ARTIFACT confirms it; the bug-class "a diagnostic's NAME can lie"). Worse, even if written, parser.mn:893-894 SKIPS the row — `Hβ.parser-handler-effect-row-record` is the named, unbuilt peer for extracting it. Nothing "already proves" anything; there is no gate to preserve.

What disproved me where I attacked and failed: the diagnosis itself is real and UNDERSTATED. ide/index.html:687-693's `const APPL` table hand-duplicates `diag_applicability` (types.mn:1534-1555) and has ALREADY DRIFTED — it lists `E_ImportNameCollision` and `W_EmptyRow`, which are NOT DiagKind constructors (grep of src/types.mn returns nothing; the 47 constructors at 1284-1339 contain neither). Two phantom codes the compiler can never emit, live in tree. That is proof-by-artifact that the second representation drifts, which the design merely predicted. All seven projections have EXACTLY 47 arms each (verified by count over 1354-1402, 1405-1453, 1456-1504, 1568-1616), so the `mentl diagnostics` exhaustiveness argument is sound and is the design's strongest part. Every other cited line number is exact.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the Row interrogation. There is no `!Diagnostic` gate at pipeline.mn:576 (bare `handler diagnostics_handler {`; the only `!Diagnostic` occurrences in src/ and lib/ are comments at types.mn:1343 and pipeline.mn:573), and parser.mn:893-894 would skip the row if written. Do not claim it proves anything; do not "preserve" it. The honest statement: the recursion gate is unbuilt, its named peer is `Hβ.parser-handler-effect-row-record`, and a stale comment at pipeline.mn:573 asserts a row the grammar discards — that comment is doc-truth debt to fix in the same landing.

2. The design must say `result() -> [DiagKind]` (or `drain()`) is a NEW OP on the Diagnostic effect, not "no new effect". This is not a concession — it is the prelude's proven form (prelude.mn:163-165, 150-153): `{ compile_route(); result() } ~> diagnostics_collect`. Then state its consequence honestly: each route body performs `result()` before its install closes, and the 21 install sites (main.mn ×14, pipeline.mn ×7) NEST, so the design must NAME which single install accumulates. Today pipeline.mn:83's inner install absorbs every compile diagnostic and main.mn:235's outer install never sees it.

3. Do NOT have both `with !Diagnostic` and `with diags = []` on one handler — parser.mn:889-904 admits one or the other and SILENTLY drops the state on the `TBang` branch. Pick state, or land `Hβ.parser-handler-effect-row-record` first.

4. REVERSE the LSP rewiring. The design says to read the accumulated list "instead of lsp.mn's QVerifyDebt-only diagnostic path (lsp.mn:330-341)". That path's own comment (lsp.mn:325-329) reads: "consult(QVerifyDebt) for the live pending obligations the graph holds ... The verify-debt IS the diagnostic set (the graph proved it) — read live, never an empty []-fabrication." That is Law 1 satisfied ALREADY. Replacing a live graph read with a snapshotted event list is Law 1 INVERTED ("a consumer cached X instead of reading live"). The design's flat "Graph? — none of this is a graph fact" is also wrong: types.mn:445 declares `NErrorHole(Reason)  // inference observed failure (Hazel marked hole)`, and V_Pending is a live graph fact QVerifyDebt already projects (query.mn:29,173). The correct split: diagnostics with a graph home (V_Pending, inference failures) stay LIVE READS; only genuinely event-shaped diagnostics with no graph node (parser-stage P_*, format-liftable E_RedundantBraces) may accumulate — because for those there is nothing live to read.

5. KILL the "per-class count map". `diag_code` returns a String, so a per-class map is string-keyed (drift-8) and O(n) str_eq. PLAN §11's named-peer audit explicitly INVERTED this sequencing: "the banked smap-first sequencing is INVERTED (str_hash-keyed maps re-key by handle when interning lands — the migration paid twice); intern-at-lex leads, the maps land handle-keyed once." A new str_hash map lands exactly what §11 ruled against. The native form: the constructor's own tag IS the class identity (sum = tag + payload), so counting is a fold over the accumulated events by `diag_severity` — LESS code, no index.

6. FIX the novelty verdict; it manufactures its differentiator by misdescribing rustc. rustc does NOT keep "two renderers hand-kept in sync by convention and CI vigilance": it has ONE datum (`Diag`/`DiagInner`) and an `Emitter` trait, with `HumanEmitter` and `JsonEmitter` both consuming the same value via `emit_diagnostic(&Diagnostic)`, plus `#[derive(Diagnostic)]` typed diagnostic structs. "Exactly one datum, never two representations to keep synchronized" describes rustc too, so as written the design claims no novelty at all. The REAL native difference, which the design failed to name and should argue: in rustc the emitter is a CONFIG FIELD read at session construction (`--error-format` → `ErrorOutputType`, threaded through `Session`); in Mentl the renderer is a `~>` HANDLER SWAP at the install site — `route ~> diagnostics_json` vs `route ~> diagnostics_handler`, an EDGE, typed by `row(expr ~> h) = row(expr) − handled(h) + row(h)`, no flag and no session field. That is strictly stronger and it is Mentl-structural.

7. DELETE `mentl check --json` from the gate — it is literally rustc's `--error-format=json`, the borrowed shape the design claims to REPLACE, re-imported at the exact moment the handler swap made it unnecessary. The task's rule is: name a kept borrow as scaffold-tier or replace it; the design does neither. It also does not parse: parse_cli_args (main.mn:120-121) is `mode == "check" && len(argv) >= 3 → VCheck(path_to_module(argv[2]))`, so `mentl check --json foo.mn` compiles a module named `--json`. The native gate is the handler swap at the install site, no flag.

8. REPLACE the gate. "Two outputs report the same two diag_code/diag_severity values" is weak — two hand-synced renderers pass it on a 2-diagnostic fixture, so it does not prove the one-datum property it claims. The gate that actually bites is the one the design identified but did not make the gate: add a 48th DiagKind constructor and assert the BUILD FAILS until every projection covers it (all seven projections currently have exactly 47 arms — verified at 1354-1402, 1405-1453, 1456-1504, 1568-1616). That is mechanical, non-vacuous, H6-exhaustiveness-backed, and proves precisely the claim rustc's error index cannot make.

9. ADD the blast-radius statement the design omits entirely (drift-9, deferred-by-omission). A new Diagnostic op touches 75 `report(` call sites and the sandbox-boundary handler on every compile route. Per PLAN §5.R band D the io.mn precedent measured a source-only change holding m2 == m3 in ONE generation ("the transition form is for EMIT changes only"), so this is likely Law-7-additive — but the design must SAY so and gate it against the board (frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros, march m2 == m3), not stay silent.

10. STRENGTHEN the motivation with the artifact. ide/index.html:687-693's `APPL` table already carries `E_ImportNameCollision` and `W_EmptyRow` — phantom codes absent from the 47-constructor DiagKind ADT. Alongside `FIXABLE` (index.html:696) and `FORMAT_LIFTABLE` (698), that is three hand-maintained JS tables duplicating diag_applicability/diag_severity, one already drifted. The design predicted drift; the tree already has it. Lead with that.

11. Fix the counts: 47 constructors, not 46; 75 `report(` sites, not ~60; and the text says "Six total projections exist" while the ITEM says "seven" and the artifact has eight counting `applicability_code` (types.mn:1558).
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.cli.audit-row-var-render
  mentl audit prints an open effect-row variable as its raw internal handle-and-epoch token instead of a clean, teaching-appropriate letter — a one-arm, precisely-located display fix.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is factually true (I reproduced `apply : r70@e9` live), but the item mis-audits the site: the raw token is currently the ONLY signal warning a reader that the line beside it — "unlocks ... Real-time safe (proven zero allocation)" on an unproven-open row — is a fabricated proof, so the "cosmetic first landing" would launder a silent-wrong capability claim; the letter mechanism is provably a constant, the novelty justification is false against SYNTAX.md's actual text, and the gate invokes a verb that does not exist.
  objection: THE FIX LAUNDERS A FABRICATED PROOF ON THE SAME LINE IT PRETTIFIES.

Live, via the installed CLI on a row-polymorphic fixture (`fn apply(f, x) = f(x)`):

    apply : r70@e9
      severable: Network + IO + Alloc — unlocks Sandbox (proven no network access),
      Sandbox (proven no network access), Compile-time evaluation,
      Real-time safe (proven zero allocation)
    main : Pure
      severable: Network + IO + Alloc — unlocks ... Real-time safe (proven zero allocation)

A proven-empty row (`Pure`) and an unproven-open row (`r70@e9`) receive BYTE-IDENTICAL capability advice. The word "proven" is literally in the user-facing string: `CRealTime => "Real-time safe (proven zero allocation)"` (src/mentl.mn:579).

The chain, each link read: `row_names(EfOpen(names, _)) => names` (src/infer.mn:4538) DISCARDS the row variable — the one fact distinguishing "proven nothing" from "proven empty". `severance_candidates(reached)` (src/pipeline.mn:693-696) then sees `[]` for both and marks Alloc/IO/Network severable; `capabilities_for_severance` (src/mentl.mn:341-349) cashes that into CRealTime/CSandbox. The graph distinguishes the two by CONSTRUCTOR (EfPure vs EfOpen(names,v)); the consumer collapses them by reading only `names`. That is the Carried-Truth Law verbatim — "the graph proved X; a consumer discarded X" — and infer.mn:4539's `_ => []` masks EfNeg/EfSub/EfInter into the same hole (PLAN §9's `match … with _` bug class).

Why this refutes the ITEM and not merely its neighbourhood: today `apply : r70@e9` reads as debug spew, and a human distrusts the whole line. The design renders it `apply : ..r` — polished, authoritative — while "proven zero allocation" sits untouched beside it. The credibility of an unproven claim rises; no proof is added. The item's SEQUENCING ("smallest unit of work here — a reasonable first landing") makes it worse: it polishes the only warning light first. CLAUDE.md ⊕ names this exact move — "AUDIT BEFORE THE SYMPTOM ... debugging a symptom's mechanism before auditing whether the structure should EXIST is the drift itself." The item's sentence "This is CORRECT data (the row genuinely is open) rendered in an internal-debug form" is the drift in miniature: the token is correct in isolation; the line it sits on is not, and the item never asked.

The tell that this was reasoned from the text rather than the artifact (Law 5): the very first line of real output also duplicates "Sandbox (proven no network access), Sandbox (proven no network access)" (severance_unlocks_loop concats without a set, src/pipeline.mn:720-726). One run surfaces both defects. The item ran nothing.

FOUR INDEPENDENT REFUTATIONS, each sufficient:

1. THE LETTER IS A CONSTANT. render_audit's header is `let header = "{fn_name} : {row_str}\n"` (src/pipeline.mn:739) — the row, and only the row. A free var appears EXACTLY ONCE per entry (proven: `apply : r70@e9`). A letter's sole job is coreference; with one occurrence there is nothing to corefer. Scoped-per-entry + one-occurrence-per-entry means the counter never reaches the design's own "second `..s`". It is a state machine that provably never leaves its first state, threaded through a renderer to emit a constant.

2. THE NOVELTY JUSTIFICATION IS FALSE. SYNTAX.md:747 reads: "`...` is anonymous rest; `...R` binds the rest to a row variable `R`". The design writes `..r` and claims it "directly reuses Mentl's OWN existing `...`/`...R` vocabulary ... rather than inventing a new sigil." Two dots is not three; lowercase `r` is not capital `R`, and Mentl's case rule is load-bearing (SYNTAX.md: nominal types capitalized, lowercase = type parameters). Worse: `...R` is RECORD row polymorphism. SYNTAX.md gives EFFECT rows no open-variable surface at all. So `IO + ..r` is a form the developer cannot type and SYNTAX.md does not define — a renderer inventing surface syntax, inverting the contract ("SYNTAX.md is the wheel, the parser is the lathe"; every form needs a kernel correspondence and one home).

3. THE GATE CANNOT RUN. Verified: `mentl why /tmp/rowpoly.mn` → "mentl: unrecognized or under-specified command: why". The gate's control leg — "the same grep against `mentl why` output should still find the raw form" — invokes a non-existent verb; it greps help text, finds zero, and reports a false failure. Deeper: `mentl query /tmp/rowpoly.mn "effects apply"` → `→ r70@e9`, and the design puts query in the TEACHING set. After the fix there is no reachable CLI surface that keeps the raw form, so "register-scoped, not a global deletion" has no witness by construction. And the pattern `r[0-9]+@e[0-9]+` structurally cannot catch the TVar half the design says it also fixes — that renders `t{h}@e{epoch}` (src/types.mn:1821), a `t`.

4. THE REGISTER SPLIT IS A SHAPE SYNTAX.md ALREADY DISSOLVED. §Comments: "Register is a projection, not a delimiter ... NOT an author-chosen `//`-vs-`///` audience split" and "**Dissolved:** the `//`-vs-`///` split." Both offered shapes are that split at the render layer: `show_effrow_teaching` chosen at the call site, or "a register parameter threaded through the existing one" (flag-as-parameter, drift 8 kin). PLAN §5.U's BOUNDARY already names the home: show_type/show_reason/show_effrow are "a `~> Format` projection of the compiler's own metaschema." src/format.mn exists. The register is an installed handler read live, never a second fn.

BLAST RADIUS, corrected: "a small, additive change" is wrong. show_effrow → show_handle → show_type → show_row_suffix → show_effrow is a mutual-recursion CYCLE (src/types.mn:1798, 1816-1823, 2013-2026). Threading a counter through it is a coordinated arity change across the whole render family — the §5.U unpatchability shape. The fixpoint itself is NOT at risk (a source-only render change holds m2 == m3 in one generation, per band D's `Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc` precedent) — but the design should say that rather than be silent, and "one-arm, precisely-located" should not survive contact with the cycle.
  fixes    : WHAT SURVIVES: the gap is real and reproduces — `mentl audit` on a row-polymorphic fn prints `apply : r70@e9` (src/types.mn:2024-2026 → src/types.mn:1822). Every cited line number checks out. Keep the finding; discard the framing, the mechanism, the novelty argument, the sequencing, and the gate.

1. IT IS NOT COSMETIC, AND IT IS NOT FIRST. PLAN.md:999 tags it "(cosmetic)" — that tag is a PLAN correction to make. The render sits on a line carrying a fabricated proof. Invert the order: the severance fix lands FIRST; the render is then a projection of an honest line. Landing the render first polishes the only warning a reader currently gets.

2. THE REAL ITEM, at a different file:line, with LESS code:
   `Hβ.audit.severance-reads-row-openness` — severance advice reads the EfOpen/EfPure constructor LIVE. An open row is not "severable with proven unlocks"; it is unassessable until it resolves, and adding `!Alloc` to it CONSTRAINS callers rather than unlocking a proof. The audit's job is to report what is proven; on `r70@e9` nothing is.
   `Hβ.infer.row-names-discards-openness` — `row_names` (src/infer.mn:4535-4540) must carry openness, not just names; its `_ => []` catch-all masking EfNeg/EfSub/EfInter goes too (explicit enumeration, per Law 2 — delete the surrender arm, don't wrap it).
   `Hβ.audit.severance-unlocks-dedup` — the live duplicate "Sandbox (proven no network access), Sandbox (proven no network access)" (src/pipeline.mn:720-726 concats without a set).

3. DROP THE LETTER ALLOCATOR. One occurrence per entry makes it a constant (proven by output + src/pipeline.mn:739). If the audit surface ever renders the full signature — params AND row — coreference becomes real and a letter earns its keep. Name that positively (`Hβ.audit.signature-projection-coreference`) instead of building the state machine now for a surface that has one slot.

4. IF A MARKER IS WANTED, SYNTAX.md GROWS IT FIRST. `Hβ.syntax.effect-row-open-surface` — SYNTAX.md today defines `...`/`...R` for RECORDS only (SYNTAX.md:747) and gives effect rows no open-variable form. A renderer may not mint surface syntax; amend SYNTAX.md, then render its form, capital-`R` and three dots intact (the case rule is load-bearing). Until then the honest render of an unresolved row is the fact itself, not a borrowed decoration.

5. THE REGISTER IS A HANDLER, NOT A SECOND FUNCTION. `Hβ.format.render-register-as-handler` — the teaching/internal split is a `~> Format` install read live from the handler stack (the same resolve_in_stack every op uses), exactly as PLAN §5.U already names show_effrow "a `~> Format` projection" and as src/format.mn already declares. Not `show_effrow_teaching`; not a threaded flag. SYNTAX.md dissolved the `//`-vs-`///` audience split — do not rebuild it one layer down.

6. THE GATE, REBUILT SO IT CAN FAIL. Drop `mentl why` (it does not exist — verified). Assert the property that actually matters and that FAILS today: on a fixture whose top-level fn has a genuinely open row, `mentl audit` must NOT print the string "proven" for a severance it cannot prove — that gate fails at HEAD on my fixture, right now, which is what makes it a gate. If a render check rides along, grep BOTH `r[0-9]+@e[0-9]+` and `t[0-9]+@e[0-9]+` (src/types.mn:1821-1822), or the TVar half the design claims is uncoverable by its own pattern.

7. NOVELTY, HONESTLY RESTATED. The ML-family fresh-letter convention (OCaml `'a`, GHC `a`) is the right ADOPTION only where coreference exists — that is what those printers buy: `'a -> 'a` says the same variable twice. Mentl's audit surface prints one row and no params, so the borrowed form's entire payload is absent and adopting it copies the shape without the reason. That is the novelty audit failing in the direction it usually fails: not "borrowed and rejected", but "borrowed, and the thing it was for isn't here."

REFUTED: Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin
  The release ritual becomes one script, tools/repin.sh, that computes a pin's sha256 live and writes boot/PROVENANCE.md from that computation instead of by hand — closing a staleness that exists in the repository's HEAD right now.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the artifact_state is true and well-measured (PROVENANCE.md:32 claims e2babb24…, the HEAD/disk blob is 2d4ccff8…, one pin stale since f7a1b3a5), but the design automates a re-derivation the Carried-Truth Law says to DELETE: git's DAG already holds every fact PROVENANCE caches (I reconstructed all 12 pins — sha, Reason, order — from `git log -- boot/mentl.wasm` + `git show <c>:boot/mentl.wasm|sha256sum`, matching lines 68 and 81-89 exactly), so the fix is a deletion plus a projection, not a new script plus a new gate to keep the cache coherent.
  objection: THE PIN CHAIN IS ALREADY AN EDGE SET; THE DESIGN MECHANIZES THE PARALLEL COPY INSTEAD OF DELETING IT. Measured: `for c in $(git log --format=%h -12 -- boot/mentl.wasm); do git show "$c:boot/mentl.wasm" | sha256sum; done` yields 2d4ccff8←f7a1b3a5, e2babb24←80215c38, 26bfe90a←3a53d38a, 94e449dc←82a7e420, 4b7f998f←e887bdeb, 701c7024←10999e61, cf479f9d←9611c52b, c323a40f←0da85779, 7b188a31←1255a76f, 9c8b23ba←bdec4605, 7bd9e3e7←fede0039, 289b2886←39fcffc8 — every prefix matching boot/PROVENANCE.md:68 and :81-89 hand-typed, in order, each bound to its Reason (the commit body). boot/PROVENANCE.md:76-103 is therefore drift-7 (parallel-arrays) at the docs layer: a hand-maintained copy of a projection git computes live. The design's step (4) — "move the old top entry down into the 'prior pins, one line each' list mechanically" — AUTOMATES that ledger; step (1) — "read the CURRENT top entry's sha256 LIVE ... it becomes the new entry's 'prior pin' pointer" — hand-rolls git's parent pointer as a name-keyed lookup (CLAUDE.md red-flag: "'Look up X by name' (ledger/index/map) | If an edge already connects it → re-derivation (Anchor 1); follow the edge, read the live node"). The design's own INTERROGATIONS section diagnoses this correctly ("PROVENANCE.md retyping it by hand is the exact re-derivation Law 1 forbids") and then inverts the prescription: Law 1 says "the fix is always toward LESS code"; this is +1 script, +1 gate, cache retained. THREE CONSEQUENCES. (a) The INTERCONNECTION claim is FALSE against the artifact: "a project can't select a historical pin by content hash until pins are tagged and the record is trustworthy" — I selected all twelve with zero tags (git tag -l: only first-light, rust-vm-final) against a record measurably wrong. The named dependency does not exist. (b) Step (2) "cp the new wasm into place" SCRIPTS A TWICE-PAID TRAP: tools/march.sh assembles m3.wasm only at :127 (transition) and :143 (--fixpoint); the clean-fixpoint branch :116-117 prints "✓✓ FIXED POINT holds: m2 == m3" and never assembles it, so $OUT/m3.wasm is leftover — measured live, .build/march/m3.wasm is Jul 16 20:43 vs .build/march/m3.wat Jul 17 00:13. That is exactly boot/PROVENANCE.md:64 ("assemble from the wat, NEVER cp on a clean fixed point"), which 3a53d38a's body calls "the stale-copy trap, now twice in the ledger"; f7a1b3a5's body records the correct move, "re-pinned from m3.wat". The design converts an occasional human slip into a scripted certainty. Relatedly the artifact_state is imprecise: march.sh:132 fires ONLY on the TRANSITION path — on a clean m2==m3 re-pin (real and common; PLAN §5.R's Hβ.io.scratch-dissolves-into-alloc records "a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation") march.sh prints no re-pin guidance at all. (c) TRIPLE STORE: PROVENANCE.md:19-22 is a compressed retype of 80215c38's commit body (same fd-0-filetype sentence and parenthetical); 3a53d38a's body already carries the sha ("Boot re-pinned 26bfe90a… (PROVENANCE)"); step (5)'s annotated tag makes a third. "Zero duplicate authoring" is zero duplicate TYPING — Law 1 is about STORES. GATE: non-vacuous today (correctly measured to fail, e2babb24 vs 2d4ccff8), but post-landing it guards CACHE COHERENCE, not blessing — repin.sh run on a miscompiled binary produces perfectly matching shas. The design concedes the real proof is the deep tier (re-run the march), and tools/march.sh:153 (`[ -z "$TRAP" ] && [ "$m3rc" = 0 ] && [ "$fixok" = 1 ]`) already IS that gate. Delete the cache and the sha-gate dissolves with it. WHAT I TRIED AND COULDN'T REFUTE: the novelty verdict holds — WASM+WASI as one-artifact-many-platform is a genuine substrate-layer answer to per-platform release binaries (boot/mentl.wasm is one 2.1MB file everywhere), and march.sh's fixpoint-vs-transition arbitration is strictly more precise than a human "is this breaking" judgment; I found no overclaim against rustup/nvm/semver. The staleness itself is real, reproduced exactly as described. And PROVENANCE.md:1-13 + :105-109 are genuinely irreducible — no git command yields "why m2==m3 IS the fixpoint now that boot is wheel-emitted."
  fixes    : THE SURVIVING FORM IS A DELETION PLUS A PROJECTION, NOT A SCRIPT. Rename the item; Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin names the wrong mechanism.

1. DELETE from boot/PROVENANCE.md: every `sha256` line (:32, :33, :68, :69), every "prior pin" pointer (:34-36, :70-74), and the whole "Prior pins, one line each" list (:76-103). All are computable live: `sha256sum boot/mentl.wasm` (identity), `git log -- boot/mentl.wasm` (the chain), `git show <c>:boot/mentl.wasm | sha256sum` (any historical pin). Measured: git reproduces all twelve prefixes exactly.

2. DELETE the per-pin "source:" paragraph (:17-31, :40-67). It is a compressed retype of the commit body git already binds to the blob by an edge (PROVENANCE:19-22 vs 80215c38's body). The Reason is not lost — it is read where it already lives.

3. KEEP what no git command yields: :1-13 (what boot/mentl.wasm IS; why m2==m3 is the fixed point now that boot is wheel-emitted, superseding the seed-era "NOT m2==m3" rule) and :105-109 (the seed deletion, the cold-ladder tag pointer). That header explains the artifact CLASS, not any pin. The file shrinks from 109 lines to ~20 and can never go stale, because it asserts nothing about a particular binary.

4. THE CHAIN BECOMES A PROJECTION — the native form PLAN already names. PLAN §7 says of its own ledger: "This ledger is prose until the medium projects repo state itself — state-as-PROJECTION is §7's own destiny (the docs-as-projection endpoint)." PROVENANCE.md is the same side-ledger one layer down, so the endpoint is `mentl provenance`: the cursor following git's edges to project (commit, content-hash, Reason) per pin. Positive-form peer: Hβ.provenance.chain-is-a-projection. Scaffold-tier bash NOW, named as scaffold exactly like tools/state.sh, dissolving when the verb lands — that is the one honest borrowing here (git as the transport holding the DAG), and it is strictly stronger than the design's tags because it duplicates nothing.

5. THE GATE THAT SURVIVES IS NOT SHA-MATCH. Sha-match guards a cache's coherence; repin.sh on a miscompiled binary passes it perfectly. The thing actually at risk is a boot binary no march blessed. .githooks/pre-commit ALREADY EXISTS and is live (core.hooksPath = /home/suds/Projects/mentl/.githooks; two gates fire today: drift-audit, then verify.sh on staged .mn). Add gate 3: refuse a staged boot/mentl.wasm whose commit body states no march verdict. That cannot pass vacuously and guards blessing, not bookkeeping. Name it Hβ.tools.boot-bless-refusal. Note the design never mentions this hook exists — it proposed a new enforcement point beside a live one.

6. IF ANY RE-PIN SCRIPT LANDS, IT MUST NOT `cp` m3.wasm. It assembles from the wat — `wat2wasm .build/march/m3.wat -o boot/mentl.wasm` — the move f7a1b3a5's own body records ("re-pinned from m3.wat"). Measured hazard: .build/march/m3.wasm (Jul 16 20:43) vs m3.wat (Jul 17 00:13). Independently, tools/march.sh:132's printed advice ("cp $OUT/m3.wasm boot/mentl.wasm") is itself wrong for the clean-fixpoint path and should be corrected at the source rather than copied into a new script — and the clean-fixpoint branch (:116-117) should print the re-pin move at all, which today it does not.

7. BLAST RADIUS (the design is silent; it is nil either way): no .mn source changes, so m2==m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, and the 71 micros are untouched by any of this. Say so explicitly rather than leaving it unstated.

NET: the design adds a script and a gate to keep a cache coherent. The correction deletes ~89 lines of cache, deletes the gate's reason to exist, and reads the chain live from the edges git already draws. That direction — LESS code — is Law 1's signature, and it is the difference between the diagnosis (which the design got right) and the prescription (which inverted it).

REFUTED: Hβ.f1.doc-handler-substrate
  mentl doc becomes a live projection of the comment weave and the checked signature — the SAME functions hover/audit/diagnostics already call — with code examples inside comments verified by compiling them through the ordinary frontend, closing the doc-rot failure mode structurally rather than by CI convention.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the gate is unpassable in its honest form (the fixture's `with !Alloc` at lib/tutorial/00-hello.mn:30 is rejected live as `!Alloc vs Alloc + Memory`, so a doc surface reading the checked type can never print what the gate demands), and both properties the novelty verdict claims as native replacements are ones rustdoc measurably has today. The design's substrate spine survives and is buildable after correction.
  objection: THE GATE IS UNPASSABLE IN ITS HONEST FORM, AND THE FIXTURE INVERTS THE DESIGN'S OWN THESIS.

The gate demands `mentl doc lib/tutorial/00-hello.mn` print greet's "live signature (`with !Alloc`, read from the checked type, not copied text)". Measured against the pinned boot:

  effects: E_EffectMismatch error: effect row mismatch: !Alloc vs Alloc + Memory at 30:4-30:35

`lib/tutorial/00-hello.mn:30` declares `fn greet(ref name) with !Alloc`; its body (:31-34) concats three strings, so the checker REJECTS the declaration. `mentl audit tutorial/00-hello` renders the live row as `greet : Alloc + Memory` (build_audit_report → extract_row(ty) → show_effrow, src/pipeline.mn:283-289). So: read the checked type → prints `Alloc + Memory` → gate FAILS. Print `with !Alloc` → you copied an authored annotation the compiler refuses → you violated the gate's own "not copied text" clause and committed the exact "hand-copied claim about it" the novelty verdict says Mentl eliminates. There is no third option. The design picked as its flagship a file that does not typecheck, and `00-hello.mn:22` ("`with !Alloc` proves zero allocation under inference (real-time eligible)") is LIVE DOC-ROT — which the design's own example-verification cannot catch, because that rot is prose, not a compilable fragment, while the ordinary row check already catches it today with no doc surface at all. The design's real novelty was sitting in its own gate and it read it backwards.

NOVELTY VERDICT REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, not by opinion. Claim: "a doc-example silently bit-rots unless a separate doctest runner is deliberately wired into CI." I built a stock crate and ran `cargo test --offline` with zero configuration: it compiled and RAN the doctest, failed it, and pinned it to `src/lib.rs:5:1` — the comment's own span, the exact property the design claims as its replacement. Claim: doc tools are "disconnected from the type checker's own internal model" so "a signature can drift out of sync with its documented parameters." I put a lying prose line above `pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32` and ran `cargo doc`: rustdoc rendered `pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32` from rustc's model while the false prose sat untouched beside it. rustdoc runs on rustc's front end; `@param/@returns/@throws` is javadoc/jsdoc, not Rust or Go (Rust doc comments are markdown with no param tags; godoc reads go/types). Both claimed native properties are convergence with rustdoc, not novelty — and neither tool, Mentl included under this design, closes PROSE rot.

WHAT I TRIED AND WHAT DISPROVED ME: my strongest structural objection was that the design's foundation traps. `check` and `doc` on EVERY lib module entry (prelude, runtime/io, tutorial/01-graph, tutorial/08-reasons) exit 134 with `list_index` ← `region_bucket_scan` ← `op_region_tracker_check_escape` ← `infer_fn` ← `pipeline_check`. I expected the design's walk to inherit that. It does not: `audit`/`teach` — via `analyze_fns` → `driver_check_entry` (src/pipeline.mn:256-259) — exit 0 on the same targets, including `tutorial/00-hello` (794 lines, reaching greet). The trap lives only in `pipeline_check` (check_run) and `driver_check` (doc_run). That disproves the objection AND empirically VINDICATES the design's sequencing note: "doc_run must call something other than driver_check for this content to be reachable at all" is exactly right — driver_check traps, driver_check_entry does not. I dropped the objection. Likewise the design's core substrate reading holds: map_fn_stmts IS the one walk (pipeline.mn:621-631), it DOES filter FnStmt only (:626), audit+teach ARE its two projections (:277, :280), the Stmt ADT carries TypeDefStmt/EffectDeclStmt/HandlerDeclStmt (types.mn:1047-1061), the comment weave is real and honest (graph.mn:180-197, `""` on absent), render_comment_for is formatter-only (format.mn:424-429), doc_run does reduce to a bare check naming its own gap (main.mn:332-355), and the tutorial 1:1 numbering is real.
  fixes    : 1. THE GATE — invert it; the doc page IS the refusal. Never demand doc print an authored row the checker rejects. Honest form: `mentl doc lib/tutorial/00-hello.mn` must print the PROVEN row `greet : Alloc + Memory` and must SURFACE `E_EffectMismatch: !Alloc vs Alloc + Memory at 30:4-30:35`. Separately, `lib/tutorial/00-hello.mn` does not typecheck and must be fixed (drop the false `!Alloc` or make greet non-allocating) — a curriculum file that the compiler refuses cannot be the fixture for a doc surface whose thesis is "the signature is what the compiler believes." Also fix :22's prose and :3's dead "DESIGN.md §0.5" reference (retired by the three-doc contract).

2. NOVELTY — drop the rustdoc/godoc characterization entirely; it is measurably false on both counts. Move `@param/@returns/@throws` to javadoc/jsdoc alone — that tag ledger IS a genuine hand-copied side-ledger and IS the honest target of SYNTAX.md's prohibition. Name signature-provenance and same-frontend example-checking as CONVERGENCE with rustdoc (scaffold-tier, kept because they genuinely empower). Claim the residue no doc tool on Earth can render, all three already computed by the artifact: the effect ROW (`!E` as transitive proof-of-absence — "provably does not allocate, transitively" is unsayable in rustdoc because Rust cannot prove it), the REASON chain (arm 8, walkable to root), and PROOF STATE (arm 6 — discharged obligations vs honest V_Pending debt). That is strictly stronger and it is true.

3. THE LEDE — the parser recognizes NO paragraph break. `collect_comment` (parser.mn:341-348) joins flat via `"{acc}\n{next_line}"`; the lexer emits one flat `TComment(str_slice(source, pos+2, after))` per LINE (lexer.mn:179-187); the weave stores one flat String per handle (graph.mn:188-193). "Split on the paragraph break the parser already recognizes at parse time" is false, and its only repair inside the design is a renderer scanning a flattened string for structure the lexer already saw and threw away — Carried-Truth violated plus a new scanner, both forbidden. Ultimate form: the weave carries the comment as a STRUCTURED value (a sequence of paragraphs — the L1 sequence node-kind), written at parse where the line boundaries are already in hand; the lede is then a read, not a re-derivation. Name it `Hβ.parser.comment-weave-structured`. Also: "first sentence" and "the paragraph break" are two different splits conflated in one parenthetical; they coincide on 00-hello.mn:16-17, so the gate cannot discriminate the rule it encodes.

4. ATTACH SITES — the claim is inverted at three of four cited lines. `graph_attach_comment` has exactly ONE parser call site: parser.mn:315, inside parse_stmt's TComment arm. parser.mn:72 (skip_ws) passes comments over as LAYOUT at every non-decl position; parser.mn:796 states "An op is not a top-level decl handle in the comment weave, so the comment is not attached here; it advances past"; parser.mn:1785 says "skip_ws passes over block-interior comments as layout." Two of them already name the unbuilt peer `Hβ.parser.interior-comment-to-enclosing-block`. Correct the artifact_state to cite :315 alone, and state the bound positively: doc coverage is TOP-LEVEL DECLS ONLY — an effect's page renders the effect's comment and every op's signature (the ADT carries them, types.mn:1051-1055) with NO op prose, until that peer lands. The design's central premise survives: parse_stmt's TComment arm attaches to whatever inner_stmt it returns, so the weave IS populated uniformly for Fn/Let/TypeDef/EffectDecl/HandlerDecl/Import.

5. "IDENTICAL IN EFFECT" — false. doc_run → doc_project → `driver_check` (main.mn:355); check_run → `pipeline_check` (main.mn:438-445). Different functions, different handler chains, measurably different output: `doc runtime/io` = 166 errors, `check runtime/io` = 311 errors. Say "doc_run routes through driver_check, which is neither pipeline_check nor the driver_check_entry that analyze_fns uses" — that distinction is load-bearing for item 3's dispatch fix.

6. EXAMPLE-VERIFICATION — "heuristically: contains a recognizable expression/statement shape" is a scanner guessing whether prose is code, and it misfires on the design's own fixture: 00-hello.mn:19 contains `Graph + Env` (a valid Mentl effect-row expression), :21 contains `|>` and `~>`, :22 contains `with !Alloc`. It either compiles prose and emits spurious diagnostics at the comment's span or silently skips real examples. SYNTAX.md's "a comment containing Mentl source IS Mentl source" implies a LEXER-MARKED code region, not a sniffed one; the lexer today has no region marking (lexer.mn:179-187). Name the lexer peer and build the region, or drop the leg — do not ship the sniff.

7. DISCOVERED PEER, name it positively: `check` and `doc` trap on every lib module entry — exit 134, `list_index` ← `region_bucket_scan` ← `op_region_tracker_check_escape` ← `infer_fn` ← `pipeline_check`, reproduced on prelude / runtime/io / tutorial/01-graph / tutorial/08-reasons. `driver_check_entry` is unaffected. This is a live blocker for PLAN §11 column 2's "no known silent-wrong class" bar (loud, not silent, but it refuses the check verb on the whole stdlib), and `region_bucket_scan` is itself a scan — a §5.O Carried-Truth site. It is not this item's work, but the doc/test/new cluster cannot claim green while it stands.
