REFUTED: Hβ.cli.cursor-address-endpoint
  mentl <address> is a third, distinct verb form — not a replacement for check/query/teach — that resolves a file position, a qualified name, or (once persisted) a raw handle to a live node and projects the SAME eight-aspect CursorView mentl edit already computes, in one shot, no session required.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design misreads which engine `cursor_at` uses: it cites `find_tightest` (span_contains, honest refusal) but `cursor_at`'s only arm calls `handle_at_span`/`scan_for_span` (four-field EXACT span equality, fabricates handle 0 on miss), so the gate's own example `mentl src/infer.mn:2687` would silently project the graph's first node; and the address's `<target>` half has no edge to resolve against at all, because Span carries no path, span_index carries no module key, and the graph's own module→handle overlay op `graph_fork` has ZERO callers.
  objection: FIVE independent refutations; any one is fatal. What I could NOT refute is listed last.

**1. FACTUAL (fatal) — the wrong engine.** The design's enabling claim is "the eight-aspect projection this item needs already exists and is correct" and "position-to-handle resolution over a Span already works and is proven: `find_tightest` (query.mn:577-592) — exactly the mechanism QTypeAt already uses". The artifact says otherwise. `cursor_at`'s ONLY arm (cursor.mn:101-102) opens `let handle = handle_at_span(span)` → cursor.mn:525-532 → `scan_for_span(span, 0, next)` → cursor.mn:534-543, which matches on `span_eq` — FOUR-FIELD EXACT equality (types.mn:718-722) — and returns **0** on exhaustion (cursor.mn:535). `find_tightest` uses `span_contains` (types.mn:752-757) and is never on `cursor_at`'s path. A user-typed `:2687` is not a graph-minted span, so it exact-matches nothing → handle 0 → `graph_chase(0)` is a real node (`next = 0` at graph_handler init, graph.mn:58; `Handle = Int where 0 <= self`) → the eight aspects render **for the first node in the graph**. That is a silent-wrong answer from the medium's own teaching surface — the exact class PLAN §11 column 2 forbids ("no known silent-wrong class"). The existing callers are safe only because they round-trip a graph-minted span: `cursor_at(span_of_cursor(cursor))` where `span_of_cursor` = `span_of_handle(handle)` (cursor_transport.mn:251, 309-311). The design reuses `cursor_at` outside its validity domain.

**2. FACTUAL (fatal) — `<target>` is decorative; nothing can resolve it.** `type Span = Span(Int, Int, Int, Int)` (types.mn:709-711) — no path. `span_index: [(ValidSpan, Int)]` (types.mn:503) — no module key. And `driver_check_entry` (driver.mn:229) parses the entry PLUS every dep — `driver_collect_dag_with_deps |> … |> each((layer) => each(driver_check_module, layer))` → `driver_check_module` (driver.mn:144-156) does `fs_read_file(path) |> lex |> parse_program |> infer_program` into the SAME graph, each mint calling `graph_index_span` (parser.mn:19-22). So one span_index holds every module's spans and `src/infer.mn:2687` is indistinguishable from `lib/prelude.mn:2687`. The design's enabling fact — "spans are PER-FILE HONEST on the check path" — is necessary but NOT sufficient; it conflates "line 1 is that file's line 1" (true) with "I can find THIS file's line 2687" (false). This also breaks the round-trip promise on the check path, not just compile: `show_span` prints `sl:sc-el:ec` with no path (types.mn:2128-2131) and diagnostics_handler prints `… at {show_span(diag_span(diag))}` (pipeline.mn:582), so a pasted location cannot name its own target on ANY path. The design blames `driver_compile_entry`'s concatenation; the real missing fact is span→file identity, absent everywhere.

**3. DRIFT (Carried-Truth + dormant substrate) — the graph HAS the slot, inert, and the design walks past it.** `Graph`'s 4th field is `overlays: [(module_name, [handle])]` (types.mn:502), exposed by `graph_snapshot` (graph.mn:106-108); every fresh allocation "registers in the CURRENT overlay" in O(1) (graph.mn:123-163); and `graph_fork(String) -> ()` (types.mn:1247, arm graph.mn:318-340) is a complete, correct overlay-entry op — "Enter the named module's overlay… Subsequent fresh allocations register into the current overlay in O(1)". **`graph_fork` has ZERO callers** (`grep -rn graph_fork src/ lib/` outside graph.mn/types.mn returns nothing), so every handle lands in overlay 0 = "global". The module→handle edge is BUILT, EXPOSED, and INERT — and it is precisely the `<target>` half's slot (protocol_audit_dormant_first: "defined but uncalled IS the slot for what you're about to build"). The design never mentions it, nor the artifact's own named peers `Hβ.graph.handle-at-span-effect-op` (cursor_transport.mn:314-317) and `Hμ.cursor.cache` (cursor.mn:528).

**4. DRIFT (Law 2) — it builds ON a surrender-fallback instead of deleting it.** `scan_for_span`'s `if i >= n { 0 }` (cursor.mn:535) is a fabricated handle — the canonical surrender-fallback CLAUDE.md's red-flag table says to DELETE, not wrap. The honest sibling exists and the design cites it without noticing the contrast: QTypeAt refuses — `None => resume(QRError("no enclosing span found in AST index"))` (query.mn:94). The design wires the address onto the dishonest engine. `scan_for_span` is also O(n) with a `graph_chase(i)` per node, re-deriving what `span_index` already connects (§5.O: a scan re-derives what an edge connects); cursor.mn:526-528's own comment concedes it — "Today this is a forward scan via graph_snapshot; the indexed lookup is peer-handle territory (Hμ.cursor.cache)" — while the design calls that same range "already exists and is correct".

**5. GATE (fatal, twice) — vacuous, and built on a stale line.** (a) `fn instantiate` is at **infer.mn:3063**, not near 2687; line 2687 sits inside an `each((pair) => …)` minting `VarLookup` nodes — a different function 376 lines away. The gate's core assertion ("when the line address falls inside `instantiate`'s own body, report the SAME enclosing function name") is unsatisfiable as written, and the hedge "(or an equivalent real decl name confirmed to exist near that line)" admits it was never confirmed. The number was lifted from **PLAN.md:689's own stale citation** — "`instantiate` → `subst_ty` tree-clone (infer.mn:2687)" — which the perf loop (§7) invalidated when it rewrote infer.mn. The design cites prose, not code. (b) Worse, the gate asserts only "BOTH exit 0 and print a non-empty projection" — handle 0 chases to a real node and prints a non-empty projection, so **the gate PASSES on the fabrication**. It cannot fail for the reason it exists.

**6. NOVELTY overclaim.** The headline claim — "there is no second address-resolution engine to keep in sync with the compiler's own truth, because there is only the one graph" — is the one claim the artifact directly refutes. There are already TWO engines and they disagree: `find_tightest` (span_contains / tightest-enclosing / honest `None`, query.mn:577-592) and `scan_for_span` (span_eq / exact / fabricates 0, cursor.mn:534-543). Mentl today HAS the divergence the design says it is structurally immune to, and the design adds a third caller onto the weaker one. Structurally forced: the ONLY `CursorView` producer is cursor.mn:118, inside `cursor_at(Span)`'s arm; `cursor_pinned(Handle) -> Cursor` (cursor.mn:68) returns a `Cursor`, not the eight aspects. So there is NO door to CursorView from a handle — any handle resolved by `find_tightest` must be thrown away and re-derived by the exact-match scan. That is Carried-Truth violated by the op signature itself.

**WHAT I TRIED AND FAILED TO REFUTE (credit where the design is right):** I ran both address forms through the pinned boot — `wt_run boot/mentl.wasm src/infer.mn:2687` and `… infer.instantiate` both print "mentl: unrecognized or under-specified command", so main.mn:137-138 is exactly as claimed. I grepped for an offset table and found none (`source_offset|line_offset|file_offset` in driver.mn → no matches), confirming `driver_compile_entry` (driver.mn:209-227) concatenates via `fold("", (acc, m) => "{acc}{fs_read_file(p)}\n")` into ONE `parse_program(lex(source))` with no per-file offset recorded. `ide/index.html:668` (`rtLines = RT.split("\n").length + 1`) and 706-709 are exactly the two-piece hand-coded subtraction described, valid only because index.html:667 concatenates `RT + "\n" + user`. `show_span` genuinely has no path (types.mn:2128-2131); `CursorView` is the 8-field record at types.mn:704-706; QTypeAt genuinely uses find_tightest (query.mn:86-106); `defining_span_of` genuinely walks `Located(sp,_)` (query.mn:542-553). The novelty verdict's KEEP decision — retaining gdb/LSP `file:line` grammar rather than inventing a Mentl-specific address syntax — is honest and correct, and survives intact. The design's factual failure is not in what it read; it is in the one hop it did not take: from `cursor_at`'s signature into its arm body.
  fixes    : The item's GOAL (a one-shot cursor-address projection) is sound. Its mechanism is not. What the design must say instead:

**1. Carry the handle — delete both scanners, don't wire a third caller onto them.** `handle_at_span` + `scan_for_span` (cursor.mn:525-543) must be DELETED: they re-derive by O(n) `graph_chase`-per-node what `span_index` already connects, and they fabricate handle 0 (cursor.mn:535). Resolve span→handle ONCE at the edge via `find_tightest` over `span_index` — the engine with the honest `None` (query.mn:94's `QRError` is the precedent) — and carry the HANDLE inward. This requires the door that does not exist: the sole CursorView producer is cursor.mn:118 inside `cursor_at(Span)`, and `cursor_pinned(Handle)` returns a `Cursor`, not the eight aspects. Name it `Hβ.cursor.view-from-handle` — the op becomes handle-keyed, span resolution happens once at the CLI edge, and the two disagreeing engines collapse to one. The fix is LESS code (both scanners die), which is the tell that it is the right one.

**2. Activate the dormant module edge — it IS the `<target>` half.** `graph_fork(module_name)` (types.mn:1247, arm graph.mn:318-340) is built, correct, exposed through `graph_snapshot`'s `overlays` (graph.mn:106-108, types.mn:502) and has ZERO callers. Call it at `driver_check_module`'s per-module parse (driver.mn:144-156) and at `driver_check_entry`'s entry parse (driver.mn:229-240), so each module's handles register into its own overlay. Then `<target>` resolves target→overlay→handles and `<position>` resolves within that overlay. Name it `Hβ.graph.overlay-fork-at-module-parse`. Without it the design's `<target>` is decorative and two files' line 2687 are the same address.

**3. Drop the "no second engine" novelty claim; state the truth.** Mentl currently HAS two disagreeing resolution engines (find_tightest vs scan_for_span). The honest novelty claim is the one the corrections earn: after (1) and (2), address resolution IS the graph's own span_index + overlay reads with no side index — which is genuinely stronger than gdb's/LSP's maintained symbol table. Today that claim is aspirational, not artifact-true. Keep the gdb/LSP `file:line` grammar KEEP-verdict; it survives.

**4. The round-trip promise needs the span to name its file — on every path.** The design's carve-out (blaming only `driver_compile_entry`'s concatenation, deferring to `Hβ.driver.per-module-env-overlay`) misdiagnoses. `show_span` (types.mn:2128-2131) and diagnostics_handler (pipeline.mn:582) print no path on ANY path, so a pasted location cannot name its target even on check. Either `show_span` projects the overlay the handle belongs to (once (2) lands, the graph knows it — read it live, never re-derive), or the promise is withdrawn. Name it `Hβ.diag.span-names-its-module`.

**5. Rewrite the gate so it can FAIL.** Three assertions, all currently absent: (a) an address resolving to no node must exit NON-ZERO with a diagnostic — assert that `mentl <target>:999999` REFUSES; today it would print handle 0's projection and exit 0, and the gate as written would call that a pass; (b) use a VERIFIED line — `fn instantiate` is **infer.mn:3063**, not 2687; (c) assert cross-file discrimination — the same line number in two modules of one DAG must project two different nodes. Without (a) and (c) the gate passes vacuously on the fabrication and on the collision, which is precisely the state the item exists to fix.

**6. Fix the stale citation the design inherited.** PLAN.md:689 says "`instantiate` → `subst_ty` tree-clone (infer.mn:2687)"; `fn instantiate` is at infer.mn:3063. The perf loop moved it. Correct PLAN, or the next reader repeats this exact error.

**7. Correct the sequencing claim.** "No dependency on any other item in this cluster — buildable immediately" is false. It depends on (1) the handle-door to CursorView and (2) `graph_fork` activation. It is not the cheapest item in the cluster; it is the one that surfaces a latent silent-wrong (`AccOverride(span)`/`AccCaretMove(span)` at cursor_transport.mn:262-267 feed user-caret spans into the same exact-match scan) as the headline CLI verb. Sequence it AFTER the two corrections above, and note that fixing (1) fixes those IDE paths for free — which is the interconnection the design should have claimed.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.diag.reachability-scoped-report
  diagnostics_handler.report prints every diagnostic unconditionally the instant it fires, regardless of the surrounding declaration's reachability or the diagnostic's own format-liftability — so mentl new's own scaffolded hello-world program prints 668 stderr lines about code it never calls before printing its correct output.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the measurement is exact and the problem is real, but the diagnosis is wrong (71% of the 668 is one manifest bug, not reachability noise), the mechanism is unbuildable (report(DiagKind) carries no decl handle; 236/668 carry no span), and the filter would hide two real bugs rather than delete them. The peer dissolves into fixes that already have homes.
  objection: The design's own numbers refute its diagnosis. It frames 668 stderr lines as noise about code hello.mn never calls. I ran its own `import prelude` variant: E_MissingVariable 241 -> 0 and E_OccursCheck 236 -> 0 (my 91-error count matches the design's stated 91 exactly). 477/668 = 71% are NOT reachability noise — they are ONE real bug. src/types.mn uses len/alloc/list_index/store_i32 (defined lib/runtime/lists.mn:38, :90) at exactly the reported spans 181:51-181:54, 182:50, 183:50, and declares ZERO imports (grep -c '^import' src/types.mn = 0), free-riding on the wheel's one-namespace concatenation (PLAN §7 driver_compile_entry). Census: types.mn is the ONLY src module with an empty manifest while using runtime vocabulary (24 uses, one file). lib/prelude.mn:13-16 already declares its manifest — the landed precedent types.mn violates. PLAN §11's own law is 'the imports ARE the manifest'; the design proposes to FILTER the diagnostic that reports the manifest is a lie. That is Law 2 verbatim: a patch where a restructure is required.

The other 236 are provably FALSE: 236/236 are self-loops ('7077 occurs in t7077', verified by regex over all 236), all at span 0:0-0:0, all raised at graph.mn:273. graph_bind_row at graph.mn:291-299 already states the law: 'Union-find never unions a root with itself; the row sort is no different... NOT reported: unlike a type occurs (a real infinite type), a row self-loop is benign set-idempotence.' The ROW sort got the guard; the TYPE sort at graph.mn:271-274 did not. unify (infer.mn:1757-1767) has the root-equality guard `if ra == rb { () }`; the direct graph_bind(handle, TVar(other)) sites (infer.mn:681, 689, 736) bypass it. Filtering a false diagnostic by reachability WRAPS it — Law 2 forbids exactly this.

MECHANISM UNBUILDABLE: `report(DiagKind) -> ()` (types.mn:1346) takes ONE argument; every DiagKind constructor carries only a Span (types.mn:1284-1339). The handler cannot buffer an 'originating-decl-handle' it never receives. So 'Two additive fixes to the one report arm (pipeline.mn:576)' is false, and 'Depends on nothing new — reachable_from_main and diag_severity already exist as the facts to read' is false: the decl handle is NOT an available fact. Obtaining it needs a span->decl scan (re-derivation, O(n·d)) or an ambient current-decl threaded through 77 report sites (side-ledger) — the exact drift the design claims to cure. Worse: 236/668 (35%) carry NO span (0:0-0:0), so the filter cannot classify a third of its own input; the design's gate `wc -l == 0` is unreachable by this mechanism.

PRECEDENT INVERTED: executable_gate (pipeline.mn:119-140) WALKS the already-pruned tree and MINTS diagnostics from it (collect_value_holes_decls(lowered); report(EUnresolvedHole(h, parse_span_of(h)))) — a projection, reading the span from the tree it holds. The design buffers diagnostics minted during infer and attributes them BACKWARD to decls. Opposite direction. Not a generalization of the precedent.

STRING-KEYED: reachable_from_main (lower.mn:5509-5539) is a NAME-STRING-keyed membership set over LOWERED decls (reach_decl_name(d) == "main"; reach_has(rbuf, rn, n)). Matching infer-time diagnostics into it is drift-8 / string-keyed, contradicting PLAN §5.O 'a name is a HANDLE'. And a decl that fails inference may not lower at all -> in no lowered set -> unclassifiable by construction.

MISSED CARRIED-TRUTH BUG IN THE ARM IT TOUCHES: graph_bind(handle, ty, reason) receives Located(span, _) (types.mn:607, 'site-annotated reasoning edge') carrying the PROVEN span, and graph.mn:273 fabricates span_zero() (types.mn:733 = Span(0,0,0,0)). The span is proven and discarded — the actual Law 1 violation present, which the design walks past while claiming the handler 'reads none of diag's already-available facts'.

FALSE INDEPENDENCE: the design asserts it is 'Independent of Hβ.diag.refusal-law-per-class'. After the root fixes, the entire 73-line residue (E_OwnershipViolation 30, T_OverDeclared 20, E_TypeMismatch 11, E_EffectMismatch 6, E_PurityViolated 4, P_UnexpectedToken 2) IS the refusal-law census. The gate is a JOINT gate with that peer, not an independent one.

WHAT I TRIED AND COULDN'T REFUTE: the artifact_state measurements. Reproduced from a clean temp dir through the installed shim: 668 lines, exit 0, correct stdout, all nine code counts exact (241/236/118/30/20/11/6/4/2); 528/140 confirmed (E_RedundantBraces is severity=Warning). Every cited line is real: pipeline.mn:143 chain, pipeline.mn:576-585 arm, main.mn:640-662 scaffold, types.mn 2,161 lines / zero imports, diag_severity types.mn:1507, io.mn:35, strings.mn:49, executable_gate between reachability and emit. The problem is real; the design is not.
  fixes    : The peer DISSOLVES. Delete Hβ.diag.reachability-scoped-report — no reachability-scoped filter should be built. The 668 has three roots, each with an existing home, and the fix is LESS code:

(1) Hβ.types.manifest-is-honest (NEW — the true peer this audit surfaced). src/types.mn declares its imports (`import runtime/lists`, `import runtime/memory`), exactly as lib/prelude.mn:13-16 already does. MEASURED: kills 477/668 = 71% (241 E_MissingVariable + the 236 E_OccursCheck cascade) — verified by the design's own `import prelude` run reaching 0/0 for both classes. Kills them at EVERY reachability, not only when pruned, which is the difference between a fix and a filter. This IS PLAN §11's 'the imports ARE the manifest' applied to the medium's own source: the wheel's one-namespace concatenation hides the lie from the self-compile; the user's real DAG surfaces it. Census is complete: types.mn is the ONLY src module with an empty manifest while using runtime vocabulary. Blast radius named honestly: this changes a src manifest, not the emit — per the io.mn precedent (PLAN §5.R band D: 'a lib-source-only change holds m2 == m3 in ONE generation'), expect fixpoint-stable, but it must be marched (tools/march.sh arbitrates TRANSITION vs BROKEN); the design's own gate section never mentions the march.

(2) The self-loop guard in graph_bind (graph.mn:271-274) mirrors graph_bind_row's law verbatim (graph.mn:291-299: 'Union-find never unions a root with itself; the row sort is no different'). graph_bind(h, TVar(h)) is a NO-OP, not an infinite type. The row sort has the guard; the type sort does not. This is Law 2 (delete the false diagnostic at its root, never wrap it) and Law 1 (the fix is LESS code). Belt-and-braces after (1), since (1) is what exposed the cascade — but it stands on its own: 236/236 self-loops is a false diagnostic class regardless of who triggers it.

(3) Read the span live at graph.mn:273. `graph_bind(handle, ty, reason)` receives Located(span, _) (types.mn:607) carrying the proven span; the raise fabricates span_zero() (types.mn:733). Read the Located span from the reason. This is the ACTUAL Carried-Truth violation in the code the design is touching, and it is the reason the design's own mechanism cannot work (no span -> no decl). Fix this and every diagnostic carries a real coordinate — which is what SYNTAX.md's governing principle 5 already requires ('Diagnostics carry coordinates and Quick Fixes').

(4) The format-liftable half SURVIVES, standalone — but it needs NO buffering and NO reachability. It is a pure diag_applicability(diag) read at the existing arm (types.mn:1534 already projects it; ERedundantBraces is in SYNTAX.md's own format-liftable band, and SYNTAX's words are 'the redundant input never survives parse... the user sees no diagnostic'). Covers 118/668 = 18%. Keep it; it is the only part of the design both implementable and correct as written. Name the deeper form as a positive-form peer rather than hiding it: Hβ.fmt.wheel-source-is-canonical — 118 E_RedundantBraces in the wheel's own src is the medium failing to run its own formatter on itself, and silencing is the display half of that truth, not the whole of it.

(5) The 73-line residue (E_OwnershipViolation 30, T_OverDeclared 20, E_TypeMismatch 11, E_EffectMismatch 6, E_PurityViolated 4, P_UnexpectedToken 2) is TRUE self-diagnosis about the wheel's own source and belongs to Hβ.diag.refusal-law-per-class's existing ratchet. The design's claim of independence from that peer is refuted: after (1)-(4) the residue IS that census. Record the gate as JOINT.

GATE CORRECTION: the design's gate (`wc -l < err.txt` == 0 from a fresh clone) is the RIGHT gate and MEASURED to fail today at 668 — keep it verbatim, it is not vacuous. But it cannot be met by the design's mechanism (236 unclassifiable), and it is met by (1)+(2)+(3)+(4)+(5) for the right reason: the diagnostics stop firing because the facts stop being false or unresolved, not because a filter hides them. Add the leg the design omits: `bash tools/march.sh` must rule m2 == m3 (or TRANSITION with an honest re-pin) after (1), and tools/state.sh must stay green (frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros) — the design asserts no blast radius at all, which for a change to src/types.mn (the module every other module imports) is the one claim it should have made and didn't.

NOVELTY VERDICT CORRECTION: the design's rejection of rustc --cap-lints is right in conclusion but wrong in reasoning — it congratulates itself for avoiding a flag while proposing the same thing without one. A reachability filter IS suppression; reading a graph fact rather than a config flag makes it better-implemented suppression, not a different kind of thing. The genuinely native form is that Mentl has no lints to cap: a diagnostic is either TRUE (fix the source — (1), (5)), FALSE (delete it at the root — (2)), or a display-tier projection of an already-canonical graph ((4)). 'Which of my true diagnoses do I hide from you' is a question the medium should never need to answer, and the design's whole mechanism exists to answer it.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.felt.mentl-edit-runtime
  mentl edit becomes a live session — proven candidates commit into the graph instead of always rolling back and being re-derived from text, so the module is never re-parsed mid-session, cursor_step becomes an honest recursive loop instead of the currently-inert <~/accumulate wrapper, and the two hole classifiers collapse into one by deletion, exactly as PLAN's own S11 audit ruled.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's causal story is contradicted by the artifact: the three-generation weave comes from a single driver_check triple-parsing (driver.mn:95-96, 261-263, 150-153), not from driver_check-on-accept, so removing the accept-path calls leaves main.mn:289's session-start driver_check minting all three generations before cursor_step runs, and step (4)'s deletion of authored_hole's generation filter is unsound.
  objection: One driver_check(m) parses m THREE times into one monotonic never-freed graph (the whole call runs inside edit_run's `~> graph_handler`, main.mn:283): (1) driver_collect_dag_with_deps → driver_collect_dag → driver_collect_visit reads the source and calls driver_extract_imports (driver.mn:95-96), which is `lex |> parse_program` (driver.mn:121-126); (2) driver_collect_dag_with_deps maps driver_module_in_dag_deps over the same modules, re-reading and re-calling driver_extract_imports (driver.mn:261-263); (3) driver_check_module does `fs_read_file(path) |> lex |> parse_program |> infer_program` (driver.mn:150-153). That is exactly the measured "three nodes, ids 17/41/65, one span" at cursor.mn:253 — gaps of 24, one small module minted three times, all coexisting in `nodes`.

This refutes the design's attribution of the multiplicity to "driver_check(target_module) on every ACCEPT" (cursor_transport.mn:468, 477). mentl_edit_session's own driver_check(entry_module) (main.mn:289) — which the design explicitly KEEPS, removing only the accept-path calls — produces all three generations before cursor_session is called. Therefore step (4) ("authored_hole and executable_hole are now the identical predicate with nothing left to differ on; delete authored_hole ... pure deletion, no new filter") is unsound: enumerate_hole_positions (cursor.mn:268-272) filters `authored_hole` over `range(0, next)` — every handle, stale generations included — and the caret test is what drops generations 1 and 2, because driver_extract_imports runs `lex |> parse_program` with NO infer_program, so those nodes carry no unification class root and caret_span_from_reason (cursor.mn:548-551) collapses to span_zero. That is verbatim the mechanism cursor.mn:255-257 describes. Repoint to parse_span_of and one `??` yields three cursor positions — all three carry the span, as the measurement itself states ("three nodes... one span"). Hβ.cursor.session-weave-epoch-scope does not dissolve; PLAN §11's ruling that it does rests on the same misdiagnosis ("the edit transport re-parsing per projection") and is itself refuted by the artifact — the re-parse is per driver_check, three-fold, from the DAG collector.

Corroborating: the two predicates were never generation-twins. authored_hole reads caret_span_of_handle (the union-find class root's reason, cursor.mn:548-551); executable_hole reads parse_span_of (a linear scan of span_index, query.mn:569-575). lower.mn:5437-5439 states the compile tree "is a SINGLE parse generation, so the session-weave multiplicity ... cannot occur here" and STILL cannot use the caret test, because "a body/let-init hole unifies with its binder's fresh var, the class root becomes that unlocated mint, and the reason-flavored caret collapses to span_zero (the miss that let `fn main() = ??` slip the gate)". The caret test fails within a single generation; no re-parse removal unifies them.

I tried to rescue the design by checking whether the accept path was the only re-parse source (grep of every driver_check call site: cursor_transport.mn:468/477, pipeline.mn:165, voice.mn:1655, main.mn:289/354) and whether driver_extract_imports was a text scan rather than a parse — driver.mn:121-126 shows it is `lex |> parse_program`, and driver_collect_visit (driver.mn:95-96) plus driver_module_in_dag_deps (driver.mn:261-263) each call it on the same source, which driver_check_module then parses a third time. The count is exactly three and matches the measurement, so the rescue fails.

What the artifact CONFIRMS: graph_handler has no commit op — graph_push_checkpoint (graph.mn:115-118) pushes trail_len, graph_rollback (graph.mn:365-376) reverts and pops, and both consumers unconditionally roll back before the filter reads the verdict (mentl.mn:247-251; synth_proposer.mn:152-154, 547-549) — a real Carried-Truth violation worth fixing. The <~ diagnosis is exact: wasm.mn:3462-3477 loads $s<h>, emits the body once, tees, stores, reloads — no loop; emit_state_globals (wasm.mn:787-793) declares one `(global $s<n> (mut i32) (i32.const 0))` per site; LF.M is a bare code comment (wasm.mn:785, 3456) never promoted to a PLAN peer. The seven <~ accumulate sites are real (oracle.mn:439, cursor.mn:185, cursor_transport.mn:239, eight_loop.mn:245, format.mn:282, mentl.mn:746, pipeline.mn:367).
  fixes    : 1. INVERT THE SEQUENCING — fix the root before deleting the filter. The Carried-Truth violation the design walked past is driver_check itself: it re-derives the import DAG by re-parsing source the graph already holds as ImportStmt nodes after the first parse. driver_extract_imports is called twice on identical source (driver.mn:95-96 and 261-263) and parse_program runs a third time in driver_check_module (driver.mn:150-153). Apply PLAN §11's own novelty ruling ("the imports ARE the manifest; the kernel already holds the dep DAG as edges") to the driver: parse ONCE, read ImportStmt from the graph (driver_extract_imports_from_ast, driver.mn:128-136, already projects them from an AST — feed it the live nodes, not a fresh parse). Then there is ONE generation, and only then do the classifiers re-unify and the deletion become safe. Name this as the load-bearing peer this item depends on; it is not optional and it is not "layer 1".

2. STEP (4) MUST NOT SHIP AS "PURE DELETION". The design's claim "the identical predicate with nothing left to differ on" is false: caret_span_of_handle (cursor.mn:548-551) chases the union-find class root's reason; parse_span_of (query.mn:569-575) scans span_index. One must be REWRITTEN into the other, and the rewrite changes cursor behavior — `fn f() = ??` becomes a cursor position where it is not one today (cursor.mn:257-259 names this as the caret test's known cost). State that behavior change explicitly as the fix it is, and gate it.

3. DO NOT PROMOTE parse_span_of AS-IS — it is an O(n²) regression on the felt path. It calls graph_snapshot() then a linear `find` over span_index per handle, and enumerate_hole_positions (cursor.mn:268-272) calls the classifier for every handle in range(0, next). Today's caret_span_of_handle is a flat-array graph_chase. This violates PLAN §5.O ("O(1) is the only acceptable complexity"; "every scanner in the compiler is a place we forgot the graph already knew"). The O(1) form is a handle-keyed span read as a GraphRead op — graph_span_at(handle) — the sibling of the already-named Hβ.graph.handle-at-span-effect-op (cursor_transport.mn:315-317, the reverse direction). Name it (e.g. Hβ.graph.span-at-handle-o1) and land the classifier ON it, never on the scan. The design's claim of independence from §5.O is false in both directions.

4. FIX THE GATE — both legs are vacuous exactly where the risk is. "graph_next() grows by O(candidate-size) after ACCEPT" and "hole 2's handle unchanged after accepting hole 1" both PASS once the accept-path driver_check is removed, while the session-start triple-parse and the unsound deletion go uncaught. Nothing proposed would fail on step (4). Add the gate that actually bites: a crucible containing exactly one `fn f() = ??` asserting enumerate_hole_positions() returns exactly ONE handle. It fails today (returns 0 — the caret test misses the unlocated class root) and fails under the design as written (returns 3 — three generations, each with the span). Additionally assert graph_next() after mentl_edit_session's session-start driver_check is ~1× module-size, not 3× — that is the leg that catches the real root.

5. RESOLVE graph_commit_checkpoint's signature gap. graph_mutated(Int, Mutation) (types.mn:1650-1652) demands a Mutation value; the design writes "perform graph_mutated(epoch, ...)" and never says which Mutation N committed writes collapse to. Note also the asymmetry with graph_fresh_ty's rule (graph.mn:134): that arm emits one graph_mutated PER WRITE when the stack is empty at write time; a commit emits ONE for N writes. Say what the committed Mutation is, or extend the ADT.

6. KEEP steps (1), (2), (3), (5) — they are sound and artifact-confirmed. graph_commit_checkpoint is a genuine Carried-Truth fix (both consumers compute the verdict and unconditionally discard the graph state that proved it: mentl.mn:247-251, synth_proposer.mn:547-549). Removing driver_check from the accept arms (cursor_transport.mn:468, 477) is correct. The <~ diagnosis and the plain-tail-recursion replacement for cursor_step are correct and verified — wasm.mn:3462-3477 emits no loop.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.install.pin-selection
  The pin IS the release answers the two failure modes lockfiles exist to prevent elsewhere — a silently-broken new release, an unreviewed dependency bump — both structurally impossible here; it does not yet answer the one residual case that survives scrutiny: a real regression slips the gate and a project needs to keep building against a specific known-good prior pin without a full extra clone.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the mechanism selects ONE FILE and calls it a pin, while the project's own `wt_state_key` (tools/wt-env.sh:96-104) defines the verdict-determining state as hash(src ∪ lib ∪ boot) and src/driver.mn:58 makes lib/ a live disk read from the CURRENT tree at user-compile time; the design also misreads today's granularity (install.sh's shim is a live pointer, so pulling — not re-running install.sh — changes which bytes run), and re-derives git's object store, which already holds every prior boot with no extra clone.
  objection: The design's own residual case is "keep building against a specific KNOWN-GOOD prior pin," and its mechanism cannot deliver one. `MENTL_PIN=<sha>` substitutes only `~/.mentl/pins/<sha>.wasm` for `$MENTL_HOME/boot/mentl.wasm` (install.sh:32), leaving `--dir "$MENTL_HOME::/mentl-home"` pointed at the CURRENT checkout. src/driver.mn:45-66 resolves every user import through `/mentl-home/lib/{name}.mn` (:58) — read from disk as SOURCE at compile time, which is why PROVENANCE's own measured note says a user program's one `import prelude` pulls the whole runtime through the dep walk. So the selector constructs (old boot, current lib) — a pairing no march ever confirmed. The project already computed what a pin must contain: tools/wt-env.sh:96-104 hashes `wt_wheel src lib` together with `cat boot/mentl.wasm` into one key, commented "include every file whose change can change the verdict." By the artifact's own accounting, boot alone is not the pin. "The pin IS the release" means the release is the TREE at a commit; the design stores a blob and calls it a release.

WHAT DISPROVED MY SHARPER FORM: I predicted the hybrid would BREAK, citing boot/PROVENANCE.md:28 ("boot cannot lower an import it predates" — the reason the welcome arc needed two rungs). I extracted three real chain pins from git and ran them against the current tree. All four compiled the same program from a project dir, exit 0, module emitted: current 2d4ccff8, e2babb24, 94e449dc (8 commits back), c323a40f (15 commits back). The hybrid RUNS. My "it crashes" thesis is dead. But the measurement sharpened the objection instead of removing it: the pins DIVERGE while succeeding — current and e2babb24 emit `E_OwnershipViolation` at prelude 291:24; 94e449dc and c323a40f emit nothing. The unattested world runs and reports different truths quietly. Known-good only by coincidence, and PROVENANCE:28 is the standing proof the coincidence expires.

THREE FACTUAL FAILURES (attack order 1):
1. The ARTIFACT STATE's granularity claim is FALSE. "Never re-running install.sh from a git pull-ed checkout keeps the old shim pointed at old bytes" inverts the mechanism. install.sh:18 derives MENTL_HOME from the checkout; :32 runs `$MENTL_HOME/boot/mentl.wasm`; :57 prints "(live pointer)"; the header says every re-pin is "instantly the global CLI with zero sync." Pulling REPLACES those bytes in place and the never-re-run shim runs the NEW compiler. Re-running install.sh is irrelevant to which bytes execute. Today's granularity is the checkout's git state — the exact pointer that names the native form, missed.
2. The verification substrate is broken RIGHT NOW. PROVENANCE.md's head claims `boot/mentl.wasm sha256 e2babb24…`; the actual boot at HEAD is `2d4ccff89e62f953…`. Commit f7a1b3a5 re-pinned without updating the record (boot/ is clean in the worktree — this is committed state). The design proposes discharging `sha256(self) == declared_sha` against a ledger that is currently wrong.
3. `declared_sha` does not exist for the pins this design targets. PROVENANCE carries full sha256 on 4 lines (2 pins × wasm+wat); the other 23 prior pins are 8-hex-char prefixes (`94e449dc…`). A 32-bit abbreviation is not a cryptographic commitment. The predicate cannot be discharged for exactly the historical pins the selector exists to reach.

DEPENDENCY: `Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin` does not exist anywhere in the artifact (`grep -rn` → empty; the only `Hβ.tools.*` peer is `Hβ.tools.march-transition-native`, PLAN.md:844). As a sibling cluster proposal that is legitimate sequencing, but the design states its discipline in the present tense ("the sha256 discipline Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin MAKES mechanical") and the ARTIFACT STATE section does not disclose that its root dependency is unbuilt. Verified-true claims, credited: install.sh bakes one MENTL_HOME into one shim path and overwrites on re-run (:18, :27, and the installed ~/.local/bin/mentl reads `MENTL_HOME="/home/suds/Projects/mentl"`); parse_cli_args (src/main.mn:105-141) has no pin verb; `Hβ.persist.module-image-cache` is at PLAN.md:866 in band O and does answer a different question — the rule-out is correct.

DRIFT: `~/.mentl/pins/<sha256>.wasm` re-derives git's object store. Measured: `git cat-file blob HEAD~3:boot/mentl.wasm` returns 2,161,339 bytes from the clone the user already has — install.sh:18 proves every installed user HAS that clone, since MENTL_HOME is derived from it. Git is content-addressed with sha verification by construction; a second store beside it is Carried-Truth violated (the commit→tree→blob EDGE already connects the fact) and the fix is LESS code, not a fetcher. The "no full extra clone" premise is answered twice over: by the clone in hand, and by `git worktree` (shares the object store). `<sha-prefix>` is address-by-abbreviation requiring a scan and ambiguity handling, against "address by IDENTITY." The raw-blob-URL fetch puts a network dependency in the toolchain trust path and does not name it as an `!Outside` — a hidden gap where PLAN names its residuals (external SMT, correctness oracle) scrupulously.

NOVELTY (attack 3 — the verdict is not honest): it rejects Cargo.lock as a FILE FORMAT and keeps the content-addressed store unexamined — but the store IS the borrowed shape (Nix store, ~/.cargo/registry/cache, npm _cacache), and it is kept while git sits in the same directory doing it correctly. Waving at the lockfile and keeping the cache is the move the audit exists to catch. The refinement claim is a slogan: `sha256sum -c` in a bash shim is not a Mentl refinement discharged by Verify, and PLAN's R3 names the decidable arithmetic fragment as unbuilt — asserting "not a metaphor" does not discharge it. The TCont symmetry is INVERTED: `TCont(R, S, discipline, World)` carries the world WITH the continuation (PLAN §4③); this design carries bytes WITHOUT their world. It is the negative example of the law it claims kinship with.

GATE: constructible and non-vacuous in the narrow sense — my run proves pins genuinely diverge, so a divergence crucible is real. But it does not discriminate this mechanism: worktree/commit selection passes the identical gate with less code, so it is not a gate FOR the content store. Worse, it greenlights the defect — it asserts the bytes changed, never that the world is one the chain attests.

BLAST RADIUS: honestly stated. Nothing here touches the wheel; m2 == m3 and the gates are untouched. That part of the design is sound.
  fixes    : THE PIN IS THE COMMIT, NOT THE BLOB. The item's motivating need survives — a real regression can slip a sound-and-incomplete gate, and a project may need a prior known-good pin. The mechanism must be replaced:

1. Selection is MENTL_HOME selection, not wasm-path selection. tools/install.sh:27 currently clobbers (`MENTL_HOME="$MENTL_HOME"` → a literal assignment in the installed shim). Change it to honor an inherited home: `MENTL_HOME="${MENTL_HOME:-<baked>}"`. One line. Then `MENTL_HOME=<tree> mentl run foo.mn` selects a whole attested world — boot + src + lib + PROVENANCE — instead of orphaned bytes, and `--dir "$MENTL_HOME::/mentl-home"` (install.sh:32) carries the lib that boot was marched against, closing the (old boot, current lib) hole src/driver.mn:58 opens.

2. Materialize a prior pin with `git worktree add`, not a fetcher. Worktrees share the object store, so this IS the stated "without a full extra clone" requirement, met by the tool already present. Delete `~/.mentl/pins/<sha>.wasm`, the raw-blob-URL fetch, the network !Outside, and the hand-maintained sha verification: git verifies content by construction, and a commit is an identity — no prefix scan, no ambiguity, no second content store. This is strictly less code, and it is the Carried-Truth form (follow the commit→tree edge; do not copy the blob into a parallel store).

3. State the novelty verdict honestly: the rejected borrowed shape is the CONTENT-ADDRESSED PACKAGE CACHE (Nix store / cargo registry cache / npm _cacache), rejected because git already is one and is already installed — not merely "Cargo.lock as a file format." Drop the refinement-type claim (`sha256sum -c` in bash is not Verify discharging a predicate; R3's fragment is unbuilt) or build it as an actual refinement. Drop the TCont symmetry claim or invert it into the correction it actually is: TCont carries the world with the continuation, which is precisely why pin-selection must carry the tree with the bytes.

4. Fix the self-contradiction. The ITEM says a silently-broken new release is "structurally impossible here"; the NOVELTY VERDICT concedes march.sh is sound-and-incomplete and "the gate never lets a real regression through" is an empirical record, not a proof. Both cannot stand. Two failure modes remain open, not one — and the measured PROVENANCE staleness (head claims e2babb24; HEAD's boot is 2d4ccff8, re-pinned by f7a1b3a5) is a live instance: the release record can be wrong about which release shipped. Say that plainly; it strengthens the case for the sibling peer rather than weakening it.

5. Re-scope the ARTIFACT STATE: today's granularity is the checkout's git state (install.sh:18/:32/:57 — a live pointer), NOT whether install.sh was re-run. Disclose that `Hβ.tools.provenance-mechanical-repin` is an unbuilt sibling, and that PROVENANCE records full sha256 for only the head pins while 23 prior pins carry 8-char prefixes — the record must carry full shas (or, better, commit ids) before anything can verify against it.

6. Re-cut the gate so it discriminates the mechanism and asserts the world: with MENTL_HOME pointed at a worktree of pin P, a program crafted to diverge (the ownership diagnostic at prelude 291:24 that current/e2babb24 emit and 94e449dc/c323a40f do not is a real, measured divergence to build on) must reproduce P's behavior AND its lib; and the negative leg must FAIL — boot bytes from P against the current tree must be refused, not silently run, or the gate blesses the exact unattested pairing this refutation names.

7. Name the residue positively: `Hβ.install.world-selection-is-commit-selection` (the shim honors an inherited MENTL_HOME; a pin is a worktree at a commit) and `Hβ.tools.provenance-records-full-identity` (the chain carries commit ids / full shas, mechanically, so it cannot drift from the binary it describes — the head is drifted today).
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.plan.projection-not-copy
  S11's five columns are a legitimate second axis over the same peer set §5.R already indexes, but at least five peers are fully redefined in both places instead of cited once and pointed at, so the two copies already disagree.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's factual premise ("five peers fully redefined ... the two copies already disagree") is false 5/5 on the artifact, and its gate returns identical numbers before and after by its own admission while being structurally blind to the single real contradiction it found (PLAN.md:750 gates the arena on `Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb`, live at src/infer.mn:552; PLAN.md:1368 calls the same arc "unblocked") — the salvage is that contradiction, a claim-not-name gate, and the orthogonal-axes reading.
  objection: The design misidentifies the defect class, then builds a gate for the class it misidentified — so the gate is blind to the one real defect the design itself stumbled on.

The stated defect does not exist. "At least five peers are fully redefined in both places ... so the two copies already disagree" is false 5/5 on the artifact. Two are disqualified by the item's own evidence paragraph (`Hβ.diag.duplicate-type-name` — "the ONE instance in the document that already does this right"; `Hβ.persist.module-image-cache` — "a legitimate distinct USE (not a duplicate definition)"). The other three are not redefinitions: `Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection` at PLAN.md:1389 carries ZERO prose (`doc/test/new verb transports; Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection;`) against a bare parenthetical at PLAN.md:860 (`(report takes a DiagKind ADT)`) — silence cannot disagree; `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` at PLAN.md:1375 is a bare parenthetical under a header reading `### Column 3 — the performance floor (§5.O completion)` (PLAN.md:1361), so the claim "pointing at neither" is false and the pointer already exists one level up; `Hβ.effects.parameterized-negation-instance` at PLAN.md:1348 (`(instance-precise !Sample(44100))`) is a verbatim truncation of PLAN.md:405's own words, not "new descriptive text." Zero disagreements among the five.

The one genuine contradiction in the document is the per-decl-arena arc, which the item files as a "Separately," aside: PLAN.md:750 gates layer 3 on `Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb` — still a LIVE open peer at src/infer.mn:552 — while PLAN.md:1368 declares the arc "unblocked 2026-07-16." And `Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena` appears EXACTLY ONCE in PLAN.md (line 749), so a name-multiplicity gate can never see it. The gate measures duplicated NAMES; the defect is a contradicted CLAIM at name-count 1.

The gate is independently refuted three ways. Run verbatim it returns 2 / 3 / 3, and the item concedes "after the fix each still returns >1" — identical before and after, so it cannot fail if the design is not landed. `grep -c` cannot evaluate "carries prose beyond a bare parenthetical," so the pass condition is a human read, not a check. And its stated precondition is factually wrong about its own first probe peer: ZERO occurrences of `Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection` carry prose beyond a bare parenthetical today, not "more than one" — while for probe peers 2 and 3 the postcondition ("exactly one occurrence carries prose") already holds PRE-fix (PLAN.md:846 and PLAN.md:405 are the sole substantive sites).

Two supporting refutations. NOVELTY: "edge-labeled pointers ... makes disagreement structurally impossible" is a slogan — `(band L)` is a string a reader re-resolves by scanning, i.e. the name-keyed lookup CLAUDE.md red-flags, not an edge; and the item's own INTERCONNECTION defers enforcement to `Hβ.plan.one-home-gate`, conceding this item makes disagreement temporarily absent. ONE-HOME ALTITUDE: PLAN.md:773 already declares "full detail with SOTA refs + file:line anchors in `docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md`", and the real anchors are in source (src/effects.mn:345/:891, src/driver.mn:176, src/pipeline.mn:332), where src/effects.mn:891 already points back at "§5.O layer 1" — the codebase practices the pointer discipline in the opposite direction from the one the item installs. Band prose is itself a copy; "full definition in the band ONLY" canonizes it as the home.

What I tried and could not refute: the orthogonal-axes reading survives — bands carry arm tags (PLAN.md:814 "arm 4", 846 "arm 3", 860 "arm 8/1") and columns carry audiences, genuinely two labels on one peer. And the blast-radius claim holds: `grep -rln "PLAN.md" tools/` returns empty, no gate reads PLAN.md, m2 == m3 cannot move. Also noted: HEAD is 7e47e850, not the claimed 4a20b52f — the doc moved one more commit under the item, which the item itself anticipated.
  fixes    : 1. DROP the headline. On the artifact, 0 of 5 disagree and 3 of 5 are not redefinitions. `Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection` at PLAN.md:1389 is a bare name carrying zero prose (`doc/test/new verb transports; Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection;`) against a bare parenthetical at 860 (`(report takes a DiagKind ADT)`) — silence cannot disagree. `Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` at 1375 (`Layer 4: parallel cursors (…multi-core)`) is a strict abbreviation of 757/846 sitting under a header that already reads `### Column 3 — the performance floor (§5.O completion)` — so "pointing at neither" is false, and per-bullet `(§5.O)` tags would copy N times what the header carries once. `Hβ.effects.parameterized-negation-instance` at 1348 (`(instance-precise !Sample(44100))`) is a verbatim truncation of 405's own words, not "restated with new descriptive text." `duplicate-type-name` (1356) and `module-image-cache` (1222) are disqualified by the item's own evidence paragraph.

2. THE FINDING IS THE PER-DECL-ARENA CONTRADICTION — promote it from the "Separately," aside to the whole item. PLAN.md:749-750 gates the arena on `Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb`, which is still a LIVE open peer at src/infer.mn:552 (`// Positive-form peer: Hβ.infer.region-on-tee-alloc-absorb. Until then the`), while PLAN.md:1368 declares the same arc "unblocked 2026-07-16 — regions live, returns transfer, Hylo-quiet on the wheel." A reader of §5.O alone is misled about what blocks layer 3. That is the one place two projections of one fact contradict.

3. RE-AIM THE GATE at claim-contradiction, not name-multiplicity. `Hβ.perf.per-decl-arena` occurs exactly once (PLAN.md:749) — the proposed gate is structurally blind to the item's own finding. Name-count is a fragile proxy for the thing that matters (SYNTAX.md's own "identity, not position" ruling applies). A gate that WOULD fail today: for every peer PLAN.md calls landed/unblocked/discharged, assert no live `Positive-form peer: <name>` comment survives at its source anchor (`grep -rn "Positive-form peer" src/*.mn`). Mechanical, and it fails right now on region-on-tee-alloc-absorb.

4. FIX THE ALTITUDE OF "ONE HOME." PLAN.md:773 already declares the full-detail home: "full detail with SOTA refs + file:line anchors in `docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md`". §5.R is itself the pointer layer, not the definition layer. The real anchors are in source — src/effects.mn:345 and :891, src/driver.mn:176, src/pipeline.mn:332 — and src/effects.mn:891 already points back ("— Hβ.effects.parameterized-negation-instance — and is §5.O layer 1"), i.e. the codebase already practices the pointer discipline, in the opposite direction. "Full definition in the band ONLY" canonizes a copy as the home.

5. DROP "structurally impossible" and "edge-labeled pointer." `(band L)` is a string a reader re-resolves by scanning PLAN.md — a name-keyed lookup, the exact re-derivation CLAUDE.md red-flags, not an edge. Call it what it is: a prose convention, scaffold-tier, enforced by a gate. The item's own INTERCONNECTION defers enforcement to `Hβ.plan.one-home-gate`, so this item makes disagreement temporarily absent, not impossible. The honest endpoint is already named in the artifact: `Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface` (PLAN.md:860) plus §7's declared destiny at PLAN.md:941-942 ("state-as-PROJECTION is §7's own destiny (the docs-as-projection endpoint)") — the peer index projected from the source anchors, at which point band and column are both projections and neither can disagree because neither is written. Sequence the text edit UNDER that, do not sell it as that.

6. FIX THE SCOPE. The design says "same-file text edit" but `Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection` lives in 5 files / 7 sites: PLAN.md x2, docs/SYNTAX.md:1933, docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md x2, docs/research/sota-convergence.md x2, tests/micros/mn-diag-projection.mn. A PLAN.md-only sweep leaves 5 of 7 untouched and a PLAN.md-only gate sees 2.

7. KEEP, unchanged: the orthogonal-axes reading (band = which kernel arm, column = which bar audience — two labels on one peer, never two copies) is sound and is the item's real contribution. Keep the 1356 form as the template. Keep the blast-radius statement — verified: `grep -rln "PLAN.md" tools/` returns empty, so no gate reads PLAN.md and the fixpoint cannot move.

REFUTED: Hβ.plan.one-home-gate
  The one-home law has no enforcement inside PLAN.md itself, only across the three top-level docs, so the projection-not-copy fix will silently rot the next time a column bullet is added — a bash gate closes that the same way drift-audit.sh already closes the nine code-level drift modes.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the allowlist's cited exemplar (Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse "spanning band G and §5.O") appears ZERO times in PLAN.md, and the classifier, implemented exactly as specified and run, passes the §5.O↔§11 duplication it exists to catch while flagging 14 tokens (not the claimed 5), most of them bare pointers.
  objection: THE ALLOWLIST EXEMPLAR IS FABRICATED, AND INVERTED FROM THE CLUSTER'S OWN FINDING. The design says: "Maintain a small allowlist for legitimate multi-home peers (e.g. ... genuinely dual-homed peers like Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse spanning band G and §5.O)." `grep -rn "shared-node-cse"` over the whole repo returns exactly one hit: src/backends/wasm.mn:2056 ("the named peer Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse. Dedup-the-decl is its correct floor"). It is in neither band G nor §5.O nor anywhere in PLAN.md nor in docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md. It cannot be dual-homed; it has ZERO homes in PLAN.md. It is an ORPHAN — a member of the exact class this cluster's brief names ("peers named in the artifact but nowhere in PLAN.md"). The design took a specimen of the problem and wrote it into the allowlist as an exemption. A design built on a misread of the code is refuted outright.

THE GATE IS VACUOUS ON ITS MOTIVATING CASE (I built and ran it). Implementing the classifier exactly as specified — extract Hβ tokens, POINTER = bare parenthetical under ~12 words, DEFINITION = longer prose, flag >1 DEFINITION:
- FALSE NEGATIVE: Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk is NOT flagged. PLAN.md:757 (§5.O layer 4, "(`Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` multi-core half + `Hβ.native.deterministic-handle-partition`)") and PLAN.md:1375 (§11 col 3, "- Layer 4: parallel cursors (`Hβ.driver.level-set-par-walk` multi-core).") state the same roadmap fact twice — precisely the §5.O↔§11 copy the item exists to prevent. Both are short parentheticals, so both classify POINTER and the gate passes. It is blind to its own reason for existing.
- FALSE POSITIVES dominate: it flags 14, not 5. The heuristic recognizes a pointer only INSIDE parentheses, so every bare-backtick bullet reference — §11's dominant pointer form — counts as a DEFINITION: PLAN.md:1389 "- doc/test/new verb transports; `Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection`;" is a pure pointer with no prose and gets flagged. Signal-to-noise is inverted; the allowlist then grows until the gate is silent, and the allowlist IS the ledger the sibling item exists to delete.

THREE MORE FACTUAL BREAKS. (1) The state.sh chain is EIGHT gates, not seven: PHANTOMS → tools/comment-ratchet.sh landed at HEAD (7e47e850, "tools: the prose has a census too, and it was wired to nothing"). The design's stated evidence is `bash tools/state.sh --quick`, which returns before the chain (state.sh: `if [ "${1:-}" != "--quick" ]; then`) — the evidence cannot observe the claim. (2) "commit 75246329 (pre-fix HEAD)" — HEAD is 7e47e850, four commits later; PLAN.md gained 70 lines in between (`git diff --stat 75246329 HEAD -- PLAN.md`). (3) The PostToolUse hook DOES run drift-audit.sh (.claude/hooks/post-edit-mn.sh:26 — CLAUDE.md is true here), but it early-exits unless the path is `*.mn` or `*/bootstrap/src/*.wat` (post-edit-mn.sh:22-24). PLAN.md is `.md`. The edit-time delivery half is inert against its own target file, unmentioned.

DRIFT. "~12 words ⇒ POINTER" is drift 8 (flag-as-int) at the prose layer: a numeric proxy for the identity "is this the peer's home?" A spec cannot be "approximately 12." The allowlist is a side-ledger of the exemption fact — Carried-Truth violated inside a gate built to enforce Carried-Truth (unlike comment-audit.sh's ALLOW, a closed 4-entry kernel-vocabulary set, this one grows with the corpus). And comment-audit.sh's own header names this exact boundary as what a grep CANNOT do — "It cannot judge SEMANTIC staleness... That needs the LLM sweep — and the realized form is `mentl fmt` + `mentl audit`" — while comment-audit checks a DECIDABLE fact (`grep -qwF "$sym" "$SURFACE"`). The design crosses the line its own precedent draws and calls the result a gate.

SCOPE — IT GATES THE NOISE AND PASSES THE SIGNAL. 98 of the 131 Hβ peers named in src/lib appear nowhere in PLAN.md; PLAN.md and docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md share only 63 of 115 each (52/52 fork; roadmap untouched since Jul 4). A green one-home gate wired into state.sh — which §11 makes the release gate — would read "the one-home law holds" while the law is broken ~98 times over.

WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTEMPT TO REFUTE (I tried to kill these and could not): the TIER judgment is sound — PLAN §6 names bash scaffolds legitimate and comment-ratchet.sh is live precedent in the exact chain slot, so "scaffold that dissolves into `mentl audit`" is honest, not a hedge. The BLAST RADIUS is honestly stated and true — no .mn touched; m2==m3, 71 micros, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5 all untouched. And the MOTIVATION is CONFIRMED: the one-home law genuinely has no in-file enforcement, and the artifact shows it breached. My hypothesis that PLAN.md is a copy of post-first-light-roadmap.md (115 == 115 distinct peers) was itself REFUTED by `comm` — 63 shared, 52/52 forked. The need is real; the mechanism is not.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE the word-count classifier and the allowlist. Both are refuted: the heuristic is vacuous on §5.O↔§11 (PLAN.md:757/1375) and noisy on §11's bullet pointers (PLAN.md:1389); the allowlist is a growing side-ledger whose one cited entry does not exist. The decidable check the artifact supports is EXACT-DUPLICATE PROSE, and it needs no heuristic and no ledger:
   awk '{gsub(/^[ \t·*-]+/,""); if (length($0)>40) print}' PLAN.md | sort | uniq -d
   Run today it returns exactly one line: "`Hβ.infer.alias-preserving-unify` (the wrapper-peel erases the alias from" — verbatim at PLAN.md:989-990 (named-residue index) and PLAN.md:1350-1351 (§11 column 2). That is the one real, mechanically-decidable one-home violation in the file. The "five violations found by one grep pass" evidence does not reproduce under either mechanism (verbatim: 1; word-count: 14) and must be re-derived before anything is built on it.

2. FIX THE FOUR FACTUAL CLAIMS. Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse is an ORPHAN (src/backends/wasm.mn:2056 only), not a dual-homed peer — it belongs in the exhaustiveness-sweep item's finding list, never in an allowlist. The state.sh chain has eight gates (PHANTOMS/comment-ratchet.sh, HEAD 7e47e850). `--quick` cannot observe the chain. 75246329 is not HEAD (7e47e850 is; PLAN.md differs by 70 lines).

3. IF the pointer/definition distinction is wanted at all, make it STRUCTURAL, never lexical. PLAN.md:986 already declares the law as structure — "Named-residue index (entry-born peers not yet in a §5.R band — one home each)". A peer's home IS its §5.R band entry or its residue-index entry; that is an edge the document already draws. Read it: build the declared-home set from band/index membership, then assert every other occurrence in the file is a bare backtick reference. That reads the edge instead of re-deriving "is this a home?" from word count — the Carried-Truth fix, and it is strictly less code than a classifier plus a ledger.

4. RATCHET, NOT HARD GATE. The precedent sits one line above the slot this wants to join, with its reason stated in the artifact (tools/comment-ratchet.sh header: "refusing every commit until all 554 die stops the medium from being worked on... a rise is a NEW lie and blocks; a fall is banked immediately"). Landing a hard exit-1 gate that flags 14 today red-boards §11's own release gate on arrival. Ratchet the count in tools/verify-baseline.txt beside phantom_comments_max.

5. RETARGET TO THE LIVE BREACH. Intra-file duplication is 1 verbatim copy. The one-home law's actual breach is 98 of 131 code-named Hβ peers absent from PLAN.md, plus the 52/52 fork between PLAN.md and docs/research/post-first-light-roadmap.md (the second peer registry, last touched Jul 4, which PLAN §5.R still cites as "full detail"). A one-home gate that cannot see either is gating the wrong invariant.

6. DELIVERY: the PostToolUse route requires post-edit-mn.sh:22-24's matcher to admit PLAN.md, and drift-audit.sh is scoped to .mn/.wat against tools/drift-patterns.tsv — neither is a home for a markdown structure check. Either state a positive-form peer for the prose-audit hook or drop the edit-time leg and keep the chain leg only.

REFUTED: Hβ.felt.hole-is-dormant-continuation
  A hole's propose-verify-pick cycle becomes a real MultiShot continuation, backtracking over live graph checkpoints via k1's own LMakeContinuation record — reusing the mechanism Mentl already built for durable execution and search, not a hole-specific mechanism the way Hazel's hole-closures are.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's artifact_state is accurate in every cited line, but the mechanism it proposes to build already exists as `backtrack` (lib/runtime/search.mn:134-149) line-for-line, so minting `effect HoleFill` + `hole_search_driver` + `try_candidates` is a third copy of a driver the medium already has, and the design's own novelty thesis ("not a hole-specific mechanism the way Hazel's hole-closures are") refutes it.
  objection: THE DESIGN BUILDS THE HOLE-SPECIFIC MECHANISM IT PROMISES NOT TO BUILD, BESIDE AN IDENTICAL ONE ALREADY IN THE STDLIB.

The item's own title: "reusing the mechanism Mentl already built for durable execution and SEARCH, not a hole-specific mechanism the way Hazel's hole-closures are." The mechanism Mentl already built for search is `lib/runtime/search.mn:134-149`:

```
handler backtrack with Choice + GraphRead + GraphWrite + Abort {
  choose(options) => {
    fn try_each(i) =
      if i >= len(options) { abort() }
      else {
        let checkpoint = graph_push_checkpoint()
        try_with_abort_catch(
          () => resume(list_index(options, i)),
          () => { graph_rollback(checkpoint) try_each(i + 1) })
      }
    try_each(0)
  }
}
```

Set that beside the design's `hole_search_driver`/`try_candidates`: per-candidate `graph_push_checkpoint()`, `resume(candidate)` running the live remainder, dead-end → `graph_rollback` → next, exhausted → abort/fail, MultiShot by recursive-resume structure. Same driver. The design's own novelty verdict says the choice point "IS graph_push_checkpoint's trail entry... so hole-backtracking and annotation-backtracking are ONE mechanism, not two" — and then constructs the second. It wins the novelty argument at the RECORD layer (the LMakeContinuation is shared, true, lower.mn:378) and loses it at the EFFECT layer, where `effect HoleFill { resolve_hole(...) }` is exactly the bespoke hole-specific mechanism the item exists to avoid. `resolve_hole` is `synth_propose |> choose` welded under a third name — SYNTAX Governing Principle 2 ("if two forms produce the same graph, one is rejected") at the effect layer.

Four artifact facts turn this from a stylistic objection into a refutation:

(1) `src/backends/wasm.mn:884` — "the wheel's own backtrack/pick_first are **uninstalled dead code**". The design's claimed contribution ("the first real consumer of k1's continuation machinery outside its own DSP/search crucibles") is delivered by INSTALLING the stdlib driver already written, not by writing a third. And `PLAN.md:1352` already carries the real blocker on the production bar: "backtrack's `() vs Option(())` mismatch (search.mn)." The design walks past a named, live bar item and builds beside it — Anchor 2 inverted, and the fix is LESS code, not more.

(2) The design's central line is not typeable, and the reason is that bar item. `let ok = resume(node); if ok {...}` presumes a Bool verdict. The typed-resume law (infer.mn:817-828) binds `resume`'s value to `answer` off `TCont(resume_input, answer, _, _)` — resume yields S, the REMAINDER's answer, never a verdict. `if ok` forces S ~ Bool, silently pinning every hole-bearing program's answer type to Bool while the same arm returns `node : Node`. `backtrack` shows what the medium actually requires: `try_with_abort_catch` + `~> catch_abort` converting the remainder's dead-end into an `Option` (search.mn:108-114), because the driver cannot ask "did the remainder succeed?" — it only gets S. `tests/micros/mn-backtrack-full.mn` proves it works only because the remainder's answer type IS Option (`attempt(v) = if v > 25 { Some(v) } else { abort() }`). The design elides the single hardest part of the mechanism it claims to reuse, and PLAN.md:1352 is that elision, already named.

(3) The snippet raises a hard error on its own compile. `fn try_candidates(survivors)` is a TOP-LEVEL fn holding `resume(node)`. `inf_arm_tys()` (infer.mn:47, 157-160) reads a dynamic `arm_stack` pushed by `inf_enter_arm` only while infer walks an arm body; at a top-level decl it is empty → `None` → `report(EResumeOutsideArm(span))` (infer.mn:832), catalogued as a hard error at docs/SYNTAX.md:1981 whose Quick Fix names the exact defect: "move the resume into an arm; the continuation only exists there." Not lathe-lag — outside an arm there is no continuation handle, so `resume`'s type would have to be fabricated, and infer.mn:829-831 refuses on purpose ("surface the structural fact rather than fabricating a type"). Both working analogues nest the driver INSIDE the arm (search.mn:136; mn-backtrack-full.mn's header: "The resume's k resolves through the CAPTURE chain (arm → try_each → lambda, all lexical)"). Reachability does not save it: "reachability==emission" prunes emit, not infer.

(4) The gate is `tests/micros/mn-backtrack-full.mn` with the numbers changed. That micro is the design's proposed gate already green in the 71-micro battery: `choose([10,20,30])`, continuation `k = [?? |> attempt]`, a DOWNSTREAM predicate rejecting the first two, checkpoint/rollback per option, run reaches 30 — one continuation resumed three times, not a restart. The design's `hole-backtrack.mn` would fail today (no `resolve_hole`), so it is not vacuous in the strict sense, but it discriminates only the duplicate's existence, never a capability the medium lacks.

Two smaller ones. `graph_commit_checkpoint()` is not a new speculation primitive at this layer: graph.mn:115-117 makes a checkpoint `trail_len`, an int mark into an inverse log; writes land in `nodes` immediately and are undone by `revert_trail` (graph.mn:365-378). Commit is the ABSENCE of rollback. The only real residue is the `checkpoint_stack` pop on the success path (graph.mn:74-81 gates `graph_mutated` on the stack emptying) — and `backtrack`'s `Some(value) => value` (search.mn:111) has that identical gap today, so it is one graph_handler arm shared with backtrack, not "item 1's primitive, this is its second consumer." And Synth already declares the multi-shot op the design says is missing: mentl.mn:101-102 "three ops: propose (main), verify_candidate (Mentl-internal), **enumerate_inhabitants (multi-shot raw)**", declared at mentl.mn:108, armed at synth_proposer.mn:140, flipped by the classifier (infer.mn:3852: "choose/enumerate_inhabitants flip MultiShot"), and already emitting as MultiShot (wasm.mn:1152: "enumerate_inhabitants MultiShot: lambda_16603 collected 4x"). The design adds a fourth op to a three-op effect whose third op is the one it wants, without auditing it.

WHAT I TRIED THAT DID NOT REFUTE ME. I attacked the factual layer first and it held: types.mn:843 `NHole(Int)` ✓; parser.mn:39 `nhole` ✓ (the nonzero-counter fact lives at cursor.mn:241-243, not :39 — imprecise anchor, not a misread); mentl.mn:105-106 `synth_propose` ✓ exact; synth_proposer.mn:107-118 `synth_default`'s arm ✓ exact, and it IS OneShot — one resume at :117, straight-line, no loop/recursion ancestry, exactly as claimed; the header's "real MultiShot candidate enumeration" (synth_proposer.mn:4-5) IS the doc/artifact mismatch claimed; mentl.mn:244-255 `try_each_annotation` ✓ exact, `graph_rollback(cp)` unconditional at :250 — the winner is rolled back, as claimed; `verify_each_enriched` is 536-554 (cited 540-555, inside the fn); lower.mn `LYield(Int, String, List, LowExpr)` and `LMakeContinuation(Int, LowFn, List, List, Int, Int, Int)` = handle/resume_fn/captures/ev_slots/state_index/ret_slot/world_tag ✓ exact; cursor.mn:638-642 `propose_at` ✓, :644-661 `mentl_context_at` ✓; cursor_transport.mn:413-416 `show_one_proposal` ✓. I also tried to refute the MultiShot-classification claim via the named peer `Hβ.infer.tail-recursion-resume-cardinality` and failed: infer.mn:3846-3853 shows per-fn summaries with call-substitution as a monotone fixpoint, and `try_candidates` saturates UMany by round 2 exactly as claimed (the peer at infer.mn:3840 concerns re-invocation through a passed lambda, not this shape). PLAN's anchor for that peer (infer.mn:3174) is stale — that line is `subst_row`. The design's read of the code is honest; its construction is the failure.
  fixes    : The peer `Hβ.felt.hole-is-dormant-continuation` is real and the design's diagnosis is right: the proof cycle rolls back the winner (mentl.mn:250, synth_proposer.mn:549) and returns a value for something else to re-derive from text, and synth_default's arm is OneShot despite its header. Keep that. Replace everything after it.

1. DELETE `effect HoleFill`, `hole_search_driver`, and `try_candidates` from the design. The op is `choose`; the handler is `backtrack` (lib/runtime/search.mn:134-149); the driver is `try_each` (search.mn:136). The design must say: the hole's fill is `synth_propose(...) |> survivors_of |> choose` under `~> backtrack` — a `|>` composing two ops that already exist, the pure OneShot query and the MultiShot picker. State plainly that `resolve_hole` was `synth_propose` welded to `choose` under a third name, and that Governing Principle 2 rejects it at the effect layer exactly as it does at the surface. The fix is LESS code: zero new effects, zero new handlers, zero new drivers.

2. THE ITEM'S REAL CONTENT IS AN INSTALL, NOT A BUILD. wasm.mn:884 — "backtrack/pick_first are uninstalled dead code." The design's own stated contribution (first real consumer of k1's machinery outside the crucibles) is delivered by drawing the `~> backtrack` install edge at the hole site. Say that.

3. OWN THE ACTUAL BLOCKER, WHICH IS ALREADY ON THE BAR. PLAN.md:1352 (§11 column 2): "backtrack's `() vs Option(())` mismatch (search.mn)." That is this item's gate-blocking work, not a new primitive. The design's `let ok = resume(node)` must be deleted and replaced with backtrack's abort protocol — `resume` yields the remainder's answer S (infer.mn:817-828), never a verdict; failure reaches the driver only as an `Abort` caught into the remainder's own answer type (search.mn:108-114). The design must state that the driver cannot ask "did it succeed?" and that routing a downstream Verify rejection into `abort()` is the mechanism, then say what the answer-type protocol is when the remainder answers `()` — which is the mismatch, verbatim.

4. FIX `graph_commit_checkpoint`'s framing. graph.mn:115-117 makes a checkpoint an int mark (`trail_len`) into an inverse log; writes land immediately; `revert_trail` (graph.mn:365-378) undoes them. Commit is the absence of rollback. The only residue is the `checkpoint_stack` POP on the success path so the outermost boundary fires `graph_mutated` (graph.mn:74-81) — and backtrack's `Some(value) => value` (search.mn:111) leaks that entry today. Say: one arm on graph_handler, shared with backtrack, not "item 1's new primitive with this as its second consumer."

5. AUDIT THE DORMANT OP BEFORE ADDING A NEW ONE. Synth's third op is already the multi-shot raw one (mentl.mn:101-102, :108; armed at synth_proposer.mn:140; flipped at infer.mn:3852; emitted MultiShot per wasm.mn:1152). The design must state what `enumerate_inhabitants` does today and why it is or is not the seat, before proposing any fourth op.

6. NEST THE DRIVER. Whatever the driver is named, it is a nested fn inside the arm body, as search.mn:136 and mn-backtrack-full.mn already are. A top-level `fn` holding `resume` is `E_ResumeOutsideArm` (infer.mn:832, docs/SYNTAX.md:1981) — infer's arm_stack is walked lexically (infer.mn:157-160) and dead code is still inferred, so reachability does not hide it.

7. REPLACE THE GATE. `tests/micros/mn-backtrack-full.mn` already runs the design's proposed experiment (three candidates, a downstream predicate rejecting the first two, checkpoint/rollback per option, one continuation resumed three times → 30) and is green in the 71-micro battery. A gate that discriminates must exercise what the medium cannot do today: a `??` at a live authoring position, `~> backtrack` installed, a first survivor passing its own check and failing a DOWNSTREAM refinement, the second survivor's write COMMITTING (checkpoint_stack empty, `graph_mutated` fired) and surviving into emit — the commit being the thing mentl.mn:250 unconditionally rolls back today. Site it where the authoring workflows are gated (tools/frontier-gate.sh, tools/proof-exactness-gate.sh), not in tests/native-cont/, whose crucibles are the native-continuation probe set.

8. NAME THE BLAST RADIUS, WHICH THE DESIGN IS SILENT ON. wasm.mn:880-891 is explicit: a redrive dispatches `call $op_<hname>_<op>` against arm fns that exist only when the handler survives reachability, and "backtrack/pick_first are uninstalled dead code — their redrives referenced dropped arms and **m3 failed assembly**." Installing a live MultiShot handler on the compiler's own authoring path changes the redrive/arm-emission seam. The design must declare this a TRANSITION-class change (march.sh arbitrates m3 == m4, re-pin from m3) and say so before landing, not discover it at the gate.

9. CORRECT TWO SMALL ANCHOR CLAIMS. The nonzero-hole-id fact lives at cursor.mn:241-243, not parser.mn:39 (which is the mint fn). `Hβ.lower.arm-internal-perform-scope` is not an open gap to argue around — PLAN.md:816 records it "closed by the M3 lexical-evidence fence."
