REFUTED: Hβ.plan.bar-as-query-debt
  S11 is not a project tracker sitting beside the medium — it is a V_Pending-shaped debt against two peers already named in band L, and writing it that way now is what lets it delete itself later instead of needing a rewrite.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design mislocates the debt: it defers §11 to an unbuilt `Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface` when the graph already holds the peers live in the comment weave (`graph_comment_at`, types.mn:1229 / graph.mn:195, read at format.mn:426), which makes PLAN.md's peer list a present-tense Carried-Truth violation (33 copies, ~75 orphans) that the design relabels as future-tense "honest debt" — and V_Pending cannot license it, because the obligation is decidable and SYNTAX.md:289 says decidable-false refuses rather than pends.
  objection: THE DEBT IS AGAINST THE WRONG PEER, AND IT IS PRESENT-TENSE, NOT PENDING.

The design's premise is that S11's peer tags are "exactly the shape Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface will eventually read live off real graph nodes," so hand-maintenance today is honest V_Pending debt awaiting that peer. The artifact refutes every link in that chain.

1. THE FACT IS ALREADY GRAPH CONTENT, WITH A LIVE READER. The comment weave is complete, not planned: effect ops declared at types.mn:1239 (`graph_attach_comment(Int, String) -> ()`) and types.mn:1229 (`graph_comment_at(Int) -> String`); handled at graph.mn:188/195 (a handle-co-indexed weave — graph.mn:184 explains it is deliberately separate from the Reason slot "graph_bind overwrites... at infer"); written at parser.mn:315 (`graph_attach_comment(h, full_text)`); and READ LIVE at format.mn:426 (`let text = graph_comment_at(h)` — "never a copied string"). 116 Hβ peers already live in .mn comments, i.e. attached to decl handles today.

2. SO PLAN.md IS A SNAPSHOT OF A LIVE WEAVE — LAW 1 VIOLATED NOW. Measured census (regex over `Hβ.<area>.<name>`, corrected for PLAN's band-relative shorthand by matching the distinctive last component): 115 peers named in PLAN.md; 116 anchored in source comments; 33 in BOTH (PLAN re-derives by name what `graph_comment_at(h)` returns live); ~75 TRUE ORPHANS in the weave but absent from PLAN.md entirely (Hβ.infer.scc-ordered-walk, Hβ.lower.multi-effect-ev-index-map, Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse, Hβ.parser.block-needs-braces, Hβ.types.tcont-carries-cardinality-and-world, ...). The Universal Audit's verdict is unambiguous: copied where it could be read live = the bug. The design schedules that fix behind an unbuilt query instead of doing it.

3. V_PENDING DOES NOT LICENSE THIS — THE OBLIGATION IS DECIDABLE. SYNTAX.md:287-289 grounds the whole metaphor: "undecidable residue accrues visibly, never assume-true... Decidable-FALSE still refuses at the claim site (E_RefinementRejected)." V_Pending is honest ONLY because you cannot discharge it. "Is this peer's home the comment on its node?" is decidable — two greps decided it, and the answer is FALSE for 33 (copied) and FALSE for 75 (missing). A decidable-false claim recorded as pending is precisely the "silent assume-true" PLAN §0 names as the betrayal. The design's central metaphor inverts the law it cites.

4. THE ARTIFACT_STATE IS FALSE WHERE IT IS LOAD-BEARING. "Neither is wired to anything" — `mentl query` IS wired: src/query.mn is 23,089 bytes carrying a real `Question`/`QueryResult` ADT pair (QTypeOf, QWhy, QVerifyDebt, QFlowOf...), dispatched at src/main.mn:125 (`Invocation(VQuery(path_to_module(argv[2]), argv[3]))`); `mentl audit` dispatches at src/main.mn:123; PLAN §7's own ledger (2026-07-14, 91755d6+f1b13e2) says "compile/check/audit/teach/query WORK". The misread is what makes the debt look far away. Worse, main.mn:125 shows the query's domain is `(module_path, question)` — a compiled module's inference result. PLAN.md is not a module, so "a query reading PLAN.md's own content" is not an unbuilt feature, it is a category error; the design's dissolution path points at something that cannot exist in the shape described.

5. THE EXPIRY CONDITION — THE NOVELTY VERDICT'S ENTIRE STRENGTH CLAIM — CANNOT FIRE, AND DEMONSTRABLY DID NOT. The claim is that "debt against a peer" beats "a board" because the peer landing is an automatic expiry. But `Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface` at PLAN.md:860 is the ONLY band-L peer with no gloss — literally `*(new)*`, nothing else, while every sibling carries one (`Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection` = "report takes a DiagKind ADT"; `Hβ.audit.carried-truth-projection` = the §0 keystone). An expiry whose predicate is undefined cannot fire. And empirically it already failed: the query verb landed 2026-07-14; §11 was authored 2026-07-16 (a09026c) — two days LATER, by hand, in full board shape. So the novelty verdict correctly NAMES the borrowed form (Jira/Notion board) and then keeps its shape, adding an expiry label the artifact shows does not fire. That is waving at the borrowed shape and keeping it.

6. THE GATE PASSES VACUOUSLY. "every S11 bullet added AFTER this note lands must pass" grandfathers 100% of existing §11; the predicate is a human judgment with no script (tools/ holds no plan gate; tools/plan-one-home-gate.sh does not exist). Applied honestly to today's §11 it would DELETE the section's most load-bearing prose — the "One bar, both audiences" definition, the novelty audit's rejected-shapes table (which this task brief itself cites as the canonical worked example), the named-peer audit's four verdicts — none of which is (name, status, band, column, DEP) shaped. Vacuous, or destructive. Both refute.

Blast radius is the one clean part: doc-only, touching no gate (m2==m3, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9, crown 5/5, 71 micros), and the design says so honestly.
  fixes    : The core intent survives — §11 must not be a project board, and PLAN.md:941-942 ("state-as-PROJECTION is §7's own destiny... each line is a POINTER") is the right north star. Five corrections make it true to the artifact.

1. RELOCATE THE DEBT. Not "S11 is debt against Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface." The debt is: A PEER'S ONE HOME IS THE COMMENT ON THE NODE IT CONSTRAINS, and PLAN.md's band list is a snapshot of a weave that is already live (`graph_comment_at`, types.mn:1229; handled graph.mn:195; read format.mn:426). For the 33 copied peers this is not pending — it is Law 1 violated today, decidable, and fixable by restructure. State it that way or the note canonizes the copy.

2. SPLIT THE TWO CLASSES — THE DESIGN'S UNIFORM TREATMENT IS THE CATEGORY ERROR. Anchored peers (116) are graph content and projectable now. Unanchored peers (82) have NO NODE and no query will ever render them; PLAN.md:854 already admits the class in its own words ("no code anchor yet — the peer is named here, not in a comment"). Treating both as future-query-output hides a live violation behind a future tense. Name the residue positively: `Hβ.plan.peer-home-is-the-comment-weave` (the 33 copies dissolve into a read) and `Hβ.plan.unanchored-peer-needs-a-node` (a peer with no node is prose, not debt — either give it the node it constrains or admit it is band-level strategy that belongs in a band, never in a query-shaped list).

3. THE DISTANCE IS ONE QUESTION VARIANT, NOT AN UNBUILT SURFACE. query.mn already carries the `Question`/`QueryResult` ADT pair and main.mn:125 already dispatches `VQuery(module, question)`. The mechanical discharge is a `QPeers` arm reading `graph_comment_at` across the module's decls, returning a `QRPeers` list — the same shape every other arm has. Say THAT, so the expiry is a real, checkable condition instead of an undefined `*(new)*`. And fix the peer itself: `Hβ.query.graph-projection-surface` must acquire a gloss naming its discharge, or it cannot serve as anyone's expiry.

4. CORRECT THE ARTIFACT_STATE SENTENCE. Drop "Neither is wired to anything" — query and audit are wired (main.mn:123,125; query.mn 23KB; §7 ledger 2026-07-14). The true, narrower statement: no Question variant projects the peer weave, and PLAN.md is not a module, so PLAN.md's prose is not a query domain at all — only the peers living in real modules' comment weaves can ever be projected.

5. MAKE THE GATE MECHANICAL AND NON-GRANDFATHERED, OR DROP IT. The census run here IS a script: extract `Hβ.<area>.<name>` from PLAN.md and from src/**.mn + lib/**.mn comments, `comm` the two sets, and fail on any peer copied into PLAN.md that already lives in a comment (33 today) or any weave peer absent from PLAN.md (~75 today). That gate FAILS at HEAD and passes at zero — it can actually fail, unlike "could this line be query output?" applied only to future bullets. And stop DEP-gating on tools/plan-one-home-gate.sh, which does not exist.

Finally, keep §11's prose where the design would have starved it: the bar definition, the novelty audit's rejected-shapes table, and the named-peer audit's four verdicts are NOT (name, status, band, column, DEP) shaped and never will be. They are the reasoning that makes the bar a bar. A query-shaped §11 is not the ultimate form of that content; it is its deletion.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.parser.handler-arm-doc-attachment
  SYNTAX.md states unconditionally that a comment is graph content and never dropped, but src/parser.mn's own comment admits doc comments immediately preceding a handler arm are silently skipped today — a live violation of one of the three canonical docs, uncredited anywhere in PLAN.md.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the fix site is dead code (skip_ws eats the comment 5 lines before the branch the design patches), the mechanism it names (CommentReason) does not exist and was deliberately rejected by the artifact, the arm has no handle to attach to so "parser-only, no plumbing" is false, and the peer duplicates one already named in the same file (Hβ.parser.interior-comment-to-enclosing-block, parser.mn:75-79). The underlying grievance — an arm-preceding comment is silently dropped — is REAL and survives.
  objection: THE PROPOSED FIX SITE NEVER SEES A COMMENT — the design's own gate would fail against its own fix. The design: "calling the same attachment step at the exact point parse_handler_arms currently discards the token and retries" (parser.mn:989-993). But parse_handler_arms opens `let p = skip_ws(tokens, pos)` (parser.mn:984), and skip_ws is `TComment(_) => skip_ws(tokens, pos + 1)` (parser.mn:83). The TComment is consumed BEFORE the `!is_param_name_tok(tokens, p)` test; is_param_name_tok (parser.mn:400-405) is true only for TIdent/TOwn/TRef, so that branch fires on stray non-comment tokens only. Both recursion sites eat comments too — `skip_sep(tokens, p + 1)` (993; skip_sep is TComment(_) => skip_sep at parser.mn:92) and `skip_ws(tokens, p6)` (1008). The design absorbed the STALE code comment at 989-993 ("e.g., `///` doc-comment preceding the arm") instead of reading the five lines above it — the discipline's own "a diagnostic's NAME can lie", here a code comment lying and the design taking it as artifact state.

Three more, each independently fatal:

(1) MECHANISM DOES NOT EXIST, AND WAS DELIBERATELY REJECTED. `CommentReason` = 0 occurrences across all .mn files. The real mechanism is a handle-keyed weave: `graph_attach_comment(Int, String) -> ()` (types.mn:1239), `graph_comment_at(Int) -> String` (types.mn:1229), implemented graph.mn:188-197, called ONLY at parser.mn:315. graph.mn:184-187 states why the design's route is wrong: "A SEPARATE weave, not the node's single Reason slot: graph_bind overwrites that slot at infer with the inference Reason, so a parse-written comment Reason would be clobbered." The design's "nothing downstream needs new plumbing since CommentReason edges are already read generically" is false on the name and backwards on the architecture.

(2) ARMS HAVE NO NODE — "parser-only" is false. graph_attach_comment needs an Int handle. The arm is `{args: args, body: body, op_name: op_name}` (parser.mn:1012) — a bare record, no handle, no span — pinned in three CLOSED structural annotations (`a: {args: List, body: N, op_name: String}`, parser.mn:925/926/932). Attaching "to the ARM's own AST node" requires arms to become handle-carrying nodes, rippling to query.mn:513, infer.mn:2785/2794/2802/3577/3748, lower.mn:164/169/1236/1842/3459/3483 — a real fixpoint risk the design does not mention (blast radius: unstated).

(3) THE DESIGN CONTRADICTS THE LAW IT CLAIMS TO ENFORCE. SYNTAX.md Comments: the comment attaches "to the immediately-following declaration (`fn`/`type`/`effect`/`handler`/`let`), or — with none — to the enclosing block / the file's `Module` handle." A handler ARM is not in that list; SYNTAX.md's stated home is the ENCLOSING BLOCK. The design demands attachment "to the ARM's own AST node (not the enclosing handler node)" — strictly stricter than the doc — while its novelty_verdict claims "the fix makes the artifact agree with the law that already exists." It proposes a NEW law wearing a conformance fix's costume.

(4) DUPLICATE PEER — the cluster's own charge. parser.mn:75-79, directly above skip_ws: "Per-interior-position attachment to the enclosing block is the positive-form refinement Hβ.parser.interior-comment-to-enclosing-block." An arm comment IS an interior comment, dropped by that exact skip_ws. Two names, one drop site, one design. Minting Hβ.parser.handler-arm-doc-attachment is a side-ledger of peer names — the very thing PLAN §11's named-peer audit killed via the partial-effectful-callee + partial-local-callee → partial-via-lambda-recipe MERGE. The item's grep is right that neither name is in PLAN.md; the honest conclusion is "these are one peer", not "add the arm-specific one."

WHAT I TRIED AND COULD NOT REFUTE — the grievance is real, and broader than claimed. A comment preceding a handler arm IS silently dropped: skip_ws (parser.mn:83) consumes it, nothing reports POrphanDocstring, nothing attaches — the third outcome SYNTAX.md says cannot exist. The design's claim that top-level decls DO get attachment is TRUE (parse_stmt's TComment arm, parser.mn:305-318, reached because parse_stmt_list uses skip_seps_only — parser.mn:279-283 — which deliberately does not skip comments). But the drop is the WHOLE interior class, not handler arms: skip_ws/skip_sep are the general layout skippers, and parser.mn:795 shows the same hole at effect ops. Fixing only handler arms treats one mole in a class the file itself already censused and named.
  fixes    : The design must say, instead:

1. SITE — target skip_ws/skip_sep (parser.mn:80-95), never parse_handler_arms:989. The 989 branch never sees a TComment (skip_ws:83 ate it at 984); an attachment step there is dead code. Separately, the stale code comment at parser.mn:989-993 must be CORRECTED — it names a doc-comment case that cannot reach it, and it is the sole source of this item's misreading.

2. MECHANISM — `graph_attach_comment(handle, text)` into the handle-keyed comment weave (types.mn:1239, graph.mn:188-197), read back via `graph_comment_at(handle)` (types.mn:1229; format.mn:426 is the live reader). NOT a `CommentReason(text, span)` edge — that constructor does not exist, and graph.mn:184-187 rejects the Reason slot by design because graph_bind clobbers it at infer.

3. HOME — per SYNTAX.md the interior comment's home is the ENCLOSING BLOCK, i.e. the handler decl's node, which HAS a handle. That is genuinely parser-only and needs no record change. ARM-scoped attachment ("the arm's own node") is a strictly larger design: it requires arms to stop being bare records `{args, body, op_name}` (parser.mn:1012, pinned at 925/926/932) and become handle-carrying nodes, rippling through query.mn:513, infer.mn:2785-3748, lower.mn:164-3483. If arm-scoped prose is wanted, argue it as a SYNTAX.md amendment with that ripple named — do not smuggle it in as a conformance fix.

4. PEER / ONE-HOME — the peer is `Hβ.parser.interior-comment-to-enclosing-block`, already named at parser.mn:75-79. DELETE `Hβ.parser.handler-arm-doc-attachment` from parser.mn:991 rather than promote it; handler arms, effect ops (parser.mn:795), match arms, and record fields are one class with one drop site. If PLAN.md gains a line it gains exactly ONE, for the general peer, in band L (Hβ.diag.catalog-as-projection's Why-engine kin) — the cluster's exhaustiveness sweep should record the general peer, and record that the arm-specific name was a duplicate.

5. GATE — re-specify; the stated gate is unbuildable (no CommentReason, no arm node, no wired `mentl why` on arms). A mechanically checkable gate: compile a micro whose handler carries a `//` run before an arm, then assert `graph_comment_at(h)` for the handler decl's handle contains the prose. Today it returns "" — fails pre-fix, passes post-fix, non-vacuous. Add a parity leg asserting the "never a silent discard" law holds for the other interior positions (effect ops, match arms) so the fix is proven at CLASS scope, not per-site.

6. BLAST RADIUS — state it. The enclosing-block fix is parse-time only and writes a weave no emit path reads, so it should be Law-7 byte-identical (m2 == m3) — but the wheel's own source is dense with interior comments, so the comments weave newly populates for many handles (list_extend_to per attach, graph.mn:188-193): an allocation/working-set change to verify by marching, never to assume. The arm-record-shape variant DOES touch infer/lower and is a genuine fixpoint risk. The design currently claims neither.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.test.lib-test-mn-substrate
  Mentl has a working, effect-native test framework with two explicitly named open follow-ups, and none of S11's five columns mentions testing anywhere — a real hole in what production-ready should mean for an early adopter shipping code.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the premise ("§11 has a testing hole") is false at PLAN.md:1389, the "working test framework" has zero consumers and has never executed, `verify_assert` exists only inside a prose comment, the design's thesis is already named as `Hβ.closure.correctness-oracle-internal` (band J, PLAN.md:856, cited in §11 Column 5 at :1408), its cited precedent f7a1b3a5 *deletes* the kind of special-case it proposes to *add*, and its gate cannot pass because `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) rejects both of its worked examples.
  objection: The design's premise is refuted by its own inventory, and the artifact refutes the rest.

**1. The "hole" does not exist — and the design's search proves it looked in one place.** The design states: "none of S11's five columns mentions testing anywhere." Its own artifact-state claim reveals the search: "Column 2's full text (1278-1360) has zero occurrences of 'test.'" That is true and irrelevant — it checked ONE column and asserted the conclusion for FIVE. An exhaustive sweep of §11 (PLAN.md:1200-1440) returns five hits:
- **PLAN.md:1389 (Column 4): `doc/test/new verb transports`** — the test verb, named, in a column the design says has no mention.
- PLAN.md:1208 (the bar definition): "the green board (`tools/state.sh`) IS the release gate."
- PLAN.md:1401-1402 (Column 5): "**CI, host-agnostic and REQUIRED**: every push → the full board."
- PLAN.md:1408 (Column 5): "the micro battery is an external oracle until the wheel's Verify subsumes it."
§11 addresses testing under its real name — the board, the micro battery, the test verb transport. The design grepped for the token "test", missed the edge, and called the miss a hole. That is the Carried-Truth Law violated on the document: look up by name, fail to follow the edge, then re-derive a "gap" the graph already connects.

**2. The design's thesis is an already-named peer, homed twice.** Band J, PLAN.md:856: "`.correctness-oracle-internal` (**the external micro-battery → the wheel's own Verify**; until then first-light's correctness half is itself an !Outside)", cited again at §11 Column 5 (:1408) and §0 (:124). The design's novelty claim — "no split-brain between what the type checker proves and what the test suite claims, because there is one obligation ledger" — IS "the external micro-battery → the wheel's own Verify," restated. It reinvents a named peer under a new name attached to a dead module.

**3. "Working" is false — zero consumers, zero performs, never run.** Repo-wide grep (excluding .build): `expect_eq` / `expect_ne` / `expect_true` / `expect_false` / `run_tests` / `run_suite` / `test_counter` / `suite_counter` occur ONLY inside lib/test.mn. The wheel never performs `check` — the sole non-lib/test.mn occurrences are a prose comment (pipeline.mn:9) and assert_reporter's own *arm declarations* (main.mn:484, 491), which are handler arms, not call sites. It typechecks only because march.sh:70-71 sweeps `find lib -name '*.mn'` into the wheel input. It has never executed.
And `mentl test` does not run tests: `test_run` (main.mn:463) calls `pipeline_check` (pipeline.mn:164 → driver_check), which typechecks and returns diagnostics; `assert_reporter` (main.mn:483) is installed over a computation performing zero Test ops. main.mn:459-461 admits it: "Execution of the test fns reaches the process boundary (Hβ.cli.process-exec-wire, shared with run); the check + reporter chain is the structural form, real now."

**4. `verify_assert` does not exist.** The claim "src/main.mn:445-460 wires test_run through assert_reporter/**verify_assert**" is false: the chain (main.mn:468-475) installs `assert_reporter` only. Grep returns exactly one occurrence of `verify_assert` in the repo — the prose comment at main.mn:450 naming it. Zero definitions, zero call sites. The same comment (main.mn:449) says "assert / assert_eq / assert_near" — no such ops exist; `effect Test` declares `check` and `get_results` (lib/test.mn:12-15). The design quoted stale prose (it cites "EH §1.3" and "DESIGN.md", both archaeology; lib/test.mn:1 still reads "**Lux** Test Framework", two renames stale) as artifact state.

**5. The gate cannot pass even if the design lands.** `node_const` (verify.mn:85-91) accepts only `NExpr(LitInt|LitFloat|LitString|LitBool)`; everything else returns `None`. verify.mn:53-54 names the boundary: "None at the undecidable boundary (a variable, **a call, a compound expr**)."
- Gate example `expect_eq(2 + 2, 4)`: `2 + 2` is a compound expr → `node_const` = None → `compare_decide` (verify.mn:74-80) = None → undecidable → nothing lifts → the dispatch remains → **the positive leg fails**.
- Gradient example `expect_eq(f(3), 9)`: a call — verify.mn:54's named boundary. Needs interprocedural const-eval, beyond even R3.
So "this reuses band F's machinery outright; zero new mechanism" and "Buildable now — band F's fragment already exists... not new proof machinery" are both false. The gate tests **R3** (PLAN.md:1354, Column 2, unlanded), not the design.

**6. The gate self-contradicts.** "Both must produce identical pass semantics when run." A lifted check never reaches `test_counter`, so it never increments `passed` (lib/test.mn:46); `run_tests` fold-sums the runtime tuples (lib/test.mn:87-91), so a lifted `expect_eq` vanishes from the tally — "1 passed" becomes "0 passed". Zero dispatch and identical pass semantics are mutually exclusive under lib/test.mn:42-53 as written. The design never says where a discharged obligation gets counted.

**7. The cited precedent inverts.** f7a1b3a5 **deleted** a special case: the `++` arm fabricated `Pure` instead of reading its callee's declared row. Its own comment: "a second home for it in infer is the re-derivation this fix exists to delete." The design cites it as license to **add** a second home — "recognize Test.check sites" — putting one stdlib effect's op name inside infer_call. That is drift 6 (primitive-special-case) + drift 8 (string-keyed), and PLAN §9's "seed's name-keyed intrinsic table" bug class. It also lands on the hot path: infer_call runs ~1e5 times per compile, and §5.O records the perf loop going 1400s→10s by *deleting* name scans.

**8. Novelty foil is dishonest.** pytest/xUnit is a trivially weak opponent. The real prior art for "an assertion over compile-time-known operands is checked at compile time" is `static_assert` (C++11), Rust const-eval, Dafny/Liquid Haskell static assertions, and above all **Zig's `comptime`**, which does this uniformly for any evaluable expression with no special-casing of a test effect. "The more of a suite's inputs are compile-time-known, the more of it becomes free" is Zig comptime restated. The design proposes a weaker, name-keyed `static_assert` and claims novelty against pytest.

**9. Blast radius unstated.** The design says nothing about m2 == m3 or any gate. lib/test.mn is in the wheel input (march.sh:70-71), so `effect Test` sits in the wheel's namespace and infer_call's behavior on it is on the self-compile path. The change is probably inert on the fixpoint (the wheel never calls check) — but the design neither verifies nor claims it.
  fixes    : **Drop the premise.** §11 has no testing hole. Column 4 (PLAN.md:1389) names the test verb transport; Column 5 (:1401-1408) makes the board REQUIRED in CI and names testing's ultimate-form peer; the bar definition (:1208) makes the green board the release gate. Any future audit of §11 must sweep all five columns before asserting an omission — the design's own note ("Column 2's full text (1278-1360) has zero occurrences of 'test'") is the whole search it ran.

**Say what Mentl's test framework actually is.** It is the micro battery, and it is already the bar: 127 files in tests/micros/, 71 wired as `micro:NAME=EXPECTED_EXIT` in tools/verify-baseline.txt, asserted by **exit code** (tools/run-micro.sh:33-34). `tests/micros/mn-A.mn` returns 6; the baseline says `micro:A=6`. A micro's `main` return value IS its assertion. No `check`, no `expect_eq`, no lib/test.mn — plus the frontier (47), proof-exactness (9), and crown (5) batteries on the same bash-driven contract.

**Correct the verdict on the peer: DISSOLVE, do not home.** `Hβ.test.lib-test-mn-substrate` should be retired the way §11's own named-peer audit retired `Hβ.cursor.session-weave-epoch-scope` ("DISSOLVED by the peer audit"). Its content already has two homes:
- Band J's `Hβ.closure.correctness-oracle-internal` (PLAN.md:856) — "the external micro-battery → the wheel's own Verify" — is the design's one-ledger thesis, verbatim.
- PLAN §6's scaffold dissolution — "the bash scaffolds (state.sh, verify.sh, run-micro.sh, drift-audit.sh — each dissolves into the medium's own where/verify/audit verbs)" — is the mechanism.
A third name for a truth two homes already hold is the side-ledger the one-home discipline forbids. Only `Hβ.test.per-test-output-buffer` (lib/test.mn:77) is a genuine entry-born residue, and its home is §7's **Named-residue index** (PLAN.md:983 — "entry-born peers not yet in a §5.R band — one home each"), DEP-tagged to band E's `Hβ.lower.schedule-specialized-callee` per its own comment. That index is the designated home the design missed; §11 POINTS at peers, it does not HOME them, so "add one Column 2 bullet" inverts the discipline.

**Correct the verdict on lib/test.mn: it is drift — delete it or make it the micro battery's home.** It is a second test substrate with zero consumers, duplicating what the board already does, with a stale header ("Lux Test Framework", citing DESIGN.md), a `mentl test` verb that typechecks instead of running, and a comment (main.mn:449) describing ops (assert/assert_eq/assert_near) that do not exist beside a handler (verify_assert) that does not exist. CLAUDE.md's rule applies without exception: "Delete, don't decorate." The stronger move is the one §6 already names — run the micro battery THROUGH it: run-micro.sh's exit-code comparison becomes `check`, verify-baseline.txt becomes the wheel's own verb, and Column 4's "test verb transport" (:1389) is the §11 item that lands it. That is a real, honest §11 correction, and it is the opposite of the design's: wire the framework to the tests that already exist, rather than add compile-time lifting to a framework nobody calls.

**If the compile-time-lifting idea survives at all, it must be rebuilt on the row, not on a name.** The native form is already named in PLAN §5 and the design misses it: **`!E`-gated speculation** — "proving an effect's ABSENCE is the license to *live-run* a candidate (only `!IO` runs freely; the rest caught by a virtualizing handler)". A `check(cond, msg)` whose `cond` carries an inferred `Pure`/`!IO` row may be live-run at compile time. The row already proves the license — Carried-Truth, no name-recognition in infer_call, no second home for Test knowledge, and it generalizes to every pure expression instead of one effect's op. That is strictly stronger than Zig's `comptime`, which needs a keyword to mark what Mentl's row already proves — and *that* is the honest novelty claim, against the right foil. It is DEP-gated on R3 (PLAN.md:1354) at minimum, and its worked example needs const-eval through a call, so it is not "buildable now."

**Fix the gate.** It must exercise the design, not R3: a lift whose operand is already a literal (`expect_eq(4, 4)`) is what verify.mn:85-91 can decide today; `2 + 2` cannot. And it must resolve the tally contradiction — state where a discharged obligation is counted, because "zero runtime dispatch" and "identical pass semantics" cannot both hold against lib/test.mn:42-53 and :87-91 as written.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.emit.shared-node-cse
  A correct-but-shallow dedup-the-decl floor for repeated pure subexpressions has shipped, but the deeper form — reading the e-graph's own congruence classes to share a node's emission, not just its declaration — is named in one comment and credited in neither band G nor §5.O.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the design's central premise is a misread: the e-graph holds no congruence table (every equivalence edge points a node at its OWN subnode or a minted literal, never at a structurally-equal sibling), so it does not compute the classes CSE needs; and the relation it does hold was already fully cashed out at lower.mn:2122, making the proposed emit-time `egraph_extract` read identity by construction. The named peer survives as a real item, but nothing the design says about its mechanism, prerequisite, or gate is true.
  objection: The premise — that the e-graph already holds a CSE-usable equivalence relation emit can read — is false in both halves, and each half is independently fatal.

(a) NO CONGRUENCE TABLE EXISTS. Equivalence edges are drawn from exactly two sites: `rewrite_to` (src/egraph.mn:111-117) and `mint_fold` (src/egraph.mn:247-252). Enumerating every `rewrite_to` call — src/egraph.mn:125, 126, 139, 140, 155, 156, 168, 180 — the target is always `lh`/`rh`, the canonical form of the node's OWN operand, or the zero literal beneath it; `mint_fold`'s target is a freshly minted literal. No site ever points a node at a structurally-equal SIBLING. The module states this itself (src/egraph.mn:15-19): congruence "falls out of reading operands' canonical forms... a≡b ⇒ f(a)≡f(b) with no congruence table, because the parent re-reads its operands live." That is upward rule-matching after a rewrite — it lets `x*1` fire once `x` was rewritten. It is NOT the (op, canonical-children) → class merge CSE requires. Two separately-authored `a*b` at different positions remain two singleton class roots, forever. So "the e-graph already computes exactly the equivalence classes a CSE pass would rediscover from scratch" is exactly backwards: the classes CSE needs are precisely the ones the e-graph does not compute. Six rules exist (five algebraic identity/absorb + const-fold); the set of non-singleton classes is the set of algebraically-simplifiable nodes, which has near-zero overlap with "repeated pure subexpressions."

(b) THE RELATION THAT EXISTS IS ALREADY SPENT. `saturate_pass` runs BEFORE lower in the pipeline spine (src/pipeline.mn:64 and :143: `... |> infer_program |> saturate_pass |> lower_program |> reachable_from_main |> executable_gate |> emit_module`), so the relation is at fixpoint before lower runs. src/lower.mn:2122 then does `let canon = egraph_extract(handle)` and threads `canon` into every LowExpr (`lower_expr_body(e, canon, span)`, src/lower.mn:2126). `egraph_extract` chases to the root (src/egraph.mn:40-45) and a root's canon pointer is itself (src/types.mn:1230: "returns the handle itself if it is its own e-class root"). Therefore for every handle emit sees, `egraph_extract(h) == h`. The design's proposed action — "asking egraph_extract for a node's canonical representative before emitting it" — provably returns its input. It is a no-op, and it is itself a Carried-Truth violation: lower proved the canonical form; the design has emit re-derive it.

The fact emit actually lacks is IN-DEGREE on the lowered DAG and a dominating bind point. The e-graph computes neither. Against the LLVM comparison the design invokes: GVN does (1) value numbering and (2) dominance placement. The design claims the e-graph makes (1) free — it does not — and never addresses (2), which is the entire remaining delta the wasm.mn:2050-2058 comment describes. The novelty verdict inverts the work: it declares free the one thing that must be built, and skips the thing actually open.
  fixes    : 1. STRIKE the factual claim. "graph_canon_set/graph_canon_at already give every pure node a canonical class via congruence closure" must become: they give every node a canonical POINTER, which is itself unless one of six rules (src/egraph.mn:122-190 identities/absorb, :247-252 const-fold) fired on it. No (op, canonical-children) hashcons exists; separately-authored congruent expressions never merge.

2. STRIKE the mechanism. "Ask egraph_extract at emit" is identity by construction (see objection (b)). The mechanism must be: read IN-DEGREE on the LOWERED DAG and bind at the dominating point. The design's ONE surviving correct instinct is the composition point — src/backends/wasm.mn:284-292's `project_emit_state` already walks the LowExpr tree once with `~>`-chained visitors, and in-degree per handle is genuinely one more projection that walk threads. (Note the stale comment there: wasm.mn:286-288 says "four visitor-handlers... returns a 4-tuple" while the code destructures six — `all_fn_names, top_fn_names, string_entries, state_slots, wasi_ops, yield_seen`. Doc-truth debt at the exact line the design cites.)

3. STRIKE the DEP inversion. `Hβ.egraph.per-expr-effect-row` is NOT a soundness prerequisite. Today's gate is `is_pure(handle) = body_is_pure(handle) && row_subsumes(effs_at(handle), EfPure)` (src/egraph.mn:71-72), and `body_is_pure` (src/egraph.mn:79-89) matches `graph_node_body` on LitInt/LitFloat/LitString/LitBool/VarRef/BinOpExpr with `_ => false` — that is AST shape, exactly what the design says it is not. The design asserted the POST-peer state as the current state while naming the peer as prerequisite; the two claims cannot both hold. Since `body_is_pure` returns false for every call/perform/hole, `is_pure` UNDER-approximates purity, and an under-approximation shares less, never more — safe. per-expr-effect-row is a REACH multiplier, not a gate. The item is buildable without it.

4. NAME the absent structure positively: `Hβ.egraph.congruence-hashcons` — the (op, canonical-children) → class merge that would let two separately-authored congruent expressions share. Absent today; the design assumed it. THIS is the band-G (arm 1) item. Once separated, the emit-placement item is not an e-graph item at all, which dissolves the design's dual-home justification — its claim to be "the positive contrast case" to the five one-home violations is earned by the misclassification, not despite it.

5. FIX the gate — it is self-contradictory. It demands emitted WAT bytes shrink AND `m2 == m3` hold. An emit change is definitionally the TRANSITION case: tools/march.sh:11 ("TRANSITION (m3 == m4 — an emit/import change crossed one generation; re-pin boot from m3)") and :119-131; PLAN §5.R band D's io entry states it outright — "the transition form is for EMIT changes only." Correct assertion: m2 ≠ m3, m3 == m4, re-pin boot from m3. As written the gate rules BROKEN on a correct landing or blesses the wrong generation.

6. FIX the gate's reach. "N syntactically-identical pure subexpressions repeated across a body" measures ZERO reduction under the artifact — N singleton classes, no sharing. The design's own gate refutes the design. The corrected gate: a body where lower's canonicalization actually produces in-degree ≥ 2 (the `$call_<h>` three-position case wasm.mn:2050-2058 observed empirically), measure emitted bytes, assert m3 == m4.

7. ADD the second-canonicalization hazard the design ignores. src/lower.mn:2113-2121 documents the authored-hole carve-out: canonicalizing first "discards the authored truth and the surviving hole slips the gate," so `NHole(_) => LConst(handle, LInt(0))` keeps its own handle. A second canonicalization site at emit must re-implement that carve-out or silently break proof-exactness 9/9 (E_UnresolvedHole). One home for canonicalization: lower.

8. CORRECT the novelty verdict. The genuine Mentl-native edge over egg is effect-gated rewriting (src/egraph.mn:21-26: "egg and every other e-graph rewrite syntactically; Mentl reads each operand's effect ROW... and fires a DROPPING rewrite only when the dropped operand is provably Pure") — and it already landed, and it governs DROPPING, not sharing. The design borrows its prestige for an item it does not cover. The honest verdict: the equivalence half of CSE is NOT free in Mentl today, and the placement half is what the item actually is.
==============================================================================
REFUTED: Hβ.infer.pcompose-branch-stage-type
  E_BranchNotStage is a fully built, tested, SYNTAX.md-documented diagnostic — not a code gap but a roadmap bookkeeping gap — and it shows PLAN's exhaustiveness claim already under-counts finished work, the more dangerous direction to be wrong in.
  verdict  : REFUTED — the binary contradicts the design's load-bearing premise ("not a code gap but a roadmap bookkeeping gap"): E_BranchNotStage is neither tested nor correct, so the proposed "LANDED" line would canonize a false closure in PLAN.md in exactly the optimistic-but-wrong direction the design's own novelty verdict names as the more dangerous one.
  objection: `check_branch_is_stage` (src/infer.mn:1614-1634) short-circuits on an AST FORM match — `match body { NExpr(PipeExpr(_,_,_)) => true, _ => false }` (infer.mn:1616-1620) — before ever reaching the graph read at 1622-1628. Probed through the pinned boot (boot/mentl.wasm):

  (dbl(7)) >< (dbl(8))     -> infer: E_BranchNotStage error: `><` branch is a value, not a stage at 4:15-4:25   (x2)
  (7 |> dbl) >< (8 |> dbl) -> clean

Both emit BYTE-IDENTICAL WAT (md5 2890ec21efc3567b0cfc9714dc83eeb4, 5772 bytes each), both run to exit 30. Same program, same bytes, same answer, opposite verdicts. Three sources say this cannot be:
  - SYNTAX.md:617 — "Branch validity is a TYPE fact, not a layout fact."
  - SYNTAX.md's hole-completion law (§«Partial application») — "The `|>` pipe is hole-completion, not a rewrite", so `x |> f(a)` and `f(a,x)` are ONE graph. The identical md5 is that law holding while the check denies it.
  - infer.mn:1611-1613's own comment — "The check reads the branch node shape AND its inferred type live — never a parse-time form commitment (the parser never inspects branch shape)." The parser doesn't; infer does, at 1616-1620. It is the eager-form-commitment drift the comment disclaims by name.

So the peer carries a live false-positive class. Writing "LANDED" into PLAN.md — the document CLAUDE.md @-imports as substance every session, and the document the destiny audit's pillar percentages are computed off — asserts closure for a check that misfires on the artifact's own canonical example. The design commits the error it diagnoses.

The design's true claims, verified: report(EBranchNotStage(span)) at infer.mn:1631 inside infer_fanout's FanDistribute arm; the peer cited by name at infer.mn:1551; types.mn:1315/1383/1434/1485/1597 all as stated; SYNTAX.md:620 (prose) and :1978 (Hard Errors table) both cite it; `grep -c pcompose-branch-stage-type PLAN.md` = 0. The bookkeeping gap is real. Its content is not.

Attempted and disproved (recorded so it is not re-run): I first read "hard error that emits anyway" as a defect — E_BranchNotStage sits in SYNTAX.md's Hard Errors table yet compiles with exit 0 and 4909 bytes of WAT. Probing `1 + "hello"` shows E_TypeMismatch behaves identically (exit 0, 2624 bytes). That is the house productive-under-error norm; only E_UnresolvedHole carries the refuse-and-emit-nothing gate. Not an objection. Also disproved: `(extract_x) >< (extract_y)`, SYNTAX.md's own inline example, compiles clean (5581 bytes) — the TFun arm works.
  fixes    : 1. DELETE "LANDED"; the entry is OPEN. Zero gates exercise it: `grep -rln 'BranchNotStage|branch_is_stage|branch-stage' tests/ tools/ lib/ ide/` returns nothing. All three `><` micros (tests/micros/mn-fanout-{seq,simd,persist}.mn) use PipeExpr branches — mn-fanout-seq.mn is `(7 |> dbl) >< (8 |> dbl)` — so `is_pipe` short-circuits true and infer.mn:1631 is never reached. The 71 micros, frontier 47/0, proof-exactness 9/9 and crown 5/5 all pass without executing the report. PLAN §11's own bar: "Each item below is BUILT (landing whole, gated, marched) or the label does not apply." Law 7: "write the gate that exercises the NEW path." LANDED does not apply. The ITEM's "fully built, tested" and "not a code gap" are both false.

2. DELETE the four file:line coordinates (infer.mn:1631, types.mn:1315/1485/1597). No entry in the index (PLAN.md:983-1000) carries any — each is a peer name plus a prose parenthetical. Coordinates are snapshots that go stale on the first insertion above infer.mn:1631; PLAN §7 is explicit that "each line is a POINTER". The peer's home is already the code comment (infer.mn:1551) and SYNTAX.md:620/1978; a fourth citation with copied coordinates is the side-ledger the one-home discipline forbids.

3. The cited precedent is misread. `Hβ.own.region-return-transfer` is in the index because it has an OPEN REMAINDER — "the caller-side re-tag under region polymorphism is the arena increment"; "LANDED at the check" is context for the open half, not a convention for closed work. The header (PLAN.md:983) reads "entry-born peers NOT YET in a §5.R band". A wholly-closed peer with zero remainder is not residue; landed work's home is the landing ledger. The design's interrogation ("complete and consistent... a pure one-line addition with no design risk") is exactly what the artifact refutes.

4. The honest entry, pointer-shaped and open: `Hβ.infer.pcompose-branch-stage-type` (the check reads the FORM — the PipeExpr arm short-circuits before the graph read — so a pipe branch and its byte-identical call form draw opposite verdicts; no gate reaches the report).

5. REPLACE the gate. `grep -c "pcompose-branch-stage-type" PLAN.md` checks that a string was typed, not that its claim is true — it passes identically whether LANDED is true or false, so it cannot fail on the design's own failure mode. The gate that earns an entry compiles both forms and asserts the SAME verdict; it FAILS today (2 diagnostics vs 0). Prerequisite: tools/run-micro.sh:23 `diags=$(grep -c '^[EW]_' "$base.err")` never matches the real `infer: E_… error: …` format — my `(1) >< (2)` probe reports `PASS … diags=0` while emitting two hard errors. The diag counter is structurally dead, so no added micro would gate this until the pattern is fixed. That dead counter is plausibly why "tested" looked true.

6. Do not present the fix as mechanical. `infer_fanout_value_boundary` (infer.mn:1568-1600) thunks each branch as `() -> carrier` and tuples the RESULT types (1556), so a legitimate pipeline branch's type is a plain value type (Int for `7 |> dbl`). A type-only check therefore REJECTS the green micro mn-fanout-seq.mn. The `is_pipe` arm exists precisely to exempt pipelines from the type test — the check is two unrelated predicates OR'd, not one type fact. So "read the type" is not a one-line deletion: SYNTAX.md's stage-typed law and the implemented thunk-and-tuple semantics disagree about what a `><` branch IS, and whether `(1) >< (2)` — two independent constant computations, tupled — violates any kernel correspondence at all (governing principle 4) is unresolved. That disagreement is the peer's real content; asserting LANDED buries it.

7. Blast radius, corrected. The design is right that a PLAN.md-only edit cannot move m2 == m3. It under-reads the radius it has: CLAUDE.md @-imports PLAN.md, so a false LANDED loads as substance every session, and the roadmap is the input to the destiny audit's pillar scoring — the precise corruption channel the design's novelty verdict identifies and then feeds.
